## À Ma Mère / 献给我的母亲

## Prologue / 序曲

## I Paris: 1783 / 一 巴黎：1783 年

===Original===
“Coward! Coward! Coward!”

The words rang out, clear, strident, passionate, in a crescendo of agonised humiliation.

The boy, quivering with rage, had sprung to his feet, and, losing his balance, he fell forward clutching at the table, whilst with a convulsive movement of the lids, he tried in vain to suppress the tears of shame which were blinding him.

“Coward!” He tried to shout the insult so that all might hear, but his parched throat refused him service, his trembling hand sought the scattered cards upon the table, he collected them together, quickly, nervously, fingering them with feverish energy, then he hurled them at the man opposite, whilst with a final effort he still contrived to mutter: “Coward!”

===Chinese===
“懦夫！懦夫！懦夫！”

那喊声回荡开来，清晰、尖锐、激愤，带着饱受屈辱的痛楚层层升高。

少年气得浑身发抖，霍地站起身来，身子一晃失去了平衡，向前扑倒在桌上；他眼皮一阵痉挛，竭力想忍住羞愤的泪水，却终究无济于事，泪水已模糊了他的双眼。

“懦夫！”他想把这句辱骂喊得人人听见，可干渴的喉咙不听使唤。他颤抖的手在桌上搜寻散乱的纸牌，飞快地、神经质地把它们拢作一处，手指发狂似的捻弄着，随即朝对面那人掷去，同时拼上最后的力气，仍旧挤出一句嘶哑的低语：“懦夫！”

===Original===
The older men tried to interpose, but the young ones only laughed, quite prepared for the adventure which must inevitably ensue, the only possible ending to a quarrel such as this.

Conciliation or arbitration was out of the question. Déroulède should have known better than to speak disrespectfully of Adèle de Montchéri, when the little Vicomte de Marny’s infatuation for the notorious beauty had been the talk of Paris and Versailles these many months past.

Adèle was very lovely and a veritable tower of greed and egotism. The Marnys were rich and the little Vicomte very young, and just now the brightly-plumaged hawk was busy plucking the latest pigeon, newly arrived from its ancestral cote.

The boy was still in the initial stage of his infatuation. To him Adèle was a paragon of all the virtues, and he would have done battle on her behalf against the entire aristocracy of France, in a vain endeavour to justify his own exalted opinion of one of the most dissolute women of the epoch. He was a first-rate swordsman too, and his friends had already learned that it was best to avoid all allusions to Adèle’s beauty and weaknesses.

===Chinese===
年长些的人想要劝解，年轻人却只是哄笑，早就存了心思，等着看这场必然接踵而至的冒险——像这样的争吵，本就只有一种可能的结局。

调解或仲裁根本无从谈起。小马尔尼子爵（Vicomte de Marny）迷恋那位声名狼藉的美人，已是好几个月来巴黎和凡尔赛街谈巷议的话头；德鲁莱德（Déroulède）竟还口出无礼之言，冒犯阿黛尔·德·蒙谢里（Adèle de Montchéri），这就怪不得旁人了。

阿黛尔生得千娇百媚，骨子里却是一座贪婪与自私垒起的高塔。马尔尼家财雄势大，小子爵又年少无知；此刻，这只羽色鲜亮的鹰隼正忙着宰拔最新到手的鸽子——一只刚刚离开祖巢的雏鸽。

少年还沉在痴迷的头一阵热浪里。在他眼中，阿黛尔集万般美德于一身；哪怕要与法兰西全体贵族为敌，他也会为她挺剑而战，徒劳地想为自己对这位当世最放荡女人之一的崇高评价辩护。他偏偏又是一流的剑客，朋友们早已学乖：对阿黛尔的美貌与品行，最好讳莫如深。

===Original===
But Déroulède was a noted blunderer. He was little versed in the manners and tones of that high society in which, somehow, he still seemed an intruder. But for his great wealth, no doubt, he never would have been admitted within the intimate circle of aristocratic France. His ancestry was somewhat doubtful and his coat-of-arms unadorned with quarterings.

But little was known of his family or the origin of its wealth; it was only known that his father had suddenly become the late King’s dearest friend, and commonly surmised that Déroulède gold had on more than one occasion filled the emptied coffers of the First Gentleman of France.

Déroulède had not sought the present quarrel. He had merely blundered in that clumsy way of his, which was no doubt a part of the inheritance bequeathed to him by his bourgeois ancestry.

He knew nothing of the little Vicomte’s private affairs, still less of his relationship with Adèle, but he knew enough of the world and enough of Paris to be acquainted with the lady’s reputation. He hated at all times to speak of women. He was not what in those days would be termed a ladies’ man, and was even somewhat unpopular with the sex. But in this instance the conversation had drifted in that direction, and when Adèle’s name was mentioned, everyone became silent, save the little Vicomte, who waxed enthusiastic.

===Chinese===
可德鲁莱德是个出了名的冒失鬼。上流社会的那套做派与腔调，他知之甚少；不知怎的，他在这圈子里始终像个不速的闯入者。若非家资巨万，他无疑永远跨不进法兰西贵族那个亲密的小圈子。他的家世来历有些可疑，纹章上也没有四分纹饰可以夸耀。

至于他的家族、他家财富的来路，世人所知甚少；人们只知道他父亲一夜之间成了先王最亲近的朋友，又纷纷揣测：德鲁莱德家的金子，不止一次填满过法兰西第一绅士空空如也的库房。

这场争吵并非德鲁莱德有意寻来。他不过是以他那种笨拙方式失了言——那无疑是他市民祖先留给他的遗产的一部分。

小子爵的私事他一无所知，子爵与阿黛尔的关系他更不知情；但他阅世够深，对巴黎也够熟识，知道那位女士的名声。他素来厌恶谈论女人。他不是当年所谓情场得意的翩翩男子，在女性中间甚至颇不讨好。可这一回，话头偏偏漂向了那个方向；阿黛尔的名字一被提起，众人都噤了声，唯有小子爵兴致勃发。

===Original===
A shrug of the shoulders on Déroulède’s part had aroused the boy’s ire, then a few casual words, and, without further warning, the insult had been hurled and the cards thrown in the older man’s face.

Déroulède did not move from his seat. He sat erect and placid, one knee crossed over the other, his serious, rather swarthy face perhaps a shade paler than usual: otherwise it seemed as if the insult had never reached his ears, or the cards struck his cheek.

He had perceived his blunder, just twenty seconds too late. Now he was sorry for the boy and angered with himself, but it was too late to draw back. To avoid a conflict he would at this moment have sacrificed half his fortune, but not one particle of his dignity.

He knew and respected the old Duc de Marny, a feeble old man now, almost a dotard whose hitherto spotless *blason*, the young Vicomte, his son, was doing his best to besmirch.

When the boy fell forward, blind and drunk with rage, Déroulède leant towards him automatically, quite kindly, and helped him to his feet. He would have asked the lad’s pardon for his own thoughtlessness, had that been possible: but the stilted code of so-called honour forbade so logical a proceeding. It would have done no good, and could but imperil his own reputation without averting the traditional sequel.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德耸了耸肩，惹得少年火起；接着是几句随口的话，再无半点先兆，辱骂便劈头掷来，纸牌也摔在了这位年长者的脸上。

德鲁莱德没有离开座位。他端坐不动，神色平静，一条腿搭在另一条腿上，那张严肃而微黑的脸或许比平日白了一分：除此之外，仿佛那句辱骂从未入耳，那把纸牌也不曾打上他的面颊。

他察觉了自己的失言，只是迟了二十秒。此刻他为那少年难过，也对自己恼怒，但要抽身已来不及。倘若能免去这场交锋，他此刻宁可舍去半份家财——唯独尊严，一丝一毫也不能让。

他敬重老公爵马尔尼——如今已是衰颓老人，几乎老糊涂了；而他那面迄今一尘不染的*纹章*（blason），正被他的儿子、年轻的子爵拼命抹黑。

少年扑倒在桌上，气得目盲心醉，德鲁莱德不由自主地朝他俯过身去，颇为和善地扶他站直。若有可能，他本想为自己这番欠考虑的言语向少年赔罪：可是那套矫情的所谓荣誉准则，容不得如此合乎情理的举动。赔罪于事无补，非但避不开那传统的一环，反倒会搭上他自己的名声。

===Original===
The panelled walls of the celebrated gaming saloon had often witnessed scenes such as this. All those present acted by routine. The etiquette of duelling prescribed certain formalities, and these were strictly but rapidly adhered to.

The young Vicomte was quickly surrounded by a close circle of friends. His great name, his wealth, his father’s influence, had opened for him every door in Versailles and Paris. At this moment he might have had an army of seconds to support him in the coming conflict.

Déroulède for a while was left alone near the card table, where the unsnuffed candles began smouldering in their sockets. He had risen to his feet, somewhat bewildered at the rapid turn of events. His dark, restless eyes wandered for a moment round the room, as if in quick search for a friend.

But where the Vicomte was at home by right, Déroulède had only been admitted by reason of his wealth. His acquaintances and sycophants were many, but his friends very few.

For the first time this fact was brought home to him. Everyone in the room must have known and realised that he had not wilfully sought this quarrel, that throughout he had borne himself as any gentleman would, yet now, when the issue was so close at hand, no one came forward to stand by him.

===Chinese===
那间大名鼎鼎的赌厅，四壁护板早已见惯了这样的场面。在场诸人各按惯例行事。决斗的礼节自有规定的一套章程，众人一丝不苟又急速地照办起来。

小子爵身边很快围拢了一群密友。他显赫的姓氏、万贯的家财、父亲的势力，早已为他敲开凡尔赛与巴黎的每一扇门。此刻若他愿意，替他压阵的副手尽可以拉出一支队伍来。

德鲁莱德被独自留在了牌桌旁，桌上未剪烛芯的蜡烛开始在烛座里闷烧冒烟。他站起身来，对事态的急转直下有些发懵。他那双乌黑不安的眼睛在屋里逡巡了片刻，仿佛在急急寻找一位朋友。

可子爵在此地是名正言顺的主人，德鲁莱德不过是凭钱财才被容许进来。他的相识与趋奉者很多，朋友却寥寥无几。

这一回，他头一次把这件事看进了心里。屋中人人想必都清楚、都明白：这场争吵并非他蓄意挑起，他自始至终的举止也无愧于任何一位绅士；可如今事到临头，却没有一个人上前站在他身边。

===Original===
“For form’s sake, monsieur, will you choose your seconds?”

It was the young Marquis de Villefranche who spoke, a little haughtily, with a certain ironical condescension towards the rich parvenu, who was about to have the honour of crossing swords with one of the noblest gentlemen in France.

“I pray you, Monsieur le Marquis,” rejoined Déroulède coldly, “to make the choice for me. You see, I have few friends in Paris.”

The Marquis bowed, and gracefully flourished his lace handkerchief. He was accustomed to being appealed to in all matters pertaining to etiquette, to the toilet, to the latest cut in coats, and the procedure in duels. Good-natured, foppish, and idle, he felt quite happy and in his element thus to be made chief organiser of the tragic farce, about to be enacted on the parquet floor of the gaming saloon.

He looked about the room for a while, scrutinising the faces of those around him. The gilded youth was crowding round De Marny; a few older men stood in a group at the farther end of the room: to these the Marquis turned, and addressing one of them, an elderly man with a military bearing and a shabby brown coat:

===Chinese===
“先生，循例请问：您的副手选定了没有？”

说话的是年轻的维尔弗兰什侯爵（Marquis de Villefranche），语气略带倨傲，对这位即将荣膺同法兰西最高贵绅士之一交锋的富有的暴发户，摆出几分讥诮的俯就。

“还请侯爵先生，”德鲁莱德冷冷答道，“替我选定吧。您知道，我在巴黎没有几个朋友。”

侯爵鞠了一躬，优雅地挥了挥他的蕾丝手帕。凡是关乎礼节、关乎梳妆、关乎外套最新式样、关乎决斗章程的事，人们素来都要来请教他。他脾气好、爱打扮、又清闲，此刻被推为这场悲喜闹剧的总司仪，即将在赌厅的拼花地板上开演——他着实觉得快活，如鱼得水。

他在屋内环顾了片刻，仔细打量周围的面孔。一帮纨绔子弟正围着德·马尔尼；几位年长者站在屋子那头，聚作一处。侯爵朝他们转过身去，向其中一人开口——那是个上了年纪的人，一派军人风度，穿着一件寒素的褐色外套：

===Original===
“Mon Colonel,” he said, with another flourishing bow; “I am deputed by M. Déroulède to provide him with seconds for this affair of honour, may I call upon you to﻿—”

“Certainly, certainly,” replied the Colonel. “I am not intimately acquainted with M. Déroulède, but since you stand sponsor, M. le Marquis﻿—”

“Oh!” rejoined the Marquis, lightly, “a mere matter of form, you know. M. Déroulède belongs to the entourage of Her Majesty. He is a man of honour. But I am not his sponsor. Marny is my friend, and if you prefer not to﻿—”

“Indeed I am entirely at M. Déroulède’s service,” said the Colonel, who had thrown a quick, scrutinising glance at the isolated figure near the card table, “if he will accept my services﻿—”

“He will be very glad to accept, my dear Colonel,” whispered the Marquis with an ironical twist of his aristocratic lips. “He has no friends in our set, and if you and De Quettare will honour him, I think he should be grateful.”

===Chinese===
“我的上校，”他说着又风风光光地鞠了一躬，“德鲁莱德先生委托我为这场关乎荣誉的事端替他延请副手，不知可否有劳您——”

“当然，当然，”上校答道，“我与德鲁莱德先生并无深交，但既然侯爵先生出面作保——”

“哦！”侯爵轻描淡写地接道，“不过是走个形式罢了，您知道。德鲁莱德先生在王后陛下侍从之列，是位有荣誉的人。可我并不是他的保人。马尔尼是我的朋友，您若是不情愿——”

“我悉听德鲁莱德先生差遣，”上校说着，朝牌桌旁那个孤单的身影飞快而审视地瞥了一眼，“只要他肯接受我的效力——”

“他会非常乐意接受的，亲爱的上校，”侯爵压低了嗓音，贵族的嘴角讥诮地一撇，“他在我们这圈子里没有朋友，您和德·克塔尔（De Quettare）若肯赏光，我想他该感激不尽。”

===Original===
M. de Quettare, adjutant to M. le Colonel, was ready to follow in the footsteps of his chief, and the two men, after the prescribed salutations to M. le Marquis de Villefranche, went across to speak to Déroulède.

“If you will accept our services, monsieur,” began the Colonel abruptly, “mine, and my adjutant’s, M. de Quettare, we place ourselves entirely at your disposal.”

“I thank you, messieurs,” rejoined Déroulède. “The whole thing is a farce, and that young man is a fool; but I have been in the wrong and﻿—”

“You would wish to apologise?” queried the Colonel icily.

The worthy soldier had heard something of Déroulède’s reputed bourgeois ancestry. This suggestion of an apology was no doubt in accordance with the customs of the middle-classes, but the Colonel literally gasped at the unworthiness of the proceeding. An apology? Bah! Disgusting! cowardly! beneath the dignity of any gentleman, however wrong he might be. How could two soldiers of His Majesty’s army identify themselves with such doings?

===Chinese===
德·克塔尔先生是上校先生的副官，自然乐于追随长官的脚步。两人向维尔弗兰什侯爵先生行过了例行的礼节，便走过去同德鲁莱德交涉。

“先生，您若肯接受我们的效力，”上校唐突地开口，“我与我的副官德·克塔尔先生，悉听您的调遣。”

“多谢二位，先生们，”德鲁莱德答道，“这整桩事是一场闹剧，那个年轻人是个傻瓜；可错的是我，而且——”

“您是想赔罪？”上校冷冰冰地问。

这位可敬的军人约略听说过德鲁莱德那以市民出身著称的家世传闻。赔罪的建议无疑符合中产阶层的规矩，可上校听见这等不体面的行径，几乎倒抽一口冷气。赔罪？呸！恶心！怯懦！无论错到什么地步，都有失任何绅士的体面。陛下军中的两名军人，怎能与这等行径同流？

===Original===
But Déroulède seemed unconscious of the enormity of his suggestion.

“If I could avoid a conflict,” he said, “I would tell the Vicomte that I had no knowledge of his admiration for the lady we were discussing and﻿—”

“Are you so very much afraid of getting a sword scratch, monsieur?” interrupted the Colonel impatiently, whilst M. de Quettare elevated a pair of aristocratic eyebrows in bewilderment at such an extraordinary display of bourgeois cowardice.

“You mean, Monsieur le Colonel?”﻿—queried Déroulède.

“That you must either fight the Vicomte de Marny tonight, or clear out of Paris tomorrow. Your position in our set would become untenable,” retorted the Colonel, not unkindly, for in spite of Déroulède’s extraordinary attitude, there was nothing in his bearing or his appearance that suggested cowardice or fear.

“I bow to your superior knowledge of your friends, M. le Colonel,” responded Déroulède, as he silently drew his sword from its sheath.

===Chinese===
可德鲁莱德似乎全然不知自己这建议有多骇人听闻。

“若能避免一场厮杀，”他说，“我会告诉子爵，我全然不知他倾慕着方才谈及的那位女士，而且——”

“先生，您就这么怕剑尖划出一道口子吗？”上校不耐烦地打断他；德·克塔尔先生扬起一对贵族式的眉毛，对这般登峰造极的市民式怯懦大惑不解。

“上校先生，您这话是什么意思？”德鲁莱德问道。

“意思是：您要么今晚同马尔尼子爵决斗，要么明天就滚出巴黎。不然，您在我们这个圈子里的地位就站不住了，”上校回敬道，语气倒并不刻薄；因为尽管德鲁莱德的态度出奇，他的举止仪表却看不出半点怯懦或惧意。

“您比谁都了解您的那些朋友，我领教了，上校先生，”德鲁莱德应道，一边默默把剑从鞘中抽出。

===Original===
The centre of the saloon was quickly cleared. The seconds measured the length of the swords and then stood behind the antagonists, slightly in advance of the groups of spectators, who stood massed all round the room.

They represented the flower of what France had of the best and noblest in name, in lineage, in chivalry, in that year of grace 1783. The storm-cloud which a few years hence was destined to break over their heads, sweeping them from their palaces to the prison and the guillotine, was only gathering very slowly in the dim horizon of squalid, starving Paris: for the next half-dozen years they would still dance and gamble, fight and flirt, surround a tottering throne, and hoodwink a weak monarch. The Fates’ avenging sword still rested in its sheath; the relentless, ceaseless wheel still bore them up in their whirl of pleasure; the downward movement had only just begun: the cry of the oppressed children of France had not yet been heard above the din of dance music and lovers’ serenades.

The young Duc de Châteaudun was there, he who, nine years later, went to the guillotine on that cold September morning, his hair dressed in the latest fashion, the finest Mechlin lace around his wrists, playing a final game of piquet with his younger brother, as the tumbril bore them along through the hooting, yelling crowd of the half-naked starvelings of Paris.

There was the Vicomte de Mirepoix, who, a few years later, standing on the platform of the guillotine, laid a bet with M. de Miranges that his own blood would flow bluer than that of any other head cut off that day in France. Citizen Samson heard the bet made, and when De Mirepoix’s head fell into the basket, the headsman lifted it up for M. de Miranges to see. The latter laughed.

===Chinese===
赌厅中央很快清了出来。两位副手量了量两柄剑的长短，随即立到决斗双方身后，稍稍前出于围观的人群；看客们密密匝匝，站满了屋子四周。

在主历 1783 年，他们代表着法兰西名号、门第与骑士风度中最优秀最高贵的精英。几年后注定要劈到他们头顶的那团风暴之云——要把他们从宫殿卷进监狱、送上断头台的那团云——此刻才在肮脏饥饿的巴黎那昏暗的地平线上缓缓聚集：此后六年间，他们还将跳舞、豪赌、决斗、调情，簇拥着摇摇欲坠的王座，蒙蔽着一位软弱的君王。命运女神的复仇之剑仍在鞘中；那无情的、不停转动的轮子仍载着他们在欢愉的漩涡里浮沉；下坠的轨迹才刚刚开始：被欺凌的法兰西儿女的哭喊，尚未能盖过舞曲与恋人小夜曲的喧闹，被人听见。

年轻的肖托丹公爵（Duc de Châteaudun）也在场。九年之后，正是在那个九月的寒晨，他发型梳得入时，腕上罩着最精美的梅赫伦蕾丝（Mechlin lace），同幼弟玩着最后一局皮克牌（piquet），被囚车押着，穿过巴黎那些半裸饥民嘘声哭喊的夹道人潮，走向断头台。

米尔普瓦子爵（Vicomte de Mirepoix）也在。几年后，他站在断头台的行刑台上，同德·米朗热（M. de Miranges）打赌，说自己流出的血定比那一天在法兰西被砍下的任何头颅的血都要蓝。桑松公民（Citizen Samson）亲耳听了这场赌约；当米尔普瓦的头颅落进箩筐，刽子手便把它高高举起，给德·米朗热看。后者大笑。

===Original===
“Mirepoix was always a braggart,” he said lightly, as he laid his head upon the block.

“Who’ll take my bet that my blood turns out to be bluer than his?”

But of all these comedies, these tragico-farces of later years, none who were present on that night, when the Vicomte de Marny fought Paul Déroulède, had as yet any presentiment.

They watched the two men fighting, with the same casual interest, at first, which they would have bestowed on the dancing of a new movement in the minuet.

De Marny came of a race that had wielded the sword of many centuries, but he was hot, excited, not a little addled with wine and rage. Déroulède was lucky; he would come out of the affair with a slight scratch.

===Chinese===
“米尔普瓦向来是个牛皮匠，”他把头搁上砧板时，轻描淡写地说。

“我的血会比他的更蓝——谁跟我赌？”

然而那一夜，当马尔尼子爵与保罗·德鲁莱德交剑之时，在场诸人对日后这一出出喜剧、一场场悲闹剧，尚无半分预感。

起初，他们看着两人过招，兴致淡然，就像看小步舞里新添的一个舞步。

德·马尔尼出身于好几个世纪以来世代仗剑的家族，可他此刻头脑发热、情绪激愤，被酒气和怒火烧得晕头转向。德鲁莱德运气不错，他本可以从这场事端里只带出一道浅浅的划伤。

===Original===
A good swordsman too, that wealthy parvenu. It was interesting to watch his swordplay: very quiet at first, no feint or parry, scarcely a riposte, only en garde, always en garde very carefully, steadily, ready for his antagonist at every turn and in every circumstance.

Gradually the circle round the combatants narrowed. A few discreet exclamations of admiration greeted Déroulède’s most successful parry. De Marny was getting more and more excited, the older man more and more sober and reserved.

A thoughtless lunge placed the little Vicomte at his opponent’s mercy. The next instant he was disarmed, and the seconds were pressing forward to end the conflict.

Honour was satisfied: the parvenu and the scion of the ancient race had crossed swords over the reputation of one of the most dissolute women in France. Déroulède’s moderation was a lesson to all the hotheaded young bloods who toyed with their lives, their honour, their reputation as lightly as they did with their lace-edged handkerchiefs and gold snuffboxes.

Already Déroulède had drawn back. With the gentle tact peculiar to kindly people, he avoided looking at his disarmed antagonist. But something in the older man’s attitude seemed to further nettle the over-stimulated sensibility of the young Vicomte.

===Chinese===
那位富有的暴发户剑术也着实高明。看他的剑法很有意思：起初沉静得很，不虚晃，不格挡，几乎不还击，只是摆好架势（en garde），始终小心翼翼、稳稳当当地摆着架势，无论对手如何变化、形势如何变幻，都严阵以待。

决斗者周围的圈子渐渐收拢。德鲁莱德几记最漂亮的格挡，赢得几声克制的赞叹。德·马尔尼越来越激愤，年长者却越来越清醒、越缄默。

小子爵一记欠考虑的直刺，把自己送到了对手的掌握之中。转瞬之间，他的剑已被击落，两位副手忙不迭地挤上前，要结束这场交锋。

荣誉已得满足：暴发户与古老世家的裔孙，已为法兰西最放荡的女人之一的名声交过了剑。那些轻薄的少年纨绔，把性命、荣誉、名声当蕾丝镶边的手帕、金鼻烟壶一般随意摆弄；德鲁莱德此刻的节制，正给他们上了一课。

德鲁莱德已经收剑退开。怀着善者特有的那种温柔的体贴，他避免去看那位被缴了械的对手。可年长者这副姿态里的某种东西，似乎反倒进一步刺痛了小子爵那根被撩拨到极致的神经。

===Original===
“This is no child’s play, monsieur,” he said excitedly. “I demand full satisfaction.”

“And are you not satisfied?” queried Déroulède. “You have borne yourself bravely, you have fought in honour of your liege lady. I, on the other hand﻿—”

“You,” shouted the boy hoarsely, “you shall publicly apologise to a noble and virtuous woman whom you have outraged﻿—now﻿—at﻿—once﻿—on your knees﻿—”

“You are mad, Vicomte,” rejoined Déroulède coldly. “I am willing to ask your forgiveness for my blunder﻿—”

“An apology﻿—in public﻿—on your knees﻿—”

===Chinese===
“先生，这不是儿戏，”他激动地说，“我要求十足的满足。”

“难道您还不满足吗？”德鲁莱德问道。“您表现得十分勇敢，您为效忠您尊贵的女君而战。至于我——”

“你，”少年嘶声吼道，“你要当众向一位被你侮辱的高贵贤淑的女人赔罪——现在——立刻——马上——跪着——”

“您疯了，子爵，”德鲁莱德冷冷答道，“我的失言，我愿意求您宽恕——”

“赔罪——当众——跪着——”

===Original===
The boy had become more and more excited. He had suffered humiliation after humiliation. He was a mere lad, spoilt, adulated, pampered from his boyhood: the wine had got into his head, the intoxication of rage and hatred blinded his saner judgment.

“Coward!” he shouted again and again.

His seconds tried to interpose, but he waved them feverishly aside. He would listen to no one. He saw no one save the man who had insulted Adèle, and who was heaping further insults upon her, by refusing this public acknowledgment of her virtues.

De Marny hated Déroulède at this moment with the most deadly hatred the heart of man can conceive. The older man’s calm, his chivalry, his consideration only enhanced the boy’s anger and shame.

The hubbub had become general. Everyone seemed carried away with this strange fever of enmity, which was seething in the Vicomte’s veins. Most of the young men crowded round De Marny, doing their best to pacify him. The Marquis de Villefranche declared that the matter was getting quite outside the rules.

===Chinese===
少年越来越亢奋。他自觉得受了一辱再辱。他不过是个孩子，自幼娇纵、被阿谀、被奉养惯了：酒力涌上了头，愤怒与仇恨的迷醉蒙蔽了他尚存清明的判断。

“懦夫！”他一遍又一遍地吼。

他的副手们想上前劝阻，他却发狂似的把他们挥开。他谁的话也不听，谁也看不见，只看得见那个侮辱了阿黛尔、如今又拒绝当众承认她的美德、在她身上再添一重羞辱的人。

此刻，德·马尔尼怀着人心所能燃起的最歹毒的仇恨憎着德鲁莱德。年长者的平静、他的骑士风度、他的体贴，只把少年的怒火与羞耻烧得更旺。

哗声四起，满堂沸腾。人人似乎都被这股奇异的仇怨热狂卷了去——那热狂正在子爵的血脉里翻腾。大多数年轻人围住德·马尔尼，竭力安抚他。维尔弗兰什侯爵宣称，这事已经完全出了决斗的规矩。

===Original===
No one took much notice of Déroulède. In the remote corners of the saloon a few elderly dandies were laying bets as to the ultimate issue of the quarrel.

Déroulède, however, was beginning to lose his temper. He had no friends in that room, and therefore there was no sympathetic observer there, to note the gradual darkening of his eyes, like the gathering of a cloud heavy with the coming storm.

“I pray you, messieurs, let us cease the argument,” he said at last, in a loud, impatient voice. “M. le Vicomte de Marny desires a further lesson, and, by God! he shall have it. En garde, M. le Vicomte!”

The crowd quickly drew back. The seconds once more assumed the bearing and imperturbable expression which their important function demanded. The hubbub ceased as the swords began to clash.

Everyone felt that farce was turning to tragedy.

And yet it was obvious from the first that Déroulède merely meant once more to disarm his antagonist, to give him one more lesson, a little more severe perhaps than the last. He was such a brilliant swordsman, and De Marny was so excited, that the advantage was with him from the very first.

===Chinese===
没有人多理会德鲁莱德。赌厅僻远的角落里，几个上了年纪的花花公子，正就这场争吵的最终结局下注。

然而德鲁莱德也开始按捺不住了。那间屋子里没有他的朋友，因而也没有一个怀着同情的旁观者，去留意他眼中渐渐聚起的阴翳——像一朵酝酿着暴风雨的浓云。

“诸位先生，我拜托你们，休要再争了，”他终于开口，声音高亢而不耐烦。“马尔尼子爵先生还想再领教一课，那么，上天作证！就让他领教。预备，子爵先生！”

人群迅速后退。两位副手重新摆出这一要职所要求的仪态与不动声色的表情。剑刃开始相击，哗声随之止息。

人人都觉出，闹剧正变成悲剧。

然而明眼人一开始便看得出，德鲁莱德无非想再一次缴下对手的剑，再给他上一课，或许比上一课更重些。他的剑术那样精绝，德·马尔尼又那样亢奋，优势从一开始就在他这边。

===Original===
How it all happened, nobody afterwards could say. There is no doubt that the little Vicomte’s swordplay had become more and more wild: that he uncovered himself in the most reckless way, whilst lunging wildly at his opponent’s breast, until at last, in one of these mad, unguarded moments, he seemed literally to throw himself upon Déroulède’s weapon.

The latter tried with lightning-swift motion of the wrist to avoid the fatal issue, but it was too late, and without a sigh or groan, scarce a tremor, the Vicomte de Marny fell.

The sword dropped out of his hand, and it was Déroulède himself who caught the boy in his arms.

It had all occurred so quickly and suddenly that no one had realised it all, until it was over, and the lad was lying prone on the ground, his elegant blue satin coat stained with red, and his antagonist bending over him.

There was nothing more to be done. Etiquette demanded that Déroulède should withdraw. He was not allowed to do anything for the boy whom he had so unwillingly sent to his death.

===Chinese===
一切究竟如何发生，事后无人说得清。无疑，小子爵的剑法越来越狂乱：他朝对手胸口猛刺时，破绽大开，全然不遮不护；终于，在这样一次疯狂的、门户大开的时刻，他几乎是整个扑到了德鲁莱德的剑尖上。

后者手腕闪电般一转，想避开那致命的结局，但已经太迟；马尔尼子爵倒了下去，没有一声叹息，没有一声呻吟，连一丝颤动都几乎没有。

剑从他手中脱落，是德鲁莱德自己把少年接进了怀里。

一切发生得太快、太突然，无人来得及看清全貌，直到一切已成定局：少年直挺挺躺在地上，那身雅致的蓝缎外衣浸染了红色，他的对手俯身在他上方。

再没有什么可做的了。礼节要求德鲁莱德退场。对于这个被他万般不情愿地送进死亡的孩子，谁也不许他做任何事。

===Original===
As before, no one took much notice of him. Silence, the awesome silence caused by the presence of the great Master, fell upon all those around. Only in the far corner a shrill voice was heard to say:

“I hold you at five hundred louis, Marquis. The parvenu is a good swordsman.”

The groups parted as Déroulède walked out of the room, followed by the Colonel and M. de Quettare, who stood by him to the last. Both were old and proved soldiers, both had chivalry and courage in them, with which to do tribute to the brave man whom they had seconded.

At the door of the establishment, they met the leech who had been summoned some little time ago to hold himself in readiness for any eventuality.

The great eventuality had occurred: it was beyond the leech’s learning. In the brilliantly lighted saloon above, the only son of the Duc de Marny was breathing his last, whilst Déroulède, wrapping his mantle closely round him, strode out into the dark street, all alone.

===Chinese===
同先前一样，没有谁多注意他。一片死寂笼罩了周围众人——那是大师在场才有的、令人屏息的死寂。只有远处的角落里，一个尖利的嗓音在说：

“侯爵，我押五百路易（louis），跟你赌。那暴发户剑术不坏。”

德鲁莱德走出房间时，人群分开了。上校与德·克塔尔先生跟在他身后，一直相送到最后。两人都是久经战阵的老兵，胸中自有骑士的肝胆，足以向他们所副手的勇者致敬。

在那座府邸的门口，他们迎面遇上大夫——片刻之前被请来、随时待命以防万一的大夫。

那桩最大的「万一」已经发生了：它超出了大夫的学识。楼上灯火辉煌的赌厅里，马尔尼公爵的独子正在咽气；而德鲁莱德裹紧了大氅，独自一人，大步走进漆黑的街道。

### II / 二

===Original===
The head of the house of Marny was at this time barely seventy years of age. But he had lived every hour, every minute of his life, from the day when the Grand Monarque gave him his first appointment as gentleman page in waiting when he was a mere lad, barely twelve years of age, to the moment﻿—some ten years ago now﻿—when Nature’s relentless hand struck him down in the midst of his pleasures, withered him in a flash as she does a sturdy old oak, and nailed him﻿—a cripple, almost a dotard﻿—to the invalid chair which he would only quit for his last resting place.

Juliette was then a mere slip of a girl, an old man’s child, the spoilt darling of his last happy years. She had retained some of the melancholy which had characterised her mother, the gentle lady who had endured so much so patiently, and who had bequeathed this final tender burden﻿—her baby girl﻿—to the brilliant, handsome husband whom she had so deeply loved, and so often forgiven.

When the Duc de Marny entered the final awesome stage of his gilded career, that deathlike life which he dragged on for ten years wearily to the grave, Juliette became his only joy, his one gleam of happiness in the midst of torturing memories.

In her deep, tender eyes he would see mirrored the present, the future for her, and would forget his past, with all its gaieties, its mad, merry years, that meant nothing now but bitter regrets, and endless rosary of the might-have-beens.

And then there was the boy. The little Vicomte, the future Duc de Marny, who would in *his* life and with *his* youth recreate the glory of the family, and make France once more ring with the echo of brave deeds and gallant adventures, which had made the name of Marny so glorious in camp and court.

===Chinese===
马尔尼家的家主此时年近七十。但他这一生，一个钟头、一分钟也不曾虚度：从大君主（Grand Monarque）赐下他头一个差事、让他当上侍童（gentleman page in waiting）的那一天起——那时他不过是个不满十二岁的孩子——直到如今算来已是十年前的那个时刻：自然那无情的巨手在他寻欢的正中间把他击倒，像击倒一株茁壮的老橡树，霎时间使他枯萎，把他钉在了轮椅上——一个残废，一个几乎老朽的人；那轮椅，他此后唯有进入长眠之所时才会离开。

那时朱丽叶特（Juliette）还是个纤弱的小姑娘，是老来得的女儿，是他最后几年幸福岁月里娇纵的心肝。她身上还留着她母亲那份忧郁——那位温柔的夫人，隐忍地熬过了那么多苦难，临终把这最后的柔软牵挂——她的女婴——托付给那个光彩照人、俊美出众的丈夫：她曾那样深爱、又屡屡宽恕的男人。

当马尔尼公爵踏入他锦绣生涯那最后一段森然可畏的行程——那段死气沉沉的岁月，他拖了十年，疲惫地拖向坟墓——朱丽叶特成了他唯一的欢乐，是煎熬着他的种种回忆之中唯一的一线幸福。

从她那双深沉温柔的眼睛里，他能照见现在，照见属于她的将来，于是便忘了过去——忘了那一切的欢宴、那些癫狂而快活的日子；如今那些只意味着苦涩的悔恨，意味着一串无穷无尽的「倘若当初」。

而且还有那孩子。小子爵，未来的马尔尼公爵，他将在*他*的一生里、凭*他*的青春重振家族的光荣，让法兰西再一次回荡起英勇事迹与风流壮举的回声——正是这些，曾使马尔尼的姓氏在沙场与宫廷都那样煊赫。

===Original===
The Vicomte was not his father’s love, but he was his father’s pride, and from the depths of his huge, cushioned armchair, the old man would listen with delight to stories from Versailles and Paris, the young Queen and the fascinating Lamballe, the latest play and the newest star in the theatrical firmament. His feeble, tottering mind would then take him back, along the paths of memory, to his own youth and his own triumphs, and in the joy and pride in his son, he would forget himself for the sake of the boy.

When they brought the Vicomte home that night, Juliette was the first to wake. She heard the noise outside the great gates, the coach slowly drawing up, the ring for the doorkeeper, and the sound of Matthieu’s mutterings, who never liked to be called up in the middle of the night to let anyone through the gates.

Somehow a presentiment of evil at once struck the young girl: the footsteps sounded so heavy and muffled along the flagged courtyard, and up the great oak staircase. It seemed as if they were carrying something heavy, something inert or dead.

She jumped out of bed and hastily wrapped a cloak round her thin girlish shoulders, and slipped her feet into a pair of heelless shoes, then she opened her bedroom door and looked out upon the landing.

Two men, whom she did not know, were walking upstairs abreast, two more were carrying a heavy burden, and Matthieu was behind moaning and crying bitterly.

===Chinese===
子爵不是他父亲的心头溺爱，却是他父亲的骄傲。老人陷在那把宽大的软垫扶手椅深处，津津有味地听凡尔赛和巴黎的见闻：年轻的王后、迷人的朗巴勒（Lamballe）夫人、新上演的戏、戏剧天空里最新升起的明星。他那衰弱摇曳的神智，便会沿着记忆的小径，回到他自己的青年时代、他自己的得意岁月；为着对儿子的喜悦与骄傲，他会忘了自己。

那天夜里他们把子爵送回家时，朱丽叶特第一个惊醒。她听见大门外的人声，听见马车缓缓停下，听见唤门房的铃声，还有马蒂厄（Matthieu）嘟嘟囔囔的声音——他最不喜欢深更半夜被人叫起来开门。

不知怎的，一种不祥的预感霎时攫住了少女：石板庭院里、大橡木楼梯上，那脚步声那样沉重、那样发闷。仿佛他们抬着什么沉重的东西，一件瘫软的、死了的东西。

她跳下床，慌忙把一件斗篷裹上单薄的少女肩膀，趿上一双无跟鞋，随即打开卧房的门，朝楼梯平台望去。

两个她不认识的男人并肩走上楼来，另外两个抬着一件沉重的重物，马蒂厄跟在后面，哀声呻吟，痛哭失声。

===Original===
Juliette did not move. She stood in the doorway rigid as a statue. The little cortège went past her. No one saw her, for the landings in the Hotel de Marny are very wide, and Matthieu’s lantern only threw a dim, flickering light upon the floor.

The men stopped outside the Vicomte’s room. Matthieu opened it, and then the five men disappeared within, with their heavy burden.

A moment later old Pétronelle, who had been Juliette’s nurse, and was now her devoted slave, came to her, all bathed in tears.

She had just heard the news, and she could scarcely speak, but she folded the young girl, her dear pet lamb, in her arms, and rocking herself to and fro she sobbed and eased her aching, motherly heart.

But Juliette did not cry. It was all so sudden, so awful. She, at fourteen years of age, had never dreamed of death; and now there was her brother, her Philippe, in whom she had so much joy, so much pride﻿—he was dead﻿—and her father must be told﻿—

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特没有动。她僵立在门口，像一尊石像。那一小支队伍从她身边经过。没有人看见她，因为马尔尼公馆（Hotel de Marny）的楼梯平台非常宽阔，马蒂厄的灯笼只在地上投下一点昏暗摇曳的光。

男人们在子爵房门外停下。马蒂厄开了门，随后五个人抬着那沉重的重物，一齐消失在门里。

片刻之后，老佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）来到她身边——她曾是朱丽叶特的奶妈，如今是她忠心耿耿的仆妇——已是泪人儿一般。

她刚得了消息，几乎说不出话，只把少女——她疼爱的小羊羔——搂进怀里，前后摇晃着身子，一边啜泣，一边让那颗作母亲的、痛楚的心得以疏解。

可朱丽叶特没有哭。一切来得这样突然，这样可怕。她才十四岁，从不曾梦见过死亡；而此刻，她的哥哥，她的菲利普（Philippe），那个给过她多少欢喜、多少骄傲的人——他死了——而父亲，还必须有人去告诉——

===Original===
The awfulness of this task seemed to Juliette like unto the last Judgment Day; a thing so terrible, so appalling, so impossible, that it would take a host of angels to proclaim its inevitableness.

The old cripple, with one foot in the grave, whose whole feeble mind, whose pride, whose final flicker of hope was concentrated in his boy, must be told that the lad had been brought home dead.

“Will you tell him, Pétronelle?” she asked repeatedly, during the brief intervals when the violence of the old nurse’s grief subsided somewhat.

“No﻿—no﻿—darling, I cannot﻿—I cannot﻿—” moaned Pétronelle, amidst a renewed shower of sobs.

Juliette’s entire soul﻿—a child’s soul it was﻿—rose in revolt at thought of what was before her. She felt angered with God for having put such a thing upon her. What right had He to demand a girl of her years to endure so much mental agony?

===Chinese===
这桩差事的可怕，在朱丽叶特看来不啻于末日的最后审判；一件如此恐怖、如此骇人、如此不可能的事，须得大队天使齐声宣告，才显出它的无可规避。

那个一只脚已踏进坟墓的老残废，他那衰弱的整个心神、他的骄傲、他那最后一点希望的微光，全都系在儿子身上——必须有人告诉他，孩子被抬回家了，是死的。

“你去告诉他好吗，佩特罗内勒？”在老奶妈悲恸稍缓的短暂间歇里，她一遍又一遍地问。

“不——不——心肝儿，我不能——我不能——”佩特罗内勒呻吟着，随即又落下一阵新的泪雨。

一想到眼前等着自己的是什么，朱丽叶特的整个灵魂——那原是个孩子的灵魂——便造起反来。她恼恨上帝，恨他把这样的事压到她身上。他有什么权利，要求她这样年纪的女孩去忍受这般精神上的酷刑？

===Original===
To lose her brother, and to witness her father’s grief! She couldn’t! she couldn’t! she couldn’t! God was evil and unjust!

A distant tinkle of a bell made all her nerves suddenly quiver. Her father was awake then? He had heard the noise, and was ringing his bell to ask for an explanation of the disturbance.

With one quick movement Juliette jerked herself free from the nurse’s arms, and before Pétronelle could prevent her, she had run out of the room, straight across the dark landing to a large panelled door opposite.

The old Duc de Marny was sitting on the edge of his bed, with his long, thin legs dangling helplessly to the ground.

Crippled as he was, he had struggled to this upright position, he was making frantic, miserable efforts to raise himself still further. He, too, had heard the dull thud of feet, the shuffling gait of men when carrying a heavy burden.

===Chinese===
失去哥哥，还要亲眼看着父亲的悲恸！她受不了！她受不了！她受不了！上帝是邪恶的，是不公的！

远处一声铃响，她每根神经霎时战栗起来。这么说，父亲醒了？他听见了响动，正摇铃要人解释这阵骚动。

朱丽叶特猛地一挣，从奶妈怀里脱出身来；不等佩特罗内勒阻拦，她已跑出房间，径直穿过昏暗的平台，奔向对面一扇高大的嵌板门。

老马尔尼公爵正坐在床沿上，一双细长的腿悬着，无助地垂向地面。

虽是残废之躯，他已挣扎着撑成这个直立的姿势，还在疯狂而凄惨地用力，想把自己再撑高些。他也听见了那沉闷的脚步声，那人们抬着沉重之物时拖沓的步伐。

===Original===
His mind flew back half-a-century, to the days when he had witnessed scenes wherein he was then merely a half-interested spectator. He knew the cortège composed of valets and friends, with the leech walking beside that precious burden, which anon would be deposited on the bed and left to the tender care of a mourning family.

Who knows what pictures were conjured up before that enfeebled vision? But he guessed. And when Juliette dashed into his room and stood before him, pale, trembling, a world of misery in her great eyes, she knew that he guessed and that she need not tell him. God had already done that for her.

Pierre, the old Duc’s devoted valet, dressed him as quickly as he could. M. le Duc insisted on having his *habit de cérémonie*, the rich suit of black velvet with the priceless lace and diamond buttons, which he had worn when they laid le Roi Soleil to his eternal rest.

He put on his orders and buckled on his sword. The gorgeous clothes, which had suited him so well in the prime of his manhood, hung somewhat loosely on his attenuated frame, but he looked a grand and imposing figure, with his white hair tied behind with a great black bow, and the fine jabot of beautiful point d’Angleterre falling in a soft cascade below his chin.

Then holding himself as upright as he could, he sat in his invalid chair, and four flunkeys in full livery carried him to the deathbed of his son.

===Chinese===
他的神智飞回了半个世纪之前，飞回那些日子：他曾目睹相似的场景，那时他不过是个看得半心半意的旁观者。他认得这支队伍——仆役与亲友随行，大夫走在那件珍贵重物的旁边；片刻之后，重物便会被安放到床上，留给一个哀恸的家庭去温柔照料。

谁知道那衰弱的眼前浮现出了什么图景？但他猜到了。当朱丽叶特冲进他的房间，立在他面前，脸色惨白，浑身发抖，一双大眼睛里盛着满世界的悲苦——她知道他已猜到，她不必说了。上帝已经替她说了。

皮埃尔（Pierre），老公爵忠心的贴身男仆，尽快替他更衣。公爵先生执意要穿他的*礼服*（habit de cérémonie）——那身华贵的黑天鹅绒套装，缀着无价的蕾丝和钻石纽扣；当年安葬太阳王（le Roi Soleil）、送他长眠之时，穿的就是这一身。

他戴上他的勋章，佩上剑。那身华服，在他壮年时曾何等相称，如今罩在他枯瘦的躯干上已有些松垮；但他看上去依然堂皇威严：白发在脑后系着一个大大的黑蝴蝶结，领口那方精美的「英格兰」花边（point d’Angleterre）柔和地泻垂在颏下。

随后，他竭力挺直了身子，坐进轮椅；四名穿着全套号衣的仆役，抬着他到他儿子的灵床前。

===Original===
All the house was astir by now. Torches burned in great sockets in the vast hall and along the massive oak stairway, and hundreds of candles flickered ghostlike in the vast apartments of the princely mansion.

The numerous servants were arrayed on the landing, all dressed in the rich livery of the ducal house.

The death of an heir of the Marnys is an event that history makes a note of.

The old Duc’s chair was placed close to the bed, where lay the dead body of the young Vicomte. He made no movement, nor did he utter a word or sigh. Some of those who were present at the time declared that his mind had completely given way, and that he neither felt nor understood the death of his son.

The Marquis de Villefranche, who had followed his friend to the last, took a final leave of the sorrowing house.

===Chinese===
到这时候，整座府邸都已惊动。巨大的火炬在宽阔大厅里、沿着厚重的橡木楼梯一路燃烧，王侯巨邸浩瀚的厅室中，数百支蜡烛幽幽闪烁，如鬼火一般。

众多仆役在楼梯平台上列成一排，个个穿着公爵府华美的号衣。

马尔尼家的继承人死去，是历史也要记上一笔的大事。

老公爵的轮椅被安放到床边，年轻的子爵的遗体就躺在那里。他一动不动，一言不发，也没有一声叹息。当时在场的有些人后来说，他的神智已经完全垮了，他既感觉不到，也不明白儿子的死。

维尔弗兰什侯爵把他的朋友送到了最后，此刻向这悲伤的门第作最后的告别。

===Original===
Juliette scarcely noticed him. Her eyes were fixed on her father. She would not look at her brother. A childlike fear had seized her, there, suddenly, between these two silent figures: the living and the dead.

But just as the Marquis was leaving the room, the old man spoke for the first time.

“Marquis,” he said very quietly, “you forget﻿—you have not yet told me who killed my son.”

“It was in a fair fight, M. de Duc,” replied the young Marquis, awed in spite of all his frivolity, his lightheartedness, by this strange, almost mysterious tragedy.

“Who killed my son, M. le Marquis?” repeated the old man mechanically. “I have the right to know,” he added with sudden, weird energy.

“It was M. Paul Déroulède, M. le Duc,” replied the Marquis. “I repeat, it was in fair fight.”

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特几乎没有注意他。她的眼睛盯着父亲。她不敢看哥哥。就在这两尊沉默的身影之间——一个活着，一个已死——一种孩子式的恐惧，霎时攫住了她。

可就在侯爵要离开房间的时候，老人头一次开了口。

“侯爵，”他说得非常平静，“你忘了——你还没有告诉我，是谁杀了我的儿子。”

“那是一场光明正大的决斗，公爵先生，”年轻的侯爵答道。尽管他素来轻浮、没心没肺，这桩离奇得近乎神秘的惨祸仍使他心生敬畏。

“是谁杀了我的儿子，侯爵先生？”老人机械地重复道。“我有权利知道，”他忽然带着一股怪异的力气补上一句。

“是保罗·德鲁莱德先生，公爵先生，”侯爵答道。“我再说一遍，那是一场光明正大的决斗。”

===Original===
The old Duc sighed as if in satisfaction. Then with a courteous gesture of farewell reminiscent of the *grand siècle* he added:

“All thanks from me and mine to you, Marquis, would seem but a mockery. Your devotion to my son is beyond human thanks. I’ll not detain you now. Farewell.”

Escorted by two lackeys, the Marquis passed out of the room.

“Dismiss all the servants, Juliette; I have something to say,” said the old Duc, and the young girl, silent, obedient, did as her father bade her.

Father and sister were alone with their dead. As soon as the last hushed footsteps of the retreating servants died away in the distance, the Duc de Marny seemed to throw away the lethargy which had enveloped him until now. With a quick, feverish gesture he seized his daughter’s wrist, and murmured excitedly:

===Chinese===
老公爵叹了口气，仿佛倒是满意了。随后，他带着一个令人想起*伟大世纪*（grand siècle）的温文尔雅的告别手势，又说：

“我与我家的人向你道谢，侯爵，都只怕近于讥诮。你对我儿子的情义，已非人间的感谢所能报答。此刻我就不留你了。别了。”

在两名仆役的引导下，侯爵退出了房间。

“把仆人们都打发走，朱丽叶特；我有话要说，”老公爵说。少女默默顺从地照办了。

父亲和女儿，同他们的死者单独留在了这里。退出去的仆人们最后那几声轻悄的脚步在远处消逝之后，马尔尼公爵仿佛一下子甩掉了裹住他至今的那层麻木。他一把攥住女儿的手腕，动作急促而狂热，激动地低声说：

===Original===
“His name. You heard his name, Juliette?”

“Yes, father,” replied the child.

“Paul Déroulède! Paul Déroulède! You’ll not forget it?”

“Never, father!”

“He killed your brother! You understand that? Killed my only son, the hope of my house, the last descendant of the most glorious race that has ever added lustre to the history of France.”

“In fair fight, father!” protested the child.

===Chinese===
“他的名字。你听见他的名字了吗，朱丽叶特？”

“听见了，父亲，”孩子答道。

“保罗·德鲁莱德！保罗·德鲁莱德！你不会忘了吧？”

“永远不会，父亲！”

“他杀了你的哥哥！你明白吗？杀了我唯一的儿子，我这门第的希望，有史以来为法兰西历史增添过光彩的最光荣世家的最后一名后裔。”

“那是一场光明正大的决斗，父亲！”孩子申辩道。

===Original===
“ ’Tis not fair for a man to kill a boy,” retorted the old man, with furious energy.

“Déroulède is thirty: my boy was scarce out of his teens: may the vengeance of God fall upon the murderer!”

Juliette, awed, terrified, was gazing at her father with great, wondering eyes. He seemed unlike himself. His face wore a curious expression of ecstasy and of hatred, also of hope and exultation, whenever he looked steadily at her.

That the final glimmer of a tottering reason was fast leaving the poor, aching head she was too young to realise. Madness was a word that had only a vague meaning for her. Though she did not understand her father at the present moment, though she was half afraid of him, she would have rejected with scorn and horror any suggestion that he was mad.

Therefore when he took her hand and, drawing her nearer to the bed and to himself, placed it upon her dead brother’s breast, she recoiled at the touch of the inanimate body, so unlike anything she had ever touched before, but she obeyed her father without any question, and listened to his words as to those of a sage.

===Chinese===
“成年男人杀死一个孩子，这不叫光明正大，”老人反驳道，力大气粗。

“德鲁莱德三十岁了：我的孩子还刚脱离少年：愿上帝的报应降在那凶手头上！”

朱丽叶特又惊又怕，睁大一双惶惑的眼睛望着父亲。他仿佛变了一个人。每当他凝视她的时候，那张脸上便现出一种奇怪的表情，狂喜与仇恨交织，还有希望，还有快意。

那摇摇欲坠的神智的最后一点微光，正飞速离开那颗可怜的、痛苦的头脑——她年纪太小，还看不出来。「疯」这个字眼，对她只有一种模糊的意思。虽然此刻她不能理解父亲，虽然她对他有几分害怕，可谁若暗示他疯了，她都会轻蔑而惊骇地加以驳斥。

因此，当他握住她的手，把她拉得离床更近、离自己更近，把那只手放到她死去的哥哥的胸口上时，她触到那具失去生命的躯体，不由得缩了缩——那触感与她生平所触过的任何东西都不同；但她对父亲没有一丝疑问，依言而行，听他的话，如同听一位智者的话。

===Original===
“Juliette, you are now fourteen, and able to understand what I am going to ask of you. If I were not chained to this miserable chair, if I were not a hopeless, abject cripple, I would not depute anyone, not even you, my only child, to do that, which God demands that one of us should do.”

He paused a moment, then continued earnestly:

“Remember, Juliette, that you are of the house of Marny, that you are a Catholic, and that God hears you now. For you shall swear an oath before Him and me, an oath from which only death can relieve you. Will you swear, my child?”

“If you wish it, father.”

“You have been to confession lately, Juliette?”

“Yes, father; also to holy communion, yesterday,” replied the child. “It was the Fête-Dieu, you know.”

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特，你如今十四岁，能够明白我要请求你做的事了。倘若我不是被锁在这把可怜的椅子上，倘若我不是一个无可救药、卑屈可怜的残废，这件事——上帝要我们中的一个去做的这件事——我原不必托付给任何人，哪怕是你，我唯一的孩子。”

他停了片刻，又恳切地继续说下去：

“记住，朱丽叶特，你是马尔尼家的人，你是天主教徒，上帝此刻正听着你。你要在他和我的面前立一个誓，一个唯有死亡才能解除的誓。你愿意立誓吗，我的孩子？”

“您要如此，父亲。”

“你近来告解过吗，朱丽叶特？”

“告解过了，父亲；昨天还领了圣体，”孩子答道。“昨天是基督圣体节（Fête-Dieu），您知道的。”

===Original===
“Then you are in a state of grace, my child?”

“I was yesterday morning, father,” replied the young girl naively, “but I have committed some little sins since then.”

“Then make your confession to God in your heart now. You must be in a state of grace when you speak the oath.”

The child closed her eyes, and as the old man watched her, he could see the lips framing the words of her spiritual confession.

Juliette made the sign of the cross, then opened her eyes and looked at her father.

“I am ready, father,” she said; “I hope God has forgiven me the little sins of yesterday.”

===Chinese===
“那么你是处于恩宠之中的了，我的孩子？”

“昨天早晨是的，父亲，”少女天真地答道，“可打那以后，我又犯了几桩小小的罪过。”

“那么现在就在心里向上帝告解吧。你说誓词的时候，必须处于恩宠之中。”

孩子闭上了眼睛。老人注视着她，看得出她嘴唇的翕动，正在默诵灵魂告解的词句。

朱丽叶特画了个十字，睁开眼睛，望着父亲。

“我准备好了，父亲，”她说；“愿上帝宽恕了我昨天那些小小的罪过。”

===Original===
“Will you swear, my child?”

“What, father?”

“That you will avenge your brother’s death on his murderer?”

“But, father﻿—”

“Swear it, my child!”

===Chinese===
“你愿意立誓吗，我的孩子？”

“立什么誓，父亲？”

“你要为你哥哥之死向凶手复仇——立这个誓。”

“可是，父亲——”

“立誓，我的孩子！”

===Original===
“How can I fulfil that oath, father?﻿—I don’t understand﻿—”

“God will guide you, my child. When you are older you will understand.”

For a moment Juliette still hesitated. She was just on that borderland between childhood and womanhood when all the sensibilities, the nervous system, the emotions, are strung to their highest pitch.

Throughout her short life she had worshipped her father with a wholehearted, passionate devotion, which had completely blinded her to his weakening faculties and the feebleness of his mind.

She was also in that initial stage of enthusiastic piety which overwhelms every girl of temperament, if she be brought up in the Roman Catholic religion, when she is first initiated into the mysteries of the Sacraments.

===Chinese===
“这个誓，我怎样才能践行，父亲？——我不明白——”

“上帝会指引你，我的孩子。等你长大些，你就会明白了。”

朱丽叶特仍迟疑了片刻。她正站在童年与成年之间的那道边界上，一切感受、整个神经系统、全部情感，都绷到了最紧的一弦。

在她短暂的一生里，她一直全心全意、热烈地崇拜着父亲；这种崇拜使她对父亲日渐衰退的体力与衰弱的头脑全然视而不见。

她也正处在热忱虔敬的发端阶段——凡有性情的女孩子，若自幼在罗马天主教中长大，初次领受圣事的奥秘时，无不为此心醉神迷。

===Original===
Juliette had been to confession and communion. She had been confirmed by Monseigneur, the Archbishop. Her ardent nature had responded to the full to the sensuous and ecstatic expressions of the ancient faith.

And somehow her father’s wish, her brother’s death, all seemed mingled in her brain with that religion, for which in her juvenile enthusiasm she would willingly have laid down her life.

She thought of all the saints, whose lives she had been reading. Her young heart quivered at the thought of *their* sacrifices, their martyrdoms, their sense of duty.

An exaltation, morbid perhaps, superstitious and overwhelming, took possession of her mind; also, perhaps, far back in the innermost recesses of her heart, a pride in her own importance, her mission in life, her individuality: for she was a girl after all, a mere child, about to become a woman.

But the old Duc was waxing impatient.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特告解过，领过圣体。她已由大主教大人（Monseigneur）行了坚振礼。她那炽热的天性，对这古老信仰那些诉诸感官、令人心醉神迷的仪轨，曾全力回应。

不知怎的，父亲的愿望、哥哥的死，这一切在她脑中似乎都与那信仰混在了一起；凭着少年人的热忱，她本甘愿为那信仰献出生命。

她想起她一直在读的那些圣徒的生平。想到*他们*的牺牲、他们的殉道、他们的尽责，她那颗年轻的心便战栗不已。

一种亢奋攫住了她的心神——或许是病态的、迷信而不可抗拒的亢奋；也许，在她内心最幽深的角落里，还有一份骄傲：对自己之重要、自己之使命、自己之独特性的骄傲。因为她毕竟是个女孩，一个孩子，即将长成的女人。

可老公爵渐渐不耐烦了。

===Original===
“Surely you do not hesitate, Juliette, with your dead brother’s body clamouring mutely for revenge? You, the only Marny left now!﻿—for from this day I too shall be as dead.”

“No, father,” said the young girl in an awed whisper, “I do not hesitate. I will swear, just as you bid me.”

“Repeat the words after me, my child.”

“Yes, father.”

“Before the face of Almighty God, who sees and hears me﻿—”

“Before the face of Almighty God, who sees and hears me,” repeated Juliette firmly.

===Chinese===
“你总不至于迟疑吧，朱丽叶特？你哥哥的遗体正无声地呼喊着要复仇呢。你，如今仅存的马尔尼家的人！——因为从今天起，我也同死人无异了。”

“不，父亲，”少女带着敬畏低声说，“我不迟疑。您吩咐什么，我就立什么誓。”

“跟着我念这些话，我的孩子。”

“好的，父亲。”

“在全能的上帝面前——他看见我，听见我——”

“在全能的上帝面前——他看见我，听见我，”朱丽叶特坚定地跟着念道。

===Original===
“I swear that I will seek out Paul Déroulède.”

“I swear that I will seek out Paul Déroulède.”

“And in any manner which God may dictate to me encompass his death, his ruin or dishonour, in revenge for my brother’s death.”

“And in any manner which God may dictate to me encompass his death, his ruin or dishonour, in revenge for my brother’s death,” said Juliette solemnly.

“May my brother’s soul remain in torment until the final Judgment Day if I should break my oath, but may it rest in eternal peace the day on which his death is fitly avenged.”

“May my brother’s soul remain in torment until the final Judgment Day if I should break my oath, but may it rest in eternal peace the day on which his death is fitly avenged.”

===Chinese===
“我发誓，我必寻见保罗·德鲁莱德。”

“我发誓，我必寻见保罗·德鲁莱德。”

“并且以上帝启示于我的任何方式，置他于死地，毁他、辱他，以报我哥哥之仇。”

“并且以上帝启示于我的任何方式，置他于死地，毁他、辱他，以报我哥哥之仇，”朱丽叶特庄严地说。

“倘若我背弃此誓，愿我哥哥的灵魂受煎熬，直到末日审判；而他蒙受合宜昭雪的那一天，愿他的灵魂得享永远的安宁。”

“倘若我背弃此誓，愿我哥哥的灵魂受煎熬，直到末日审判；而他蒙受合宜昭雪的那一天，愿他的灵魂得享永远的安宁。”

===Original===
The child fell upon her knees. The oath was spoken, the old man was satisfied.

He called for his valet, and allowed himself quietly to be put to bed.

One brief hour had transformed a child into a woman. A dangerous transformation when the brain is overburdened with emotions, when the nerves are overstrung and the heart full to breaking.

For the moment, however, the childlike nature reasserted itself for the last time, for Juliette, sobbing, had fled out of the room, to the privacy of her own apartment, and thrown herself passionately into the arms of kind old Pétronelle.

===Chinese===
孩子跪了下去。誓言已立，老人满足了。

他唤来贴身男仆，安静地听凭人们服侍着上了床。

短短一个钟头，孩子变成了女人。当头脑被情感压得超载，当神经绷得过紧、心满得将裂，这样的蜕变是危险的。

不过此时此刻，孩子气的天性最后一次冒了头：朱丽叶特啜泣着逃出房间，躲回自己闺房的私密里，一头扑进善良的老佩特罗内勒怀中，忘情恸哭。

## I Paris: 1793 / I 巴黎：1793

===Original===
It would have been very difficult to say why Citizen Déroulède was quite so popular as he was. Still more difficult would it have been to state the reason why he remained immune from the prosecutions, which were being conducted at the rate of several scores a day, now against the moderate Gironde, anon against the fanatic Mountain, until the whole of France was transformed into one gigantic prison, that daily fed the guillotine.

But Déroulède remained unscathed. Even Merlin’s law of the suspect had so far failed to touch him. And when, last July, the murder of Marat brought an entire holocaust of victims to the guillotine—from Adam Lux, who would have put up a statue in honour of Charlotte Corday, with the inscription: “Greater than Brutus,” to Charlier, who would have had her publicly tortured and burned at the stake for her crime—Déroulède alone said nothing, and was allowed to remain silent.

The most seething time of that seething revolution. No one knew in the morning if his head would still be on his own shoulders in the evening, or if it would be held up by Citizen Samson the headsman, for the sansculottes of Paris to see.

Yet Déroulède was allowed to go his own way. Marat once said of him: “Il n’est pas dangereux.” The phrase had been taken up. Within the precincts of the National Convention, Marat was still looked upon as the great protagonist of Liberty, a martyr to his own convictions carried to the extreme, to squalor and dirt, to the downward levelling of man to what is the lowest type in humanity. And his sayings were still treasured up: even the Girondins did not dare to attack his memory. Dead Marat was more powerful than his living presentment had been.

And he had said that Déroulède was not dangerous. Not dangerous to Republicanism, to liberty, to that downward, levelling process, the tearing down of old traditions, and the annihilation of past pretensions.

===Chinese===
要说清德鲁莱德（Déroulède）公民为何竟如此深得人心，实在不是件容易事。而要说清他何以能置身于种种控诉之外，就更加困难了——那时控诉正以一日数十起的速度进行着，忽而冲着温和的吉伦特派，忽而冲着狂热的山岳派，直到整个法兰西变成一座巨大的监狱，每天向断头台输送食粮。

但德鲁莱德始终安然无恙。就连梅尔兰（Merlin）的《嫌疑犯法令》至今也未能碰他分毫。去年七月，马拉（Marat）遇刺，把整整一批殉葬者送上了断头台——从要为夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）塑像、并题上「比布鲁图斯更伟大」字样的亚当·卢克斯（Adam Lux），到主张把她当众拷打、绑上火刑柱烧死以惩其罪的沙利耶（Charlier）——唯独德鲁莱德一言不发，而人们也容许他保持沉默。

那是这场沸腾的革命最沸腾的时刻。没有人知道早晨出门时，自己的脑袋傍晚是否还安在自己的肩上，还是会被刽子手桑松公民（Citizen Samson）高高举起，供巴黎的无套裤汉（sansculottes）们围观。

然而德鲁莱德却被容许自行其是。马拉曾这样说过他：「Il n’est pas dangereux.」（他并不危险。）这句话被大家接了过去。在国民公会（National Convention）里，马拉仍被视为自由的伟大旗手，一个为自己的信念走向极端的殉道者——走向污秽与肮脏，走向把人往下拉平、拉到人类最低贱类型的那条路。他的语录仍被人珍藏：连吉伦特派也不敢攻击他的身后名。死了的马拉，比他活着的模样更有力量。

而他说过，德鲁莱德并不危险。对共和主义不危险，对自由不危险，对那场向下的、拉平一切的过程——对旧传统的摧毁、对昔日僭望的泯灭——都不危险。

===Original===
Déroulède had once been very rich. He had had sufficient prudence to give away in good time that which, undoubtedly, would have been taken away from him later on.

But when he gave he gave willingly, at a time when France needed it most, and before she had learned how to help herself to what she wanted.

And somehow, in this instance, France had not forgotten: an invisible fortress seemed to surround Citizen Déroulède and keep his enemies at bay. They were few, but they existed. The National Convention trusted him. “He was not dangerous” to them. The people looked upon him as one of themselves, who gave whilst he had something to give. Who can gauge that most elusive of all things: *Popularity?*

He lived a quiet life, and had never yielded to the omni-prevalent temptation of writing pamphlets, but lived alone with his mother and Anne Mie, the little orphaned cousin whom old Madame Déroulède had taken care of, ever since the child could toddle.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德曾经非常富有。他颇有先见之明，及时把财产散了出去——那些财产，毫无疑问，日后必会被人夺走。

但他散财时给得心甘情愿，正值法兰西最需要的时候，也赶在她学会伸手自取之前。

而不知怎的，这一回法兰西没有忘记：一座无形的堡垒似乎环绕着德鲁莱德公民，把他的敌人拒之门外。敌人不多，却确实存在。国民公会信任他。在他们看来，他「不危险」。人民把他当作自己人——一个手里有东西就肯拿出来给的人。可谁能估量那世间最捉摸不定的东西：*声望*（Popularity）？

他过着安静的日子，从未屈从于那无处不在的诱惑去写小册子，只与母亲和安娜·米（Anne Mie）同住——那是老德鲁莱德夫人收留的孤女表妹，从孩子蹒跚学步起，就由她一手照看。

===Original===
Everyone knew his house in the Rue Ecole de Médecine, not far from the one wherein Marat lived and died, the only solid, stone house in the midst of a row of hovels, evil-smelling and squalid.

The street was narrow then, as it is now, and whilst Paris was cutting off the heads of her children for the sake of Liberty and Fraternity, she had no time to bother about cleanliness and sanitation.

Rue Ecole de Médecine did little credit to the school after which it was named, and it was a most unattractive crowd that usually thronged its uneven, muddy pavements.

A neat gown, a clean kerchief, were quite an unusual sight down this way, for Anne Mie seldom went out, and old Madame Déroulède hardly ever left her room. A good deal of brandy was being drunk at the two drinking bars, one at each end of the long, narrow street, and by five o’clock in the afternoon it was undoubtedly best for women to remain indoors.

===Chinese===
人人都知道他坐落在医学院街（Rue Ecole de Médecine）上的那所房子，离马拉居住并死去的那所不远；在一片恶臭而污秽的棚屋中间，它是唯一一幢坚固的石屋。

这条街那时就和现在一样狭窄；巴黎正为了自由与博爱砍自己儿女的脑袋，哪有工夫去管什么清洁与卫生。

医学院街实在给这条街借名的那门学问丢脸，平日挤满那些高低不平、泥泞不堪的人行道的，是一群最不入目的人。

整洁的长裙、干净的头巾，在这一带是罕见的景象：安娜·米很少出门，老德鲁莱德夫人更是几乎从不跨出房门。长街两端各有一家小酒馆，白兰地在那儿大量下肚；到了下午五点以后，妇女顶好待在家里，这是毫无疑问的。

===Original===
The crowd of dishevelled elderly Amazons who stood gossiping at the street corner could hardly be called women now. A ragged petticoat, a greasy red kerchief round the head, a tattered, stained shift—to this pass of squalor and shame had Liberty brought the daughters of France.

And they jeered at any passerby less filthy, less degraded than themselves.

“Ah! *voyons l’aristo!*” they shouted every time a man in decent clothes, a woman with tidy cap and apron, passed swiftly down the street.

And the afternoons were very lively. There was always plenty to see: first and foremost, the long procession of tumbrils, winding its way from the prisons to the Place de la Révolution. The forty-four thousand sections of the Committee of Public Safety sent their quota, each in their turn, to the guillotine.

===Chinese===
聚在街角闲话的那些披头散发的老年悍妇，如今几乎已不配称作女人了。破烂的衬裙，油腻的红头巾，污渍斑斑的破衬衫——自由竟把法兰西的女儿们带到了这般污秽与耻辱的境地。

而只要有个过路人比她们干净些、体面些，她们便要奚落一番。

「Ah! *voyons l’aristo!*」（啊！瞧那个贵族！）每当一个衣着体面的男人、或一个帽子围裙还算齐整的女人匆匆走过这条街，她们就这样叫喊。

下午总是很热闹，有的是可看的：头一样，便是那从各座监狱蜿蜒驶向革命广场（Place de la Révolution）的长长死囚车（tumbril）队。救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）治下的四万四千个分区，轮番把自己的一份送上断头台。

===Original===
At one time these tumbrils contained royal ladies and gentlemen, ci-devant dukes and princesses, aristocrats from every county in France, but now this stock was becoming exhausted. The wretched Queen Marie Antoinette still lingered in the Temple with her son and daughter. Madame Elisabeth was still allowed to say her prayers in peace, but ci-devant dukes and counts were getting scarce: those who had not perished at the hand of Citizen Samson were plying some trade in Germany or England.

There were aristocratic joiners, innkeepers, and hairdressers. The proudest names in France were hidden beneath trade signs in London and Hamburg. A good number owed their lives to that mysterious Scarlet Pimpernel, that unknown Englishman who had snatched scores of victims from the clutches of Tinville the Prosecutor, and sent M. Chauvelin, baffled, back to France.

Aristocrats were getting scarce, so it was now the turn of deputies of the National Convention, of men of letters, men of science or of art, men who had sent others to the guillotine a twelvemonth ago, and men who had been loudest in defence of anarchy and its Reign of Terror.

They had revolutionised the Calendar: the Citizen-Deputies, and every good citizen of France, called this 19th day of August 1793 the 2nd Fructidor of the year I of the New Era.

===Chinese===
曾几何时，死囚车上坐的是王室的贵妇与老爷，前贵族（ci-devant）的公爵与公主，法兰西各郡的贵族；如今这份存货也快耗尽了。可怜的王后玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）还带着儿子和女儿在坦普尔监狱（the Temple）里煎熬。伊丽莎白夫人（Madame Elisabeth）还被容许安静地做她的祷告，但前贵族的公爵伯爵已日渐稀少：没丧命于桑松公民手下的，都在德意志或英格兰操起了营生。

有改行做木匠、开客栈、当理发师的贵族。法兰西最显赫的姓氏，在伦敦和汉堡藏在了店铺招牌底下。不少人能活下来，靠的是那位神秘的红花侠（The Scarlet Pimpernel）——那个不知名的英格兰人，从公诉人坦维尔（Tinville）的魔爪里救走了一批批死囚，还让碰了钉子的肖夫兰先生（M. Chauvelin）灰溜溜地回了法国。

贵族越来越稀缺了，如今轮到了国民公会的议员们，轮到了文人、学者、艺术家，轮到一年前把别人送上断头台的人，轮到为无政府状态及其恐怖统治（Reign of Terror）辩护得最起劲的人。

他们连历法也革命了：公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）们，以及法兰西的每一位好公民，都把这一天（1793-08-19）叫做新纪元元年果月（Fructidor）二日。

===Original===
At six o’clock on that afternoon a young girl suddenly turned the angle of the Rue Ecole de Médecine, and after looking quickly to the right and left she began deliberately walking along the narrow street.

It was crowded just then. Groups of excited women stood jabbering before every doorway. It was the homecoming hour after the usual spectacle on the Place de la Révolution. The men had paused at the various drinking booths, crowding the women out. It would be the turn of these Amazons next, at the brandy bars; for the moment they were left to gossip, and to jeer at the passerby.

At first the young girl did not seem to heed them. She walked quickly along, looking defiantly before her, carrying her head erect, and stepping carefully from cobblestone to cobblestone, avoiding the mud, which could have dirtied her dainty shoes.

The harridans passed the time of day to her, and the time of day meant some obscene remark unfit for women’s ears. The young girl wore a simple grey dress, with fine lawn kerchief neatly folded across her bosom, a large hat with flowing ribbons sat above the fairest face that ever gladdened men’s eyes to see.

===Chinese===
那天下午六点钟，一个年轻姑娘突然转过医学院街的街角，朝左右迅速张望了一下，便不慌不忙地沿着这条窄街走了下去。

街上正拥挤。一群群情绪激动的妇女站在各家门前叽叽喳喳。这正是革命广场上例行演出散场归家的时刻。男人们在各处酒摊前停住了脚，把妇女挤到了一边。下一个就轮到这些悍妇进白兰地酒馆了；此刻她们被留在街上闲话，奚落过路人。

起初，姑娘似乎并不理会她们。她快步走着，目光挑衅地望着前方，头颅高高扬起，小心翼翼地从一块圆石踩到另一块圆石，避开那会弄脏她秀美鞋子的泥泞。

泼妇们向她「打了招呼」，而那招呼便是些女人的耳朵都听不得的脏话。姑娘穿一件朴素的灰裙，细麻布头巾整整齐齐地叠在胸前，宽檐帽上飘着缎带，帽子底下是一张绝色的面庞——自有男人睁开眼以来，最让他们赏心悦目的那种脸。

===Original===
Fairer still it would have been, but for the look of determination which made it seem hard and old for the girl’s years.

She wore the tricolour scarf round her waist, else she had been more seriously molested ere now. But the Republican colours were her safeguard: whilst she walked quietly along, no one could harm her.

Then suddenly a curious impulse seemed to seize her. It was just outside the large stone house belonging to Citizen-Deputy Déroulède. She had so far taken no notice of the groups of women which she had come across. When they obstructed the footway, she had calmly stepped out into the middle of the road.

It was wise and prudent, for she could close her ears to obscene language and need pay no heed to insult.

===Chinese===
若不是那副决绝的神情使这张脸显得生硬，显得比她的年纪苍老，它本还要更美。

她腰间系着三色绶带，否则早在先前就难免遭受更严重的纠缠了。共和派的三色就是她的护身符：只要她安安静静地走路，谁也不能伤她分毫。

突然，一种奇异的冲动仿佛攫住了她。那正是在公会议员德鲁莱德那幢大石屋的门外。一路上，她对撞见的一伙伙女人始终毫不理会；挡了路，她就从容地走到街心去。

这是明智而审慎的，因为她可以闭耳不听那些污言秽语，也无须理会那些侮辱。

===Original===
Suddenly she threw up her head defiantly.

“Will you please let me pass?” she said loudly, as a dishevelled Amazon stood before her with arms akimbo, glancing sarcastically at the lace petticoat, which just peeped beneath the young girl’s simple grey frock.

“Let her pass? Let her pass? Ho! ho! ho!” laughed the old woman, turning to the nearest group of idlers, and apostrophising them with a loud oath. “Did *you* know, citizeness, that this street had been specially made for aristos to pass along?”

“I am in a hurry, will you let me pass at once?” commanded the young girl, tapping her foot impatiently on the ground.

===Chinese===
蓦地，她挑衅地昂起了头。

「请你让开，让我过去！」她大声说道。一个披头散发的悍妇叉着腰站在她面前，讥讽地打量着那从姑娘的素灰裙下微微露出的一角蕾丝衬裙。

「让她过去？让她过去？嗬！嗬！嗬！」老太婆大笑起来，转向离得最近的一伙闲汉，扯着嗓子咒骂了一句，「女公民，*你*知不知道，这条街是特地为贵族们修来走的？」

「我有急事，请你们立刻让我过去！」年轻姑娘喝道，不耐烦地用脚点着地面。

===Original===
There was the whole width of the street on her right, plenty of room for her to walk along. It seemed positive madness to provoke a quarrel singlehanded against this noisy group of excited females, just home from the ghastly spectacle around the guillotine.

And yet she seemed to do it wilfully, as if coming to the end of her patience, all her proud, aristocratic blood in revolt against this evil-smelling crowd which surrounded her.

Half-tipsy men and noisome, naked urchins seemed to have sprung from everywhere.

“Oho, *quelle aristo*!” they shouted with ironical astonishment, gazing at the young girl’s face, fingering her gown, thrusting begrimed, hate-distorted faces close to her own.

===Chinese===
她右边是整条街的宽度，尽够她走的。单枪匹马去招惹这群喧闹的、情绪激动的妇女——她们刚从断头台四周那可怖的场面散回来——似乎纯属发疯。

然而她似乎偏要这么做，仿佛耐心已经到了尽头，她满腔高傲的贵族热血，都在反抗这团包围着她的恶臭的人群。

半醉的汉子和浑身恶臭、赤身露体的顽童，仿佛从四面八方冒了出来。

「哦嚯，*quelle aristo*!」（好一位贵族小姐！）他们喊道，又是惊讶又是讥讽，盯着姑娘的脸，伸手摸她的裙子，把一张张污黑而扭曲着仇恨的脸凑到她眼前。

===Original===
Instinctively she recoiled and backed towards the house immediately on her left. It was adorned with a porch made of stout oak beams, with a tiled roof; an iron lantern descended from this, and there was a stone parapet below, and a few steps, at right angles from the pavement, led up to the massive door.

On these steps the young girl had taken refuge. Proud, defiant, she confronted the howling mob, which she had so wilfully provoked.

“Of a truth, Citizeness Margot, that grey dress would become you well!” suggested a young man, whose red cap hung in tatters over an evil and dissolute-looking face.

“And all that fine lace would make a splendid jabot round the aristo’s neck when Citizen Samson holds up her head for us to see,” added another, as with mock elegance he stooped and with two very grimy fingers slightly raised the young girl’s grey frock, displaying the lace-edged petticoat beneath.

===Chinese===
她本能地退缩，退向紧挨着她左边的那幢房子。房子前有一道粗栎木梁搭成的门廊，覆着瓦顶；门廊下挂着一盏铁灯笼，下面是一道石栏，几级台阶从人行道成直角通向那扇厚重的大门。

姑娘就在这几级台阶上避了身。她高傲地、挑衅地直面那群被她存心招惹来的、狂吼乱叫的暴众。

「说真的，玛戈女公民（Citizeness Margot），那身灰裙子穿到你身上才漂亮呢！」一个年轻人提议道，他的红帽子破破烂烂，耷拉在一张邪恶而放荡的脸上。

「等桑松公民把她的头举起来给咱们看的时候，那些好蕾丝正好给贵族的脖颈配一副漂亮的褶皱胸饰（jabot），」另一个接口道，一边假惺惺地装出优雅姿态，弯下腰去，用两根乌黑的手指轻轻掀起了姑娘的灰裙，露出底下镶着蕾丝边的衬裙。

===Original===
A volley of oaths and loud, ironical laughter greeted this sally.

“ ’Tis mighty fine lace to be thus hidden away,” commented an elderly harridan. “Now, would you believe it, my fine madam, but my legs are bare underneath my kirtle?”

“And dirty, too, I’ll lay a wager,” laughed another. “Soap is dear in Paris just now.”

“The lace on the aristo’s kerchief would pay the baker’s bill of a whole family for a month!” shouted an excited voice.

===Chinese===
一阵咒骂和高声的讥笑，迎着这句俏皮话炸开了。

「这么好的蕾丝居然藏起来不露，真是可惜，」一个上了年纪的悍妇评论道，「怎么，我的漂亮太太，你信不信，我那衬裙底下，两条腿可是光着的？」

「还脏着呢，我敢打赌，」另一个笑道，「如今巴黎的肥皂可不便宜。」

「贵族头巾上那点蕾丝，够一大家子人交一个月的面包钱了！」一个激动的人声喊道。

===Original===
Heat and brandy further addled the brains of this group of French citizens; hatred gleamed out of every eye. Outrage was imminent. The young girl seemed to know it, but she remained defiant and self-possessed, gradually stepping back and back up the steps, closely followed by her assailants.

“To the Jew with the gewgaw, then!” shouted a thin, haggard female viciously, as she suddenly clutched at the young girl’s kerchief, and with a mocking, triumphant laugh tore it from her bosom.

This outrage seemed to be the signal for the breaking down of the final barriers which ordinary decency should have raised. The language and vituperation became such as no chronicler could record.

The girl’s dainty white neck, her clear skin, the refined contour of shoulders and bust, seemed to have aroused the deadliest lust of hate in these wretched creatures, rendered bestial by famine and squalor.

===Chinese===
暑热和白兰地进一步搅浑了这群法国公民的头脑；每双眼睛里都闪着仇恨。暴行迫在眉睫。姑娘似乎明白这一点，却依旧挑衅地、镇定自若地一步步退上台阶，袭击者们紧紧跟在后面。

「那就让那小玩意儿见鬼去吧！」一个干瘦憔悴的女人恶狠狠地喊道，猛地一把抓住姑娘的头巾，带着讥讽而得胜的大笑，把它从她胸口扯了下来。

这一暴行仿佛是一个信号，从此连寻常的体面本该筑起的最后一道防线也崩溃了。那语言，那谩骂，是任何史官都无法记录下来的。

姑娘那秀美白皙的脖颈，那洁净的肌肤，那肩与胸的清雅轮廓，似乎在这群因饥馑与污秽而沦为兽性的可怜虫心中，激起了最疯狂的仇恨之欲。

===Original===
It seemed almost as if one would vie with the other in seeking for words which would most offend these small aristocratic ears.

The young girl was now crouching against the doorway, her hands held up to her ears to shut out the awful sounds. She did not seem frightened, only appalled at the terrible volcano which she had provoked.

Suddenly a miserable harridan struck her straight in the face, with hard, grimy fist, and a long shout of exultation greeted this monstrous deed.

Then only did the girl seem to lose her self-control.

===Chinese===
她们仿佛在彼此较劲，专拣最能冒犯这双小小的贵族耳朵的字眼。

姑娘此时正蜷缩在门边，双手捂着耳朵，想挡开那些可怕的声音。她似乎并不害怕，只是被自己挑起的这座可怖的火山吓呆了。

突然，一个穷凶极恶的悍妇抡起又硬又乌黑的拳头，照她脸上就是一记；一声长长的欢呼庆祝着这桩恶行。

直到这时，姑娘才似乎失去了自制。

===Original===
“*À moi*,” she shouted loudly, whilst hammering with both hands against the massive doorway. “*A moi!* Murder! Murder! Citoyen Déroulède, *à moi*!”

But her terror was greeted with renewed glee by her assailants. They were now roused to the highest point of frenzy: the crowd of brutes would in the next moment have torn the helpless girl from her place of refuge and dragged her into the mire, an outraged prey, for the satisfaction of an ungovernable hate.

But just as half-a-dozen pairs of talon-like hands clutched frantically at her skirts, the door behind her was quickly opened. She felt her arm seized firmly, and herself dragged swiftly within the shelter of the threshold.

Her senses, overwrought by the terrible adventure which she had just gone through, were threatening to reel; she heard the massive door close, shutting out the yells of baffled rage, the ironical laughter, the obscene words, which sounded in her ears like the shrieks of Dante’s damned.

===Chinese===
「*À moi*!」（救命啊！）她大声喊着，双手捶打着那厚重的大门。「救命啊！杀人啦！杀人啦！德鲁莱德公民（Citoyen Déroulède），救命啊！」

但她的惊呼只换来了袭击者更大的快意。他们此刻已被煽到了癫狂的顶点：再过一瞬，这群兽类就要把无助的姑娘从避身之处撕扯出来，拖进泥沼，让她成为一桩暴行的猎物，去满足那无法遏制的仇恨。

然而，就在五六双鹰爪似的手发狂般抓住她的裙裾之时，她身后的门很快打开了。她觉得胳膊被一把攥住，整个人被迅速拽进了门槛内的荫庇。

方才那场可怖的历险把她的神思绷到了极限，阵阵眩晕袭来；她听见厚重的大门阖上，把那班受挫者的狂吼、讥讽的笑声和污言秽语统统关在了外面——那些声音在她耳中，就像但丁笔下受罚亡魂的哀号。

===Original===
She could not see her rescuer, for the hall into which he had hastily dragged her was only dimly lighted. But a peremptory voice said quickly:

“Up the stairs, the room straight in front of you, my mother is there. Go quickly.”

She had fallen on her knees, cowering against the heavy oak beam which supported the ceiling, and was straining her eyes to catch sight of the man, to whom at this moment she perhaps owed more than her life: but he was standing against the doorway, with his hand on the latch.

“What are you going to do?” she murmured.

===Chinese===
她看不清救她的人，因为他把她匆匆拖进来的这个门厅只有微弱的光。但一个不容置辩的声音迅速说道：

「上楼去，正对着你的那间房，我母亲在那儿。快去。」

她跪倒在地，蜷在支撑天花板的那根沉重栎木梁旁，竭力睁大眼睛想看清那个人的模样——此刻她欠他的，也许远不止一条性命：可他正背靠着门洞站着，一只手搭在门闩上。

「您要去干什么？」她喃喃道。

===Original===
“Prevent their breaking into my house in order to drag you out of it,” he replied quietly; “so, I pray you, do as I bid you.”

Mechanically she obeyed him, drew herself to her feet, and, turning towards the stairs, began slowly to mount the shallow steps. Her knees were shaking under her, her whole body was trembling with horror at the awesome crisis she had just traversed.

She dared not look back at her rescuer. Her head was bent, and her lips were murmuring half-audible words as she went.

Outside the hooting and yelling was becoming louder and louder. Enraged fists were hammering violently against the stout oak door.

===Chinese===
「不让他们闯进我的屋子把您拖出去，」他平静地答道，「所以，求您了，照我说的做。」

她机械地依了他，撑起身子站起来，转向楼梯，慢慢地一级级踏上那浅浅的台阶。她的双膝在发抖，全身因方才经历的骇人一幕而战栗不止。

她不敢回头看她的救命恩人。她低着头，嘴里喃喃念着几乎听不见的词句，一边走。

门外的嘘声和叫喊越来越响。狂怒的拳头重重地擂着那扇坚实的栎木大门。

===Original===
At the top of the stairs, moved by an irresistible impulse, she turned and looked into the hall.

She saw his figure dimly outlined in the gloom, one hand on the latch, his head thrown back to watch her movements.

A door stood ajar immediately in front of her. She pushed it open and went within.

At that moment he too opened the door below. The shrieks of the howling mob once more resounded close to her ears. It seemed as if they had surrounded him. She wondered what was happening, and marvelled how he dared to face that awful crowd alone.

===Chinese===
到了楼梯顶上，她被一股无法抗拒的冲动所驱使，回过身，向门厅望去。

她看见他的身影在昏暗中依稀勾勒出来，一只手搭在门闩上，头向后仰着，注视她的动静。

一扇门正对着她，虚掩着。她推门进去。

就在那一刻，楼下的他也打开了大门。狂吼的人群的嘶叫又一次震响在她耳畔。他们仿佛把他团团围住了。她不知道外面发生了什么，暗自惊异他怎敢孤身直面那可怕的人群。

===Original===
The room into which she had entered was gay and cheerful-looking with its dainty chintz hangings and graceful, elegant pieces of furniture. The young girl looked up, as a kindly voice said to her, from out the depths of a capacious armchair:

“Come in, come in, my dear, and close the door behind you! Did those wretches attack you? Never mind. Paul will speak to them. Come here, my dear, and sit down; there’s no cause now for fear.”

Without a word the young girl came forward. She seemed now to be walking in a dream, the chintz hangings to be swaying ghostlike around her, the yells and shrieks below to come from the very bowels of the earth.

The old lady continued to prattle on. She had taken the girl’s hand in hers, and was gently forcing her down on to a low stool beside her armchair. She was talking about Paul, and said something about Anne Mie, and then about the National Convention, and those beasts and savages, but mostly about Paul.

The noise outside had subsided. The girl felt strangely sick and tired. Her head seemed to be whirling round, the furniture to be dancing round her; the old lady’s face looked at her through a swaying veil, and then—and then—

Tired Nature was having her way at last; she folded the quivering young body in her motherly arms, and wrapped the aching senses beneath her merciful mantle of unconsciousness.

===Chinese===
她走进的这间屋子，有着雅致的印花布帷幔和优美考究的家具，看上去明快而宜人。一个和蔼的声音从一张宽大的扶手椅深处传来，姑娘抬起了头：

「进来，进来，亲爱的，把身后的门关上！那些恶棍欺负你了？不要紧。保罗会去跟他们说的。到这儿来，亲爱的，坐下；现在没什么可怕的了。」

姑娘一言不发地走上前来。她仿佛走在梦里，印花布帷幔幽灵似的在她四周摇曳，楼下的吼叫与嘶喊仿佛来自地心的深处。

老太太絮絮地说个不停。她握着姑娘的手，轻轻地把她按到自己扶手椅旁的一张矮凳上坐下。她一会儿说保罗，一会儿提到安娜·米，然后又说到国民公会，说到那些畜生和野人，可说来说去，多半还是保罗。

外面的喧闹平息了。姑娘只觉得说不出的恶心和疲惫。她的头仿佛在打转，家具仿佛绕着她起舞；老太太的脸隔着一层晃动的纱幕望着她，然后——然后——

疲惫的天性终于遂了她的心愿：她把那颤抖的年轻身躯搂进慈母般的怀抱，用她那仁慈的无意识斗篷，裹住了伤痛欲裂的知觉。

## II Citizen-Deputy / II 公会议员

===Original===
When, presently, the young girl awoke, with a delicious feeling of rest and well-being, she had plenty of leisure to think.
So, then, this was his house! She was actually a guest, a rescued protégé, beneath the roof of Citoyen Déroulède.
He had dragged her from the clutches of the howling mob which she had provoked; his mother had made her welcome, a sweet-faced, young girl scarce out of her teens, sad-eyed and slightly deformed, had waited upon her and made her happy and comfortable.
Juliette de Marny was in the house of the man, whom she had sworn before her God and before her father to pursue with hatred and revenge.

===Chinese===
不久之后，年轻姑娘醒了过来，带着一种休憩与康宁的甜美感觉，她有的是闲暇去思索。
原来，这就是他的家！她居然成了客人，一个受救助的被庇护者，就待在德鲁莱德公民（Citoyen Déroulède）的屋檐之下。
是他把她从那群被她招惹来的、狂吼乱叫的暴众的魔爪里拖了出来；他的母亲对她欢迎备至；一个容貌甜美的少女——刚满二十的光景，眼神忧郁，身形微有残疾——侍奉着她，让她欢喜，让她舒适。
朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette de Marny）如今就在这个人的家里——她曾在上帝面前、在父亲面前立誓，要带着仇恨与复仇之心追索到底的那个人。

===Original===
Ten years had gone by since then.
Lying upon the sweet-scented bed which the hospitality of the Déroulèdes had provided for her, she seemed to see passing before her the spectres of these past ten years—the first four, after her brother's death, until the old Duc de Marny's body slowly followed his soul to its grave.
After that last glimmer of life beside the deathbed of his son, the old Duc had practically ceased to be. A mute, shrunken figure, he merely existed; his mind vanished, his memory gone, a wreck whom Nature fortunately remembered at last, and finally took away from the invalid chair which had been his world.
Then came those few years at the Convent of the Ursulines. Juliette had hoped that she had a vocation; her whole soul yearned for a secluded, a religious, life, for great barriers of solemn vows and days spent in prayer and contemplation, to interpose between herself and the memory of that awful night when, obedient to her father's will, she had made the solemn oath to avenge her brother's death.

===Chinese===
自那时起，十年过去了。
她躺在德鲁莱德家殷勤待客的、散发着香气的床上，仿佛看见过去十年的种种魅影从眼前走过——头四年，是在她哥哥死后，直到老马尔尼公爵（Duc de Marny）的躯体缓缓地追随他的灵魂进了坟墓。
在儿子的灵榻旁燃尽最后一点生命之光以后，老公爵实际上已不复为人。一个缄默的、萎缩的身影，只是苟延残喘；神志散了，记忆没了，一具残躯——所幸造化终于记起了他，最后把他从那把曾是他整个世界的病榻上带走了。
然后是乌尔苏拉会（Ursulines）修道院里的那几年。朱丽叶特曾指望自己有修道的圣召；她的整个灵魂渴望一种隐修的、虔敬的生活，渴望用庄严誓愿筑成的重重屏障、用祈祷与冥想的日日夜夜，横隔在她自己与那个可怕夜晚的记忆之间——那一夜，她遵从父命，立下了为兄长之死复仇的庄严誓言。

===Original===
She was only eighteen when she first entered the convent, directly after her father's death, when she felt very lonely—both morally and mentally lonely—and followed by the obsession of that oath.
She never spoke of it to anyone except to her confessor, and he, a simple-minded man of great learning and a total lack of knowledge of the world, was completely at a loss how to advise.
The Archbishop was consulted. He could grant a dispensation, and release her of that most solemn vow.
When first this idea was suggested to her, Juliette was exultant. Her entire nature, which in itself was wholesome, lighthearted, the very reverse of morbid, rebelled against this unnatural task placed upon her young shoulders. It was only religion—the strange, warped religion of that extraordinary age—which kept her to it, which forbade her breaking lightly that most unnatural oath.

===Chinese===
她初次进修道院时只有十八岁，就在父亲去世之后；那时她觉得自己非常孤单——道德上与精神上的双重孤单——而那个誓言的魔念又紧追着她不放。
这件事她从不对任何人提起，除了听她告解的神师；而那是一位学问渊博、却不谙世事的质朴之人，全然不知该如何劝解。
他们去请教了总主教。他可以颁一道豁免，解除她那最庄严的誓愿。
这个想法初提给她时，朱丽叶特欣喜若狂。她那天性本是健全的、轻快的，与病态恰恰相反，她整个心性都在反抗压在她年轻肩头的这份违背人性的差事。只有宗教——那个非凡年代里被扭曲的怪异宗教——把她拴在这上面，不许她轻易背弃那道最违背人性的誓言。

===Original===
The Archbishop was a man of many duties, many engagements. He agreed to give this strange "*cas de conscience*" his most earnest attention. He would make no promises. But Mademoiselle de Marny was rich: a munificent donation to the poor of Paris, or to some cause dear to the Holy Father himself, might perhaps be more acceptable to God than the fulfilment of a compulsory vow.
Juliette, within the convent walls, was waiting patiently for the Archbishop's decision at the very moment, when the greatest upheaval the world has ever known was beginning to shake the very foundations of France.
The Archbishop had other things now to think about than isolated cases of conscience. He forgot all about Juliette, probably. He was busy consoling a monarch for the loss of his throne, and preparing himself and his royal patron for the scaffold.
The Convent of the Ursulines was scattered during the Terror. Everyone remembers the Thermidor massacres, and the thirty-four nuns, all daughters of ancient families of France, who went so cheerfully to the scaffold.

===Chinese===
总主教是个身兼数职、应酬繁多的人。他答应以最郑重的态度来考虑这个奇特的「*cas de conscience*」（良知问题）。他不作任何许诺。不过马尔尼小姐（Mademoiselle de Marny）很富有：给巴黎的穷人、或给教皇本人（Holy Father）所关切的事业一笔慷慨的捐赠，也许比履行一桩被迫立下的誓言更能蒙上帝悦纳。
朱丽叶特在修道院的高墙之内，正耐心等候总主教的裁决；就在此时，人类历史上从未有过的最大剧变，已开始撼动法兰西的根基。
如今总主教有比孤例良知更要紧的事要操心了。他大概把朱丽叶特忘得一干二净。他忙着安慰一位失去王位的君王，忙着让自己和那位王家庇护人做好上断头台的准备。
恐怖统治（Reign of Terror）期间，乌尔苏拉会修道院被解散了。热月（Thermidor）屠杀，人人记忆犹新：那三十四位修女，全是法兰西古老世家的女儿，欢欢喜喜地走上了断头台。

===Original===
Juliette was one of those who escaped condemnation. How or why, she herself could not have told. She was very young, and still a postulant; she was allowed to live in retirement with Pétronelle, her old nurse, who had remained faithful through all these years.
Then the Archbishop was prosecuted and imprisoned. Juliette made frantic efforts to see him, but all in vain. When he died, she looked upon her spiritual guide's death as a direct warning from God, that nothing could relieve her of her oath.
She had watched the turmoils of the Revolution through the attic window of her tiny apartment in Paris. Waited upon by faithful Pétronelle, she had been forced to live on the savings of that worthy old soul, as all her property, all the Marny estates, the *dot* she took with her to the convent—everything, in fact—had been seized by the Revolutionary Government, self appointed to level fortunes, as well as individuals.
From that attic window she had seen beautiful Paris writhing under the pitiless lash of the demon of terror which it had provoked; she had heard the rumble of the tumbrils, dragging day after day their load of victims to the insatiable maker of this Revolution of Fraternity—the Guillotine.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特是逃过判决的人之一。何以能逃、为何能逃，连她自己也说不清。她年纪很轻，还是个望会生；她被准许退居静处，与佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）——她那位在这些年头里始终忠心不渝的老奶妈——同住。
后来总主教被起诉、下狱。朱丽叶特拼了命想见他一面，却终究没能如愿。他死时，她把这位神师的死看作上帝直接的警示：什么都解除不了她的誓言。
她在巴黎那间小小寓所的老虎窗前，冷眼看着大革命的滚滚风潮。由忠心的佩特罗内勒侍奉着，她只能靠那位可敬老人的一点积蓄过活，因为她的一切财产、马尔尼家的全部田产、她带进修道院的嫁妆（*dot*）——事实上是一切——都被革命政府没收了；那个自封的政府，要拉平财产，也要拉平人。
从那扇老虎窗里，她眼看着美丽的巴黎在它自己招来的恐怖恶魔的无情鞭笞下翻滚扭动；她听着死囚车（tumbril）的辚辚声，日复一日拖着满车的牺牲者，送往这场「博爱革命」那永不知足的缔造者——断头台。

===Original===
She had seen the gay, lighthearted people of this Star-City turned to howling beasts of prey, its women changed to sexless vultures, with murderous talons implanted in everything that is noble, high or beautiful.
She was not twenty when the feeble, vacillating monarch and his imperious consort were dragged back—a pair of humiliated prisoners—to the capital from which they had tried to flee.
Two years later, she had heard the cries of an entire people exulting over a regicide. Then the murder of Marat, by a young girl like herself, the pale-faced, large-eyed Charlotte, who had committed a crime for the sake of a conviction. "Greater than Brutus!" some had called her. Greater than Joan of Arc, for it was to a mission of evil and of sin that she was called from the depths of her Breton village, and not to one of glory and triumph.
"Greater than Brutus!"

===Chinese===
她眼看着这座星辰之城（Star-City）里快活而无忧的人们变成了嚎叫的猛兽，看着它的女人们变成了失去女性特征的兀鹰，用杀气腾腾的利爪，攫住一切高贵、崇高而美丽的东西。
她还不满二十岁，那位软弱而优柔的君王和他威严的王后，就被当作一对受尽屈辱的囚徒，押回了他们曾试图逃离的都城。
两年之后，她听见全体人民为弑君而欢呼的声音。接着是马拉（Marat）之死，杀死他的也是一个和她一样的姑娘——那位面色苍白、眼睛大大的夏洛特（Charlotte），她为了一个信念而犯下了罪行。有人称她「比布鲁图斯更伟大！」她比贞德更伟大，因为从她布列塔尼村庄的深处召她去担当的，是一个邪恶与罪恶的使命，而非光荣与凯旋的使命。
「比布鲁图斯更伟大！」

===Original===
Juliette followed the trial of Charlotte Corday with all the passionate ardour of her exalted temperament.
Just think what an effect it must have had upon the mind of this young girl, who for nine years—the best of her life—had also lived with the idea of a sublime mission pervading her very soul.
She watched Charlotte Corday at her trial. Conquering her natural repulsion for such scenes, and the crowds which usually watched them, she had forced her way into the foremost rank of the narrow gallery which overlooked the Hall of the Revolutionary Tribunal.
She heard the indictment, heard Tinville's speech and the calling of the witnesses.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特以她那热烈天性所能有的全部炽情，紧盯着夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）受审的经过。
想一想吧，这对一个年轻姑娘的心灵该是怎样的影响：九年来——那正是她生命中最好的年华——她同样活在一个崇高使命的念头里，那念头浸透了她的整个灵魂。
她注视着受审中的夏洛特·科黛。她克制了自己对这类场面、以及惯常围观这类场面的人群的本能反感，硬挤进了俯临革命法庭（Tribunal Révolutionnaire）大厅的那道狭窄楼廊的最前一排。
她听了起诉书，听了坦维尔（Tinville）的公诉陈词和证人的传唤。

===Original===
"All this is unnecessary. I killed Marat!"
Juliette heard the fresh young voice ringing out clearly above the murmur of voices, the howls of execration; she saw the beautiful young face, clear, calm, impassive.
"I killed Marat!"
And there in the special space allotted to the Citizen-Deputies, sitting among those who represented the party of the Moderate Gironde, was Paul Déroulède, the man whom she had sworn to pursue with a vengeance as great, as complete, as that which guided Charlotte Corday's hand.

===Chinese===
「这一切都是多余的。马拉是我杀的！」
朱丽叶特听见那清脆年轻的声音，在人声的嗡嗡嘈杂和咒骂的吼叫之上清亮地响起；她看见那张美丽的年轻面孔，明净、沉静、不动声色。
「马拉是我杀的！」
而就在专留给公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）们的那个特别席区里，坐在代表温和的吉伦特派（Gironde）的人们中间的，正是保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）——她曾立誓要以同夏洛特·科黛之手所指引的那样宏大、那样彻底的复仇去追索的那个人。

===Original===
She watched him during the trial, and wondered if he had any presentiment of the hatred which dogged him, like unto the one which had dogged Marat.
He was very dark, almost swarthy, a son of the South, with brown hair, free from powder, thrown back and revealing the brow of a student rather than that of a legislator. He watched Charlotte Corday earnestly, and Juliette who watched him saw the look of measureless pity, which softened the otherwise hard look of his close-set eyes.
He made an impassioned speech for the defence: a speech which has become historic. It would have cost any other man his head.
Juliette marvelled at his courage; to defend Charlotte Corday was equivalent to acquiescing in the death of Marat: Marat, the friend of the people; Marat, whom his funeral orators had compared to the Great, the Sacred Leveller of Mankind!

===Chinese===
审判期间她一直注视着他，心里纳闷，他是否预感到那纠缠着他的仇恨——与那纠缠过马拉的仇恨如出一辙。
他肤色很深，近乎黝黑，是个南方人的儿子，棕色的头发不扑粉，向后梳着，露出一个更像学者而非立法者的额头。他专注地望着夏洛特·科黛；而注视着他的朱丽叶特，看见一种无边的怜悯软化了他那双靠得很近的眼睛里原本严厉的神情。
他为被告作了一篇慷慨激昂的辩护陈词：一篇已成历史的陈词。换了任何别人，这颗脑袋是要保不住的。
朱丽叶特惊叹他的胆量：为夏洛特·科黛辩护，就等于认可马拉之死；而马拉是「人民之友」，是悼词作者们拿来比作人类那位伟大而神圣的拉平者（the Great, the Sacred Leveller of Mankind）的马拉！

===Original===
But Déroulède's speech was not a defence, it was an appeal. The most eloquent man of that eloquent age, his words seemed to find that hidden bit of sentiment which still lurked in the hearts of these strange protagonists of Hate.
