Everyone round Juliette listened as he spoke: "It is Citoyen Déroulède!" whispered the bloodthirsty Amazons, who sat knitting in the gallery.
But there was no further comment. A huge, magnificently-equipped hospital for sick children had been thrown open in Paris that very morning, a gift to the nation from Citoyen Déroulède. Surely he was privileged to talk a little, if it pleased him. His hospital would cover quite a good many defalcations.
Even the rabid Mountain, Danton, Merlin, Santerre, shrugged their shoulders. "It is Déroulède, let him talk an he list. Murdered Marat said of him that he was not dangerous."

===Chinese===
但德鲁莱德的陈词不是辩护，而是呼吁。作为那个雄辩时代最雄辩的人，他的言辞仿佛探进了这些以恨为主角的怪人心中那一线尚在潜伏的柔情。
朱丽叶特四周的人都在听他讲：「是德鲁莱德公民！」坐在楼廊里一边织着毛线的那些嗜血悍妇悄声说。
但再没有别的议论了。就在当天早晨，一座规模宏大、设备精良的儿童医院在巴黎落成启用，那是德鲁莱德公民献给国家的一份厚礼。他自然有资格在高兴时说上几句。他那座医院，足可以抵掉不少的亏空呢。
连狂热的山岳派（Mountain）——丹东（Danton）、梅尔兰（Merlin）、桑特尔（Santerre）——也只是耸耸肩：「是德鲁莱德嘛，他爱说就让他说去。被害的马拉说过，他并不危险。」

===Original===
Juliette heard it all. The knitters round her were talking loudly. Even Charlotte was almost forgotten whilst Déroulède talked. He had a fine voice, of strong calibre, which echoed powerfully through the hall.
He was rather short, but broad-shouldered and well knit, with an expressive hand, which looked slender and delicate below the fine lace ruffle.
Charlotte Corday was condemned. All Déroulède's eloquence could not save her.
Juliette left the court in a state of mad exultation. She was very young: the scenes she had witnessed in the past two years could not help but excite the imagination of a young girl, left entirely to her own intellectual and moral resources.

===Chinese===
这一切朱丽叶特都听见了。她四周织毛线的女人们在大声交谈。德鲁莱德讲话的时候，连夏洛特也几乎被忘了。他嗓音洪亮，音质浑厚，在大厅里回荡，极具气势。
他个子偏矮，却肩宽体健，肌肉匀称，有一只富有表情的手——在那细雅的蕾丝褶袖之下，显得修长而秀气。
夏洛特·科黛被判处了死刑。德鲁莱德全部的雄辩也救不了她。
朱丽叶特离开法庭时，处于一种癫狂的亢奋之中。她非常年轻：过去两年里目睹的种种场面，不能不激荡一个全靠自己的智力与道德资源支撑的年轻姑娘的想象。

===Original===
What scenes! Great God!
And now to wait for an opportunity! Charlotte Corday, the half-educated little provincial should not put to shame Mademoiselle de Marny, the daughter of a hundred dukes, of those who had made France before she took to unmaking herself.
But she could not formulate any definite plans. Pétronelle, poor old soul, her only confidante, was not of the stuff that heroines are made of. Juliette felt impelled by duty, and duty at best is not so prompt a counsellor as love or hate.
Her adventure outside Déroulède's house had not been premeditated. Impulse and coincidence had worked their will with her.

===Chinese===
那些场面！我的上帝！
现在只等时机了！夏洛特·科黛，那个受教育不全的小省城姑娘，不该让马尔尼小姐蒙羞——她是一百位公爵的后裔，属于那些缔造了法兰西的人，尽管后来法兰西亲手开始拆毁自己。
但她还定不出任何明确的计划。佩特罗内勒，可怜的老人，她唯一的倾诉对象，不是当女主角的那块材料。朱丽叶特感到被责任驱使着，而责任充其量也不及爱或恨那样催人行动。
她在德鲁莱德家门外的那场历险，并非预谋。是冲动与巧合主宰了她。

===Original===
She had been in the habit, daily, for the past month, of wandering down the Rue Ecole de Médecine, ostensibly to gaze at Marat's dwelling, as crowds of idlers were wont to do, but really in order to look at Déroulède's house. Once or twice she saw him coming or going from home. Once she caught sight of the inner hall, and of a young girl in a dark kirtle and snow-white kerchief bidding him goodbye at his door. Another time she caught sight of him at the corner of the street, helping that same young girl over the muddy pavement. He had just met her, and she was carrying a basket of provisions: he took it from her and carried it to the house.
Chivalrous—eh?—and innately so, evidently, for the girl was slightly deformed: hardly a hunchback, but weak and unattractive-looking, with melancholy eyes, and a pale, pinched face.
It was the thought of that little act of simple chivalry, witnessed the day before, which caused Juliette to provoke the scene which, but for Déroulède's timely interference, might have ended so fatally. But she reckoned on that interference: the whole thing had occurred to her suddenly, and she had carried it through.
Had not her father said to her that when the time came, God would show her a means to the end?

===Chinese===
一个月以来，她每天习惯性地踱到医学院街（Rue Ecole de Médecine）去，表面上像那些游手好闲的人群一样，瞻望马拉的故居，实际上是为了看德鲁莱德的房子。有一两次，她看见他从家里出来或回家。有一次，她瞥见了内部的门厅，看见一个穿深色布裙、雪白头巾的年轻姑娘在他门口向他道别。另一次，她瞥见他在街角搀扶着同一位姑娘跨过泥泞的人行道。他是刚碰上她的，她提着一篮食物：他把篮子接了过来，一直送到家门口。
多有骑士风度，呃？而且显然是天生的，因为那姑娘身形微有残疾：算不上驼背，却孱弱而不起眼，眼神忧郁，脸色苍白而憔悴。
正是头一天目睹的这桩朴素骑士风度的小事，促使朱丽叶特去挑起那场风波——若非德鲁莱德及时援手，它本可能以极惨的结局收场。但她算准了他会援手：整件事是她在刹那间想出来的，然后一气做了下去。
她的父亲不是对她说过吗？时候到了，上帝自会向她指明通往目的的门路。

===Original===
And now she was inside the house of the man who had murdered her brother and sent her sorrowing father, a poor, senseless maniac, tottering to the grave.
Would God's finger point again, and show her what to do next, how best to accomplish what she had sworn to do?

===Chinese===
而如今，她已置身于那个人的宅邸之内——就是那个害死她哥哥、又害得她悲伤的父亲，一个可怜的、神志全无的疯子，颤颤巍巍走向坟墓的人。
上帝的手指会不会再一次指给她看，下一步该做什么，怎样才能最好地完成她立誓要做的事？

## III Hospitality / III 殷勤款待

===Original===
"Is there anything more I can do for you now, mademoiselle?"
The gentle, timid voice roused Juliette from the contemplation of the past.
She smiled at Anne Mie, and held her hand out towards her.
"You have all been so kind," she said, "I want to get up now and thank you all."
"Don't move unless you feel quite well."
"I am quite well now. Those horrid people frightened me so, that is why I fainted."
"They would have half-killed you, if—"

===Chinese===
「小姐，现在还有什么需要我为您做的吗？」
那温柔而怯生生的声音，把朱丽叶特从对往事的沉思中唤醒。
她朝安娜·米（Anne Mie）笑了笑，向她伸出手去。
「你们大家都待我这么好，」她说，「我现在想起来了，要谢谢你们大家。」
「要是觉得不舒服，就别起来。」
「我现在好得很。那些可怕的人把我吓成那样，所以我才晕了过去。」
「要不是——他们差点儿就把您打个半死了。」

===Original===
"Will you tell me where I am?" asked Juliette.
"In the house of M. Paul Déroulède—I should have said of Citizen-Deputy Déroulède. He rescued you from the mob, and pacified them. He has such a beautiful voice that he can make anyone listen to him, and—"
"And you are fond of him, mademoiselle?" added Juliette, suddenly feeling a mist of tears rising to her eyes.

===Chinese===
「能告诉我这是哪儿吗？」朱丽叶特问。
「在保罗·德鲁莱德先生的府上——我该说，德鲁莱德公会议员的府上。是他把您从暴众手里救出来的，还把那些人安抚了下去。他的嗓音那么好听，谁都会听他的，而且——」
「这么说，您很爱他，小姐？」朱丽叶特接了一句，忽然觉得一片泪雾涌上了眼睛。

===Original===
"Of course I am fond of him," rejoined the other girl simply, whilst a look of the most tenderhearted devotion seemed to beautify her pale face. "He and Madame Déroulède have brought me up; I never knew my parents. They have cared for me, and he has taught me all I know."
"What do they call you, mademoiselle?"
"My name is Anne Mie."
"And mine, Juliette—Juliette Marny," she added after a slight hesitation. "I have no parents either. My old nurse, Pétronelle, has brought me up, and—But tell me more about M. Déroulède—I owe him so much, I'd like to know him better."

===Chinese===
「我当然爱他，」另一位姑娘朴实地答道，一张苍白的脸上，泛起最柔情脉脉的挚爱神色，反而显得美了。「是他和德鲁莱德夫人把我养大的；我从没见过我的父母。是他们照管了我，我所知道的一切，都是他教我的。」
「小姐，他们叫您什么？」
「我叫安娜·米。」
「我叫朱丽叶特——朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny），」她略一迟疑，又补了一句。「我也没爹没娘。是佩特罗内勒，我的老奶妈，把我带大的，还有——可是再多跟我讲讲德鲁莱德先生吧——我欠他太多了，我想更了解他一些。」

===Original===
"Will you not let me arrange your hair?" said Anne Mie as if purposely evading a direct reply. "M. Déroulède is in the salon with madame. You can see him then."
Juliette asked no more questions, but allowed Anne Mie to tidy her hair for her, to lend her a fresh kerchief and generally to efface all traces of her terrible adventure. She felt puzzled and tearful. Anne Mie's gentleness seemed somehow to jar on her spirits. She could not understand the girl's position in the Déroulède household. Was she a relative, or a superior servant? In these troublous times she might easily have been both.
In any case she was a childhood's companion of the Citizen-Deputy—whether on an equal or a humbler footing, Juliette would have given much to ascertain.
With the marvellous instinct peculiar to women of temperament, she had already divined Anne Mie's love for Déroulède. The poor young cripple's very soul seemed to quiver magnetically at the bare mention of his name, her whole face became transfigured: Juliette even thought her beautiful then.

===Chinese===
「让我替您把头发理一理，好不好？」安娜·米说，像是故意躲开正面回答。「德鲁莱德先生这会儿正和夫人在客厅里。到时候您就见着他了。」
朱丽叶特不再多问，任凭安娜·米替她理好头发，借给她一条干净的头巾，总之，把她那场可怕经历的一切痕迹都抹去了。她心里又困惑，又想哭。安娜·米的温柔，不知怎的，竟有些刺得她心神不宁。她弄不明白这姑娘在德鲁莱德家是什么身份。是亲眷呢，还是一个上等仆人？在这动乱的年月里，她完全可能两者都是。
无论如何，她是公会议员从小一起长大的伙伴——究竟是平等的地位，还是低人一等，朱丽叶特情愿付出许多，也要弄个明白。
凭着性情中女子所独具的奇妙直觉，她已猜到了安娜·米对德鲁莱德的恋慕。这个可怜的年轻残废，只要一提起他的名字，整个灵魂仿佛都起了磁性的震颤，整张脸都变了样：那时连朱丽叶特都觉得她美。

===Original===
She looked at herself critically in the glass, and adjusted a curl, which looked its best when it was rebellious. She scrutinised her own face carefully; why? she could not tell: another of those subtle feminine instincts perhaps.
The becoming simplicity of the prevailing mode suited her to perfection. The waist line, rather high but clearly defined—a precursor of the later more accentuated fashion—gave grace to her long slender limbs, and emphasised the lissomeness of her figure. The kerchief, edged with fine lace, and neatly folded across her bosom, softened the contour of her girlish bust and shoulders.
And her hair was a veritable glory round her dainty, piquant face. Soft, fair, and curly, it emerged in a golden halo from beneath the prettiest little lace cap imaginable.
She turned and faced Anne Mie, ready to follow her out of the room, and the young crippled girl sighed as she smoothed down the folds of her own apron, and gave a final touch to the completion of Juliette's attire.

===Chinese===
她对着镜子挑剔地端详自己，抚平了一绺鬈发——那鬈发偏是不服帖时最好看。她细细审视自己的脸；为什么呢？她说不上来：也许又是那种微妙的女性直觉在作祟。
时下流行的那种雅致的简朴装束，与她再合适不过。腰线收得偏高，却清晰利落——是后来那种更强调腰身式样的先声——给她修长窈窕的肢体平添了几分秀雅，也衬出了她身段的轻盈柔美。那条镶着细蕾丝的头巾，整整齐齐地折叠在胸前，柔化了她少女的胸廓与肩头的轮廓。
而她的头发，环着她那娇俏动人的脸庞，真可谓一头华彩。柔软、浅淡、蜷曲，从一顶想得出的小小蕾丝帽最美的式样底下透出来，宛如一圈金色的光晕。
她转过身来，面对着安娜·米，准备随她走出房间；那年轻的跛足姑娘叹了一口气，抚平自己围裙的褶皱，最后整理了一下，朱丽叶特的装束才算齐备。

===Original===
The time before the evening meal slipped by like a dream-hour for Juliette.
She had lived so much alone, had led such an introspective life, that she had hardly realised and understood all that was going on around her. At the time when the inner vitality of France first asserted itself and then swept away all that hindered its mad progress, she was tied to the invalid chair of her half-demented father; then, after that, the sheltering walls of the Ursuline Convent had hidden from her mental vision the true meaning of the great conflict, between the Old Era and the New.
Déroulède was neither a pedant nor yet a revolutionary: his theories were Utopian and he had an extraordinary overpowering sympathy for his fellow-men.
After the first casual greetings with Juliette, he had continued a discussion with his mother, which the young girl's entrance had interrupted.
He seemed to take but little notice of her, although at times his dark, keen eyes would seek hers, as if challenging her for a reply.
He was talking of the mob of Paris, whom he evidently understood so well. Incidents such as the one which Juliette had provoked, had led to rape and theft, often to murder, before now: but outside Citizen-Deputy Déroulède's house everything was quiet, half-an-hour after Juliette's escape from that howling, brutish crowd.
He had merely spoken to them, for about twenty minutes, and they had gone away quite quietly, without even touching one hair of his head. He seemed to love them: to know how to separate the little good that was in them, from that hard crust of evil, which misery had put around their hearts.

===Chinese===
对朱丽叶特来说，晚饭前的那段时光，像梦幻般的一小时，悄然溜了过去。
她一向活得那么孤单，过着那么内向自省的日子，以至于对身边发生的一切，几乎无从了解，也无从体会。当法兰西内在的生命力初露锋芒、继而把一切阻挡它疯狂进程的东西统统扫开的时候，她正被拴在半疯癫父亲的病榻旁；再后来，乌尔苏拉会修道院的高墙又把她遮蔽起来，使她的心眼看不见那场新旧时代之间大冲突的真正含义。
德鲁莱德既非迂腐学究，也谈不上革命家：他的理论是乌托邦式的，而对同胞却怀着一种异乎寻常、沛然莫御的同情心。
同朱丽叶特略事寒暄之后，他便接着与母亲继续讨论起来——那场谈话，本是这位年轻姑娘的进门打断的。
他似乎不大留意她，虽然他那一双黑亮的锐眼，不时会去寻她的目光，仿佛在向她挑战，要她答话。
他谈的是巴黎的暴众，显然他对那些人了如指掌。像朱丽叶特惹起的那类事，从前曾酿成过奸淫掳掠，往往还有凶杀；可如今，德鲁莱德公会议员的宅子外面，一片安静——朱丽叶特逃进这里才半个钟头，那群狂嚎的野蛮人已经散尽。
他不过对他们讲了二十来分钟的话，他们就安安静静地走开了，连他一根头发都没碰。他似乎爱着他们：懂得怎样把他们身上那一点点善，从困苦套在他们心外的那层又冷又硬的恶壳里剥出来。

===Original===
Once he addressed Juliette somewhat abruptly: "Pardon me, mademoiselle, but for your own sake we must guard you a prisoner here awhile. No one would harm you under this roof, but it would not be safe for you to cross the neighbouring streets tonight."
"But I must go, monsieur. Indeed, indeed I must!" she said earnestly. "I am deeply grateful to you, but I could not leave Pétronelle."
"Who is Pétronelle?"
"My dear old nurse, monsieur. She has never left me. Think how anxious and miserable she must be, at my prolonged absence."
"Where does she live?"
"At No. 15 Rue Taitbout, but—"

===Chinese===
有一回，他有些突兀地对朱丽叶特开口：「恕我直言，小姐，为了您自己的缘故，我们只得把您在这里当一会儿囚徒看管起来。在这片屋檐底下没人会伤害您，可是今晚您若要穿过附近几条街，就不安全了。」
「可我必须走，先生。真的，真的必须走！」她恳切地说。「我对您感激不尽，可我不能撇下佩特罗内勒。」
「佩特罗内勒是谁？」
「我亲爱的老奶妈，先生。她从来没有离开过我。请想一想，我这么久不回去，她该多么焦急、多么难受。」
「她住在哪儿？」
「泰布街（Rue Taitbout）十五号，可是——」

===Original===
"Will you allow me to take her a message?—telling her that you are safe and under my roof, where it is obviously more prudent that you should remain at present."
"If you think it best, monsieur," she replied.
Inwardly she was trembling with excitement. God had not only brought her to this house, but willed that she should stay in it.
"In whose name shall I take the message, mademoiselle?" he asked.
"My name is Juliette Marny."
She watched him keenly as she said it, but there was not the slightest sign in his expressive face, to show that he had recognised the name.
Ten years is a long time, and everyone had lived through so much during those years! A wave of intense wrath swept through Juliette's soul, as she realised that he had forgotten. The name meant nothing to him! It did not recall to him the fact that his hand was stained with blood. During ten years she had suffered, she had fought with herself, fought for him as it were, against the Fate which she was destined to mete out to him, whilst he had forgotten, or at least had ceased to think.
He bowed to her and went out of the room.
The wave of wrath subsided, and she was left alone with Madame Déroulède: presently Anne Mie came in.

===Chinese===
「容我给她捎个口信，可好？——告诉她，您很平安，就住在我的屋檐下；眼下您留在这里，显然是更稳妥的。」
「您要是觉得这样最好，先生。」她答道。
她心里正激动得发颤。上帝不仅把她领进了这座宅子，而且要她留在这里。
「小姐，口信该以谁的名义捎去呢？」他问。
「我叫朱丽叶特·马尔尼。」
她说这话时，紧紧盯着他，可他那富于表情的脸上，没有一丝一毫的迹象，表明这个名字唤起了什么。
十年是一段漫长的岁月，而那些年里，人人都熬过了太多的事！一股狂怒的巨浪席卷过朱丽叶特的灵魂，因为她明白了：他忘了。这个名字对他毫无意义！它没有让他记起，他的手上曾染着血污。十年来，她在受苦，她在和自己搏斗，仿佛是替他在搏斗，抗拒着她命定要降给他的那个命运；而他，却忘了，至少已不再去想。
他向她一躬身，走出了房间。
怒潮退了下去，屋里只剩下她和德鲁莱德夫人；不多时，安娜·米也进来了。

===Original===
The three women chatted together, waiting for the return of the master of the house. Juliette felt well and, in spite of herself, almost happy. She had lived so long in the miserable, little attic alone with Pétronelle that she enjoyed the well-being of this refined home. It was not so grand or gorgeous of course as her father's princely palace opposite the Louvre, a wreck now, since it was annexed by the Committee of National Defence, for the housing of soldiery. But the Déroulèdes' home was essentially a refined one. The delicate china on the tall chimneypiece, the few bits of Buhl and Vernis Martin about the room, the vision through the open doorway of the supper-table spread with a fine white cloth, and sparkling with silver, all spoke of fastidious tastes, of habits of luxury and elegance, which the spirit of Equality and Anarchy had not succeeded in eradicating.
When Déroulède came back, he brought an atmosphere of breezy cheerfulness with him.
The street was quiet now, and when walking past the hospital—his own gift to the Nation—he had been loudly cheered. One or two ironical voices had asked him what he had done with the aristo and her lace furbelows, but it remained at that and Mademoiselle Marny need have no fear.

===Chinese===
三个女人一起闲谈着，等候一家之主回来。朱丽叶特觉得身子很好，而且不由自主地，几乎感到快活。她同佩特罗内勒在凄凉的小阁楼里相依为命太久了，如今享受着这雅致人家的安乐，格外觉得受用。它自然比不上她父亲那座卢浮宫对面的王侯般的宅邸气派华美——那宅子如今已成一片废墟，因为被国防委员会（Committee of National Defence）征了去驻兵。可德鲁莱德家这个家，骨子里是精致的。高高的壁炉架上陈设的细瓷器，屋里那几件布尔镶嵌与马丁漆（Buhl and Vernis Martin）的家具，敞开的门口望进去，铺着细白桌布、银器闪烁的晚餐桌，处处诉说着主人的考究趣味、奢华与雅致的习惯——平等与无政府的精神，终究没能把这些根除。
德鲁莱德回来时，带着一身轻快爽朗的气息。
街上如今安静了；他走过那座医院——那是他自己献给国家的一份礼——时，还赢得了一片响亮的欢呼。有一两个讥诮的声音问他，把那个「aristo」（贵族婆娘）和她的蕾丝花边裙子弄到哪里去了，但也就到此为止，马尔尼小姐尽可放心。

===Original===
He had brought Pétronelle along with him: his careless, lavish hospitality would have suggested the housing of Juliette's entire domestic establishment, had she possessed one.
As it was, the worthy old soul's deluge of happy tears had melted his kindly heart. He offered her and her young mistress shelter, until the small cloud should have rolled by.
After that he suggested a journey to England. Emigration now was the only real safety, and Mademoiselle Marny had unpleasantly drawn on herself the attention of the Paris rabble. No doubt, within the next few days her name would figure among the "suspect." She would be safest out of the country, and could not do better than place herself under the guidance of that English enthusiast, who had helped so many persecuted Frenchmen to escape from the terrors of the Revolution: the man who was such a thorn in the flesh of the Committee of Public Safety, and who went by the nickname of The Scarlet Pimpernel.

===Chinese===
他是把佩特罗内勒一道带来的：他那豪爽而慷慨的待客之道，倘若朱丽叶特有整套的仆役家当，只怕连她全家老小都要一并举荐收留下来的。
事实上，是那位可敬老人的喜泪如泉，融化了她的仁厚心肠。他向她和她的年轻女主人许下了栖身之所，直到那一小片乌云滚过天际为止。
过后，他又提议去英格兰走一遭。如今流亡国外才是唯一真正的安全，而马尔尼小姐已不干不净地惹上了巴黎暴民的注意。毫无疑问，不出几日，她的名字就会列进「嫌疑犯」的名单。她离了国才最安全，而她最好的出路，莫过于把自己托付给那位英国的狂热侠士——他帮助过许许多多受迫害的法国人逃离革命的恐怖：这个人，是扎在救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）肉里的一根刺，诨名唤作红花侠（The Scarlet Pimpernel）。

## IV The Faithful House-Dog / IV 忠心的看家狗

===Original===
After supper they talked of Charlotte Corday.
Juliette clung to the vision of that heroine, and liked to talk of her. She appeared as a justification of her own actions, which somehow seemed to require justification.
She loved to hear Paul Déroulède talk; liked to provoke his enthusiasm and to see his stern, dark face light up with the inward fire of the enthusiast.

===Chinese===
晚饭过后，他们谈起了夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）。
朱丽叶特心里紧紧依着那位女杰的影子，喜欢谈论她。在她眼前，那个影子仿佛成了她自己行为的辩护——而她的行为，不知怎的，似乎总需要一点辩护。
她爱听保罗·德鲁莱德说话；爱去挑动他的热情，爱看他那严峻黝黑的脸膛，被狂热者胸中的火焰照亮。

===Original===
She had openly avowed herself as the daughter of the Duc de Marny. When she actually named her father, and her brother killed in duel, she saw Déroulède looking long and searchingly at her. Evidently he wondered if she knew everything: but she returned his gaze fearlessly and frankly, and he apparently was satisfied.
Madame Déroulède seemed to know nothing of the circumstances of that duel. Déroulède tried to draw Juliette out, to make her speak of her brother. She replied to his questions quite openly, but there was nothing in what she said, suggestive of the fact that she knew who killed her brother.
She wanted him to know who she was. If he feared an enemy in her, there was yet time enough for him to close his doors against her.
But less than a minute later, he had renewed his warmest offers of hospitality.
"Until we can arrange for your journey to England," he added with a short sigh, as if reluctant to part from her.

===Chinese===
她已公然表明自己是马尔尼公爵（Duc de Marny）的女儿。当她真正说出父亲的名字、说出那个死于决斗的兄长时，她看见德鲁莱德久久地、探询地凝视着她。显然他是在纳闷，她是否知道全部底细；可她坦然无惧、光明磊落地回望着他，他似乎也就放心了。
至于那场决斗的种种情形，德鲁莱德夫人似乎一无所知。德鲁莱德试探着引朱丽叶特开口，想让她谈谈她的兄长。她十分坦率地回答他的问话，可她话语里没有一丝痕迹，暗示她知道是谁杀死了自己的哥哥。
她要他知道她是什么人。倘若他惧怕她这个仇敌，还来得及对她闭门不纳。
然而不到一分钟，他又重新发出了最热忱的款待之请。
「等我们把您去英格兰的旅程安排好再说，」他补了一句，短短地叹了口气，仿佛舍不得同她分离。

===Original===
To Juliette his attitude seemed one of complete indifference for the wrong he had done to her and to her father: feeling that she was an avenging spirit, with flaming sword in hand, pursuing her brother's murderer like a relentless Nemesis, she would have preferred to see him cowed before her, even afraid of her, though she was only a young and delicate girl.
She did not understand that in the simplicity of his heart, he only wished to make amends. The quarrel with the young Vicomte de Marny had been forced upon him, the fight had been honourable and fair, and on his side fought with every desire to spare the young man. He had merely been the instrument of Fate, but he felt happy that Fate once more used him as her tool, this time to save the sister.
Whilst Déroulède and Juliette talked together Anne Mie cleared the supper-table, then came and sat on a low stool at madame's feet. She took no part in the conversation, but every now and then Juliette felt the girl's melancholy eyes fixed almost reproachfully upon her.

===Chinese===
在朱丽叶特眼里，他的这副态度，等于对他加诸她和她父亲的伤害全然漠然：她自觉是一个复仇的精灵，手执出鞘的利剑，像无情的天罚女神（Nemesis）一般，追踪着杀死她兄长的凶手；她倒宁愿看见他在自己面前畏缩，甚至怕她——尽管她不过是个娇弱年轻的姑娘。
她不明白，他只是怀着一颗质朴的心，想要弥补过失。同年轻的马尔尼子爵的那场口角，是对方强加于他的；那场决斗光明正大，他这一边出手时，处处只想手下留情，饶恕那年轻人。他不过是命运手中的一件工具罢了；可他心里欢喜，因为命运又一次拿他当了器皿——这一次，是去搭救那位妹妹。
德鲁莱德同朱丽叶特交谈的时候，安娜·米收拾了晚餐桌，随后走过来，坐在夫人脚边的一张矮凳上。她不参与谈话，可是朱丽叶特不时觉得，这姑娘那双忧郁的眼睛正盯着自己，目光里几乎带着责备。

===Original===
When Juliette had retired with Pétronelle, Déroulède took Anne Mie's hand in his.
"You will be kind to my guest, Anne Mie, won't you? She seems very lonely, and has gone through a great deal."
"Not more than I have," murmured the young girl involuntarily.
"You are not happy, Anne Mie? I thought—"
"Is a wretched, deformed creature ever happy?" she said with sudden vehemence, as tears of mortification rushed to her eyes, in spite of herself.
"I did not think that you were wretched," he replied with some sadness, "and neither in my eyes, nor in my mother's, are you in any way deformed."

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特随佩特罗内勒退下去以后，德鲁莱德把安娜·米的手握在自己的手里。
「你会好好待我的客人吧，安娜·米？她显得非常孤单，又受过许多苦。」
「不会比我受的苦更多，」年轻姑娘不由自主地喃喃道。
「你不快活吗，安娜·米？我还以为——」
「一个可怜巴巴的残废，还会有快活吗？」她突然激烈地说，羞愤的眼泪不由自主地涌上了眼睛。
「我并没有觉得你可怜，」他有些忧伤地答道，「无论在我眼里，还是在我母亲眼里，你一点也不残废。」

===Original===
Her mood changed at once. She clung to him, pressing his hand between her own.
"Forgive me! I—I don't know what's the matter with me tonight," she said with a nervous little laugh. "Let me see, you asked me to be kind to Mademoiselle Marny, did you not?"
He nodded with a smile.
"Of course I'll be kind to her. Isn't everyone kind to one who is young and beautiful, and has great, appealing eyes, and soft, curly hair? Ah me! how easy is the path in life for some people! What do you want me to do, Paul? Wait on her? Be her little maid? Soothe her nerves or what? I'll do it all, though in her eyes I shall remain both wretched and deformed, a creature to pity, the harmless, necessary house-dog—"

===Chinese===
她的心绪立时转了过来。她偎近他，把他的手紧紧夹在自己两只手中间。
「原谅我！我——我不知道今晚我是怎么了，」她神经质地轻轻一笑。「让我想想，你是要我好好待马尔尼小姐，是不是？」
他含笑点了点头。
「我当然会好好待她。一个人又年轻又美丽，生着一双大大的、惹人怜爱的眼睛，一头柔软的鬈发——谁对她不好呢？唉！有些人的人生道路，走起来多容易啊！你要我做什么呢，保罗？伺候她？当她的贴身小丫头？替她安神，还是怎么的？我都做，尽管在她眼里，我终究还是又可怜又残废，一个只配让人怜悯的东西，一条无害而少不了的看家狗——」

===Original===
She paused a moment: said "Good night" to him, and turned to go, candle in hand, looking pathetic and fragile, with that ugly contour of shoulder, which Déroulède assured her he could not see.
The candle flickered in the draught, illumining the thin, pinched face, the large melancholy eyes of the faithful house-dog.
"Who can watch and bite!" she said half-audibly as she slipped out of the room. "For I do not trust you, my fine madam, and there was something about that comedy this afternoon, which somehow, I don't quite understand."

===Chinese===
她停了片刻：向他道了声「晚安」，便转身要走，一手执烛，样子凄楚而纤弱，肩头带着那副丑陋的轮廓——尽管德鲁莱德一再向她保证，他根本看不见。
烛光在穿堂风里摇曳，照亮了这条忠心的看家狗那瘦削清苦的脸，和她那双忧郁的大眼睛。
「既能守夜，又能咬人！」她溜出房间时，半是自语地低声说。「因为我信不过你，我的漂亮夫人；今天下午那出喜剧里，有点什么名堂，不知怎的，我就是看不透。」

## V A Day in the Woods / V 林中一日

===Original===
But whilst men and women set to work to make the towns of France hideous with their shrieks and their hootings, their mock-trials and bloody guillotines, they could not quite prevent Nature from working her sweet will with the country.
June, July, and August had received new names—they were now called Messidor, Thermidor, and Fructidor, but under these new names they continued to pour forth upon the earth the same old fruits, the same flowers, the same grass in the meadows and leaves upon the trees.
Messidor brought its quota of wild roses in the hedgerows, just as archaic June had done. Thermidor covered the barren cornfields with its flaming mantle of scarlet poppies, and Fructidor, though now called August, still tipped the wild sorrel with dots of crimson, and laid the first wash of tender colour on the pale cheeks of the ripening peaches.

===Chinese===
当男男女女忙不迭地把法兰西的城镇弄得鬼哭神嚎、喧嚣刺耳，用滑稽的审判和血淋淋的断头台把它变得狰狞可怖之时，他们却终究无法阻止大自然在乡间随心所欲地施展她的妙手。
六月、七月和八月都被换了新名字——如今称作获月（Messidor）、热月（Thermidor）和果月（Fructidor）；可是在这些新名字底下，它们依旧向大地倾泻同样古老的果实、同样的花朵，草地上依旧是同样的青草，树上依旧是同样的绿叶。
获月照旧往树篱间送来它那一份野蔷薇，一如往昔的六月。热月用它那猩红罂粟织成的火焰般的披风，盖住了荒芜的麦田；果月虽然如今被唤作八月，仍照旧给野酸模点上深红的小点，并把第一抹柔嫩的颜色，涂在渐渐成熟的桃子那灰白的脸颊上。

===Original===
And Juliette—young, girlish, feminine and inconsequent—had sighed for country and sunshine, had longed for a ramble in the woods, the music of the birds, the sight of the meadows sugared with marguerites.
She had left the house early: accompanied by Pétronelle, she had been rowed along the river as far as Suresnes. They had brought some bread and fresh butter, a little wine and fruit in a basket, and from here she meant to wander homewards through the woods.
It was all so peaceful, so remote: even the noise of shrieking, howling Paris did not reach the leafy thickets of Suresnes.
It almost seemed as if this little old-world village had been forgotten by the destroyers of France. It had never been a royal residence, the woods had never been preserved for royal sport: there was no vengeance to be wreaked upon its peaceful glades and sleepy, fragrant meadows.

===Chinese===
而朱丽叶特（Juliette）——年轻、娇憨、一派女儿家心性、没个定准——早已向往着乡野和阳光，渴望到树林里漫游一番，听听鸟儿的歌唱，看看那撒满白雏菊、像撒了糖霜一般的草地。
她一大早就出了门：由佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）陪伴，乘船沿河一直划到苏雷讷（Suresnes）。她们带了一篮子面包和新鲜黄油，还有一点葡萄酒和水果；她打算从这里穿过树林，一路散步回城。
这一切是那么安宁，那么远离尘嚣：就连巴黎那嘶吼嚎叫的喧嚣，也传不到苏雷讷这绿叶成荫的灌木丛里。
这个小小的古朴村庄，几乎像是已被法兰西的破坏者们遗忘了。它从未做过王家居所，这片树林也从未被划作王家猎苑：因而在它平静的林间空地和沉睡的芳草地上，并无什么复仇的怒火需要发泄。

===Original===
Juliette spent a happy day; she loved the flowers, the trees, the birds, and Pétronelle was silent and sympathetic. As the afternoon wore on, and it was time to go home, Juliette turned townwards with a sigh.
You all know that road through the woods, which lies to the northwest of Paris: so leafy, so secluded. No large, hundred-year-old trees, no fine oaks or antique elms, but numberless delicate stems of hazelnut and young ash, covered with honeysuckle at this time of year, sweet-smelling and so peaceful after that awful turmoil of the town.
Obedient to Madame Déroulède's suggestion, Juliette had tied a tricolour scarf round her waist, and a Phrygian cap of crimson cloth, with the inevitable rosette on one side, adorned her curly head.
She had gathered a huge bouquet of poppies, marguerites and blue lupin—Nature's tribute to the national colours—and as she wandered through the sylvan glades she looked like some quaint dweller of the woods—a sprite, mayhap—with old mother Pétronelle trotting behind her, like an attendant witch.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特快快活活地过了一天；她爱那些花儿、树木和飞鸟，佩特罗内勒则默默无言、善解人意。午后的时光渐渐消磨过去，该回家了，朱丽叶特叹了口气，转身朝城里的方向走去。
你们都认得巴黎西北方向那条穿过树林的路：绿叶繁茂，幽静僻远。这里没有高大参天的百年古木，没有挺秀的橡树或苍老的榆树，只有数不清的纤细榛树干和幼梣树，一到这个时节便爬满忍冬，芬芳袭人；熬过了城里那可怕的骚乱之后，这里是这样安宁。
朱丽叶特依了德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）的提议，腰间系了一条三色饰带，鬈发上还戴着一顶深红布料的弗里吉亚帽，帽侧照例缀着一朵红色玫瑰花结。
她采了一大捧花，有罂粟、雏菊和蓝色的羽扇豆——这是大自然献给国家三色的贡礼——她漫步在林间空地上时，看去就像什么林中的古怪居民，兴许是个精灵；老妈妈佩特罗内勒小跑着跟在她身后，倒像个随侍的巫婆。

===Original===
Suddenly she paused, for in the near distance she had perceived the sound of footsteps upon the leafy turf, and the next moment Paul Déroulède emerged from out the thicket and came rapidly towards her.
"We were so anxious about you at home!" he said, almost by way of an apology. "My mother became so restless—"
"That to quiet her fears you came in search of me!" she retorted with a gay little laugh, the laugh of a young girl, scarce a woman as yet, who feels that she is good to look at, good to talk to, who feels her wings for the first time, the wings with which to soar into that mad, merry, elusive land called Romance. Ay, her wings! but her power also! that sweet, subtle power of the woman: the yoke which men love, rail at, and love again, the yoke that enslaves them and gives them the joy of kings.

===Chinese===
她蓦地停住了脚，因为就在不远的地方，她听见长草的草皮上响起了脚步声；紧接着，保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）从灌木丛里钻了出来，快步向她走来。
「家里的人都急坏了，正担心您呢！」他说，那口气几乎像是赔不是。「我母亲坐立不安——」
「于是您就来寻我，好宽她的心！」她笑着回嘴，笑得轻快——那是一个少女的笑，她还不大算是个妇人呢，只觉得自己好看、招人爱听说话，觉得自己第一次生出了翅膀，好凭着这双翅膀，飞向那片疯狂、欢畅、飘忽难捉的国土，那国土名叫浪漫。是啊，她的翅膀！可还有她的权柄！女人那种甜蜜而微妙的权柄：那是男人们又爱又骂、骂了又爱的轭，是奴役他们、却又给他们帝王般欢愉的轭。

===Original===
How happy the day had been! Yet it had been incomplete!
Pétronelle was somewhat dull, and Juliette was too young to enjoy long companionship with her own thoughts. Now suddenly the day seemed to have become perfect. There was someone there to appreciate the charm of the woods, the beauty of that blue sky peeping though the tangled foliage of the honeysuckle-covered trees. There was someone to talk to, someone to admire the fresh white frock Juliette had put on that morning.
"But how did you know where to find me?" she asked with a quaint touch of immature coquetry.
"I didn't know," he replied quietly. "They told me you had gone to Suresness, and meant to wander homewards through the woods. It frightened me, for you will have to go through the northwest barrier, and—"
"Well?"
He smiled, and looked earnestly for a moment at the dainty apparition before him.

===Chinese===
这一天过得多么快活！可它毕竟还不圆满！
佩特罗内勒有些乏味，而朱丽叶特又太年轻，还不能久久地与自己的心事为伴作乐。这一下，这一天忽然显得十全十美了。身边有了人，能赏识这树林的魅力，赏识那从爬满忍冬的繁枝密叶间探出头来的一方蓝天之美。有了人可以说话，有人会赞美她那天早晨刚换上的雪白的新连衣裙。
「可您怎么知道上哪儿找我呢？」她问道，带着一点稚气未脱、古灵精怪的卖俏腔调。
「我并不知道，」他平静地答道。「他们告诉我，您去了苏雷讷，打算穿过树林散步回来。这可把我吓着了，因为您得从西北方向的关卡进城，而且——」
「而且怎么样？」
他微微一笑，目不转睛地把眼前这个娇俏的身影端详了片刻。

===Original===
"Well, you know!" he said gaily, "that tricolour scarf and the red cap are not quite sufficient as a disguise: you look anything but a staunch friend of the people. I guessed that your muslin frock would be clean, and that there would still be some telltale lace upon it."
She laughed again, and with delicate fingers lifted her pretty muslin frock, displaying a white frou-frou of flounces beneath the hem.
"How careless and childish!" he said, almost roughly.
"Would you have me coarse and grimy to be a fitting match for your partisans?" she retorted.
His tone of mentor nettled her, his attitude seemed to her priggish and dictatorial, and as the sun disappearing behind a sudden cloud, so her childish merriment quickly gave place to a feeling of unexplainable disappointment.

===Chinese===
「喏，您自己也明白！」他快活地说，「光凭那条三色饰带和这顶红帽，是化装不过关的：您看上去哪有一点人民忠实朋友的样子。我猜您那件平纹细布裙一定干干净净，上头多半还留着什么会露馅的花边。」
她又是一笑，用纤纤手指提起她那条漂亮的细布裙，露出裙裾底下一层窸窣作响的白色荷叶边。
「多么疏忽，多么孩子气！」他说，口气几乎有些粗鲁。
「怎么，要我又粗野又邋遢，才配得上您那些同党么？」她回敬道。
他那副训导者的腔调刺痛了她，他的态度在她看来既道学又专断；就像太阳倏地躲进一团乌云背后一样，她那孩子气的欢快，顷刻间被一种莫名其妙的失望情绪取代了。

===Original===
"I humbly beg your pardon," he said quietly, "And must crave your kind indulgence for my mood: but I have been so anxious—"
"Why should you be anxious about me?"
She had meant to say this indifferently, as if caring little what the reply might be: but in her effort to seem indifferent her voice became haughty, a reminiscence of the days when she still was the daughter of the Duc de Marny, the richest and most highborn heiress in France.
"Was that presumptuous?" he asked, with a slight touch of irony, in response to her own hauteur.
"It was merely unnecessary," she replied. "I have already laid too many burdens on your shoulders, without wishing to add that of anxiety."
"You have laid no burden on me," he said quietly, "save one of gratitude."

===Chinese===
「我谦卑地请您原谅，」他平静地说，「还须求您宽宏，体谅我的心绪：可我一直提心吊胆——」
「您何必为我提心吊胆呢？」
她原想说得漠不在乎，仿佛答复如何她毫不在意似的；可是为了装出这副冷淡，她的声调反而变得高傲起来——那声调让人回想起她还是马尔尼公爵（Duc de Marny）的女儿、还是法兰西最富有、门第最高的女继承人的那些日子。
「我这话僭越了么？」他问，语带一丝讥诮，回敬她的傲慢。
「只是多此一举罢了，」她答道。「我已经把太多担子压在您肩上了，不想再添上忧虑这一副。」
「您没有压给我什么担子，」他平静地说，「只有一副感恩的担子。」

===Original===
"Gratitude? What have I done?"
"You committed a foolish, thoughtless act outside my door, and gave me the chance of easing my conscience of a heavy load."
"In what way?"
"I had never hoped that the Fates would be so kind as to allow me to render a member of your family a slight service."
"I understand that you saved my life the other day, Monsieur Déroulède. I know that I am still in peril and that I owe my safety to you—"
"Do you also know that your brother owed his death to me?"

===Chinese===
「感恩？我做了什么呢？」
「您在我的门外做了一件愚蠢而欠思量的事，却给了我一个机会，让我卸下良心上的一副重担。」
「这话怎么讲？」
「我从未奢望命运女神会如此仁慈，容我为您家中的一员略效微劳。」
「我知道前几天您救了我的命，德鲁莱德先生。我知道我眼下仍在险境之中，我的平安是您赐予的——」
「那您可也知道，您哥哥的死，是由我而起的么？」

===Original===
She closed her lips firmly, unable to reply, wrathful with him, for having suddenly and without any warning, placed a clumsy hand upon that hidden sore.
"I always meant to tell you," he continued somewhat hurriedly; "for it almost seemed to me that I have been cheating you, these last few days. I don't suppose that you can quite realise what it means to me to tell you this just now; but I owe it to you, I think. In later years you might find out, and then regret the days you spent under my roof. I called you childish a moment ago, you must forgive me; I know that you are a woman, and hope therefore that you will understand me. I killed your brother in fair fight. He provoked me as no man was ever provoked before—"
"Is it necessary, M. Déroulède, that you should tell me all this?" she interrupted him with some impatience.
"I thought you ought to know."
"You must know, on the other hand, that I have no means of hearing the history of the quarrel from my brother's point of view now."

===Chinese===
她紧紧抿住了嘴唇，答不上话来，心里对他满怀恼怒——恨他这样突如其来、毫无预警地，把一只笨拙的手按在了那处隐秘的疮口上。
「我一直打算告诉您的，」他有些匆促地继续说，「因为这几天来，我几乎觉得自个儿是在欺骗您。我想您未必能完全体会，此刻对您说出这话，对我意味着什么；可我想，这是我欠您的。往后的岁月里您也许终会发觉，那时您会悔恨在我屋檐下度过的那些日子。刚才我说您孩子气，您得原谅我；我知道您是个妇人女子，因此希望您能体谅我。我是在一场光明正大的决斗中杀死您哥哥的。他对我的挑衅，是前人从未经受过的——」
「德鲁莱德先生，您有必要把这些都讲给我听么？」她有些不耐烦地打断了他。
「我以为您应当知道。」
「可是您另一方面也该明白，如今我已无从听我哥哥一面之词，来讲述那场口角的来龙去脉了。」

===Original===
The moment the words were out of her lips she had realised how cruelly she had spoken. He did not reply; he was too chivalrous, too gentle, to reproach her. Perhaps he understood for the first time how bitterly she had felt her brother's death, and how deeply she must be suffering, now that she knew herself to be face to face with his murderer.
She stole a quick glance at him, through her tears. She was deeply penitent for what she had said. It almost seemed to her as if a dual nature was at war within her.
The mention of her brother's name, the recollection of that awful night beside his dead body, of those four years whilst she watched her father's moribund reason slowly wandering towards the grave, seemed to rouse in her a spirit of rebellion, and of evil, which she felt was not entirely of herself.

===Chinese===
话一出口，她就意识到自己说得多么刻毒。他没有作声；他太高洁、太温厚，不肯责备她。也许他这才头一回懂得，她对哥哥之死感到多么悲切，而如今她得知自己正与凶手面对面站着，内心又该受着多深的煎熬。
她含着眼泪，飞快地偷看了他一眼。她对自己方才的话深深悔恨。她几乎觉得，仿佛有两种天性正在她心里交战。
一提起哥哥的名字，一想起那可怕的一夜——她守在他尸身旁的那一夜，想起那四年光阴——她眼睁睁望着父亲垂死的神智缓缓飘向坟墓的四年——仿佛有一种叛逆而邪恶的精灵在她心里苏醒，而她觉得，那并不全然出于她自身。

===Original===
The woods had become quite silent. It was late afternoon, and they had gradually wandered farther and farther away from pretty sylvan Suresness, towards great, anarchic, death-dealing Paris. In this part of the woods the birds had left their homes; the trees, shorn of their lower branches looked like gaunt spectres, raising melancholy heads towards the relentless, silent sky.
In the distance, from behind the barriers, a couple of miles away, the boom of a gun was heard.
"They are closing the barriers," he said quietly after a long pause. "I am glad I was fortunate enough to meet you."
"It was kind of you to seek for me," she said meekly. "I didn't mean what I said just now—"
"I pray you, say no more about it. I can so well understand. I only wish—"

===Chinese===
树林里已是一片寂静。时近傍晚，他们不知不觉越走越远，离开了风景秀美的林中苏雷讷，朝着那巨大、无法无天、杀机四伏的巴黎走去。这一带的树林里，鸟儿都已离巢他去；下部的枝条被砍净的树木，看上去活像一个个瘦削的幽灵，把忧郁的头颅伸向那冷漠无言的天空。
远处，在两英里外关卡的后面，传来一声炮响。
「他们要关闭关卡了，」沉默了好一阵之后，他平静地说。「我有幸遇上了您，心里很欢喜。」
「您来寻我，真是有心，」她柔顺地说。「我刚才的话不是出本心的——」
「求您别再提了。我完全能够体谅。我只盼——」

===Original===
"It would be best I should leave your house," she said gently; "I have so ill repaid your hospitality. Pétronelle and I can easily go back to our lodgings."
"You would break my mother's heart if you left her now," he said, almost roughly. "She has become very fond of you, and knows, just as well as I do, the dangers that would beset you outside my house. My coarse and grimy partisans," he added, with a bitter touch of sarcasm, "have that advantage, that they are loyal to me, and would not harm you while under my roof."
"But you—" she murmured.
She felt somehow that she had wounded him very deeply, and was half angry with herself for her seeming ingratitude, and yet childishly glad to have suppressed in him that attitude of mentorship, which he was beginning to assume over her.

===Chinese===
「最好我还是离开府上吧，」她温言说道，「我对您的款待报答得太恶劣了。佩特罗内勒和我很容易就能回到我们原来的住处去。」
「您若是这会儿撇下她走，会把我母亲的心都揉碎的，」他说，语气近乎粗鲁。「她非常喜欢您，而且同我一样清楚，您在我的宅子外面会遭遇什么样的危险。我那些粗野肮脏的同党，」他补了一句，带着一丝辛酸的讥讽，「倒有这么一点长处：他们对我忠心，只要您在我的屋檐底下，就不会伤害您。」
「可是您——」她喃喃道。
她不知怎地觉得自己深深地伤了他，一半为自己那副忘恩负义的样子生着气，可同时又孩子气地暗自欢喜：她总算压下了他那种训导者的态度——他正开始在她面前摆出这种态度呢。

===Original===
"You need not fear that my presence will offend you much longer, mademoiselle," he said coldly. "I can quite understand how hateful it must be to you, though I would have wished that you could believe at least in my sincerity."
"Are you going away then?"
"Not out of Paris altogether. I have accepted the post of Governor of the Conciergerie."
"Ah!—where the poor Queen—"
She checked herself suddenly. Those words would have been called treasonable to the people of France.
Instinctively and furtively, as everyone did in these days, she cast a rapid glance behind her.

===Chinese===
「您不必再怕我在眼前惹您嫌恶多久了，小姐，」他冷冷地说。「我完全明白，那在您必定多么可憎，尽管我原本还希望，您至少能相信我的诚意。」
「那么您要走了？」
「不离开巴黎，只是换个地方。我已接受了巴黎古监狱（the Conciergerie）典狱长的职务。」
「啊！——可怜的王后就在那儿——」
她猛地收住了口。这话要是让人听了，会被指为背叛法兰西人民的。
她出于本能、鬼鬼祟祟地——如今人人如此——迅速朝身后扫了一眼。

===Original===
"You need not be afraid," he said; "there is no one here but Pétronelle."
"And you."
"Oh! I echo your words. Poor Marie Antoinette!"
"You pity her?"
"How can I help it?"
"But you are of that horrible National Convention, who will try her, condemn her, execute her as they did the King."

===Chinese===
「您不必害怕，」他说，「这儿除了佩特罗内勒没有旁人。」
「还有您。」
「哦！我附和您的话。可怜的玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）！」
「您怜悯她？」
「叫我怎能不呢？」
「可您是那个可怕的国民公会（National Convention）的人呀，他们会审判她、定她的罪、处死她，就像处死国王那样。」

===Original===
"I am of the National Convention. But I will not condemn her, nor be a party to another crime. I go as Governor of the Conciergerie, to help her, if I can."
"But your popularity—your life—if you befriend her?"
"As you say, mademoiselle, my life, if I befriend her," he said simply.
She looked at him with renewed curiosity in her gaze.
How strange were men in these days! Paul Déroulède, the republican, the recognised idol of the lawless people of France, was about to risk his life for the woman he had helped to dethrone.

===Chinese===
「我是国民公会的人。但我不会定她的罪，也不肯再做一桩罪行的同谋。我去当巴黎古监狱的典狱长，是为了尽力帮助她，只要我做得到。」
「可您的声望——您的性命——您要是袒护她呢？」
「正如您所说，小姐，我的性命，倘若我袒护她的话，」他淡然说道。
她凝望着他，目光里重新生出了好奇。
如今的男人们多么奇怪啊！保罗·德鲁莱德，一个共和派，法兰西那班目无王法的人众所公认的偶像，竟要为他曾出力废黜的那个女人去冒性命的危险。

===Original===
Pity with him did not end with the rabble of Paris; it had reached Charlotte Corday, though it failed to save her, and now it extended to the poor dispossessed Queen. Somehow, in his face this time, she saw either success or death.
"When do you leave?" she asked.
"Tomorrow night."
She said nothing more. Strangely enough, a tinge of melancholy had settled over her spirits. No doubt the proximity of the town was the cause of this. She could already hear the familiar noise of muffled drums, the loud, excited shrieking of the mob, who stood round the gates of Paris, at this time of the evening, waiting to witness some important capture, perhaps that of a hated aristocrat striving to escape from the people's revenge.

===Chinese===
他的怜悯之心，并不止于巴黎的暴民；它曾感召过夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday），虽未能救她的性命，如今又施及那位可怜的被废黜的王后。不知怎的，这一次，她在他脸上看到的，不是成功，便是死亡。
「您什么时候动身？」她问。
「明天夜里。」
她再没说什么。说来奇怪，一缕淡淡的愁绪，已悄悄罩上了她的心头。无疑，这是临近城池的缘故。她已经听得见那熟悉的闷鼓声，听得见人群激动的高声嘶叫——这个时辰的傍晚，他们正围在巴黎各处城门口，等着亲眼目睹什么重大的捕获，兴许是一个遭人切齿的贵族，正拼命想逃出人民的复仇之手。

===Original===
They had reached the edge of the wood, and gradually, as she walked, the flowers she had gathered fell unheeded out of her listless hands one by one.
First the blue lupins: their bud-laden heads were heavy and they dropped to the ground, followed by the white marguerites, that lay thick behind her now on the grass like a shroud. The red poppies were the lightest, their thin gummy stalks clung to her hands longer than the rest. At last she let them fall too, singly, like great drops of blood, that glistened as her long white gown swept them aside.
Déroulède was absorbed in his thoughts, and seemed not to heed her. At the barrier, however, he roused himself and took out the passes which alone enabled Juliette and Pétronelle to reenter the town unchallenged. He himself as Citizen-Deputy could come and go as he wished.
Juliette shuddered as the great gates closed behind her with a heavy clank.

===Chinese===
他们已走到树林边缘，她一路走着，采来的花朵便从她那双无精打采的手里，一朵接一朵，不知不觉地掉落在地。
最先是蓝色的羽扇豆：满缀花蕾的头沉甸甸的，坠落在地；跟着是白色的雏菊，密密地散落在她身后的草地上，像一方殓布。红罂粟最轻，它们那细瘦而有黏汁的茎梗，在她手里恋恋攀附得最久。最后她也任凭它们落下了，一朵一朵，像大颗大颗的血滴，在她曳地的白长裙扫过时闪着微光。
德鲁莱德沉思在自己的心事里，似乎没有留意她。只是到了关卡，他才惊醒过来，掏出那些通行证——全凭它们，朱丽叶特和佩特罗内勒才能无人盘问地重新进城。他自己是公会议员（Citizen-Deputy），来去尽可随意。
大门在她身后哐当一声沉重地合上，朱丽叶特不由得打了个寒噤。

===Original===
She did not know Paris very well, and wondered where lay that gloomy Conciergerie, where a dethroned queen was living her last days, in an agonised memory of the past. But as they crossed the bridge she recognised all round her the massive towers of the great city: Notre Dame, the grateful spire of La Sainte Chapelle, the sombre outline of St. Gervais, and behind her the Louvre with its great history and irreclaimable grandeur. How small her own tragedy seemed in the midst of this great sanguinary drama, the last act of which had not yet even begun. Her own revenge, her oath, her tribulations, what were they in comparison with that great flaming Nemesis which had swept away a throne, that vow of retaliation carried out by thousands against other thousands, that long story of degradation, of regicide, of fratricide, the awesome chapters of which were still being unfolded one by one?
She felt small and petty: ashamed of the pleasure she had felt in the woods, ashamed of her high spirits and lightheartedness, ashamed of that feeling of sudden pity and admiration for the man who had done her and her family so deep an injury, which she was too feeble, too vacillating to avenge.
The majestic outline of the Louvre seemed to frown sarcastically on her weakness, the silent river to mock her and her wavering purpose. The man beside her had wronged her and hers far more deeply than the Bourbons had wronged their people. The people of France were taking their revenge, and God had at the close of this last happy day of her life pointed once more to the means for her great end.

===Chinese===
她对巴黎并不很熟，心里正纳闷那座阴森的巴黎古监狱究竟坐落何处——一位被废黜的王后，正在那里靠对往事的惨痛回忆，打发她最后的日子。可是当他们过桥的时候，她认出了四周这座伟大城池的巍巍塔楼：巴黎圣母院（Notre Dame），圣礼拜堂（La Sainte Chapelle）那令人慰藉的尖顶，圣热尔韦教堂（St. Gervais）黝黑的轮廓，还有身后那座卢浮宫（the Louvre），带着它的煌煌历史和一去不返的威仪。在这场血雨腥风的大戏中间——它连最后一幕都还没有开场——她自己的那出悲剧显得多么渺小。她自己的复仇、她的誓言、她的磨难，同那场席卷了王座的烈焰般的天罚女神（Nemesis）之怒相比，同那万万人向万万人的报复誓言相比，同那部漫长的堕落、弑君、骨肉相残的史话相比，又算得了什么呢？——而那部史话惊心动魄的篇章，此刻还正在一页一页地展开。
她觉得自己渺小而卑微：羞愧于她在树林里感到的快乐，羞愧于自己的兴高采烈、无忧无虑，羞愧于心里对那个男人突然涌起的怜悯与敬慕——他对她和她的全家伤害如此之深，而她却太软弱、太摇摆，无力去复仇。
卢浮宫雄伟的轮廓，仿佛正讥诮地对着她的软弱蹙眉；沉默的河水，仿佛在嘲弄她和她的摇摆不定。她身边这个男人加于她和她的亲人的冤仇，远比波旁王室加于他们人民的更深。法兰西的人民正在讨还他们的血债；而上帝，在她这一生最后一个快乐的日子行将结束的时刻，再一次向她指明了达成她宏愿的手段。

## VI The Scarlet Pimpernel / VI 红花侠

===Original===
It was some few hours later. The ladies sat in the drawing-room, silent and anxious.

Soon after supper a visitor had called, and had been closeted with Paul Déroulède in the latter’s study for the past two hours.

A tall, somewhat lazy-looking figure, he was sitting at a table face to face with the Citizen-Deputy. On a chair beside him lay a heavy caped coat, covered with the dust and the splashings of a long journey, but he himself was attired in clothes that suggested the most fastidious taste, and the most perfect of tailors; he wore with apparent ease the eccentric fashion of the time, the short-waisted coat of many lapels, the double waistcoat and billows of delicate lace. Unlike Déroulède he was of great height, with fair hair and a somewhat lazy expression in his good-natured blue eyes, and as he spoke, there was just a soupçon of foreign accent in the pronunciation of the French vowels, a certain drawl of *o*’s and *a*’s, that would have betrayed the Britisher to an observant ear.

The two men had been talking earnestly for some time, the tall Englishman was watching his friend keenly, whilst an amused, pleasant smile lingered round the corners of his firm mouth and jaw. Déroulède, restless and enthusiastic, was pacing to and fro.

===Chinese===
那是几个小时之后的事了。女眷们坐在客厅里，默不作声，满心焦灼。

晚饭刚过，来了一位客人，至今已同保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）在书房里密谈了两个钟头。

那是个高挑个儿、看上去有几分懒散的身影，正坐在桌前，与这位公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）对面而坐。他身旁的椅子上搭着一件沉重的带披肩的大衣，沾满长途跋涉的尘土和泥浆；可他本人身上的衣着，却显出最考究的趣味和最高明的裁缝手艺。他以驾轻就熟的姿态，穿着当时那种乖张的时髦装束：多翻领的短腰身外套、双层马甲，还有层层叠叠的精致花边。他与德鲁莱德不同，身材十分高大，一头金发，和善的蓝眼睛里带着几分慵懒的神色；说话的时候，他念法语元音的口音里只带着一丝（soupçon）外国腔——把 o 音和 a 音稍稍拖长，敏锐的耳朵一听，便能听出这是个英国人。

两个人已经恳切地谈了一阵子，高个子的英国人仔细端详着他的朋友，一张坚毅的嘴和下颌的棱角边，始终挂着一抹含着笑意的、愉快的微笑。德鲁莱德呢，焦躁而热切，正踱来踱去。

===Original===
“But I don’t understand now, how you managed to reach Paris, my dear Blakeney!” said Déroulède at last, placing an anxious hand on his friend’s shoulder. “The government has not forgotten The Scarlet Pimpernel.”

“La! I took care of that!” responded Blakeney with his short, pleasant laugh. “I sent Tinville my autograph this morning.”

“You are mad, Blakeney!”

“Not altogether, my friend. My faith! ’twas not only foolhardiness caused me to grant that devilish prosecutor another sight of my scarlet device. I knew what you maniacs would be after, so I came across in the *Daydream*, just to see if I couldn’t get my share of the fun.”

“Fun, you call it?” queried the other bitterly.

“Nay! what would you have me call it? A mad, insane, senseless tragedy, with but one issue?—the guillotine for you all.”

===Chinese===
「可我到现在也不明白，你是怎么到得了巴黎的，我亲爱的布莱克尼（Blakeney）！」德鲁莱德终于开了口，一只焦灼的手搭在朋友的肩上。「政府可没有忘记红花侠。」

「嗬！这事我早安排妥了！」布莱克尼答道，发出他那短促而愉快的笑声。「今天早上，我给坦维尔（Tinville）送去了一份我的亲笔签名。」

「布莱克尼，你疯啦！」

「不全是，我的朋友。说真的！我让那个魔鬼公诉人再看一眼我的猩红记号，可不光是逞一时之勇。我早知道你们这班疯子要干出什么事来，所以就乘『白日梦号』（Daydream）渡海过来了，只想瞧瞧我能不能也分得一份乐子。」

「乐子？你管这个叫乐子？」对方苦涩地问道。

「不！那你要我管它叫什么？一场疯狂的、丧失理智的、毫无意义的悲剧，结局只有一个？——你们统统上断头台。」

===Original===
“Then why did you come?”

“To—What shall I say, my friend?” rejoined Sir Percy Blakeney, with that inimitable drawl of his. “To give your demmed government something else to think about, whilst you are all busy running your heads into a noose.”

“What makes you think we are doing that?”

“Three things, my friend—may I offer you a pinch of snuff—No?—Ah well!—” And with the graceful gesture of an accomplished dandy, Sir Percy flicked off a grain of dust from his immaculate Mechlin ruffles.

“Three things,” he continued quietly; “an imprisoned Queen, about to be tried for her life, the temperament of a Frenchman—some of them—and the idiocy of mankind generally. These three things make me think that a certain section of hotheaded Republicans with yourself, my dear Déroulède, *en tête*, are about to attempt the most stupid, senseless, purposeless thing that was ever concocted by the excitable brain of a demmed Frenchman.”

===Chinese===
「那你干吗还要来？」

「来——叫我怎么说呢，我的朋友？」珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）答道，还是他那副无可模仿的拖长腔调。「来给你们那该死的政府找点别的事琢磨琢磨，好趁你们一个个忙着把脑袋往绞索里套的时候，替你们分分心。」

「你怎么知道我们在干那种事？」

「三桩事，我的朋友——要不要来一撮鼻烟？——不要？——那好吧！——」珀西爵士摆出行家级花花公子的优雅姿态，从一尘不染的梅赫伦（Mechlin）蕾丝皱边上弹去一粒微尘。

「三桩事，」他平静地继续说道，「一位身陷囹圄、即将受审、性命难保的王后；法兰西人的脾性——其中某些人的脾性；再加上人类普遍的愚蠢。这三桩事叫我明白：有一帮头脑发热的共和派，由你自己打头阵（en tête），我亲爱的德鲁莱德，正打算去干一桩最愚蠢、最无谓、最没有名堂的事——那玩意儿，全凭一个该死的法兰西人那容易冲动的脑筋，才编排得出来。」

===Original===
Déroulède smiled.

“Does it not seem amusing to you, Blakeney, that you should sit there and condemn anyone for planning mad, insane, senseless things.”

“La! I’ll not sit, I’ll stand!” rejoined Blakeney with a laugh, as he drew himself up to his full height, and stretched his long, lazy limbs. “And now let me tell you, friend, that my League of The Scarlet Pimpernel never attempted the impossible, and to try and drag the Queen out of the clutches of these murderous rascals now, is attempting the unattainable.”

“And yet we mean to try.”

“I know it. I guessed it, that is why I came: that is also why I sent a pleasant little note to the Committee of Public Safety, signed with the device they know so well: The Scarlet Pimpernel.”

“Well?”

“Well! the result is obvious. Robespierre, Danton, Tinville, Merlin, and the whole of the demmed murderous crowd, will be busy looking after me—a needle in a haystack. They’ll put the abortive attempt down to me, and you may—ma foi! I only suggest that you *may* escape safely out of France—in the *Daydream*, and with the help of your humble servant.”

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德微微一笑。

「布莱克尼，你坐在那里，指责别人尽策划些疯狂、丧失理智、毫无意义的事情，你自己不觉得可笑么？」

「嗬！那我就不坐了，我站着！」布莱克尼笑着回敬，一边直起身子，挺到自己的全高，舒展开他那修长而懒散的四肢。「那么现在就让我告诉你，朋友：我的红花侠同盟（League of The Scarlet Pimpernel）从来不去尝试办不到的事；而如今想要把王后从这帮杀人不眨眼的恶棍的魔爪里拖出来，却是尝试办不到的事。」

「可我们还是打算试一试。」

「我知道。我猜到了，所以我才来：也正因如此，我才给救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）送去了一张令人愉快的小字条，上面签着他们再熟悉不过的记号：红花侠。」

「结果呢？」

「结果嘛！明摆着的。罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）、丹东（Danton）、坦维尔、梅尔兰（Merlin），还有那帮该死的杀人狂，全体人马，都会忙着来找我——我不过是大海捞针里的一根针。他们会把这次未遂的营救记到我的账上，而你们呢——ma foi（真的）！我只是说，你们*或许*能平安逃出法兰西——乘『白日梦号』，再加上鄙人的一点小小的帮助。」

===Original===
“But in the meanwhile they’ll discover you, and they’ll not let you escape a second time.”

“My friend! if a terrier were to lose his temper, he never would run a rat to earth. Now your Revolutionary Government has lost its temper with me, ever since I slipped through Chauvelin’s fingers; they are blind with their own fury, whilst I am perfectly happy and cool as a cucumber. My life has become valuable to me, my friend. There is someone over the water now who weeps when I don’t return—No! no! never fear—they’ll not get The Scarlet Pimpernel this journey—”

He laughed, a gay, pleasant laugh, and his strong, firm face seemed to soften at thought of the beautiful wife, over in England, who was waiting anxiously for his safe return.

“And yet you’ll not help us to rescue the Queen?” rejoined Déroulède, with some bitterness.

“By every means in my power,” replied Blakeney, “save the insane. But I will help to get you all out of the demmed hole, when you have failed.”

“We’ll not fail,” asserted the other hotly.

Sir Percy Blakeney went close up to his friend and placed his long, slender hand, with a touch of almost womanly tenderness upon the latter’s shoulder.

“Will you tell me your plans?”

===Chinese===
「可与此同时，他们会查出你的行踪，绝不会放你第二次逃脱。」

「我的朋友！猎㹟犬要是也发起脾气来，那就永远别想把老鼠逼出洞来。你的革命政府打从我打肖夫兰（Chauvelin）的指缝里溜走那一刻起，就对我恼羞成怒；他们被自己的怒火熏瞎了眼，而我呢，却快活自在，冷静得像根黄瓜。我的这条性命，如今在我自己眼里可金贵着呢，我的朋友。海峡那边，如今有人正掉着眼泪呢，只要我不回去——不！不！千万别担心——这一趟，他们抓不到红花侠的——」

他放声笑了，笑声欢快悦耳；一想到在英格兰那边、正焦灼盼着他平安归来的美丽妻子，他那张坚毅刚强的脸，仿佛也变得柔和了。

「可你还是不肯帮我们营救王后？」德鲁莱德回敬道，语气里带着几分苦涩。

「凡我力所能及的，无不应命，」布莱克尼答道，「唯独疯狂的事不干。可等你们失败了，我会帮你们全体逃出这个该死的火坑。」

「我们不会失败，」对方激烈地断言。

珀西·布莱克尼爵士走到朋友跟前，把自己修长纤细的手搭在对方的肩上，那抚触带着近乎女性般的温柔。

「愿意把你们的计划讲给我听么？」

===Original===
In a moment Déroulède was all fire and enthusiasm.

“There are not many of us in it,” he began, “although half France will be in sympathy with us. We have plenty of money, of course, and also the necessary disguise for the royal lady.”

“Yes?”

“I, in the meanwhile, have asked for and obtained the post of Governor of the Conciergerie; I go into my new quarters tomorrow. In the meanwhile, I am making arrangements for my mother and—and those dependent upon me to quit France immediately.”

Blakeney had perceived the slight hesitation when Déroulède mentioned those dependent upon him. He looked scrutinisingly at his friend, who continued quickly:

“I am still very popular among the people. My family can go about unmolested. I must get them out of France, however, in case—in case—”

“Of course,” rejoined the other simply.

“As soon as I am assured that they are safe, my friends and I can prosecute our plans. You see the trial of the Queen has not yet been decided on, but I know that it is in the air. We hope to get her away, disguised in one of the uniforms of the National Guard. As you know, it will be my duty to make the final round every evening in the prison, and to see that everything is safe for the night. Two fellows watch all night, in the room next to that occupied by the Queen. Usually they drink and play cards all night long. I want an opportunity to drug their brandy, and thus to render them more loutish and idiotic than usual; then for a blow on the head that will make them senseless. It should be easy, for I have a strong fist, and after that—”

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德顿时满腔热火、精神抖擞。

「参与其事的人不多，」他开始说道，「虽说半个法兰西都会同情我们。当然，我们有的是钱，也有为王后那位贵妇备下的必要的化装用品。」

「嗯？」

「与此同时，我已经讨来并领到了巴黎古监狱（Conciergerie）典狱长的职务；明天我就搬进新的住处。另外，我正在安排我母亲——还有那些依靠我生活的人——立即离开法兰西。」

德鲁莱德提到那些依靠他生活的人时，那一瞬的迟疑，没有逃过布莱克尼的眼睛。他细细端详着朋友，朋友却急忙往下说：

「我在民众中间仍旧很得人心。我家里人外出不会有人滋扰。可是我必须把他们送出法兰西，以防——以防——」

「那是自然，」对方淡淡地应道。

「一旦我确信他们平安无事，我和我的朋友们就可以推行我们的计划了。你知道，对王后的审判还没有定下来，可我知道这事已在酝酿之中。我们指望让她换上国民自卫军（National Guard）的一套军装，化装脱身。你知道，每晚在监狱里作最后一次巡查、确保夜间一切无虞，是我的职责。王后隔壁的房间里，有两个家伙彻夜看守。通常他们整夜喝酒打牌。我只要找个机会，在他们的白兰地里下药，让他们变得比平时更蠢笨痴愣；然后照他们脑袋上来一拳，叫他们不省人事。这应当不难，我的拳头有的是力气，然后呢——」

===Original===
“Well? After that, friend?” rejoined Sir Percy earnestly, “after that? Shall I fill in the details of the picture?—the guard twenty-five strong outside the Conciergerie, how will you pass them?”

“I as the Governor, followed by one of my guards—”

“To go whither?”

“I have the right to come and go as I please.”

“I’ faith! so you have, but ‘one of your guards’—eh? Wrapped to the eyes in a long mantle to hide the female figure beneath. I have been in Paris but a few hours, and yet already I have realised that there is not one demmed citizen within its walls, who does not at this moment suspect some other demmed citizen of conniving at the Queen’s escape. Even the sparrows on the housetops are objects of suspicion. No figure wrapped in a mantle will from this day forth leave Paris unchallenged.”

“But you yourself, friend?” suggested Déroulède. “You think you can quit Paris unrecognised—then why not the Queen?”

“Because she is a woman, and has been a queen. She has nerves, poor soul, and weaknesses of body and of mind now. Alas for her! Alas for France! who wreaks such idle vengeance on so poor an enemy? Can you take hold of Marie Antoinette by the shoulders, shove her into the bottom of a cart and pile sacks of potatoes on the top of her? I did that to the Comtesse de Tournai and her daughter, as stiff-necked a pair of French aristocrats as ever deserved the guillotine for their insane prejudices. But can you do it to Marie Antoinette? She’d rebuke you publicly, and betray herself and you in a flash, sooner than submit to a loss of dignity.”

===Chinese===
「然后？然后又怎样，朋友？」珀西爵士恳切地追问道，「然后呢？要不要我来替这幅图画补上细节？——巴黎古监狱外驻着整整二十五名卫兵，你怎么过得去？」

「我以典狱长的身份，后面跟着我的一个卫兵——」

「去哪儿？」

「我有随自己的意思进出的权利。」

「真的！这权利你倒是有的；可是『你的一个卫兵』——嗯？一件长斗篷裹到眼睛底下，好遮住底下那副女人的身段。我到巴黎不过几个钟头，可已经明白了：这城墙里面，没有一个该死的公民，此刻不在疑心另一个该死的公民暗中纵容王后逃跑。连屋顶上的麻雀都是嫌疑对象。从今往后，凡是裹着斗篷的身影，休想不受到盘查就离开巴黎。」

「可你自己呢，朋友？」德鲁莱德提醒道。「你以为自己能不被认出来就离开巴黎——那王后为什么就不能？」

「因为她是个女人，又当过王后。她的神经经不起事，可怜的人儿，如今身子骨和精神都衰弱了。她的命何其苦！法兰西又何其可悲！竟拿这样无聊的复仇，来发泄在这样一个可怜巴巴的敌人身上！你能抓住玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）的肩膀，把她塞到大车底板上，再往她身上堆一袋袋土豆么？图尔奈伯爵夫人（Comtesse de Tournai）和她女儿，我就这么干过——那是一对脖颈僵得再硬不过的法国老贵族，凭她们那疯狂至极的偏见，早就该上断头台了。可你能对玛丽·安托瓦内特来这一手么？她宁可当众申斥你，一眨眼的工夫就暴露她自己、也连累你，也决不肯忍气吞声，受这份有失尊严的委屈。」

===Original===
“But would you leave her to her fate?”

“Ah! there’s the trouble, friend. Do you think you need appeal to the sense of chivalry of my league? We are still twenty strong, and heart and soul in sympathy with your mad schemes. The poor, poor Queen! But you are bound to fail, and then who will help you all, if we too are put out of the way?”

“We should succeed if you helped us. At one time you used proudly to say: ‘The League of The Scarlet Pimpernel has never failed.’ ”

“Because it attempted nothing which it could not accomplish. But, la! since you put me on my mettle—Demm it all! I’ll have to think about it!”

And he laughed that funny, somewhat inane laugh of his, which had deceived the clever men of two countries as to his real personality.

===Chinese===
「可你难道忍心听凭她听天由命？」

「唉！难就难在这里，朋友。你以为还用得着去求我同盟的侠义心肠么？我们的人手还有二十个，对你的疯狂计划，我们是全心全意地同情。可怜、可怜的王后啊！可你们注定要失败，而到那时候，倘若我们也遭了毒手，还有谁来帮你们呢？」

「只要你肯帮忙，我们就能成功。你从前总还自豪地说过：『红花侠同盟从来不曾失败。』」

「那是因为它从来不去尝试办不到的事。不过，嗬！既然你拿话来激我——真该死！我倒非得琢磨琢磨不可了！」

说着，他放声笑了，还是他那种滑稽的、有点傻里傻气的笑——正是凭了这笑，他把两国的聪明人全都蒙在鼓里，谁也摸不透他的真面目。

===Original===
Déroulède went up to the heavy oak desk which occupied a conspicuous place in the centre of one of the walls. He unlocked it and drew forth a bundle of papers.

“Will you look through these?” he asked, handing them to Sir Percy Blakeney.

“What are they?”

“Different schemes I have drawn up, in case my original plan should not succeed.”

“Burn them, my friend,” said Blakeney laconically. “Have you not yet learned the lesson of never putting your hand to paper?”

“I can’t burn these. You see, I shall not be able to have long conversations with Marie Antoinette. I must give her my suggestions in writing, that she may study them and not fail me, through lack of knowledge of her part.”

“Better that than papers in these times, my friend: these papers, if found, would send you, untried, to the guillotine.”

“I am careful, and, at present, quite beyond suspicion. Moreover, among the papers is a complete collection of passports, suitable for any character the Queen and her attendant may be forced to assume. It has taken me some months to collect them, so as not to arouse suspicion; I gradually got them together, on one pretence or another: now I am ready for any eventuality—”

He suddenly paused. A look in his friend’s face had given him a swift warning.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德走到那张厚重的橡木写字台前——它在一面墙的正中，位置十分显眼。他开了锁，取出一束文件。

「愿意把这几份过一过目么？」他说着，把它们递给珀西·布莱克尼爵士。

「这是什么？」

「是我拟定的几种方案，以防我原先的计划不能成功。」

「烧掉它们，我的朋友，」布莱克尼言简意赅地说。「难道你还没学乖：永远不要落笔成文？」

「这几份我不能烧。你瞧，我总不能同玛丽·安托瓦内特长谈。我必须把我的建议写成文字交给她，好叫她细细揣摩，不至于因为不熟悉自己的角色而误了事。」

「我的朋友，在这年月，宁可误事，也不能留字纸：这些文件一旦被搜出来，不经审判，就能把你直接送上断头台。」

「我做事谨慎，而且眼下根本不在任何人的嫌疑之内。再说，这些文件里头，还有一整套护照，无论王后和她的随从被迫扮成什么角色，都用得上。为凑齐它们，我花了好几个月，为的是不惹人起疑；我借口这借口那，一点一点地把它们收拢来：如今无论出了什么变故，我都有备无患——」

他忽然顿住了。朋友脸上的一丝神色，给了他一个迅疾的警告。

===Original===
He turned, and there in the doorway, holding back the heavy portière, stood Juliette, graceful, smiling, a little pale, this no doubt owing to the flickering light of the unsnuffed candles.

So young and girlish did she look in her soft, white muslin frock that at sight of her the tension in Déroulède’s face seemed to relax. Instinctively he had thrown the papers back into the desk, but his look had softened, from the fire of obstinate energy to that of inexpressible tenderness.

Blakeney was quietly watching the young girl as she stood in the doorway, a little bashful and undecided.

“Madame Déroulède sent me,” she said hesitatingly, “she says the hour is getting late and she is very anxious. M. Déroulède, would you come and reassure her?”

“In a moment, mademoiselle,” he replied lightly, “my friend and I have just finished our talk. May I have the honour to present him?—Sir Percy Blakeney, a traveller from England. Blakeney, this is Mademoiselle Juliette de Marny, my mother’s guest.”

===Chinese===
他转过身去，只见门口，朱丽叶特（Juliette）一手撩开沉重的门帘，站在那里，仪态优雅，面带微笑，脸色微微发白——这多半是那几支没剪烛芯的蜡烛光影摇曳的缘故。

她穿着柔软的白色细布连衣裙，看上去那样年轻、那样一派女儿家情态，德鲁莱德一见到她，脸上的紧张仿佛便松弛了下来。他已出于本能把文件扔回写字台里，而他的目光也已变得柔和，从那团执拗炽烈的火焰，化作一种难以言喻的温柔。

布莱克尼静静地端详着这位站在门口的少女，她有几分羞怯，有几分踌躇。

「是德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）叫我来的，」她迟疑地说，「她说时候不早了，她心里很不安。德鲁莱德先生，您可愿意去宽宽她的心？」

「就来，小姐，」他轻快地答道，「我和我的朋友刚刚谈完。容我荣幸地向您介绍？——这位是珀西·布莱克尼爵士，从英格兰来的旅行家。布莱克尼，这位是朱丽叶特·德·马尔尼（Juliette de Marny）小姐，我母亲的客人。」

## VII A Warning / VII 警告

===Original===
Sir Percy bowed very low, with all the graceful flourish and elaborate gesture the eccentric customs of the time demanded.

He had not said a word, since the first exclamation of warning, with which he had drawn his friend’s attention to the young girl in the doorway.

Noiselessly, as she had come, Juliette glided out of the room again, leaving behind her an atmosphere of wild flowers, of the bouquet she had gathered, then scattered in the woods.

There was silence in the room for awhile. Déroulède was locking up his desk and slipping the keys into his pocket.

===Chinese===
珀西爵士深深鞠了一躬，把那个时代乖张礼数所要求的一招一式、繁文缛节，全都做到了十足。

自从那一声示警的惊呼——正是它让他的朋友注意到门口那位少女——之后，他一直没再说一个字。

朱丽叶特（Juliette）来时悄无声息，去时也悄然无声，又滑步出了房间，只留下一屋子野花的气息——那是她在林子里采来、又随手撒去的一束花。

屋里静了片刻。德鲁莱德（Déroulède）锁上写字台，把钥匙揣进了衣袋。

===Original===
“Shall we join my mother for a moment, Blakeney?” he said, moving towards the door.

“I shall be proud to pay my respects,” replied Sir Percy; “but before we close the subject, I think I’ll change my mind about those papers. If I am to be of service to you I think I had best look through them, and give you my opinion of your schemes.”

Déroulède looked at him keenly for a moment.

“Certainly,” he said at last, going up to his desk. “I’ll stay with you whilst you read them through.”

===Chinese===
「咱们去陪陪我妈吧，布莱克尼（Blakeney）？」他说着，朝门口走去。

「能去请安，我深感荣幸，」珀西爵士答道；「不过趁这个话头还没打住，我想我对那几份文件要改主意了。既然要我帮你的忙，依我看，最好还是让我把它们过一遍，再对你的种种计划说说我的看法。」

德鲁莱德目光锐利地打量了他片刻。

「当然可以，」他终于说道，一边走向写字台。「你读的时候，我陪着你。」

===Original===
“La! not tonight, my friend,” said Sir Percy lightly; “the hour is late, and madame is waiting for us. They’ll be quite safe with me, and you’ll entrust them to my care.”

Déroulède seemed to hesitate. Blakeney had spoken in his usual airy manner, and was even now busy readjusting the set of his perfectly-tailored coat.

“Perhaps you cannot quite trust me?” laughed Sir Percy gaily. “I seemed too lukewarm just now.”

“No; it’s not that, Blakeney!” said Déroulède quietly at last. “There is no mistrust in me, all the mistrust is on your side.”

===Chinese===
「嗬！今晚就不必了，我的朋友，」珀西爵士轻描淡写地说；「时候不早了，夫人正等着咱们呢。文件放在我这儿万无一失，你就托付给我吧。」

德鲁莱德似乎有些踌躇。布莱克尼说话时是一贯的潇洒腔调，此刻还在忙着理他那件做工无懈可击的上衣。

「莫非你不大信得过我？」珀西爵士欢快地笑道。「方才我显得太冷淡了些。」

「不，不是那个缘故，布莱克尼！」德鲁莱德终于平静地说。「我心里毫无猜疑，猜疑的全在你那一边。」

===Original===
“Faith!—” began Sir Percy.

“Nay! do not explain. I understand and appreciate your friendship, but I should like to convince you how unjust is your mistrust of one of God’s purest angels, that ever walked the earth.”

“Oho! that’s it, is it, friend Déroulède? Methought you had foresworn the sex altogether, and now you are in love.”

“Madly, blindly, stupidly in love, my friend,” said Déroulède with a sigh. “Hopelessly, I fear me!”

===Chinese===
「说真的！——」珀西爵士刚开了口。

「不！不必解释。你的情谊我明白，也领情；可我想让你相信，你这份猜疑是多么不公——你所疑心的，是上帝派到人间来的最纯洁的天使之一。」

「哦嗬！原来如此，是么，德鲁莱德朋友？我还当你已经对女人这一性别发过誓永不沾边，如今却坠入了情网。」

「疯狂地、盲目地、愚蠢地爱着，我的朋友，」德鲁莱德叹了口气说。「只怕还是无可指望地爱着！」

===Original===
“Why hopelessly?”

“She is the daughter of the late Duc de Marny, one of the oldest names in France; a Royalist to the backbone—”

“Hence your overwhelming sympathy for the Queen!”

“Nay! you wrong me there, friend. I’d have tried to save the Queen, even if I had never learned to love Juliette. But you see now how unjust were your suspicions.”

===Chinese===
「为什么无可指望？」

「她是已故马尔尼公爵（Duc de Marny）的女儿——法兰西最古老的门第之一；她自己是个地地道道的保王党——」

「所以你才对王后怀着那么一股不可遏制的同情！」

「不！朋友，这你可冤枉我了。就算我从来不曾爱上朱丽叶特，我也会去设法营救王后。可你现在该明白了，你那些疑心是多么不公。」

===Original===
“Had I any?”

“Don’t deny it. You were loud in urging me to burn those papers a moment ago. You called them useless and dangerous and now—”

“I still think them useless and dangerous, and by reading them would wish to confirm my opinion and give weight to my arguments.”

“If I were to part from them now I would seem to be mistrusting her.”

===Chinese===
「我有过疑心么？」

「别不认账。方才你一个劲儿地大声催我把那几份文件烧掉。你说它们没用又危险，可现在——」

「我依旧认为它们没用又危险，正因为如此，我才想读一读，好证实我的看法，给我的论据添些分量。」

「倘若我这会儿把它们撒手交出去，倒显得我在疑心她了。」

===Original===
“You are a mad idealist, my dear Déroulède!”

“How can I help it? I have lived under the same roof with her for three weeks now. I have begun to understand what a saint is like.”

“And ’twill be when you understand that your idol has feet of clay that you’ll learn the real lesson of love,” said Blakeney earnestly. “Is it love to worship a saint in heaven, whom you dare not touch, who hovers above you like a cloud, which floats away from you even as you gaze? To love is to feel one being in the world at one with us, our equal in sin as well as in virtue. To love, for us men, is to clasp one woman with our arms, feeling that she lives and breathes just as we do, suffers as we do, thinks with us, loves with us, and, above all, sins with us. Your mock saint who stands in a niche is not a woman if she have not suffered, still less a woman if she have not sinned. Fall at the feet of your idol an you wish, but drag her down to your level after that—the only level she should ever reach, that of your heart.”

===Chinese===
「你真是个疯狂的理想主义者，我亲爱的德鲁莱德！」

「叫我有什么法子？到如今我已经同她在同一屋檐下住了三个礼拜。我开始懂得圣人是什么模样了。」

「而等你明白了你那偶像是泥足的，那时你才学得到爱情的真谛，」布莱克尼恳切地说。「去膜拜一位天上的圣徒——你不敢触碰，只像一朵云在你头顶盘旋，你正凝望着，它却从你眼前飘然而去——这算得了爱么？爱，是感到这世上有一个人与我们心心相印，在罪孽里与在美德里同样是我们的同类。爱，对于我们男人来说，是用双臂搂住一个女人，感到她同我们一样地活着、呼吸着，同我们一样地受苦，同我们一同思想，同我们相爱，而最要紧的，是同我们一同犯罪。你那供在壁龛里的假圣徒，若不曾受过苦，就算不得女人；若不曾犯过罪，就更算不得女人。你若愿意，尽管拜倒在你偶像的脚下；可拜过之后，要把她拽下来，拽到与你同等的高度——那是她唯一应当企及的高度，也就是你的心的高度。」

===Original===
Who shall render faithfully a true account of the magnetism which poured forth from this remarkable man as he spoke: this well-dressed, foppish apostle of the greatest love that man has ever known. And as he spoke the whole story of his own great, true love for the woman who once had so deeply wronged him seemed to stand clearly written in the strong, lazy, good-humoured, kindly face glowing with tenderness for her.

Déroulède felt this magnetism, and therefore did not resent the implied suggestion, anent the saint whom he was still content to worship.

A dreamer and an idealist, his mind held spellbound by the great social problems which were causing the upheaval of a whole country, he had not yet had the time to learn the sweet lesson which Nature teaches to her elect—the lesson of a great, a true, human and passionate love. To him, at present, Juliette represented the perfect embodiment of his most idealistic dreams. She stood in his mind so far above him that if she proved unattainable, he would scarce have suffered. It was such a foregone conclusion.

Blakeney’s words were the first to stir in his heart a desire for something beyond that quasi-medieval worship, something weaker and yet infinitely stronger, something more earthy and yet almost divine.

===Chinese===
这位不同寻常的人物侃侃而谈时，从他身上倾泻而出的那种感召力，谁能忠实如实地描摹呢：这位衣着考究、一副纨绔气派的使徒，传布的正是世人所知的最伟大的爱。而他说着说着，他自己那段真挚而伟大的爱情——他爱着那个曾深深负过他的女人——的整个故事，仿佛清清楚楚地写在他那张坚毅、慵懒、和善而温厚的脸上，那张脸正因为对她的柔情而熠熠生辉。

德鲁莱德感受到了这种感召力，因此对于那层言外之意——关于他仍心甘情愿膜拜的那位圣徒的言外之意——并不觉得受了冒犯。

他是个梦幻者，又是理想主义者，整个心思都叫那些正在撼动整个国家的社会大问题给摄住了，还没有来得及去学大自然传授给她选中的儿女的那门甜蜜的功课——一门关于伟大的、真挚的、属人的、炽烈的爱恋的功课。在他眼里，眼下的朱丽叶特正是他最理想化的梦想的完满化身。她在他心目中高高凌驾于他之上，以致纵然证明她高不可攀，他也几乎不会痛苦。这原是早已注定的结论。

布莱克尼这番话，头一回在他心里撩起了一种渴望，渴望某种超出那种准中世纪式膜拜的东西：某种更柔弱、却又无限更强的东西，某种更接地气、却又近乎神圣的东西。

===Original===
“And now, shall we join the ladies?” said Blakeney after a long pause, during which the mental workings of his alert brain were almost visible, in the earnest look which he cast at his friend. “You shall keep the papers in your desk, give them into the keeping of your saint, trust her all in all rather than not at all, and if the time should come that your heaven-enthroned ideal fall somewhat heavily to earth, then give me the privilege of being a witness to your happiness.”

“You are still mistrustful, Blakeney,” said Déroulède lightly. “If you say much more I’ll give these papers into Mademoiselle Marny’s keeping until tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「可到了这一步，咱们去陪女眷们吧？」布莱克尼说，在长长的一阵沉默之后。这阵沉默里，他那机敏的头脑中的种种盘算，几乎透过他投向朋友的那道恳切的目光，一览无余。「文件呢，你就留在你的写字台里，交给你那位圣徒保管，要信就全信她，总好比一点儿也不信；倘若有朝一日，你那供在天上的理想重重地跌落尘寰，那时就请赏我一个体面，让我做你幸福的见证人。」

「你还是疑心重重，布莱克尼，」德鲁莱德轻快地说。「你再多说一句，我就把这几份文件交给马尔尼（Marny）小姐保管，直到明天。」

## VIII Anne Mie / VIII 安娜·米

===Original===
That night, when Blakeney, wrapped in his cloak, was walking down the Rue Ecole de Médecine towards his own lodgings, he suddenly felt a timid hand upon his sleeve.

Anne Mie stood beside him, her pale, melancholy face peeping up at the tall Englishman, through the folds of a dark hood closely tied under her chin.

"Monsieur," she said timidly, "do not think me very presumptuous. I—I would wish to have five minutes' talk with you—may I?"

===Chinese===
那天夜里，布莱克尼裹着斗篷，正沿医学院街朝自己的寓所走去，忽然觉出袖口上搭着一只怯生生的小手。

安娜·米就站在他身旁。暗色的风帽在颏下系得紧紧的，她那张苍白而忧郁的脸，便从风帽的褶层里仰望着这个高个子的英国人。

「先生，」她怯怯地说，「请别怪我冒昧。我——我想同您谈五分钟的话，可以吗？」

===Original===
He looked down with great kindness at the quaint, wizened little figure, and the strong face softened at the sight of the poor, deformed shoulder, the hard, pinched look of the young mouth, the general look of pathetic helplessness which appeals so strongly to the chivalrous.

"Indeed, mademoiselle," he said gently, "you make me very proud; and I can serve you in any way, I pray you command me. But," he added, seeing Anne Mie's somewhat scared look, "this street is scarce fit for private conversation. Shall we try and find a better spot?"

===Chinese===
他低下头，满怀和善地端详着这个古怪干瘪的小小身影。望着那可怜的畸残的肩膀，那张年轻却紧绷局促的嘴，还有那副楚楚可怜、孤立无援的神态——最能打动侠义心肠的，恰是这种神态——他那张刚毅的面孔变得柔和了。

「说真的，小姐，」他温和地说，「您真让我觉得荣幸；只要能为您效力，任凭差遣。不过，」他见安娜·米面有惧色，又补了一句，「这条街可不大适合私下谈话。我们找个稳妥些的地方，好不好？」

===Original===
Paris had not yet gone to bed. In these times it was really safest to be out in the open streets. There, everybody was more busy, more on the move, on the lookout for suspected houses, leaving the wanderer alone.

Blakeney led Anne Mie towards the Luxembourg Gardens, the great devastated pleasure-ground of the ci-devant tyrants of the people. The beautiful Anne of Austria, and the Medici before her, Louis XIII, and his gallant musketeers—all have given place to the great cannon-forging industry of this besieged Republic. France, attacked on every side, is forcing her sons to defend her: persecuted, martyrised, done to death by her, she is still their Mother: La Patrie, who needs their arms against the foreign foe. England is threatening the north, Prussia and Austria the east. Admiral Hood's flag is flying on Toulon Arsenal.

===Chinese===
巴黎还没有安歇。在眼下这样的年月，置身于光天化日的街市，反而真是最保险的。在那里，人人都更忙碌，更奔走不歇，只顾留意那些可疑的宅子，便顾不上去理会踽踽独行的路人。

布莱克尼领着安娜·米朝卢森堡公园走去——那片前贵族暴君们（ci-devant）昔日寻欢作乐的大园林，如今早已满目荒芜。美丽的奥地利的安娜（Anne of Austria），比她更早的美第奇家的太后，路易十三和他那帮英武的火枪手，统统让位于这座被围困的共和国轰轰烈烈的铸炮大业。法兰西四面受敌，正驱使她的儿女起来保卫她：受过她的迫害，被她折磨殉难，被她置于死地，她却依然是他们的母亲——祖国（La Patrie），仍需要他们的臂膀去抵御外敌。英格兰正威胁着北面，普鲁士和奥地利虎视东方，胡德海军上将的旗帜，已飘扬在土伦兵工厂的上空。

===Original===
The siege of the Republic!

And the Republic is fighting for dear life. The Tuileries and Luxembourg Gardens are transformed into a township of gigantic smithies; and Anne Mie, with scared eyes, and clinging to Blakeney's arm, cast furtive, terrified glances at the huge furnaces and the begrimed, darkly scowling faces of the workers within.

"The people of France in arms against tyranny!" Great placards, bearing these inspiriting words, are affixed to gallows-shaped posts, and flutter in the evening breeze, rendered scorching by the heat of the furnaces all around.

===Chinese===
共和国正遭到围困！

而共和国正在为性命拼死一搏。杜伊勒里宫和卢森堡公园，已经变成一座布满巨型锻炉的工场市镇；安娜·米紧挽着布莱克尼的手臂，满眼惶恐，偷偷地、心惊胆战地瞟着那些巨大的熔炉，还有炉边工人们一张张煤灰满面、阴沉怒视的脸。

「法兰西人民武装起来，反抗暴政！」贴着这些鼓舞人心字句的大幅标语，钉在形似绞架的木桩上，在晚风里翻卷飘动——四周炉火蒸腾，连风也被熏得灼热烫人。

===Original===
Farther on, a group of older men, squatting on the ground, are busy making tents, and some women—the same Megaeras who daily shriek round the guillotine—are plying their needles and scissors for the purpose of making clothes for the soldiers.

The soldiers are the entire able-bodied male population of France.

"The people of France in arms against tyranny!"

===Chinese===
再往前去，一帮上了年纪的汉子蹲坐在地上，忙着缝制帐篷；还有几个女人——正是那班天天围着断头台尖声叫嚣的墨盖拉（Megaera）们——在飞针走线，操着剪子，为士兵赶制衣裳。

所谓士兵，便是法兰西全国所有还能扛枪的男子。

「法兰西人民武装起来，反抗暴政！」

===Original===
That is their sign, their trademark; one of these placards, fitfully illumined by a torch of resin, towers above a group of children busy tearing up scraps of old linen—their mothers', their sisters' linen—in order to make lint for the wounded.

Loud curses and suppressed mutterings fill the smoke-laden air.

===Chinese===
这就是他们的标记，他们的招牌。一块这样的标语牌，被松脂火把照得忽明忽暗，高耸在一群孩子头顶——他们正忙着把旧麻布撕成碎条——那是他们母亲、他们姐妹的麻布——好为伤员搓制敷伤的软麻布。

烟雾弥漫的空气里，充斥着高声的咒骂和压低的嘟哝。

===Original===
The people of France, in arms against tyranny, is bending its broad back before the most cruel, the most absolute and brutish slave-driving ever exercised over mankind.

Not even medieval Christianity has ever dared such wholesale enforcements of its doctrines, as this constitution of Liberty and Fraternity.

Merlin's "Law of the Suspect" has just been formulated. From now onward each and every citizen of France must watch his words, his looks, his gestures, lest they be suspect. Of what—of treason to the Republic, to the people? Nay, worse! lest they be suspect of being suspect to the great era of Liberty.

===Chinese===
自称武装起来反抗暴政的法兰西人民，正弯下宽阔的脊梁，匍匐在人类有史以来最残忍、最专横、最野蛮的奴役鞭笞之下。

就连中世纪的基督教会，也从未敢像这自由与博爱的宪法这样，把自家的教义如此大规模地强加于人。

梅尔兰的《嫌疑犯法令》刚刚炮制出笼。从今往后，法兰西的每一个公民都得提防自己的一言、一颦、一举一动，免得落人嫌疑。嫌疑什么——嫌疑背叛共和国、背叛人民？不，还要糟！是免得让人嫌疑，说他『嫌疑』上了这个伟大的自由时代。

===Original===
Therefore in the smithies and among the groups of tent-makers a moment's negligence, a careless attention to the work, might lead to a brief trial on the morrow and the inevitable guillotine. Negligence is treason to the higher interests of the Republic.

Blakeney dragged Anne Mie away from the sight. These roaring furnaces frightened her; he took her down the Place St. Michel, towards the river. It was quieter here.

"What dreadful people they have become," she said, shuddering; "even I can remember how different they used to be."

===Chinese===
因此，无论是在锻炉边，还是在缝帐篷的人堆里，稍一懈怠，干活带半分马虎，都可能换来次日一场草草的审讯，以及那架不可避免的断头台。懈怠，便是对共和国崇高利益的背叛。

布莱克尼拉着安娜·米，离开了这片景象。那些轰鸣的熔炉吓着了她；他带她走下圣米歇尔广场（Place St. Michel），朝河边而去。这里清静些了。

「这些人变得多么可怕，」她哆嗦着说，「连我都还记得，他们从前完全不是这个样子。」

===Original===
The houses on the banks of the river were mostly converted into hospitals, preparatory for the great siege. Some hundred mètres lower down, the new children's hospital, endowed by Citizen-Deputy Déroulède, loomed, white, clean, and comfortable-looking, amidst its more squalid fellows.

"I think it would be best not to sit down," suggested Blakeney, "and wiser for you to throw your hood away from your face."

He seemed to have no fears for himself; many had said that he bore a charmed life; and yet ever since Admiral Hood had planted his flag on Toulon Arsenal, the English were more feared than ever, and The Scarlet Pimpernel more hated than most.

===Chinese===
河岸上的房屋大多已改成医院，为这场大规模围困做着准备。顺流再往下几百米处，德鲁莱德公会议员捐建的新儿童医院隐然浮现，在周围那片同样充作医院的破败房舍中间，显得洁白、干净，看上去舒舒坦坦。

「我看最好还是别坐下，」布莱克尼提议道，「再说你把风帽从脸上掀开些，才更稳妥。」

他自己似乎毫无惧色；人们常说他有神明护体，刀枪不入。然而自打胡德上将把旗帜插上土伦兵工厂，英国人便比以往任何时候都更遭忌惮，而红花侠招致的痛恨，又比大多数英国人更深。

===Original===
"You wished to speak to me about Paul Déroulède," he said kindly, seeing that the young girl was making desperate efforts to say what lay on her mind. "He is my friend, you know."

"Yes; that is why I wished to ask you a question," she replied.

"What is it?"

"Who is Juliette de Marny, and why did she seek an entrance into Paul's house?"

"Did she seek it, then?"

===Chinese===
「你想同我谈谈保罗·德鲁莱德的事，」他和蔼地说。他看出这姑娘正拼命鼓着勇气，要把心里的话吐出来。「你知道，他是我的朋友。」

「正是。所以我想向您请教一件事。」她答道。

「什么事？」

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette de Marny）究竟是什么人？她为什么要设法钻进保罗的家门？」

「怎么，她是设法钻进去的么？」

===Original===
"Yes; I saw the scene from the balcony. At the time it did not strike me as a farce. I merely thought that she had been stupid and foolhardy. But since then I have reflected. She provoked the mob of the street, wilfully, just at the very moment when she reached M. Déroulède's door. She meant to appeal to his chivalry, and called for help, well knowing that he would respond."

She spoke rapidly and excitedly now, throwing off all shyness and reserve. Blakeney was forced to check her vehemence, which might have been thought "suspicious" by some idle citizen unpleasantly inclined.

"Well? And now?" he asked, for the young girl had paused, as if ashamed of her excitement.

===Chinese===
「是的。那一幕，我是从阳台上看见的。当时我倒没觉得那是一出假戏，只当她又蠢又莽撞。可是打那以后，我细细想过。她走到德鲁莱德先生家门口的那一刻，存心去招惹街上那群暴民。她是要打动他的侠义心肠，才呼喊求救的——她心里明明白白，他一定会应声而出。」

她此刻说得又急又激动，把羞怯和矜持全抛到了九霄云外。布莱克尼不得不按住她这股激烈劲儿——哪个闲得无聊、存心找茬的公民听了，兴许就要说一声『可疑』。

「后来呢？如今呢？」他问道。姑娘停住了口，似乎为方才的激动感到羞愧。

===Original===
"And now she stays in the house, on and on, day after day," continued Anne Mie, speaking more quietly, though with no less intensity. "Why does she not go? She is not safe in France. She belongs to the most hated of all the classes—the idle, rich aristocrats of the old regime. Paul has several times suggested plans for her emigration to England. Madame Déroulède, who is an angel, loves her, and would not like to part from her, but it would be obviously wiser for her to go, and yet she stays. Why?"

"Presumably because—"

"Because she is in love with Paul?" interrupted Anne Mie vehemently. "No, no; she does not love him—at least—Oh! sometimes I don't know. Her eyes light up when he comes, and she is listless when he goes. She always spends a longer time over her toilet, when we expect him home to dinner," she added, with a touch of naive femininity. "But—if it be love, then that love is strange and unwomanly; it is a love that will not be for his good—"

"Why should you think that?"

===Chinese===
「如今她待在屋里，一住就是一天又一天，」安娜·米接着说，语气平静了些，分量却丝毫未减。「她为什么不走？她待在法兰西并不安全。她属于所有阶级里最遭人恨的那一等——旧制度下那些游手好闲的阔绰贵族。保罗几次三番提出送她移居英格兰的办法。德鲁莱德夫人是位天使，疼爱她，舍不得同她分开；可她走显然才是上策——然而她偏不走。为什么？」

「多半是因为——」

「因为爱上了保罗？」安娜·米激烈地抢过话头。「不，不是；她并不爱他——至少——唉！有时候我也说不清。他来的时候，她的眼睛就发亮；他走的时候，她就百无聊赖。逢到我们要等他回家吃晚饭，她梳妆总要费上多一倍的工夫，」她又添了一句，带着一点天真烂漫的女人心性。「可是——如果那是爱，那这份爱就太古怪，太不合女人情理了；那决不是一种会为他着想的爱——」

「你怎么会这样想？」

===Original===
"I don't know," said the girl simply. "Isn't it an instinct?"

"Not a very unerring one in this case, I fear."

"Why?"

===Chinese===
「说不上来，」姑娘率直地说。「这不就是本能么？」

「恐怕就这一桩事而言，这本能并不怎么灵验。」

「为什么？」

===Original===
"Because your own love for Paul Déroulède has blinded you—Ah! you must pardon me, mademoiselle; you sought this conversation and not I, and I fear me I have wounded you. Yet I would wish you to know how deep is my sympathy with you, and how great my desire to render you a service if I could."

"I was about to ask a service of you, monsieur."

"Then command me, I beg of you."

===Chinese===
「因为你自己对保罗·德鲁莱德的爱情，蒙住了你的眼睛——啊！您得原谅我，小姐；这场谈话是您求来的，不是我，只怕我伤了您的心。不过我愿让您知道：我对您怀着多深的同情，倘若能为您效劳，我的心愿又是多么恳切。」

「我正要请您帮个忙呢，先生。」

「那便请吩咐，我求您了。」

===Original===
"You are Paul's friend—persuade him that that woman in his house is a standing danger to his life and liberty."

"He would not listen to me."

"Oh! a man always listens to another."

"Except on one subject—the woman he loves."

He had said the last words very gently but very firmly. He was deeply, tenderly sorry for the poor, deformed, fragile girl, doomed to be a witness of that most heartrending of human tragedies, the passing away of her own scarce-hoped-for happiness. But he felt that at this moment the kindest act would be one of complete truth. He knew that Paul Déroulède's heart was completely given to Juliette de Marny; he too, like Anne Mie, instinctively mistrusted the beautiful girl and her strange, silent ways, but, unlike the poor hunchback, he knew that no sin which Juliette might commit would henceforth tear her from out the heart of his friend; that if, indeed, she turned out to be false, or even treacherous, she would, nevertheless, still hold a place in Déroulède's very soul, which no one else would ever fill.

===Chinese===
「您是保罗的朋友——您去劝他：他家里那个女人，对他的性命和自由，是时时悬着的祸根。」

「他不会听我的。」

「唉！男人总归肯听另一个男人的。」

「只有一样例外——他心爱的那个女人。」

末了这句话，他说得极温和，却极坚定。这个可怜的姑娘，身有畸残，体质纤弱，命定要亲眼目睹人间最揪心的一出悲剧——眼看着自己那本来就不敢多指望的幸福悄然逝去——他为她怀着深深的、温柔的怜惜。可他觉得，此刻最仁慈的做法，莫过于把实情和盘托出。他知道，保罗·德鲁莱德的心已经整个儿交给了朱丽叶特·马尔尼；他同安娜·米一样，也本能地不信任这位美丽的姑娘和她那些古怪沉默的行止；然而他不同于这个可怜的驼背姑娘之处在于，他明白从今往后，无论朱丽叶特犯下怎样的罪孽，都断不能把她从他朋友的心里拔除；即便她果真虚伪，甚至背信弃义，她在德鲁莱德灵魂深处占据的那个位置，也依然无人能够填补。

===Original===
"You think he loves her?" asked Anne Mie at last.

"I am sure of it."

"And she?"
