"Ah! I do not know. I would trust your instinct—a woman's—sooner than my own."

===Chinese===
「您认定他是爱她的？」安娜·米终于问道。

「我有十分的把握。」

「那她呢？」

「唉！这个我说不上来。您的本能——女人的本能——比我自己的，更叫我信得过。」

===Original===
"She is false, I tell you, and is hatching treason against Paul."

"Then all we can do is to wait."

"Wait?"

"And watch carefully, earnestly, all the time. There! shall I pledge you my word that Déroulède shall come to no harm?"

"Pledge me your word that you'll part him from that woman."

"Nay; that is beyond my power. A man like Paul Déroulède only loves once in life, but when he does, it is for always."

===Chinese===
「我告诉您，她是个虚伪的女人，正在暗中策划加害保罗的叛逆。」

「那么我们只有等待。」

「等待？」

「并且时时刻刻仔细地、认真地看着。这样吧！要不要我拿人格向您担保：德鲁莱德决计不会遭到不测？」

「您担保把他同那个女人分开。」

「不行；这是我力所不及的。像保罗·德鲁莱德这样的男人，一生只爱一回；可爱上了，便是一生一世。」

===Original===
Once more she was silent, pressing her lips closely together, as if afraid of what she might say.

He saw that she was bitterly disappointed, and sought for a means of tempering the cruelty of the blow.

"It will be your task to watch over Paul," he said; "with your friendship to guard and protect him, we need have no fear for his safety, I think."

"I will watch," she replied quietly.

===Chinese===
她又一次沉默了，紧紧抿住嘴唇，仿佛怕自己说出什么话来。

他看出她心里失望得苦极了，便想找个法子，把这一击的狠劲儿冲淡几分。

「照看保罗，往后就是您的差事，」他说；「有您的友情守护他、保护他，我想，他的安危就用不着我们担心了。」

「我会看着，」她平静地答道。

===Original===
Gradually he had led her steps back towards the Rue Ecole de Médecine.

A great melancholy had fallen over his bold, adventurous spirit. How full of tragedies was this great city, in the last throes of its insane and cruel struggle for an unattainable goal. And yet, despite its guillotine and mock trials, its tyrannical laws and overfilled prisons, its very sorrows paled before the dead, dull misery of this deformed girl's heart.

A wild exaltation, a fever of enthusiasm lent glamour to the scenes which were daily enacted on the Place de la Revolution, turning the final acts of the tragedies into glaring, lurid melodrama, almost unreal in its poignant appeal to the sensibilities.

===Chinese===
不知不觉间，他已引着她折回医学院街的方向。

一股深沉的忧郁，罩上了他那颗大胆而爱冒险的心。这座大城正为它那可望而不可即的目标，作着疯狂而残酷的挣扎，垂死挣扎的阵痛里，蕴藏着多少悲剧啊。然而，尽管这里断头台高耸、假审判频仍、暴虐的法令横行、监狱人满为患，这一切的哀痛，在这个畸残姑娘心中那死气沉沉、麻木呆滞的苦闷面前，也都黯然失色。

一种狂热的亢奋，一种激情的高烧，给革命广场上日日上演的场面镀上了一层光怪陆离的色彩，把一幕幕悲剧的终场，变成刺目炫亮的情节剧，那种痛彻心扉的感染力，几乎叫人疑心不是真的。

===Original===
But here there was only this dead, dull misery, an aching heart, a poor, fragile creature in the throes of an agonised struggle for a fast-disappearing happiness.

Anne Mie hardly knew now what she had hoped, when she sought this interview with Sir Percy Blakeney. Drowning in a sea of hopelessness, she had clutched at what might prove a chance of safety. Her reason told her that Paul's friend was right. Déroulède was a man who would love but once in his life. He had never loved—for he had too much pitied—poor, pathetic little Anne Mie.

Nay; why should we say that love and pity are akin?

===Chinese===
可这里，只有这一片死寂呆滞的凄苦，一颗隐隐作痛的心，一个可怜而纤弱的生灵，正在为一去不返的幸福苦苦挣扎，痛楚万状。

安娜·米此刻几乎记不清，当初她求见珀西·布莱克尼爵士的时候，心里到底抱着什么指望。溺沉在绝望的汪洋里，她抓住了一根或许能救命的稻草。理智告诉她，保罗的朋友是对的。德鲁莱德是个一生只爱一回的男人。他从未爱过——只因他的怜悯太多了——可怜而惹人疼惜的小安娜·米。

不；我们何苦要说，爱与怜悯本是同根生？

===Original===
Love, the great, the strong, the conquering god—Love that subdues a world, and rides roughshod over principle, virtue, tradition, over home, kindred, and religion—what cares he for the easy conquest of the pathetic being, who appeals to his sympathy?

Love means equality—the same height of heroism or of sin. When Love stoops to pity, he has ceased to soar in the boundless space, that rarefied atmosphere wherein man feels himself made at last truly in the image of God.

===Chinese===
爱，那伟大、强健、所向披靡的神明——那降服了整个世界、从原则、德行、传统，从家庭、亲伦、宗教头上踏过去的爱——对于轻易征服一个乞怜于他的同情的小小可怜人儿，他又何曾在意？

爱意味着平等——要么同样高的英雄气概，要么同样深的罪孽。当爱俯下身去俯就怜悯，他便不再在无边无际的高天翱翔——正是在那稀薄清冽的大气里，人才终于觉得自己真正是照着上帝的模样造出来的。

## IX Jealousy / IX 嫉妒

===Original===
At the door of her home Blakeney parted from Anne Mie, with all the courtesy with which he would have bade adieu to the greatest lady in his own land.

Anne Mie let herself into the house with her own latchkey. She closed the heavy door noiselessly, then glided upstairs like a quaint little ghost.

But on the landing above she met Paul Déroulède.

He had just come out of his room, and was still fully dressed.

===Chinese===
在自家门口，布莱克尼（Blakeney）同安娜·米（Anne Mie）道了别；那份殷勤周至，就仿佛是在向他自己国土上最尊贵的夫人辞行。

安娜·米用自己的门匙开了锁，走进屋去。她悄没声儿地掩上那扇沉重的门，随后像一只古怪的小幽灵似的，溜上了楼。

可在楼上的平台处，她迎面遇上了保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）。

他刚从自己房里出来，衣着一丝未卸。

===Original===
“Anne Mie!” he said, with such an obvious cry of pleasure, that the young girl, with beating heart, paused a moment on the top of the stairs, as if hoping to hear that cry again, feeling that indeed he was glad to see her, had been uneasy because of her long absence.

“Have I made you anxious?” she asked at last.

“Anxious!” he exclaimed. “Little one, I have hardly lived this last hour, since I realised that you had gone out so late as this, and all alone.”

“How did you know?”

“Mademoiselle de Marny knocked at my door an hour ago. She had gone to your room to see you, and, not finding you there, she searched the house for you, and finally, in her anxiety, came to me. We did not dare to tell my mother. I won’t ask you where you have been, Anne Mie, but another time, remember, little one, that the streets of Paris are not safe, and that those who love you suffer deeply, when they know you to be in peril.”

===Chinese===
「安娜·米！」他唤道。那分明是一声抑制不住的欢喜，少女心头怦怦直跳，在楼梯口停了片刻，仿佛巴望能再听见那一声呼唤；她此刻真切地感到，他见着她确实欢喜，她久久不归，他曾为此悬心。

「我让你担心了吗？」她终于问道。

「担心！」他嚷道。「小家伙，自从知道你这么晚还出了门，又是独自一人，这最后一个钟头我简直就没法活。」

「你是怎么知道的？」

「一个钟头前，德·马尔尼小姐（Mademoiselle de Marny）敲过我的门。她本是去你房里看你，没见着人，便满屋找你，最后急得没法，才来找我。我们不敢告诉我母亲。你去了哪儿，我不问，安娜·米；可是记住，小家伙，下回别这样——巴黎的街道并不太平，爱你的人一旦知道你身临险境，是要深深受苦的。」

===Original===
“Those who love me!” murmured the girl under her breath.

“Could you not have asked me to come with you?”

“No; I wanted to be alone. The streets were quite safe, and—I wanted to speak with Sir Percy Blakeney.”

“With Blakeney?” he exclaimed in boundless astonishment. “Why, what in the world did you want to say him?”

===Chinese===
「爱我的人！」少女低声喃喃。

「你难道不能叫我陪你一道去吗？」

「不；我想一个人待着。街上太平得很，况且——我想同珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）谈一谈。」

「同布莱克尼？」他惊愕得不知所以。「咦，你到底有什么话要对他说？」

===Original===
The girl, so unaccustomed to lying, had blurted out the truth, almost against her will.

“I thought he could help me, as I was much perturbed and restless.”

“You went to him sooner than to me?” said Déroulède in a tone of gentle reproach, and still puzzled at this extraordinary action on the part of the girl, usually so shy and reserved.

“My anxiety was about you, and you would have mocked me for it.”

===Chinese===
少女素来不惯撒谎，这句实话几乎是违背本心，脱口而出的。

「我想他能帮我开解开解，我当时心烦意乱，坐立难安。」

「你宁可去找他，也不来找我？」德鲁莱德语带温存的责备；这姑娘平素那样羞怯缄默，此刻竟有这般反常之举，他依然百思不解。

「我担心的是你，说了出来，你只会笑话我。」

===Original===
“Indeed, I should never mock you, Anne Mie. But why should you be anxious about me?”

“Because I see you wandering blindly on the brink of a great danger, and because I see you confiding in those, whom you had best mistrust.”

He frowned a little, and bit his lip to check the rough word that was on the tip of his tongue.

“Is Sir Percy Blakeney one of those whom I had best mistrust?” he said lightly.

“No,” she answered curtly.

===Chinese===
「真的，我绝不会笑话你，安娜·米。可你为什么要替我担心呢？」

「因为我眼看你昏昏然走向一桩大祸的边缘，眼看你推心置腹去信的那些人，正是你最该提防的人。」

他微微皱起眉头，咬住了嘴唇，把到了嘴边的一句粗话咽了回去。

「那么珀西·布莱克尼爵士，也是我最该提防的人么？」他轻描淡写地问。

「不是。」她干巴巴地答道。

===Original===
“Then, dear, there is no cause for unrest. He is the only one of my friends whom you have not known intimately. All those who are round me now, you know that you can trust and that you can love,” he added earnestly and significantly.

He took her hand; it was trembling with obvious suppressed agitation. She knew that he had guessed what was passing in her mind, and now was deeply ashamed of what she had done. She had been tortured with jealousy for the past three weeks, but at least she had suffered quite alone: no one had been allowed to touch that wound, which more often than not, excites derision rather than pity. Now, by her own actions, two men knew her secret. Both were kind and sympathetic; but Déroulède resented her imputations, and Blakeney had been unable to help her.

A wave of morbid introspection swept over her soul. She realised in a moment how petty and base had been her thoughts and how purposeless her actions. She would have given her life at this moment to eradicate from Déroulède’s mind the knowledge of her own jealousy; she hoped that at least he had not guessed her love.

She tried to read his thoughts, but in the dark passage, only dimly lighted by the candles in Déroulède’s room beyond, she could not see the expression of his face, but the hand which held hers was warm and tender. She felt herself pitied, and blushed at the thought. With a hasty good night she fled down the passage, and locked herself in her room, alone with her own thoughts at last.

===Chinese===
「那么，亲爱的，就不必不安了。我这些朋友里，唯独他你没有深交过。如今围在我身边的这些人，你都知道自己信得过，也知道可以敬爱，」他补上一句，语气恳切而意味深长。

他握住她的手；那只手抑制着显而易见的激动，簌簌发抖。她明白他已猜中她心里翻腾的念头，此刻对自己方才的所作所为羞愧万分。三个星期以来，嫉妒把她折磨得死去活来；但她至少是一个人暗自受苦：从没有人获准触碰那道伤口——那道惹来的往往总是讥笑、多于怜惜的伤口。如今，她一己的言行，叫两个男人知道了她的秘密。两人都心地和善、怀着体恤；可德鲁莱德对她那些指嫌心存芥蒂，布莱克尼呢，又终究没能帮得了她。

一阵病态的自省席卷过她的灵魂。她霎时看透：自己的念头是何等琐屑卑下，自己的作为又是何等徒劳无益。此刻便是舍出性命，她也在所不惜，只求把嫉妒的印记从德鲁莱德的心里抹去；她只盼望，至少他还没有猜到她的爱情。

她竭力想窥探他的心思；可是在昏暗的过道里——唯有德鲁莱德房中的烛光远远投来一点微明——她看不清他脸上的神色；但那只握着她的手，温暖而柔和。她觉出自己被人怜悯了，一想到这里，脸上便热起来。她匆匆道了一声晚安，顺着过道奔逃而去，把自己反锁进房中，终于独自一人，与自己的心事相对了。

## X Denunciation / X 告发

===Original===
But what of Juliette?

What of this wild, passionate, romantic creature tortured by a Titanic conflict? She, but a girl, scarcely yet a woman, torn by the greatest antagonistic powers that ever fought for a human soul. On the one side duty, tradition, her dead brother, her father—above all, her religion and the oath she had sworn before God; on the other justice and honour, a case of right and wrong, honesty and pity.

===Chinese===
可是，朱丽叶特（Juliette）又如何了呢？

这个狂野、热烈、耽于幻想的生命，正被一场惊天动地的内心冲突折磨着，她又如何了呢？她不过是个少女，尚且算不得妇人，却被有史以来争夺过一个人类灵魂的最强大的敌对力量撕扯着。一边是责任、传统、她的亡兄、她的父亲——尤其是她的宗教，和她在上帝面前立下的誓言；另一边是正义与荣誉，一桩是非分明的公案，是诚实与怜悯。

===Original===
How she fought with these powers now!

She fought with them, struggled with them on her knees. She tried to crush memory, tried to forget that awful midnight scene ten years ago, her brother’s dead body, her father’s avenging hand holding her own, as he begged her to do that, which he was too feeble, too old to accomplish.

His words rang in her ears from across that long vista of the past.

===Chinese===
此刻，她同这些力量搏斗得是何等惨烈！

她与它们搏斗，跪着同它们挣扎。她竭力要把记忆碾碎，竭力想忘掉十年前那个可怕的黑夜场面：兄长的遗体，父亲那只复仇的手握着她自己的手，恳求她去做那件他自己已经太衰弱、太年老而无力完成的事。

隔着往昔那悠长的岁月回望过去，他的话仍在她耳中鸣响。

===Original===
“Before the face of Almighty God, who sees and hears me, I swear—”

And she had repeated those words loudly and of her own free will, with her hand resting on her brother’s breast, and God Himself looking down upon her, for she had called upon Him to listen.

“I swear that I will seek out Paul Déroulède, and in any manner which God may dictate to me encompass his death, his ruin, or dishonour in revenge for my brother’s death. May my brother’s soul remain in torment until the final Judgment Day if I should break my oath, but may it rest in eternal peace, the day on which his death is fitly avenged.”

===Chinese===
「在明鉴万物、无所不闻的全能上帝面前，我发誓——」

而她曾亲口高声、心甘情愿地复述过这些话，一只手按在兄长的胸口，上帝亲自俯视着她，因为她曾吁请他垂听。

「我发誓，我要寻出保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède），以上帝启示于我的任何方式，置他于死地、毁他、或使他蒙羞，以为我兄长之死复仇。我若背弃此誓，愿我兄长的灵魂受尽煎熬，直至最后审判之日；而当他之死得报应得宜的那一天，愿他的灵魂得享永安。」

===Original===
Almost it seemed to her as if father and brother were standing by her side, as she knelt and prayed.—Oh! how she prayed!

In many ways she was only a child. All her years had been passed in confinement, either beside her dying father or, later, between the four walls of the Ursuline Convent. And during those years her soul had been fed on a contemplative, ecstatic religion, a kind of sanctified superstition, which she would have deemed sacrilege to combat.

Her first step into womanhood was taken with that oath upon her lips; since then, with a stoical sense of duty, she had lashed herself into a daily, hourly remembrance of the great mission imposed upon her.

To have neglected it would have been, to her, equal to denying God.

===Chinese===
她跪着祈祷的时候，几乎觉得父亲和兄长就站在自己身旁。——啊！她祈祷得何等虔诚！

在许多方面，她仍然只是个孩子。她的全部岁月都是在幽闭中度过的：不是守在弥留的父亲身边，就是后来关在乌尔苏拉会修道院（Ursuline Convent）的四堵墙内。那些年里，她的灵魂一直以一种冥想式的、狂喜迷醉的宗教为食粮——那是一种被奉为神圣的迷信，若与它对抗，在她看来便是渎神。

她迈入成年女子的第一步，便是伴着唇边的这句誓言；从那时起，她怀着斯多葛式的责任感，鞭策自己时时刻刻、日复一日地牢记压在她身上的伟大使命。

在她看来，若对这使命有片刻疏忽，便无异于背弃上帝。

===Original===
She had but vague ideas of the doctrinal side of religion. Purgatory was to her merely a word, but a word representing a real spiritual state—one of expectancy, of restlessness, of sorrow. And vaguely, yet determinedly, she believed that her brother’s soul suffered, because she had been too weak to fulfil her oath.

The Church had not come to her rescue. The ministers of her religion were scattered to the four corners of besieged, agonising France. She had no one to help her, no one to comfort her. That very peaceful, contemplative life she had led in the convent, only served to enhance her feeling of the solemnity of her mission.

It was true, it was inevitable, because it was so hard.

To the few who, throughout those troublous times, had kept a feeling of veneration for their religion, this religion had become one of abnegation and martyrdom.

A spirit of uncompromising Jansenism seemed to call forth sacrifices and renunciation, whereas the happy-go-lucky Catholicism of the past century had only suggested an easy, flowered path, to a comfortable, well-upholstered heaven.

===Chinese===
对于宗教的教义一面，她只有些模糊的观念。炼狱对她来说只是一个字眼，却是一个代表着某种真实心灵状态的字眼——一种期待、不安与悲哀的状态。她隐约然而执拗地相信，兄长的灵魂正在受苦，因为她太软弱，未能履行自己的誓言。

教会不曾来拯救她。她那宗教的司铎们已被驱散到被围困、在痛苦中挣扎的法兰西的四面八方。没有人帮助她，也没有人安慰她。她在修道院里过的那种安宁静修的生活，反倒只使她愈发感到自己使命的庄严。

这是真实的，是不可避免的，正因为它如此艰难。

对于那些在动乱年代里仍对宗教怀有虔敬之情的少数人来说，这宗教已变成一种克己与殉道的宗教。

一种毫不妥协的詹森主义（Jansenism）精神，似乎在召唤着牺牲与弃绝；而上个世纪那种随遇而安的天主教，所指出的却只是一条平坦而开满鲜花的路，通向一个舒适绵软、布置考究的天堂。

===Original===
The harder the task seemed which was set before her, the more real it became to Juliette. God, she firmly believed, had at last, after ten years, shown her the way to wreak vengeance upon her brother’s murderer. He had brought her to this house, caused her to see and hear part of the conversation between Blakeney and Déroulède, and this at the moment of all others, when even the semblance of a conspiracy against the Republic would bring the one inevitable result in its train: disgrace first, the hasty mock trial, the hall of justice, and the guillotine.

She tried not to hate Déroulède. She wished to judge him coldly and impartially, or rather to indict him before the throne of God, and to punish him for the crime he had committed ten years ago. Her personal feelings must remain out of the question.

Had Charlotte Corday considered her own sensibilities, when with her own hand she put an end to Marat?

===Chinese===
摆在她面前的使命显得越艰难，在朱丽叶特心中就变得越真实。她坚信，上帝在十年之后，终于向她指明了向残害她兄长的凶手复仇的道路。上帝把她带到这座宅子里，让她窥见并听到布莱克尼（Blakeney）与德鲁莱德谈话的一部分，而且偏偏挑在这个节骨眼上——此时哪怕只是貌似有一桩反对共和国的阴谋，也必定会招来那唯一不可避免的下场：先是身败名裂，接着是草草的假审讯、法庭大厅，最后是断头台。

她竭力不让自己恨德鲁莱德。她要冷静而公正地审判他，或者毋宁说，要在上帝的宝座前控告他，为他在十年前犯下的罪行惩罚他。她个人的感情必须排除在外。

夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）当年亲手结果了马拉（Marat）的时候，可曾顾及过自己的情感？

===Original===
Juliette remained on her knees for hours. She heard Anne Mie come home, and Déroulède’s voice of welcome on the landing. This was perhaps the most bitter moment of this awful soul conflict, for it brought to her mind the remembrance of those others who would suffer too, and who were innocent—Madame Déroulède and poor, crippled Anne Mie. They had done no wrong, and yet how heavily would they be punished!

And then the saner judgment, the human, material code of ethics gained for a while the upper hand. Juliette would rise from her knees, dry her eyes, prepare quietly to go to bed, and to forget all about the awful, relentless Fate which dragged her to the fulfilment of its will, and then sink back, brokenhearted, murmuring impassioned prayers for forgiveness to her father, her brother, her God.

The soul was young and ardent, and it fought for abnegation, martyrdom, and stern duty; the body was childlike, and it fought for peace, contentment, and quiet reason.

The rational body was conquered by the passionate, powerful soul.

Blame not the child, for in herself she was innocent. She was but another of the many victims of this cruel, mad, hysterical time, that spirit of relentless tyranny, forcing its doctrines upon the weak.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特跪了好几个钟头。她听见安娜·米（Anne Mie）回来了，听见德鲁莱德在楼梯口迎候的话音。这也许是这场可怕的灵魂搏斗中最痛苦的时刻，因为它让她想起了另外那些也将受苦、却是无辜的人——德鲁莱德夫人和可怜的、腿有残疾的安娜·米。她们没有做错任何事，却要受到多么沉重的惩罚！

而后，较为清醒的判断、人世间务实的伦理准则，会一时占了上风。朱丽叶特会从跪处站起，拭干眼泪，静静准备上床睡觉，要把那拖着她去实现其意志的可怕而无情的命运统统忘掉；可随即又颓然跪倒回去，心碎欲裂，喃喃地向父亲、兄长和上帝热烈地祷告，祈求宽恕。

灵魂年轻而炽热，它为克己、殉道和严苛的责任而战；肉体天真稚嫩，它为安宁、知足与平和的理性而战。

讲理性的肉体，终于被炽热而强有力的灵魂征服了。

不要责备这个孩子，因为她本身是无辜的。她不过是这个残酷、疯狂、歇斯底里的时代的又一个牺牲品，是那种专制的铁腕精神把它的教条强加于弱者身上的又一个受害者。

===Original===
With the first break of dawn Juliette at last finally rose from her knees, bathed her burning eyes and head, tidied her hair and dress, then she sat down at the table, and began to write.

She was a transformed being now, no longer a child, essentially a woman—a Joan of Arc with a mission, a Charlotte Corday going to martyrdom, a human, suffering, erring soul, committing a great crime for the sake of an idea.

She wrote out carefully and with a steady hand the denunciation of Citizen-Deputy Déroulède which has become an historical document, and is preserved in the chronicles of France.

You have all seen it at the Musée Carnavalet in its glass case, its yellow paper and faded ink revealing nothing of the soul conflict of which it was the culminating victory. The cramped, somewhat schoolgirlish writing is the mute, pathetic witness of one of the saddest tragedies, that era of sorrow and crime has ever known:

===Chinese===
天刚破晓，朱丽叶特终于从跪处站起身来，洗了洗灼热发烫的双眼和额头，理好头发和衣衫，然后在桌前坐下，开始写信。

此刻的她已是一个脱胎换骨的人，不再是孩子，而是一个不折不扣的妇人——是身负使命的贞德（Joan of Arc），是走向殉道的夏洛特·科黛，是一个有血有肉、受苦受难、会犯过失的灵魂，正为一个观念而犯下弥天大罪。

她用沉稳的手，一丝不苟地写下了对公会议员德鲁莱德的告发信——这封信如今已成了一份历史文献，保存在法兰西的史册之中。

你们大家都在卡纳瓦莱博物馆（Musée Carnavalet）的玻璃柜里见过它：泛黄的纸页、褪色的墨迹，全然看不出它曾是那场灵魂搏斗的最高胜利的产物。那拘谨而略带女学生气的笔迹，是那个苦难与罪恶的时代所经历的、最悲惨的悲剧之一的无声而令人心碎的见证：

===Original===
> You trust and believe in the Representative of the people: Citizen-Deputy Paul Déroulède. He is false, and a traitor to the Republic. He is planning, and hopes to effect, the release of ci-devant Marie Antoinette, widow of the traitor Louis Capet. Haste! ye representatives of the people! proofs of his assertion, papers and plans, are still in the house of the Citizen-Deputy Déroulède. This statement is made by one who knows.
> I, The 23rd Fructidor.

===Chinese===
> 你们所信赖、所尊奉的人民代表：公会议员保罗·德鲁莱德。此人虚伪，是共和国的叛徒。他正在谋划并指望促成前贵族（ci-devant）玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）——叛徒路易·卡佩（Louis Capet）之遗孀——的脱逃。人民的代表们，赶快行动！证实他这番谋划的证据、文件与计划，眼下仍存于德鲁莱德公会议员的宅邸之中。具状之人深知内情。
> 我，果月二十三日。

===Original===
When her letter was written she read it through carefully, made the one or two little corrections, which are still visible in the document, then folded her missive, hid it within the folds of her kerchief, and, wrapping a dark cloak and hood round her, she slipped noiselessly out of her room.

The house was all quiet and still. She shuddered a little as the cool morning air fanned her hot cheeks: it seemed like the breath of ghosts.

She ran quickly down the stairs, and as rapidly as she could, pushed back the heavy bolts of the front door, and slipped out into the street.

Already the city was beginning to stir. There was no time for sleep, when so much had to be done for the safety of the threatened Republic. As Juliette turned her steps towards the river, she met the crowd of workmen, whom France was employing for her defence.

Behind her, in the Luxembourg Gardens, and all along the opposite bank of the river, the furnaces were already ablaze, and the smiths at work forging the guns.

===Chinese===
信写完之后，她从头到尾仔细读了一遍，做了那一两处小小的修改——至今仍能在文书上看到——然后把信叠好，藏进头巾的褶层里，又裹上一件深色斗篷、罩上风帽，悄无声息地溜出了自己的房间。

整座宅子寂静无声。清凉的晨风拂过她发烫的双颊，她微微打了个寒噤：那风恍如幽灵的呼吸。

她快步奔下楼梯，用最快的速度拉开前门沉重的门闩，闪身出了门，来到街上。

城市已经开始苏醒。为了保卫岌岌可危的共和国，有那么多事要做，谁还顾得上睡觉。朱丽叶特转身朝河边走去时，迎面碰上了成群的工人——法兰西正征用他们来加强防务。

在她身后，卢森堡公园里，以及河对岸的沿岸一带，炉火已经熊熊燃起，铁匠们正在锻铸大炮。

===Original===
At every step now Juliette came across the great placards, pinned to the tall gallows-shaped posts, which proclaim to every passing citizen, that the people of France are up and in arms.

Right across the Place de l’Institut a procession of market carts, laden with vegetables and a little fruit, wends its way slowly towards the centre of the town. They each carry tiny tricolour flags, with a Pike and Cap of Liberty surmounting the flagstaff.

They are good patriots the market-gardeners, who come in daily to feed the starving mob of Paris, with the few handfuls of watery potatoes, and miserable, vermin-eaten cabbages, which that fraternal Revolution still allows them to grow without hindrance.

Everyone seems busy with their work this early in the morning: the business of killing does not begin until later in the day.

For the moment Juliette can get along quite unmolested: the women and children mostly hurrying on towards the vast encampments in the Tuileries, where lint, and bandages, and coats for the soldiers are manufactured all the day.

===Chinese===
此刻，朱丽叶特每走几步，就会遇见钉在绞架似的木柱上的大幅布告，向每一个过往的公民宣告：法兰西人民已经奋起，全民皆兵。

一长列菜农的货车正横穿法兰西学院广场（Place de l’Institut），满载着蔬菜和一点水果，缓缓向城中心行进。每辆车上都插着小小的三色旗，旗杆顶端饰有长矛和自由帽。

这些菜农都是善良的爱国者，他们每天进城，给巴黎挨饿的民众送来几把水汪汪的土豆和被虫蛀得残破不堪的可怜白菜——那场友爱的革命倒还容许他们不受阻挠地种这些东西。

这一大清早，人人都似乎在忙着手头的活计：杀人的营生，要到晚些时候才开始。

这一会儿，朱丽叶特还通行无阻：妇人和孩子们大多匆匆赶往杜伊勒里宫的广大营地，那里整天都在赶制军中的绒布、绷带和军大衣。

===Original===
The walls of all the houses bear the great patriotic device: “*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité, sinon La Mort*”; others are more political in their proclamation: “*La République une et indivisible.*”

But on the walls of the Louvre, of the great palace of whilom kings, where the Roi Soleil held his Court, and flirted with the prettiest women in France, there the new and great Republic has affixed its final mandate.

A great poster glued to the wall bears the words: “*La Loi concernan les Suspects.*” Below the poster is a huge wooden box with a slit at the top.

This is the latest invention for securing the safety of this one and indivisible Republic.

Henceforth everyone becomes a traitor at one word of denunciation from an idler or an enemy, and, as in the most tyrannical days of the Spanish Inquisition one-half of the nation was set to spy upon the other, that wooden box, with its slit, is put there ready to receive denunciations from one hand against another.

===Chinese===
家家户户的墙上都写着那句伟大的爱国格言：「*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité, sinon La Mort*」（自由、平等、博爱，否则就是死亡）；另一些墙上的标语则更具政治色彩：「*La République une et indivisible*」（统一而不可分割的共和国）。

而在卢浮宫的墙上——在那座昔日君王的巍峨宫殿里，「太阳王」曾在此设朝听政，与法兰西最美丽的女人调情——新生的伟大共和国贴上了它最后的政令。

一张大布告浆糊在墙上，上书：「*La Loi concernan les Suspects*」（关于嫌疑犯之法律）。布告下方放着一只巨大的木箱，箱顶开着一道投信口。

这便是为保障这个统一而不可分割的共和国之安全而设的最新发明。

从今以后，只要一个游手好闲者或仇家的一句话告发，任何人都可即刻沦为叛国者；正如西班牙宗教裁判所最专横的年月里，半个国家被派去窥伺另外半个国家那样，这只开着口的木箱就摆在那里，随时准备收受这只手告发那只手的密函。

===Original===
Had Juliette paused but for the fraction of a second, had she stopped to read the placard setting forth this odious law, had she only reflected, then she would even now have turned back, and fled from that gruesome box of infamies, as she would from a dangerous and noisome reptile or from the pestilence.

But her long vigil, her prayers, her ecstatic visions of heroic martyrs had now completely numbed her faculties. Her vitality, her sensibilities were gone: she had become an automaton gliding to her doom, without a thought or a tremor.

She drew the letter from her bosom, and with a steady hand dropped it into the box. The irreclaimable had now occurred. Nothing she could henceforth say or do, no prayers or agonised vigils, no miracles even, could undo her action or save Paul Déroulède from trial and guillotine.

One or two groups of people hurrying to their work had seen her drop the letter into the box. A couple of small children paused, finger in mouth, gazing at her with inane curiosity; one woman uttered a coarse jest, all of them shrugged their shoulders, and passed on, on their way. Those who habitually crossed this spot were used to such sights.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特哪怕只停留一瞬，哪怕停下脚步读一读那张阐明这条可憎法律的布告，哪怕只稍加思量，那么她此刻也会转身逃走，逃离那只藏纳种种罪恶勾当的可怕木箱，就像逃离一条危险而污秽的毒蛇，或一场瘟疫。

然而长夜的守祷、她的祈祷、她脑中那些英勇殉道者的迷醉幻象，此刻已把她的心智完全麻木了。她的生气、她的感受力都消失了：她已变成一具自动滑向自身劫数的木偶，没有一丝思虑，没有一分战栗。

她从怀中取出那封信，用一只沉稳的手把它投进了木箱。覆水难收的事已成定局。从此以后，任凭她说什么做什么，任凭她如何祈祷、如何彻夜痛苦地守祷，甚至任凭奇迹出现，都再不能挽回她的作为，也救不了保罗·德鲁莱德免受审判和断头台之刑。

有一两群赶着上工的人看见她把信投进了箱子。两个小孩子停住脚，手指含在嘴里，呆头呆脑地好奇打量着她；一个女人说了一句粗俗的俏皮话，众人耸耸肩膀，各自赶路去了。惯常经过此地的人，对这类景象早已司空见惯。

===Original===
That wooden box, with its mouthlike slit was like an insatiable monster that was constantly fed, yet was still gaping for more.

Having done the deed Juliette turned, and as rapidly as she had come, so she went back to her temporary home.

A home no more now; she must leave it at once, today if possible. This much she knew, that she no longer could touch the bread of the man she had betrayed. She would not appear at breakfast, she could plead a headache, and in the afternoon Pétronelle should pack her things.

She turned into a little shop close by, and asked for a glass of milk and a bit of bread. The woman who served her eyed her with some curiosity, for Juliette just now looked almost out of her mind.

She had not yet begun to think, and she had ceased to suffer.

Both would come presently, and with them the memory of this last irretrievable hour and a just estimate of what she had done.

===Chinese===
那只木箱，开着一道嘴似的投信口，活像一头贪得无厌的怪物，不停地被喂食，却仍旧大张着嘴索要更多。

事情做完，朱丽叶特转过身，来时多么急促，回去时也多么急促，奔回她那临时的家。

如今已不再是家了；她必须马上离开，最好就在今天。这一点她是明白的：她再不能去碰那个被她出卖的人的面包了。早饭她不会露面，可以推说头痛，下午就让佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）收拾她的行李。

她拐进附近一家小铺，要了一杯牛奶和一小块面包。伺候她的女人好奇地打量着她，因为此刻的朱丽叶特看上去简直像失了神智。

她还没有开始思索，而她已不再痛苦。

两者不久都会到来，随之而来的还有对这最后一个无可挽回的时辰的记忆，以及对她所作所为的清醒估量。

## XI "Vengeance Is Mine" / XI 伸冤在我

===Original===
The pretence of a headache enabled Juliette to keep in her room the greater part of the day. She would have liked to shut herself out from the entire world during those hours which she spent face to face with her own thoughts and her own sufferings.

The sight of Anne Mie's pathetic little face as she brought her food and delicacies and various little comforts, was positive torture to the poor, harrowed soul.

At every sound in the great, silent house she started up, quivering with apprehension and horror. Had the sword of Damocles, which she herself had suspended, already fallen over the heads of those who had shown her nothing but kindness?

She could not think of Madame Déroulède or of Anne Mie without the most agonising, the most torturing shame.

And what of him—the man she had so remorselessly, so ruthlessly betrayed to a tribunal which would know no mercy?

===Chinese===
借口头痛，朱丽叶特（Juliette）得以在房间里待了大半个白天。在那几个钟头里，她同自己的思想、自己的痛苦面对面相守，恨不得把自己同整个世界隔绝开来。

安娜·米（Anne Mie）那张惹人怜爱的小脸，端着饭菜、精美吃食和种种体贴的小物件进来时，对她那可怜的、备受煎熬的灵魂来说，简直是一种酷刑。

这座大宅子一片死寂，任何一点声响都会让她惊跳起来，惊惧与恐怖使她浑身发颤。那柄由她亲手悬起的达摩克利斯之剑，难道已经落在了那些对她只有善意的人的头上？

一想到德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）或安娜·米，她便会被最剧烈、最折磨人的羞愧攫住。

那么他呢——那个被她如此无情、如此冷酷地出卖给一个绝无仁慈可言的法庭的人，又如何了呢？

===Original===
Juliette dared not think of him.

She had never tried to analyse her feelings with regard to him. At the time of Charlotte Corday's trial, when his sonorous voice rang out in its pathetic appeal for the misguided woman, Juliette had given him ungrudging admiration. She remembered now how strongly his magnetic personality had roused in her a feeling of enthusiasm for the poor girl, who had come from the depths of her quiet provincial home, in order to accomplish the horrible deed which would immortalise her name through all the ages to come, and cause her countrymen to proclaim her "greater than Brutus."

Déroulède was pleading for the life of that woman, and it was his very appeal which had aroused Juliette's dormant energy, for the cause which her dead father had enjoined her not to forget. It was Déroulède again whom she had seen but a few weeks ago, standing alone before the mob who would have torn her to pieces, haranguing them on her behalf, speaking to them with that quiet, strong voice of his, ruling them with the rule of love and pity, and turning their wrath to gentleness.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特不敢想他。

她从未试图剖析自己对他的感情。夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）受审之时，他洪亮的声音响起，为那个误入歧途的女子作凄楚动人的辩护，朱丽叶特曾对他怀着毫不吝惜的敬慕。她如今还记得，他那种磁石般的人格曾多么强烈地在她心中唤起对那可怜姑娘的同情与热忱——那姑娘从她宁静的外省家宅深处走出来，去完成那桩可怕的行径，使自己的名字永垂万世，令国人称她「比布鲁图斯更伟大」。

德鲁莱德当时正为那个女人的性命申辩，而恰恰是他这番恳求，唤起了朱丽叶特沉睡的力量，使她记起亡父叮嘱她不可忘怀的使命。又是德鲁莱德，就在几个星期之前，她亲眼看见他孤身站在那群恨不得把她撕成碎片的暴民面前，代她向他们慷慨陈词，用他那平静而有力的声音对他们说话，以爱与怜悯统御他们，把他们的怒火化为温良。

===Original===
Did she hate him, then?

Surely, surely she hated him for having thrust himself into her life, for having caused her brother's death and covered her father's declining years with sorrow. And, above all, she hated him—indeed, indeed it was hate!—for being the cause of this most hideous action of her life: an action to which she had been driven against her will, one of basest ingratitude and treachery, foreign to every sentiment within her heart, cowardly, abject, the unconscious outcome of this strange magnetism which emanated from him and had cast a spell over her, transforming her individuality and will power, and making of her an unconscious and automatic instrument of Fate.

She would not speak of God's finger again: it was Fate—pagan, devilish Fate!—the weird, shrivelled women who sit and spin their interminable thread. They had decreed; and Juliette, unable to fight, blind and broken by the conflict, had succumbed to the Megaeras and their relentless wheel.

===Chinese===
那么，她恨他吗？

当然，当然她恨他：恨他闯进她的生活，恨他害死了她的兄长，恨他给她父亲风烛残年的岁月蒙上悲哀。而尤其是，她恨他——是的，是的，这就是恨！——因为他是她此生这桩最丑恶行径的根源：一桩违背她本心而被逼出来的行径，一桩忘恩负义、背信弃义到极点的行径，与她心中的一切情感都格格不入，怯懦而卑贱——那是从他身上散发出来的奇异磁力的无意识产物，那磁力对她施了魔咒，改造了她的个性与意志，把她变成一件无意识、机械听命的命运的器具。

她不愿再提上帝的手指：这是命运——异教的、魔鬼的命运！——是那几个枯槁诡异的老妇，坐在那里纺着永无穷尽的线。是她们作出了判决；而朱丽叶特无力抗争，被这场搏斗弄得目盲心碎，终于向墨盖拉（Megaera）们——复仇女神——和她们那无情转动的纺轮屈服了。

===Original===
At length silence and loneliness became unendurable. She called Pétronelle, and ordered her to pack her boxes.

"We leave for England today," she said curtly.

"For England?" gasped the worthy old soul, who was feeling very happy and comfortable in this hospitable house, and was loth to leave it. "So soon?"

"Why, yes; we had talked of it for some time. We cannot remain here always. My cousins De Crécy are there, and my aunt De Coudremont. We shall be among friends, Pétronelle, if we ever get there."

===Chinese===
寂静与孤独终于变得令人难以忍受。她唤来佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle），吩咐她收拾箱子。

「我们今天动身去英国。」她生硬地说道。

「去英国？」那位可敬的老妇人倒抽了一口气。她在这座好客的宅子里过得快活自在，很不愿离开。「这么快？」

「可不是嘛，这事我们商量了有些时候了。我们总不能一直待在这里。我的表亲德·克雷西（De Crécy）家在英国，还有我的姑母德·库德蒙（De Coudremont）。只要我们能到得了，佩特罗内勒，我们就会在朋友中间了。」

===Original===
"If we ever get there!" sighed poor Pétronelle; "we have but very little money, *ma chérie*, and no passports. Have you thought of asking M. Déroulède for them?"

"No, no," rejoined Juliette hastily; "I'll see to the passports somehow, Pétronelle. Sir Percy Blakeney is English; he'll tell me what to do."

"Do you know where he lives, my jewel?"

"Yes; I heard him tell Madame Déroulède last night that he was lodging with a provincial named Brogard at the Sign of the Cruche Cassée. I'll go seek him, Pétronelle; I am sure he will help me. The English are so resourceful and practical. He'll get us our passports, I know, and advise us as to the best way to proceed. Do you stay here and get all our things ready. I'll not be long."

===Chinese===
「要是能到得了的话！」可怜的佩特罗内勒叹道，「我们的钱少得可怜，*ma chérie*（我亲爱的），又没有护照。你想过向德鲁莱德先生去讨吗？」

「不，不，」朱丽叶特慌忙接口，「护照我自有办法，佩特罗内勒。珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）是英国人，他会告诉我该怎么办的。」

「你知道他住在哪儿吗，我的宝贝？」

「知道；昨天夜里我听见他对德鲁莱德夫人说，他正寄宿在一个名叫布罗加（Brogard）的外省人开的「破壶」客栈（Sign of the Cruche Cassée）里。我去找他，佩特罗内勒；我敢肯定他会帮我。英国人办法多，又讲实际。他会替我们弄到护照的，我知道，还会指点我们最好的走法。你留在这儿，把我们的东西都收拾好。我不会去很久的。」

===Original===
She took up a cloak and hood, and, throwing them over her arm, she slipped out of the room.

Déroulède had left the house earlier in the day. She hoped that he had not yet returned, and ran down the stairs quickly, so that she might go out unperceived.

The house was quite peaceful and still. It seemed strange to Juliette that there did not hang over it some sort of pall-like presentiment of coming evil.

===Chinese===
她拿起一件斗篷和一顶风帽，搭在臂上，悄悄溜出了房间。

德鲁莱德那天早些时候出了门。她盼着他还没有回来，便快步奔下楼梯，好不被人察觉地走出去。

宅子里十分安宁平静。朱丽叶特觉得奇怪，它的上空竟没有笼罩着一层丧幕似的、大祸将临的凶兆。

===Original===
From the kitchen, at some little distance from the hall, Anne Mie's voice was heard singing an old ditty:

> “De ta tige détachée
> Pauvre feuille désséchée
> Où vas-tu?”"

Juliette paused a moment. An awful ache had seized her heart; her eyes unconsciously filled with tears, as they roamed round the walls of this house which had sheltered her so hospitably, these three weeks past.

And now whither was she going? Like the poor, dead leaf of the song, she was wastrel, torn from the parent bough, homeless, friendless, having turned against the one hand which, in this great time of peril, had been extended to her in kindness and in love.

Conscience was beginning to rise up against her, and that hydra-headed tyrant Remorse. She closed her eyes to shut out the hideous vision of her crime; she tried to forget this home which her treachery had desecrated.

===Chinese===
从离大厅稍远的厨房那边，传来安娜·米哼唱一支古老小调的歌声：

> 从你的枝头飘落
> 可怜的枯叶啊
> 你飘向何方？

朱丽叶特停了片刻。一阵剧烈的疼痛攫住了她的心；她的目光环顾着这座宅子的四壁——三个星期以来，这里曾那样殷勤地庇护过她——不知不觉间泪水盈眶。

而如今，她又要飘向何处？正如歌里那片可怜的枯叶，她已成飘零的废物，从母枝上撕落，无家可归，无亲无友，还背弃了在这大难临头的时世里唯一向她善意与爱心伸出过的那只手。

良心开始起来反对她了，还有那九头蛇似的暴君——悔恨。她闭上眼睛，想挡开自己罪行的那幅狰狞图景；她竭力想忘掉这个被她的背叛亵渎了的家。

===Original===
> “Je vais où va toute chose
> Où va la feuille de rose
> Et la feuille de laurier,”

sang Anne Mie plaintively.

A great sob broke from Juliette's aching heart. The misery of it all was more than she could bear. Ah, pity her if you can! She had fought and striven, and been conquered. A girl's soul is so young, so impressionable; and she had grown up with that one, awful, all-pervading idea of duty to accomplish, a most solemn oath to fulfil, one sworn to her dying father, and on the dead body of her brother. She had begged for guidance, prayed for release, and the voice from above had remained silent. Weak, miserable, cringing, the human soul, when torn with earthly passion, must look at its own strength for the fight.

And now the end had come. That swift, scarce tangible dream of peace, which had flitted through her mind during the past few weeks, had vanished with the dawn, and she was left desolate, alone with her great sin and its lifelong expiation.

Scarce knowing what she did, she fell on her knees, there on that threshold, which she was about to leave forever. Fate had placed on her young shoulders a burden too heavy for her to bear.

===Chinese===
> 我去万物同去的地方
> 玫瑰的叶子去的地方
> 还有月桂的叶子

安娜·米哀哀地唱道。

一声巨大的呜咽从朱丽叶特痛楚的心中迸出。这一切的悲惨，她再也承受不住了。啊，倘若你能够，就可怜可怜她吧！她挣扎过，奋斗过，终于被征服了。一个少女的灵魂那样年轻，那样易受感染；而她是伴着那唯一可怕又无处不在的念头长大的：去完成一项责任，去履行一个最庄严的誓言——对着弥留的父亲、对着兄长的遗体立下的誓言。她曾乞求指引，祈祷解脱，而来自上天的声音始终沉默。人的灵魂被尘世的激情撕裂时，软弱、悲惨、瑟缩，只能靠自身的力量去应战。

而现在，结局来了。那掠过她心头几个星期的、轻快得几乎捉摸不住的安宁之梦，已随黎明消散，只留下她孤零零的一个人，陪着她的弥天大罪和那赎不完的一生。

她几乎不知道自己在做什么，便跪倒下去，就跪在那道她即将永远离别的门槛上。命运放在她年轻肩头的担子，实在太重，她挑不动了。

===Original===
"Juliette!"

At first she did not move. It was his voice coming from the study behind her. Its magic thrilled her, as it had done that day in the Hall of Justice. Strong, passionate, tender, it seemed now to raise every echo of response in her heart. She thought it was a dream, and remained there on her knees lest it should be dispelled.

Then she heard his footsteps on the flagstones of the hall. Anne Mie's plaintive singing had died away in the distance. She started, and jumped to her feet, hastily drying her eyes. The momentary dream was dispelled, and she was ashamed of her weakness.

He, the cause of all her sorrows, of her sin, and of her degradation, had no right to see her suffer.

===Chinese===
「朱丽叶特！」

起初她一动不动。是他的声音，从她身后的书房里传来。那声音的魔力使她周身震颤，正如那天在法庭大厅里一样。强健、热烈、温柔，此刻它仿佛唤起了她心中每一声回应的共鸣。她以为这是梦，便仍旧跪在那里，唯恐梦被惊散。

接着她听见他的脚步声踏在大厅的石板上。安娜·米哀怨的歌声已在远处消逝。她一惊，跳起身来，慌忙拭干眼泪。那片刻的梦惊散了，她为自己的软弱感到羞愧。

他——她一切悲哀、她的罪、她堕落的根源——无权看见她受苦。

===Original===
She would have fled out of the house now, but it was too late. He had come out of his study, and, seeing her there on her knees weeping, he came quickly forward, trying, with all the innate chivalry of his upright nature, not to let her see that he had been a witness to her tears.

"You are going out, mademoiselle?" he said courteously, as, wrapping her cloak around her, she was turning towards the door.

"Yes, yes," she replied hastily; "a small errand, I—"

===Chinese===
这时她本想夺门逃出宅去，可是已经太迟。他已走出书房，看见她跪在那里哭泣，便快步上前，凭着他正直天性中全部与生俱来的骑士气度，尽量不让她看出自己已目睹了她的眼泪。

「您要出门吗，小姐？」他彬彬有礼地问。她正裹上斗篷，转身朝门口走去。

「是啊，是啊，」她慌忙答道，「办点小事，我——」

===Original===
"Is it anything I can do for you?"

"No."

"If—" he added, with visible embarrassment, "if your errand would brook a delay, might I crave the honour of your presence in my study for a few moments?"

"My errand brooks of no delay, Citizen Déroulède," she said as composedly as she could, "and perhaps on my return I might—"

===Chinese===
「有什么我能替您效劳的吗？」

「没有。」

「倘若——」他补充道，显然有些局促，「倘若您那件小事耽搁得起，能否容我冒昧，请您到书房里稍坐片刻？」

「我的事耽搁不得，德鲁莱德公民，」她竭力镇定地说，「也许等我回来的时候——」

===Original===
"I am leaving almost directly, mademoiselle, and I would wish to bid you goodbye."

He stood aside to allow her to pass, either out, through the street door or across the hall to his study.

There had been no reproach in his voice towards the guest, who was thus leaving him without a word of farewell. Perhaps if there had been any, Juliette would have rebelled. As it was, an unconquerable magnetism seemed to draw her towards him, and, making an almost imperceptible sign of acquiescence, she glided past him into his room.

===Chinese===
「我差不多马上就要动身了，小姐，我想向您道别。」

他站到一旁让她过去——要出门，走临街的大门也行；要去书房，穿过大厅也行。

他的语气里没有一句责备——对这位连一句道别的话都不留就要离他而去的客人。倘若有一句，朱丽叶特或许反倒会抗拒。而事已如此，一种无法抗拒的磁力似乎把她引向他，她做了一个几乎察觉不出的、默认的示意，从他身旁轻轻滑进了他的房间。

===Original===
The study was dark and cool; for the room faced the west, and the shutters had been closed, in order to keep out the hot August sun. At first Juliette could see nothing, but she felt his presence near her, as he followed her into the room, leaving the door slightly ajar.

"It is kind of you, mademoiselle," he said gently, "to accede to my request, which was perhaps presumptuous. But, you see, I am leaving this house today, and I had a selfish longing to hear your voice bidding me farewell."

Juliette's large, burning eyes were gradually piercing the semi-gloom around her. She could see him distinctly now, standing close beside her, in an attitude of the deepest, almost reverential respect.

The study was as usual neat and tidy, denoting the orderly habits of a man of action and energy. On the ground there was a valise, ready strapped as if for a journey, and on the top of it a bulky letter-case of stout pigskin, secured with a small steel lock. Juliette's eyes fastened upon this case with a look of fascination and of horror. Obviously it contained Déroulède's papers, the plans for Marie Antoinette's escape, the passports of which he had spoken the day before to his friend, Sir Percy Blakeney—the proofs, in fact, which she had offered to the representatives of the people, in support of her denunciation of the Citizen-Deputy.

===Chinese===
书房里又暗又凉；这间屋子朝西，为挡住八月灼热的太阳，百叶窗已经关上。起初朱丽叶特什么也看不见，但她感觉到他就在自己身旁，他跟着她进了屋，把门虚掩着。

「您真是心善，小姐，」他温和地说，「答应了我这个或许有些冒昧的请求。可是您瞧，我今天就要离开这座宅子了，我私心盼着能听见您亲口向我道一声别。」

朱丽叶特那双灼热的大眼睛渐渐刺透了周围的半明半暗。她现在能清楚地看见他了：他站在她近旁，那姿态里透着最深的、近乎虔敬的敬意。

书房同往常一样整洁利落，显出这个精力充沛、雷厉风行的人有条不紊的习惯。地上放着一只旅行皮箱，搭扣都已扣好，像是要出门的样子；箱顶上搁着一只厚猪皮的大信匣，用一把小小的钢锁锁着。朱丽叶特的目光死死盯住那只信匣，神情里既有痴迷又有恐惧。显然，里面装着德鲁莱德的文件，玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）出逃的计划，还有他前一天对友人珀西·布莱克尼爵士说起的那些护照——说到底，正是她呈交给人民代表们、用以支持她告发这位公会议员的那些罪证。

===Original===
After his request he had said nothing more. He was waiting for her to speak; but her voice felt parched; it seemed to her as if hands of steel were gripping her throat, smothering the words she would have longed to speak.

"Will you not wish me godspeed, mademoiselle?" he repeated gently.

"Godspeed?" Oh! the awful irony of it all! Should God speed him to a mock trial and to the guillotine? He was going thither, though he did not know it, and was even now trying to take the hand which had deliberately sent him there.

===Chinese===
提出请求之后，他便再没有说什么。他在等她开口；可她的嗓子干得发烫，她只觉得仿佛有钢铸的手扼住了她的喉咙，把她渴望说出的字句扼死在喉头。

「您不愿意祝我一帆风顺吗，小姐？」他又温和地问了一遍。

「一帆风顺？」啊！这一切是何等可怕的嘲弄！上帝岂能保佑他走向一场假审讯，走向断头台？他正是往那里去，只是他自己不知道，而此刻竟还想握住那只存心把他送往那里的手。

===Original===
At last she made an effort to speak, and in a toneless, even voice she contrived to murmur:

"You are not going for long, Citizen-Deputy?"

"In these times, mademoiselle," he replied, "any farewell might be forever. But I am actually going for a month to the Conciergerie, to take charge of the unfortunate prisoner there."

===Chinese===
终于，她努力开口，用一种平板而毫无起伏的声音勉强低语道：

「您不会去很久吧，公会议员？」

「在如今这年月，小姐，」他答道，「任何一次道别都可能是永诀。不过我实际是去巴黎古监狱（Conciergerie）一个月，接管关押在那里的那名不幸的囚犯。」

===Original===
"For a month!" she repeated mechanically.

"Oh yes!" he said, with a smile. "You see, our present Government is afraid that poor Marie Antoinette will exercise her fascinations over any lieutenant-governor of her prison, if he remain near her long enough, so a new one is appointed every month. I shall be in charge during this coming Vendémiaire. I shall hope to return before the equinox, but—who can tell?"

"In any case then, Citoyen Déroulède, the farewell I bid you tonight will be a very long one."

===Chinese===
「一个月！」她机械地重复道。

「是啊！」他微笑着说，「您想，我们如今的政府担心，可怜的玛丽·安托瓦内特若能让哪个副典狱长在她身边待得够久，便会对她施展魅力，所以每个月都要委派一个新的人。即将到来的这个葡月（Vendémiaire）由我负责。我盼着秋分之前回来，可是——谁能说得准呢？」

「那么无论如何，德鲁莱德公民（Citoyen Déroulède），今晚我向您道别的这一次，就会是一次很长很长的告别了。」

===Original===
"A month will seem a century to me," he said earnestly, "since I must spend it without seeing you, but—"

He looked long and searchingly at her. He did not understand her in her present mood, so scared and wild did she seem, so unlike that girlish, lighthearted self, which had made the dull old house so bright these past few weeks.

"But I should not dare to hope," he murmured, "that a similar reason would cause you to call that month a long one."

===Chinese===
「一个月于我将如一个世纪，」他恳切地说，「因为这一个月我见不到您，可是——」

他久久地、探询似的望着她。他不理解她此刻的心绪：她看上去那样惊惶、那样狂乱，同这几个星期里使这座沉闷老宅变得如此明快的那个少女情怀、无忧无虑的她，判若两人。

「可是我不敢奢望，」他低声说，「您也会出于同样的缘故，把那一个月叫作漫长。」

===Original===
She turned perhaps a trifle paler than she had been hitherto, and her eyes roamed round the room like those of a trapped hare seeking to escape.

"You misunderstand me, Citoyen Déroulède," she said at last hurriedly. "You have all been kind—very kind—but Pétronelle and I can no longer trespass on your hospitality. We have friends in England, and many enemies here—"

"I know," he interrupted quietly; "it would be the most arrant selfishness on my part to suggest, that you should stay here an hour longer than necessary. I fear that after today my roof may no longer prove a sheltering one for you. But will you allow me to arrange for your safety, as I am arranging for that of my mother and Anne Mie? My English friend Sir Percy Blakeney, has a yacht in readiness off the Normandy coast. I have already seen to your passports and to all the arrangements of your journey as far as there, and Sir Percy, or one of his friends, will see you safely on board the English yacht. He has given me his promise that he will do this, and I trust him as I would myself. For the journey through France, my name is a sufficient guarantee that you will be unmolested; and if you will allow it, my mother and Anne Mie will travel in your company. Then—"

===Chinese===
她的脸色也许比先前更白了一分，双眼像一头被擒的野兔那样在屋里乱转，寻找着逃路。

「您误会我了，德鲁莱德公民，」她终于慌忙说道。「你们待我都很好——非常好——可是佩特罗内勒和我不能再叨扰您的款待了。我们在英国有朋友，在这里却有许多仇敌——」

「我知道，」他平静地打断了她，「要是我提议您在这里多待上一个钟头，超过必要的时间，那便是我最卑劣的自私了。我担心从今天起，我这屋顶也许就护不住您了。可您肯让我来安排您的安全，如同我安排我母亲和安娜·米的安全那样吗？我的英国朋友珀西·布莱克尼爵士有一条游艇，已在诺曼底海岸外整装待发。您的护照以及到那里为止行程的一切安排，我都已办妥，珀西爵士或他的某位朋友会护送你们平安登上那艘英国游艇。他已向我许下诺言，我对他像对我自己一样信任。至于穿越法兰西的一路，我的名字就足以担保你们不受滋扰；倘若您愿意，我母亲和安娜·米还可以与你们同行。然后——」

===Original===
"I pray you stop, Citizen Déroulède," she suddenly interrupted excitedly. "You must forgive me, but I cannot allow thus to make any arrangements for me. Pétronelle and I must do as best we can. All your time and trouble should be spent for the benefit of those who have a claim upon you, whilst I—"

"You speak unkindly, mademoiselle; there is no question of claim."

"And you have no right to think—" she continued, with a growing, nervous excitement, drawing her hand hurriedly away, for he had tried to seize it.

===Chinese===
「求您别说了，德鲁莱德公民，」她突然激动地打断了他。「您必须原谅我，可我不能容许您这样替我作任何安排。佩特罗内勒和我只能尽力而为。您的时间和辛劳应该全部用来照应那些有权要求于您的人，而我——」

「您这话太不客气了，小姐；这里根本谈不上什么权利。」

「而您更没有权利想——」她继续说道，神经质的激动有增无减，一面慌忙抽回自己的手，因为他想把它握住。

===Original===
"Ah! pardon me," he interrupted earnestly, "there you are wrong. I have the right to think of you and for you—the inalienable right conferred upon me by my great love for you."

"Citizen-Deputy!"

"Nay, Juliette; I know my folly, and I know my presumption. I know the pride of your caste and of your party, and how much you despise the partisan of the squalid mob of France. Have I said that I aspired to gain your love? I wonder if I have ever dreamed it? I only know, Juliette, that you are to me something akin to the angels, something white and ethereal, intangible, and perhaps ununderstandable. Yet, knowing my folly, I glory in it, my dear, and I would not let you go out of my life without telling you of that, which has made every hour of the past few weeks a paradise for me—my love for you, Juliette."

===Chinese===
「啊！请原谅，」他诚恳地打断了她，「这一点您错了。我有权利想您、为您着想——这是我对您伟大的爱情赋予我的、不可剥夺的权利。」

「公会议员！」

「不，朱丽叶特；我知道自己的愚蠢，也知道自己的僭妄。我知道您那个阶层、您那个党派的骄傲，知道您多么看不起法兰西肮脏民众的一个党羽。可我说过我妄图赢得您的爱情吗？我也不知道自己可曾梦见过？我只知道，朱丽叶特，您之于我，如同天使一般，洁白而空灵，无从触及，或许也无从理解。然而，明知自己愚蠢，我却以此为荣，我亲爱的；我不愿让您走出我的生活，却不告诉您那件事——正是它使过去这几个星期的每个时辰于我都成了天堂——那就是我对您的爱，朱丽叶特。」

===Original===
He spoke in that low, impressive voice of his, and with those soft, appealing tones with which she had once heard him pleading for poor Charlotte Corday. Yet now he was not pleading for himself, not for his selfish wish or for his own happiness, only pleading for his love, that she should know of it, and, knowing it, have pity in her heart for him, and let him serve her to the end.

He did not say anything more for a while; he had taken her hand, which she no longer withdrew from him, for there was sweet pleasure in feeling his strong fingers close tremblingly over hers. He pressed his lips upon her hand, upon the soft palm and delicate wrist, his burning kisses bearing witness to the tumultuous passion, which his reverence for her was holding in check.

She tried to tear herself away from him, but he would not let her go:

===Chinese===
他用他那低沉动人的声音说着，带着那种柔和的、恳求的语调——她曾听见他用这种语调为可怜的夏洛特·科黛辩护。然而此刻他不是为自己恳求，不是为他自私的心愿或他个人的幸福，只是为他的爱情恳求：愿她知道这份爱，知道了，心中便对他存一分怜惜，便容他把她服侍到底。

他好一阵没有再说话；他已握住她的手，而她也不再抽回了，因为感觉到他有力的手指颤巍巍地合拢在自己手上，自有一种甜蜜的欢愉。他把嘴唇压在她的手上，压在那柔软的掌心和纤细的腕上，他灼热的吻见证着那沸腾的激情——他对她的崇敬正把这激情约束着。

她想挣脱他，他却不肯放她走：

===Original===
"Do not go away just yet, Juliette," he pleaded. "Think! I may never see you again; but when you are far from me—in England, perhaps—amongst your own kith and kin, will you try sometimes to think kindly of one who so wildly, so madly worships you?"

She would have stilled, an she could, the beating of her heart, which went out to him at last with all the passionate intensity of her great, pent-up love. Every word he spoke had its echo within her very soul, and she tried not to hear his tender appeal, not to see his dark head bending in worship before her. She tried to forget his presence, not to know that he was there—he, the man whom she had betrayed to serve her own miserable vengeance, whom in her mad, exalted rage she had thought that she hated, but whom she now knew that she loved better than her life, better than her soul, her traditions, or her oath.

Now, at this moment, she made every effort to conjure up the vision of her brother brought home dead upon a stretcher, of her father's declining years, rendered hideous by the mind unhinged through the great sorrow.

===Chinese===
「先别走，朱丽叶特，」他恳求道。「想一想！我也许再也见不到您了；可是当您远离了我——也许在英国——在您自己的亲族中间，您会不会偶尔也试着，善意地想一想这个如此狂热、如此痴癫地崇拜着您的人？」

她本想按住自己心跳——若能按得住的话——因为那颗心终于带着她那被压抑已久的伟大爱情的全部炽烈，向他奔涌而去。他说的每一个字都在她的灵魂深处激起了回响，她竭力不去听他温柔的恳求，不去看他那颗黑发低垂的头颅在她面前虔诚俯拜。她竭力忘掉他在眼前，竭力不承认他就在那里——他，那个被她出卖、以遂她那可悲的复仇的人，那个在疯狂迷乱的怒火中她自以为恨着、而如今她知道自己爱着的人——爱得胜过生命，胜过灵魂，胜过她的门第传统，胜过她的誓言。

此时此刻，她竭力要在眼前唤起那幅景象：兄长的遗体放在担架上被抬回家门，父亲风烛残年的岁月因巨大的悲痛而神智失常，变得狰狞可怕。

===Original===
She tried to think of the avenging finger of God pointing the way to the fulfilment of her oath, and called to Him to stand by her in this terrible agony of her soul.

And God spoke to her at last; through the eternal vistas of boundless universe, from that heaven which had known no pity, His voice came to her now, clear, awesome, and implacable:

"Vengeance is mine! I will repay!"

===Chinese===
她竭力去想上帝那只复仇的手指，正为她指出履行誓言的道路；她呼唤上帝，求他在她灵魂这场可怕的酷刑中站在她一边。

上帝终于对她说话了；穿过无边宇宙那永恒的深处，从那从不知怜悯为何物的天穹，他的声音此刻传到她耳中，清晰、可畏、绝不容情：

「伸冤在我，我必报应！」

## XII The Sword of Damocles / XII 达摩克利斯之剑

===Original===

“In the name of the Republic!”

Absorbed in his thoughts, his dreams, his present happiness, Déroulède had heard nothing of what was going on in the house, during the past few seconds.

At first, to Anne Mie, who was still singing her melancholy ditty over her work in the kitchen, there had seemed nothing unusual in the peremptory ring at the front-door bell. She pulled down her sleeves over her thin arms, smoothed down her cooking apron, then only did she run to see who the visitor might be.

As soon as she had opened the door, however, she understood.

===Chinese===

「以共和国之名！」

保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）正沉醉于自己的思虑、自己的梦想、自己眼前的幸福之中，方才这几秒钟里宅子里发生的事，他一概未曾听见。

起初，对安娜·米（Anne Mie）来说——她还在厨房里边做活儿边哼着那支忧郁的小曲——前门铃那阵疾促催人的铃声似乎并无异样。她把瘦瘦的胳膊拢进袖口，抹平了做饭的围裙，这才奔过去看来客究竟是谁。

可是门一打开，她顿时明白了。

===Original===

Five men were standing before her, four of whom wore the uniform of the National Guard, and the fifth, the tricolour scarf fringed with gold, which denoted service under the Convention.

This man seemed to be in command of the others, and he immediately stepped into the hall, followed by his four companions, who at a sign from him, effectively cut off Anne Mie from what had been her imminent purpose﻿—namely, to run to the study and warn Déroulède of his danger.

That it was danger of the most certain, the most deadly kind she never doubted for one moment. Even had her instinct not warned her, she would have guessed. One glance at the five men had sufficed to tell her: their attitude, their curt word of command, their air of authority as they crossed the hall﻿—everything revealed the purpose of their visit: a domiciliary search in the house of Citizen-Deputy Déroulède.

Merlin’s Law of the Suspect was in full operation. Someone had denounced the Citizen-Deputy to the Committee of Public Safety; and in this year of grace, 1793, and I of the Revolution, men and women were daily sent to the guillotine on suspicion.

Anne Mie would have screamed, had she dared, but instinct such as hers was far too keen, to betray her into so injudicious an act. She felt that, were Paul Déroulède’s eyes upon her at this moment, he would wish her to remain calm and outwardly serene.

===Chinese===

五个男人站在她面前，四个穿着国民自卫军（National Guard）的军装，第五个佩着镶金边的三色绶带——那是供职于国民公会（National Convention）的标志。

为首的这人似乎是其余几个的指挥，他随即跨进门厅，四名手下紧随其后；他一使眼色，他们便把安娜·米的去路封得死死的——她本已打定主意，要奔进书房，向德鲁莱德报信示警。

这是最确凿、也是最致命的一种凶险——这一点，她片刻也不曾怀疑。即便没有直觉示警，她也能猜到。这五个男人，只消看上一眼便足够了：他们的姿态、他们简短的口令、他们穿过门厅时那副颐指气使的神气——一切都显明了他们此行的目的：入宅搜查公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）德鲁莱德的宅邸。

梅尔兰（Merlin）的《嫌疑犯法令》正大行其道。有人已把这位公会议员告到救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）；而在这所谓的恩典之年——1793，亦即革命纪元元年——男男女女每天只凭一点嫌疑，就被送上断头台。

安娜·米若敢，本会失声尖叫；可她那样的直觉太锐敏了，断不容她做出这般失检的举动。她明白，此刻保罗·德鲁莱德的目光若正落在她身上，他定要她保持镇定，外表安然如常。

===Original===

The foremost man﻿—he with the tricolour scarf﻿—had already crossed the hall, and was standing outside the study door. It was his word of command which first roused Déroulède from his dream:

“In the name of the Republic!”

Déroulède did not immediately drop the small hand, which a moment ago he had been covering with kisses. He held it to his lips once more, very gently, lingering over this last fond caress, as if over an eternal farewell, then he straightened out his broad, well-knit figure, and turned to the door.

He was very pale, but there was neither fear nor even surprise expressed in his earnest, deep-set eyes. They still seemed to be looking afar, gazing upon a heaven-born vision, which the touch of her hand and the avowal of his love had conjured up before him.

“In the name of the Republic!”

Once more, for the third time﻿—according to custom﻿—the words rang out, clear, distinct, peremptory.

===Chinese===

走在最前面的那人——正是佩着三色绶带的那一位——已经穿过门厅，站在书房门外了。正是他的一声口令，头一回把德鲁莱德从梦境中唤醒：

「以共和国之名！」

德鲁莱德没有立刻放下那只纤小的手——片刻之前，他还把它捧在唇边印满热吻。他再一次把它送到唇上，极其轻柔，在这最后一番爱抚上流连不舍，仿佛那是永诀的缠绵；然后他挺直那宽厚结实的身躯，转向门口。

他脸色十分苍白，可那双深邃的、目光恳挚的眼睛里，既没有恐惧，甚至连惊讶也没有。那目光似乎仍在望着远方，凝视着一幅天降的幻象——她手的轻触和他爱的自白，方才在他面前唤起的那幅幻象。

「以共和国之名！」

那句话又一次响起——照例，这已是第三遍——清晰、分明、不容违抗。

===Original===

In that one fraction of a second, whilst those six words were spoken, Déroulède’s eyes wandered swiftly towards the heavy letter-case, which now held his condemnation, and a wild, mad thought﻿—the mere animal desire to escape from danger﻿—surged up in his brain.

The plans for the escape of Marie Antoinette, the various passports, worded in accordance with the possible disguises the unfortunate Queen might assume﻿—all these papers were more than sufficient proof of what would be termed his treason against the Republic.

He could already hear the indictment against him, could see the filthy mob of Paris dancing a wild saraband round the tumbril, which bore him towards the guillotine; he could hear their yells of execration, could feel the insults hurled against him, by those who had most admired, most envied him. And from all this he would have escaped if he could, if it had not been too late.

It was but a second, or less, whilst the words were spoken outside his door, and whilst all other thoughts in him were absorbed in this one mad desire for escape. He even made a movement, as if to snatch up the letter-case and to hide it about his person. But it was heavy and bulky; it would be sure to attract attention, and might bring upon him the additional indignity of being forced to submit to a personal search.

He caught Juliette’s eyes fixed upon him with an intensity of gaze which, in that same one mad moment, revealed to him the depths of her love. Then the second’s weakness was gone; he was once more quiet, firm, the man of action, accustomed to meet danger boldly, to rule and to subdue the most turgid mob.

With a quiet shrug of the shoulders, he dismissed all thought of the compromising letter-case, and went to the door.

===Chinese===

就在那六个字吐出的一瞬间，德鲁莱德的目光飞快地掠向那只沉甸甸的信匣——此刻它装着的，正是给他定罪的东西；一股狂乱痴癫的念头——那不过是想逃离危险的兽性本能——在他脑中翻腾起来。

营救玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）的计划，那一张张护照——措辞都按那不幸的王后可能乔装的种种身份拟定——所有这些文件，尽足以坐实人们将加给他的罪名：背叛共和国。

他仿佛已经听见了对自己的控诉状，已经看见巴黎龌龊的暴民围着那辆死囚车（tumbril）跳起狂野的萨拉班德舞——车正载着他驶向断头台；他听见他们咒骂的狂吼，感到那些曾最仰慕他、最嫉妒他的人劈头掷来的侮辱。这一切，若不是为时已晚，他本会逃之夭夭的。

前后不过一秒钟，甚至更短——门外的话音还在吐着，他其余的一切思虑，已尽数被这一个疯狂的求生欲念吞没。他甚至动了一动，仿佛要把信匣抢到手里，藏在身上。可它又沉又大，准会惹人注目，还可能给他招来额外的羞辱——被迫听任搜身。

他触到朱丽叶特（Juliette）的目光正死死盯着他，那凝视的专注，就在同一个疯狂的瞬间，向他袒露了她爱的深度。于是那短短一秒的软弱消散了；他重又变得沉静、坚毅，又成了那个行动的人——那个惯于直面危险、驾驭并慑服最汹涌暴民的人。

他轻轻一耸肩，把那只招灾惹祸的信匣从心头撇开，向门口走去。

===Original===

Already, as no reply had come to the third word of command, it had been thrown open from outside, and Déroulède found himself face to face with the five men.

“Citizen Merlin!” he said quietly, as he recognised the foremost among them.

“Himself, Citizen-Deputy,” rejoined the latter, with a sneer, “at your service.”

Anne Mie, in a remote corner of the hall, had heard the name, and felt her very soul sicken at its sound.

Merlin! Author of that infamous Law of the Suspect which had set man against man, a father against his son, brother against brother, and friend against friend, had made of every human creature a bloodhound on the track of his fellow-men, dogging in order not to be dogged, denouncing, spying, hounding, in order not to be denounced.

And he, Merlin, gloried in this, the most fiendishly evil law ever perpetrated for the degradation of the human race.

===Chinese===

其实，第三声口令没有等到应答，门已经从外面给推开了，德鲁莱德发觉自己正同五个人面对面站着。

「梅尔兰公民！」他平静地说，认出了走在最前面的那一位。

「正是本人，公会议员，」后者讥诮地应道，「愿为您效劳。」

安娜·米待在门厅一个僻远的角落里，听见了这个名字，只觉得整个灵魂都为之作呕。

梅尔兰！那部臭名昭著的《嫌疑犯法令》的炮制者——它使人人与人相攻，父与子相对，兄弟与兄弟相争，朋友与朋友相噬；它把每一个人都变成追踪同胞的猎犬，追踪别人，为的是自己不被追踪；告发、窥伺、撕咬别人，为的是自己不被告发。

而他梅尔兰，正以这条有史以来专为败坏人类而设的最穷凶极恶的法律自豪。

===Original===

There is that sketch of him in the Musée Carnavalet, drawn just before he, in his turn, went to expiate his crimes on that very guillotine, which he had sharpened and wielded so powerfully against his fellows. The artist has well caught the slouchy, slovenly look of his loosely knit figure, his long limbs and narrow head, with the snakelike eyes and slightly receding chin. Like Marat, his model and prototype, Merlin affected dirty, ragged clothes. The real Sanscullottism, the downward levelling of his fellow-men to the lowest rung of the social ladder, pervaded every action of this noted product of the great Revolution.

Even Déroulède, whose entire soul was filled with a great, all-understanding pity for the weaknesses of mankind, recoiled at sight of this incarnation of the spirit of squalor and degradation, of all that was left of the noble Utopian theories of the makers of the Revolution.

Merlin grinned when he saw Déroulède standing there, calm, impassive, well dressed, as if prepared to receive an honoured guest, rather than a summons to submit to the greatest indignity a proud man has ever been called upon to suffer.

===Chinese===

卡纳瓦莱博物馆（Musée Carnavalet）里存有一幅他的速写，画成于他自己轮到头上去那同一座断头台赎罪之前不久——那座他曾亲手磨利、又如此威风地挥舞来对付同胞的断头台。画家把他的神态捕捉得恰到好处：松垮邋遢的身形，长长的四肢，窄窄的脑袋，一双蛇一样的眼睛，还有微微后缩的下巴。同他的榜样与原型马拉（Marat）一样，梅尔兰也爱穿肮脏褴褛的衣裳。真正的无套裤汉主义——把同胞往下拉平、直拉到社会阶梯最低一级——贯串着这位大革命著名产物的每一个举动。

连德鲁莱德——他的整个灵魂都满盛着对人类弱点的深广怜悯——一见这个卑污与堕落的化身，一见革命缔造者们那崇高乌托邦理论所剩下的一点残滓，也不禁退缩了。

眼见德鲁莱德站在那里，安详、不动声色、衣冠楚楚，仿佛准备接待一位贵宾，而不是奉召来承受一个骄傲的人从未被迫受过的最大屈辱，梅尔兰咧开嘴笑了。

===Original===

Merlin had always hated the popular Citizen-Deputy. Friend and boon-companion of Marat and his gang, he had for over two years now exerted all the influence he possessed in order to bring Déroulède under a cloud of suspicion.

But Déroulède had the ear of the populace. No one understood as he did the tone of a Paris mob; and the National Convention, ever terrified of the volcano it had kindled, felt that a popular member of its assembly was more useful alive than dead.

But now at last Merlin was having his way. An anonymous denunciation against Déroulède had reached the Public Prosecutor that day. Tinville and Merlin were the fastest of friends, so the latter easily obtained the privilege of being the first to proclaim to his hated enemy, the news of his downfall.

He stood facing Déroulède for a moment, enjoying the present situation to its full. The light from the vast hall struck full upon the powerful figure of the Citizen-Deputy and upon his firm, dark face and magnetic, restless eyes. Behind him the study, with its closely-drawn shutters, appeared wrapped in gloom.

===Chinese===

梅尔兰一向憎恨这位深得民心的公会议员。作为马拉及其一伙的朋友和酒伴，两年多来他一直在施展全部的影响，要把德鲁莱德压进一片嫌疑的阴云。

可民众听得进德鲁莱德的话。巴黎暴民的声口，没有谁像他体会得那样透；而国民公会时刻害怕自己点燃的火山，觉得公会里这位得人心的议员，活着总比死了有用。

但这一回，梅尔兰终于遂了心愿。一纸控告德鲁莱德的匿名状子，当天就送到了公诉人（Public Prosecutor）手里。坦维尔（Tinville）同梅尔兰是过从最密的朋友，于是后者轻易讨得了那份特权：头一个把他痛恨的仇人垮台的消息宣告给他听。

他面对德鲁莱德站了片刻，把眼前的局面尽情受用。门厅的大片亮光，正打在公会议员那魁伟的身躯上，打在他刚毅的深色面孔和那双摄人心魄、焦灼不定的眼睛上。他身后，书房的百叶窗紧闭着，隐在一片昏暗里。

===Original===

Merlin turned to his men, and, still delighted with his position of a cat playing with a mouse, he pointed to Déroulède, with a smile and a shrug of the shoulders.

“*Voyez-moi donc çà*,” he said, with a coarse jest, and expectorating contemptuously upon the floor, “the aristocrat seems not to understand that we are here in the name of the Republic. There is a very good proverb, Citizen-Deputy,” he added, once more addressing Déroulède, “which you seem to have forgotten, and that is that the pitcher which goes too often to the well breaks at last. You have conspired against the liberties of the people for the past ten years. Retribution has come to you at last; the people of France have come to their senses. The National Convention wants to know what treason you are hatching between these four walls, and it has deputed me to find out all there is to know.”

“At your service, Citizen-Deputy!” said Déroulède, quietly stepping aside, in order to make way for Merlin and his men.

Resistance was useless, and, like all strong, determined natures, he knew when it was best to give in.

===Chinese===

梅尔兰转向手下人，依旧陶醉在猫耍老鼠的地位里，含着笑，一耸肩，指向德鲁莱德。

「*Voyez-moi donc çà*（瞧瞧这位爷吧），」他插了句粗鄙的玩笑，轻蔑地朝地上啐了一口，「这位贵族老爷似乎不明白，我们是奉共和国之名来这儿的。有一句很好的谚语，公会议员，」他重新转向德鲁莱德说，「您怕是忘了：常往井边去的瓦罐，终有打破的一天。十年来，您一直阴谋反抗人民的自由。报应终于落到了您头上；法兰西人民已经醒悟过来了。国民公会想知道，您在这四面墙中间正孵着什么叛国的鬼胎；公会派了我来，要把查得明的统统查明。」

「愿为您效劳，公会议员！」德鲁莱德说着，悄然让向一旁，给梅尔兰一伙让出路来。

抗拒是无用的；而且，如同一切刚强果决的天性，他知道什么时候最宜于低头。

===Original===

During this while, Juliette had neither moved nor uttered a sound. Little more than a minute had elapsed since the moment when the first peremptory order, to open in the name of the Republic, had sounded like the tocsin through the stillness of the house. Déroulède’s kisses were still hot upon her hand, his words of love were still ringing in her ears.

And now this awful, deadly peril, which she with her own hand had brought on the man she loved!

If in one moment’s anguish the soul be allowed to expiate a lifelong sin, then indeed did Juliette atone during this one terrible second.

Her conscience, her heart, her entire being rose in revolt against her crime. Her oath, her life, her final denunciation appeared before her in all their hideousness.

And now it was too late.

===Chinese===

这一会儿工夫，朱丽叶特一动未动，一声未出。从第一声专横的喝令——以共和国之名，开门！——像警钟一样响彻宅中寂静那一刻算起，过去不过一分多钟。德鲁莱德的吻还热在她的手上，他情爱的话语还在她耳畔萦绕。

而如今，这桩骇人的、致命的祸事，竟是她亲手降到她所爱的男人头上的！

倘若灵魂获准以一瞬的悲恸赎一生的罪愆，那么朱丽叶特确乎在这一可怕的秒钟里完成了赎罪。

她的良知，她的心，她整个的生命，都起而反叛她的罪行。她的誓言，她的生命，她最后的那纸告发，全都狰狞毕现地呈现在她眼前。

而此刻，已经太迟了。

===Original===

Déroulède stood facing Merlin, his most implacable enemy. The latter was giving orders to his men, preparatory to searching the house, and there, just on the top of the valise, lay the letter-case, obviously containing those papers, to which the day before she had overheard Déroulède making allusion, whilst he spoke to his friend, Sir Percy Blakeney.

An unexplainable instinct seemed to tell her that the papers were in that case. Her eyes were riveted on it, as if fascinated. An awful terror held her enthralled for one second more, whilst her thoughts, her longings, her desires were all centred on the safety of that one thing.

The next instant she had seized it and thrown it upon the sofa. Then seating herself beside it, with the gesture of a queen and the grace of a Parisienne, she had spread the ample folds of her skirts over the compromising case, hiding it entirely from view.

===Chinese===

德鲁莱德面对梅尔兰——他最不共戴天的仇敌——站着。后者正向手下发号施令，预备搜查全宅；而就在那里，旅行皮箱顶上，搁着那只信匣，里面显然装着那些文件——前一天，她偷听见德鲁莱德同他的朋友珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）说话时提到的那些文件。

一种无从解释的直觉仿佛告诉她，那些文件就在那只匣子里。她的眼睛死死钉在它上面，像被摄去了魂魄。一阵骇人的大恐怖把她镇住，又多摄住了一秒；她的思想、她的渴念、她的心愿，全都聚在那一件东西的安危上。

转瞬之间，她已一把抓起它，抛在沙发上；随即在它旁边坐下，带着女王般的手势、巴黎女子般的袅娜，把宽大的裙裾铺展开来，盖住那只招灾的匣子，遮得严严实实，不露分毫。

===Original===

Merlin in the hall was ordering two men to stand one on each side of Déroulède, and two more to follow him into the room. Now he entered it himself, his narrow eyes trying to pierce the semi-obscurity, which was rendered more palpable by the brilliant light in the hall.

He had not seen Juliette’s gesture, but he had heard the frou-frou of her skirts, as she seated herself upon the sofa.

“You are not alone Citizen-Deputy, I see,” he said, with a sneer, as his snakelike eyes lighted upon the young girl.

“My guest, Citizen Merlin,” replied Déroulède as calmly as he could﻿—“Citizen Juliette Marny. I know that it is useless, under these circumstances, to ask for consideration for a woman, but I pray you to remember, as far as is possible, that although we are all Republicans, we are also Frenchmen, and all still equal in our sentiment of chivalry towards our mothers, our sisters, or our guests.”

===Chinese===

梅尔兰在门厅里，正吩咐两个人一左一右看住德鲁莱德，另两个跟他进屋。这时他自己也走进来，一双窄眼竭力要穿透那片半明半暗——门厅里的强光，把这片昏暗衬得愈加浓重。

朱丽叶特的手势他没有看见；可她坐上沙发时裙裾的窸窣，他听见了。

「我看出来了，公会议员，您并非独自一人，」他讥诮地说，蛇一样的眼睛落在了那年轻姑娘身上。

「我的客人，梅尔兰公民，」德鲁莱德竭力平静地回答，「朱丽叶特·马尔尼公民（Citizen Juliette Marny）。我知道，在这种情形下，为一个女人乞求体恤是无用的；不过我恳请您，尽力记住：我们虽都是共和主义者，却也都是法兰西人，在对母亲、对姐妹、对宾客的那点侠义心肠上，彼此依然是平等的。」

===Original===

Merlin chuckled, and gazed for a moment ironically at Juliette. He had held, between his talon-like fingers, that very morning, a thin scrap of paper, on which a schoolgirlish hand had scrawled the denunciation against Citizen-Deputy Déroulède.

Coarse in nature, and still coarser in thoughts, this representative of the people had very quickly arrived at a conclusion in his mind, with regard to this so-called guest in the Déroulède household.

“A discarded mistress,” he muttered to himself. “Just had another scene, I suppose. He’s got tired of her, and she’s given him away out of spite.”

Satisfied with this explanation of the situation, he was quite inclined to be amiable to Juliette. Moreover, he had caught sight of the valise, and almost thought that the young girl’s eyes had directed his attention towards it.

===Chinese===

梅尔兰嗤地一笑，对朱丽叶特讥讽地端详了片刻。就在今天早晨，他爪子似的手指之间，还捏着一小片薄薄的纸，上面用女学生似的笔迹，潦潦草草地写着控告公会议员德鲁莱德的那张状子。

这位人民代表生性粗鄙，心思比天性更粗鄙，对德鲁莱德家中这位所谓的客人，他很快就在心里下了断语。

「一个被甩掉的情妇，」他暗自咕哝，「想必又闹了一场。他玩腻了她，她就赌气把他给卖了出来。」

这番解释既叫他称心，他便颇有意对朱丽叶特和颜悦色了。况且，他已瞥见那只旅行皮箱，甚至觉得，正是那年轻姑娘的目光，把他的注意引向了它。

===Original===

“Open those shutters!” he commanded, “this place is like a vault.”

One of the men obeyed immediately, and as the brilliant August sun came streaming into the room, Merlin once more turned to Déroulède.

“Information has been laid against you, Citizen-Deputy,” he said, “by an anonymous writer, who states that you have just now in your possession correspondence or other papers intended for the Widow Capet: and the Committee of Public Safety has entrusted me and these citizens to seize such correspondence, and make you answerable for its presence in your house.”

Déroulède hesitated for one brief fraction of a second. As soon as the shutters had been opened, and the room flooded in daylight, he had at once perceived that his letter-case had disappeared, and guessed, from Juliette’s attitude upon the sofa, that she had concealed it about her person. It was this which caused him to hesitate.

His heart was filled with boundless gratitude to her for her noble effort to save him, but he would have given his life at this moment, to undo what she had done.

===Chinese===

「把那些百叶窗打开！」他喝令道，「这地方跟地窖一样。」

一名手下立刻照办；八月灿烂的阳光涌进屋来，梅尔兰再次转向德鲁莱德。

「有人告发了您，公会议员，」他说，「告状的人匿名，声称您此刻手中正掌握着致卡佩寡妇（Widow Capet）的书信或其他文件；救国委员会已把这件事委派给我和这几位公民：没收这些书信，并要您对它们藏在您家中一事负责。」

德鲁莱德迟疑了短短的一瞬。百叶窗一开，日光泻满屋子，他立刻便发现自己的信匣不见了，又从朱丽叶特坐在沙发上的那副姿态，猜到她把它藏在了身上。正是这个，叫他迟疑了。

她为搭救他而作的那番高贵努力，使他心里满溢着无限的感激；可就在此刻，他宁可献出性命，去撤销她所做成的事。

===Original===

The Terrorists were no respecters of persons or of sex. A domicillary search order, in those days, conferred full powers on those in authority, and Juliette might at any moment now be peremptorily ordered to rise. Through her action she had made herself one with the Citizen-Deputy; if the case were found under the folds of her skirts, she would be accused of connivance, or at any rate of the equally grave charge of shielding a traitor.

The manly pride in him rebelled at the thought of owing his immediate safety to a woman, yet he could not now discard her help, without compromising her irretrievably.

He dared not even to look again towards her, for he felt that at this moment her life as well as his own lay in the quiver of an eyelid; and Merlin’s keen, narrow eyes were fixed upon him in eager search for a tremor, a flash, which might betray fear or prove an admission of guilt.

Juliette sat there, calm, impassive, disdainful, and she seemed to Déroulède more angelic, more unattainable even than before. He could have worshipped her for her heroism, her resourcefulness, her quiet aloofness from all these coarse creatures who filled the room with the odour of their dirty clothes, with their rough jests, and their noisome suggestions.

===Chinese===

恐怖分子不认身份，也不认性别。在那个年月，一纸入宅搜查令，便授给当权者以全权；朱丽叶特随时都可能被厉声喝令起立。经她这一举，她已把自己同公会议员结成了一体；倘若那匣子从她裙裾底下搜出来，她就会被指控为同谋，至少也要背上窝藏叛国者这一同样重大的罪名。

一想到眼前的安危要仰仗一个女人，他身上的丈夫气概便起而抗拒；可他此刻又不能抛开她的帮助，否则就要把她无可挽回地断送了。

他甚至不敢再朝她那边看一眼，因为他觉得，此刻她的性命同他自己的性命，都悬在眼睑的一颤之间；而梅尔兰那双锐利的窄眼正紧盯住他，急切地搜寻一丝战栗、一道闪光，好证明他心慌意乱，或者坐实他认罪服罪。

朱丽叶特坐在那里，安详、冷淡、带着轻蔑；在德鲁莱德看来，她比先前更圣洁，更高不可攀。为了她的英勇，她的机智，她对满屋这些粗鄙生灵——他们用脏衣裳的臭气、粗鄙的打趣和恶浊的暗示塞满了这间屋子——那份静静的疏远，他简直要向她顶礼膜拜。

===Original===

“Well, Citizen-Deputy,” sneered Merlin after a while, “you do not reply, I notice.”

“The insinuation is unworthy of a reply, citizen,” replied Déroulède quietly; “my services to the Republic are well known. I should have thought that the Committee of Public Safety would disdain an anonymous denunciation against a faithful servant of the people of France.”

“The Committee of Public Safety knows its own business best, Citizen-Deputy,” rejoined Merlin roughly. “If the accusation prove a calumny, so much the better for you. I presume,” he added with a sneer, “that you do not propose to offer any resistance whilst these citizens and I search your house.”

Without another word Déroulède handed a bunch of keys to the man by his side. Every kind of opposition, argument even, would be worse than useless.

===Chinese===

「怎么样，公会议员，」梅尔兰隔了一会儿讥笑道，「我注意到了，您不答话。」

「公民，您这影射不配一答，」德鲁莱德平静地回答，「我对共和国的贡献是尽人皆知的。我原以为，救国委员会总还不至于屑于采信一纸控告法兰西人民忠实仆人的匿名状子。」

「救国委员会自己最懂得自己的事，公会议员，」梅尔兰粗声粗气地顶了回去。「倘若控告证明是诬蔑，那您就更走运了。想必，」他讥笑着补上一句，「在这几位公民和我搜查您宅子的时候，您不打算加以抗拒吧。」

德鲁莱德一言不发，把一串钥匙递给身旁那人。任何抗拒，哪怕争辩一句，都只会更其无用。

===Original===

Merlin had ordered the valise and desk to be searched, and two men were busy turning out the contents of both on to the floor. But the desk now only contained a few private household accounts, and notes for the various speeches which Déroulède had at various times delivered in the assemblies of the National Convention. Among these, a few pencil jottings for his great defence of Charlotte Corday were eagerly seized upon by Merlin, and his grimy, clawlike hands fastened upon this scrap of paper, as upon a welcome prey.

But there was nothing else of any importance. Déroulède was a man of thought and of action, with all the enthusiasm of real conviction, but none of the carelessness of a fanatic. The papers which were contained in the letter-case, and which he was taking with him to the Conciergerie, he considered were necessary to the success of his plans, otherwise he never would have kept them, and they were the only proofs that could be brought up against him.

The valise itself was only packed with the few necessaries for a month’s sojourn at the Conciergerie; and the men, under Merlin’s guidance, were vainly trying to find something, anything that might be construed into treasonable correspondence with the unfortunate prisoner there.

Merlin, whilst his men were busy with the search, was sprawling in one of the big leather-covered chairs, on the arms of which his dirty fingernails were beating an impatient devil’s tattoo. He was at no pains to conceal the intense disappointment which he would experience, were his errand to prove fruitless.

===Chinese===

梅尔兰已吩咐搜查旅行皮箱和书桌，两名手下正忙着把两处的东西统统翻倒在地板上。可书桌里这时只剩几页私人的家用账目，还有德鲁莱德历次在国民公会会议上演讲的底稿。其中几张铅笔草草的札记——是为他替夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）所作的那篇著名辩护记下的——被梅尔兰急切地一把抓起；那只污黑的爪子似的手，像逮住了心爱的猎物，死死扣住这几片纸。

除此之外，再没有什么要紧的东西了。德鲁莱德是个有思想也有行动的人，怀着真诚信念的全部热忱，却没有狂热者的疏忽大意。信匣里装的那些文件——他原打算随身带往巴黎古监狱（the Conciergerie）——他认为对自己计划的成败是必需的，否则他绝不会把它们留着；而它们，也正是唯一能拿出来指控他的证据。

那旅行皮箱里装的，不过是去巴黎古监狱盘桓一个月所需的几样东西；那些人在梅尔兰的指点下，枉然地想找出点什么，随便什么，只要能算作同关押在那里的那名不幸囚犯往来的叛国书信就行。

趁手下们忙着搜查，梅尔兰大模大样地摊在一张大皮椅里，脏指甲在扶手上敲出一串不耐烦的、恶魔般的鼓点。倘若这趟差事到头来一场空，他将大失所望——这一点，他毫不掩饰。

===Original===

His narrow eyes every now and then wandered towards Juliette, as if asking for her help and guidance. She, understanding his frame of mind, responded to the look. Shutting her mentality off from the coarse suggestion of his attitude towards her, she played her part with cunning, and without flinching. With a glance here and there, she directed the men in their search. Déroulède himself could scarcely refrain from looking at her; he was puzzled, and vaguely marvelled at the perfection, with which she carried through her role to the end.

Merlin found himself baffled.

He knew quite well that Citizen-Deputy Déroulède was not a man to be lightly dealt with. No mere suspicion or anonymous denunciation would be sufficient in his case, to bring him before the tribunal of the Revolution. Unless there were proofs﻿—positive, irrefutable, damnable proofs﻿—of Paul Déroulède’s treachery, the Public Prosecutor would never dare to frame an indictment against him. The mob of Paris would rise to defend its idol; the hideous hags, who plied their knitting at the foot of the scaffold, would tear the guillotine down, before they would allow Déroulède to mount it.

This was Déroulède’s stronghold: the people of Paris, whom he had loved through all their infamies, and whom he had succoured and helped in their private need; and above all the women of Paris, whose children he had caused to be tended in the hospitals which he had built for them﻿—this they had not yet forgotten, and Merlin knew it. One day they would forget﻿—soon, perhaps﻿—then they would turn on their former idol, and, howling, send him to his death, amidst cries of rancour and execration. When that day came there would be no need to worry about treason or about proofs. When the populace had forgotten all that he had done, then Déroulède would fall.

But that time was not yet.

===Chinese===

他那双窄眼时不时朝朱丽叶特那边溜去，仿佛向她讨救兵、讨指点。她懂得他的心思，便回应他的眼色。她把他的态度里那层粗鄙的暗示关在心门之外，狡黠而毫不退缩地演着自己的角色。这里一瞥，那里一望，她指挥着手下们的搜查。连德鲁莱德自己也几乎忍不住要去看她；他又困惑，又隐隐叹服：她把这出戏演得何等完满，一直演到了底。

梅尔兰发觉自己被难住了。

他心里很清楚，公会议员德鲁莱德不是个轻易好对付的人。单凭一星嫌疑、一纸匿名告发，不足以把他送上革命法庭。除非握有证据——确凿的、无可辩驳的、该下地狱的证据——证明保罗·德鲁莱德叛国，公诉人决不敢对他提起公诉。巴黎的暴民会起而保卫他们的偶像；那些在断头台脚下织着毛线的丑老婆子，会把断头台整个拆掉，也不肯放德鲁莱德登上它。

这便是德鲁莱德的堡垒：巴黎的人民——纵然他们劣迹累累，他也始终爱着他们，在他们私人的困厄中接济他们、帮助他们；尤其是巴黎的妇女——他建起了医院，叫她们的孩子在那里得到照料——这一切她们还没有忘记，而梅尔兰知道这一点。总有一天她们会忘记的——也许很快——到那时，她们就会掉转头来对付从前的偶像，嚎叫着把他送上死路，四周一片怨毒与咒骂的声浪。到那一天，叛国也好，证据也好，都不必再操心。民众一旦忘了他做过的一切，德鲁莱德就要倒了。

但那个时刻还没有到来。

===Original===

The men had finished ransacking the room; every scrap of paper, every portable article had been eagerly seized upon.

Merlin, half blind with fury, had jumped to his feet.

“Search him!” he ordered peremptorily.

Déroulède set his teeth, and made no protest, calling up every fibre of moral strength within him, to aid him in submitting to this indignity. At a coarse jest from Merlin, he buried his nails into the palms of his hand, not to strike the foulmouthed creature in the face. But he submitted, and stood impassive by, whilst the pockets of his coat were turned inside out by the rough hands of the soldiers.

===Chinese===

手下们已把屋子翻检完毕；每一片纸，每一件搬得动的东西，都给急切地抓到了手里。

梅尔兰气得眼睛发黑，一跃而起。

「搜他的身！」他专横地下令。

德鲁莱德咬紧牙关，不置一词，把周身每一缕道德的力量都召唤出来，支撑自己忍受这场侮辱。梅尔兰说了句粗鄙的玩笑，他便把指甲掐进掌心——免得一巴掌打在那张满嘴喷粪的脸上。可他忍了，一动不动地站在一旁，听凭大兵们的粗手把他的外套口袋一个个翻了个底朝天。

===Original===

All the while Juliette had remained silent, watching Merlin as any hawk would its prey. But the Terrorist, through the very coarseness of his nature, was in this case completely fooled.

He knew that it was Juliette who had denounced Déroulède, and had satisfied himself as to her motive. Because he was low and brutish and degraded, he never once suspected the truth, never saw in that beautiful young woman, anything of the double nature within her, of that curious, self-torturing, at times morbid sense of religion and of duty, at war with her own upright, innately healthy disposition.

The lowborn, self-degraded Terrorist had put his own construction on Juliette’s action, and with this he was satisfied, since it answered to his own estimate of the human race, the race which he was doing his best to bring down to the level of the beast.

Therefore Merlin did not interfere with Juliette, but contented himself with insinuating, by jest and action, what her share in this day’s work had been. To these hints Déroulède, of course, paid no heed. For him Juliette was as far above political intrigue as the angels. He would as soon have suspected one of the saints enshrined in Notre Dame as this beautiful, almost ethereal creature, who had been sent by Heaven to gladden his heart and to elevate his very thought.

===Chinese===

这一切工夫里，朱丽叶特始终一言不发，盯着梅尔兰，像鹰隼盯着它的猎物。可这个恐怖分子，恰恰因为天性粗鄙，在这桩事上被完全蒙住了。

他知道告发德鲁莱德的是朱丽叶特，也自认为摸清了她的动机。正因为他自己下贱、野蛮、堕落，他从未疑心过真相，从未在那美丽的年轻女子身上，看出她内心的两重天性：那种奇特的、自我折磨的、时而近于病态的宗教感与责任感，正同她那正直的、天生康健的禀性交战着。

这个出身低贱、自甘堕落的恐怖分子，拿他自己的那套揣度去解释朱丽叶特的行为，并且就此满足了——因为那揣度正合他对人类的估价；而人类，正是他竭力要拉到禽兽那个水准上去的种族。

因此梅尔兰并不去搅扰朱丽叶特，只满足于用玩笑和动作影射：她在今天这桩差事里出了几分力。对这些暗示，德鲁莱德自然不予理会。在他眼里，朱丽叶特之超然于政治阴谋，一如天使。他宁可去疑心巴黎圣母院（Notre Dame）里供着的某位圣徒，也不会疑心这个美丽的、几乎缥缈的造物——她是上天派来欢愉他的心、升华他的思想的。

===Original===

But Juliette understood Merlin’s attitude, and guessed that her written denunciation had come into his hands. Her every thought, every living sensation within her, was centred in this one thing: to save the man she loved from the consequences of her own crime against him. And for this, even the shadow of suspicion must be removed from him. Merlin’s iniquitous law should not touch him again.

When Déroulède at last had been released, after the outrage to which he had been personally subjected, Merlin was literally, and figuratively too, looking about him for an issue to his present dubious position.

Judging others by his own standard of conduct, he feared now that the popular Citizen-Deputy would incite the mob against him, in revenge for the indignities which he had had to suffer. And with it all the Terrorist was convinced that Déroulède was guilty, that proofs of his treason did exist, if only he knew where to lay hands on them.

He turned to Juliette with an unexpressed query in his adder-like eyes. She shrugged her shoulders, and made a gesture as if pointing towards the door.

===Chinese===

可朱丽叶特懂得梅尔兰的态度，也猜到她那纸书面的告发已经落进他手里。她的每一个念头、她周身每一分鲜活的知觉，都凝在这一件事上：把她所爱的人，从她加于他的那桩罪行的后果中救出来。为此，连一丝嫌疑的影子也必须从他身上拂去。梅尔兰那部恶法，不许再碰他分毫。

德鲁莱德终于被放开时——在亲身受尽那场凌辱之后——梅尔兰正实实在在地、也是打比方地环顾四周，想给自己眼下这不明不白的处境找一条出路。

他拿自己的行为准则去度他人，此刻便害怕这位深得民心的公会议员会煽动暴民来对付他，为他遭受的那些凌辱报仇。而与此同时，这个恐怖分子又深信德鲁莱德有罪，深信他叛国的证据确乎存在，只要他知道上哪儿把它们抓到手里。

他转向朱丽叶特，蝰蛇似的眼睛里含着一个没有出口的问句。她耸了耸肩，做了个手势，仿佛指向房门。

===Original===

“There are other rooms in the house besides this,” her gesture seemed to say; “try them. The proofs are there, ’tis for you to find them.”

Merlin had been standing between her and Déroulède, so that the latter saw neither query nor reply.

“You are cunning, Citizen-Deputy,” said Merlin now, turning towards him, “and no doubt you have been at pains to put your treasonable correspondence out of the way. You must understand that the Committee of Public Safety will not be satisfied with a mere examination of your study,” he added, assuming an air of ironical benevolence, “and I presume you will have no objection, if I and these citizen soldiers pay a visit to other portions of your house.”

“As you please,” responded Déroulède drily.

===Chinese===

「这宅子里除了这间，还有的是屋子，」她的手势仿佛在说；「去试吧。证据就在那里，找不找得到，该您费神了。」

梅尔兰一直站在她和德鲁莱德中间，所以后者既没有看见那问句，也没有看见这回答。

「您真是机灵，公会议员，」梅尔兰这时转向他说，「您无疑费了心思，把叛国的书信挪了窝。您得明白，救国委员会绝不会满足于只搜一搜您的书房，」他摆出一副挖苦的和善面孔，补上一句，「我想，我和这几位公民士兵到您宅子的别处去走一走，您不至于有异议吧。」

「悉听尊便。」德鲁莱德干巴巴地答道。

===Original===

“You will accompany us, Citizen-Deputy,” commanded the other curtly.
