The four men of the National Guard formed themselves into line outside the study door; with a peremptory nod, Merlin ordered Déroulède to pass between them, then he too prepared to follow. At the door he turned, and once more faced Juliette.

“As for you, citizeness,” he said, with a sudden access of viciousness against her, “if you have brought us here on a fool’s errand, it will go ill with you, remember. Do not leave the house until our return. I may have some questions to put to you.”

===Chinese===

「您得陪我们一道走，公会议员。」对方简短地命令道。

四名国民自卫军士兵在书房门外排成一列；梅尔兰不容分说地点点头，命德鲁莱德从他们中间走过去，随后他自己也预备跟上。走到门口，他回过身来，又一次面对朱丽叶特。

「至于你，女公民，」他说，突然对她生出一股恶毒，「要是你把我们引到这儿来白跑一趟，记住，你可没有好下场。在我们回来之前，不许离开这座宅子。我也许还有些话要问你。」

## XIII Tangled Meshes / XIII 缠结的罗网

===Original===
Juliette waited a moment or two, until the footsteps of the six men died away up the massive oak stairs.

For the first time, since the sword of Damocles had fallen, she was alone with her thoughts.

She had but a few moments at her command in which to devise an issue out of these tangled meshes, which she had woven round the man she loved.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特又等候了片刻，直到那六个男人的脚步声消失在厚重的橡木楼梯高处。

达摩克利斯之剑落下之后，这是她头一回独自静处，面对自己的思绪。

她手里只有短短的片刻工夫，好设法从这缠结的罗网里找出一条生路——而这罗网，正是她亲手织来罩住她所爱的那个人的。

===Original===
Merlin and his men would return anon. The comedy could not be kept up through another visit from them, and while the compromising letter-case remained in Déroulède's private study he was in imminent danger at the hands of his enemy.

She thought for a moment of concealing the case about her person, but a second's reflection showed her the futility of such a move. She had not seen the papers themselves; any one of them might be an absolute proof of Déroulède's guilt; the correspondence might be in his handwriting.

If Merlin, furious, baffled, vicious, were to order her to be searched! The horror of the indignity made her shudder, but she would have submitted to that, if thereby she could have saved Déroulède. But of this she could not be sure until after she had looked through the papers, and this she had not the time to do.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰和他的手下随时会回来。他们若再来一趟，这出戏就再也演不下去了；而只要那只牵连罪证的信匣还留在德鲁莱德的书房里，他随时会落入仇敌之手，危在旦夕。

有一瞬间，她想把信匣藏在自己身上；可转念一想，便看出这步棋全然无用。那些文件她还没有看过；其中任何一纸，都可能是德鲁莱德获罪的铁证；那些信件或许正是他的亲笔。

倘若梅尔兰恼羞成怒、连遭挫败、凶性大发，下令对她搜身呢！这样的奇耻大辱，光想一想就令她战栗；可只要能借此救下德鲁莱德，她也甘愿忍受。但这一点，她非得先看过那些文件才能确定，而她已经没有时间看了。

===Original===
Her first and greatest idea was to get out of this room, his private study, with the compromising papers. Not a trace of them must be found here, if he were to remain beyond suspicion.

She rose from the sofa, and peeped through the door. The hall was now deserted; from the left wing of the house, on the floor above, the heavy footsteps of the soldiers and Merlin's occasional brutish laugh could be distinctly heard.

Juliette listened for a moment, trying to understand what was happening. Yes; they had all gone to Déroulède's bedroom, which was on the extreme left, at the end of the first-floor landing. There might be just time to accomplish what she had now resolved to do.

===Chinese===
她最先想到、也最要紧的一着，就是带着这包罪证文件离开这间屋子——他的私人书房。要想让他摆脱嫌疑，这里就绝不能留下他们的一丝痕迹。

她从沙发上起身，从门缝里张望。大厅此刻已空无一人；楼上左翼那边，士兵们沉重的脚步声，间或夹着梅尔兰粗野的嗤笑，听得清清楚楚。

朱丽叶特侧耳听了一会儿，想弄明白那边在做什么。不错；他们全都进了德鲁莱德的卧室——那屋子在二楼楼梯平台的最左端。她如今打定主意要做的事，或许还来得及办成。

===Original===
As best she could, she hid the bulky leather case in the folds of her skirt. It was literally neck or nothing now. If she were caught on the stairs by one of the men nothing could save her or—possibly—Déroulède.

At any rate, by remaining where she was, by leaving the events to shape themselves, discovery was absolutely certain. She chose to take the risk.

She slipped noiselessly out of the room and up the great oak stairs. Merlin and his men, busy with their search in Déroulède's bedroom, took no heed of what was going on behind them; Juliette arrived on the landing, and turned sharply to her right, running noiselessly along the thick Aubusson carpet, and thence quickly to her own room.

===Chinese===
她使尽浑身解数，把那只鼓鼓囊囊的皮匣藏进裙裾的褶层里。如今真是孤注一掷，再无退路。倘若在楼梯上被哪个人撞见，无论她——或许还有德鲁莱德——都再无得救的可能。

无论如何，若待在原地不动，听凭事态自行发展，败露是板上钉钉的。她选择冒险一试。

她悄无声息地溜出屋子，踏上那道大橡木楼梯。梅尔兰一伙正忙着搜查德鲁莱德的卧室，全然不顾身后的动静；朱丽叶特到了楼梯平台，急转向右，踏着厚厚的奥布松地毯（Aubusson）无声疾走，随即快步进了自己的房间。

===Original===
All this had taken less than a minute to accomplish. The very next moment she heard Merlin's voice ordering one of his men to stand at attention on the landing, but by that time she was safe inside her room. She closed the door noiselessly.

Pétronelle, who had been busy all the afternoon packing up her young mistress' things, had fallen asleep in an armchair. Unconscious of the terrible events which were rapidly succeeding each other in the house, the worthy old soul was snoring peaceably, with her hands complacently folded on her ample bosom.

Juliette, for the moment, took no notice of her. As quickly and as dexterously as she could, she was tearing open the heavy leather case with a sharp pair of scissors, and very soon its contents were scattered before her on the table.

===Chinese===
这一切，前后不到一分钟。紧接着，她就听见梅尔兰在吆喝一名手下到楼梯平台上立正站岗；但那时她已安然待在自己房里，正悄没声儿地关上房门。

佩特罗内勒整个下午都在忙着收拾小姐的行李，此刻已在扶手椅里睡着了。这位可敬的老好人，对宅子里接二连三的凶险变故一无所知，正安然打着鼾，两只手心满意足地交叠在丰满的胸前。

朱丽叶特此刻顾不上理她。她用一把锋利的剪刀，又快又利落地割开那只沉重的皮匣，不多时，匣中之物已摊在她面前的桌上。

===Original===
One glance at them was sufficient to convince her that most of the papers would undoubtedly, if found, send Déroulède to the guillotine. Most of the correspondence was in the Citizen-Deputy's handwriting. She had, of course, no time to examine it more closely, but instinct naturally told her that it was of a highly compromising character.

She gathered the papers up into a heap, tearing some of them up into strips; then she spread them out upon the ashpan in front of the large earthenware stove, which stood in a corner of the room.

Unfortunately, this was a hot day in August. Her task would have been far easier if she had wished to destroy a bundle of papers in the depth of winter, when there was a good fire burning in the stove.

===Chinese===
只消看上一眼，她便确信：这些文件一旦被搜出，其中大半无疑会把德鲁莱德送上断头台。信件大半出自公会议员本人的手笔。她自然没有时间细看，但本能自然而然地告诉她，那全是最要命的罪证。

她把文件拢作一堆，把其中一些撕成纸条，然后摊放在屋角那只大陶炉前的灰盘上。

不巧，这是八月里一个炎热的日子。她若是在隆冬时节要焚毁一包文件，炉膛里燃着旺火，那桩差事本会容易得多。

===Original===
But her purpose was firm and her incentive, the greatest that has ever spurred mankind to heroism.

Regardless of any consequences to herself, she had but the one object in view, to save Déroulède at all costs.

On the wall facing her bed, and immediately above a velvet-covered prie-dieu, there was a small figure of the Virgin and Child—one of those quaintly pretty devices for holding holy water, which the reverent superstition of the past century rendered a necessary adjunct of every girl's room.

===Chinese===
可她的意志坚定不移；激励着她的那股动力，正是古往今来驱策人类舍身成勇的最伟大的动力。

无论自己会招来什么后果，她眼中只有一个目标：不惜一切代价，救下德鲁莱德。

正对着她床铺的墙上，在一座蒙着丝绒的跪凳（prie-dieu）上方，供着一尊小小的圣母与圣婴像——那是旧式别致的圣水盒中的一种；上个世纪那种虔敬的迷信风气，让它成了每个姑娘闺房里必不可少的一件陈设。

===Original===
In front of the figure a small lamp was kept perpetually burning. This Juliette now took between her fingers, carefully, lest the tiny flame should die out. First she poured the oil over the fragments of paper in the ashpan, then with the wick she set fire to the whole compromising correspondence.

The oil helped the paper to burn quickly; the smell, or perhaps the presence of Juliette in the room caused worthy old Pétronelle to wake.

"It's nothing, Pétronelle," said Juliette quietly; "only a few old letters I am burning. But I want to be alone for a few moments—will you go down to the kitchen until I call you?"

===Chinese===
圣像前供着一盏小灯，长明不熄。朱丽叶特此刻小心翼翼地用手指把它端起，生怕那点微弱的火苗熄灭。她先把灯油浇在灰盘里的碎纸上，再用灯芯点着了那一整包罪证信件。

灯油助燃，纸片很快烧了起来；那股气味，或许也只是因为朱丽叶特在屋里，惊醒了可敬的老佩特罗内勒。

「没什么，佩特罗内勒，」朱丽叶特平静地说，「不过是烧几封旧信。我想一个人待一会儿——你先到楼下的厨房去，等我叫你，好不好？」

===Original===
Accustomed to do as her young mistress commanded, Pétronelle rose without a word.

"I have finished putting away your few things, my jewel. There, there! why didn't you tell me to burn your papers for you? You have soiled your dear hands, and—"

"Sh! Sh! Pétronelle!" said Juliette impatiently, and gently pushing the garrulous old woman towards the door. "Run to the kitchen now quickly, and don't come out of it until I call you. And, Pétronelle," she added, "you will see soldiers about the house perhaps."

===Chinese===
佩特罗内勒一向听惯了小姐的吩咐，一声不响地站起身来。

「您那几件零碎东西，我都归置好了，我的心肝。瞧瞧！您怎么不吩咐我替您烧信呢？瞧您把宝贝的手都弄脏了，还有——」

「嘘！嘘！佩特罗内勒！」朱丽叶特不耐烦地说，轻轻把絮絮叨叨的老妇人往门口推。「快到厨房去，我叫你之前别出来。还有，佩特罗内勒，」她补了一句，「屋子里外，你兴许会看见士兵。」

===Original===
"Soldiers! The good God have mercy!"

"Don't be frightened, Pétronelle. But they may ask you questions."

"Questions?"

===Chinese===
「士兵！仁慈的上帝发发慈悲吧！」

「别害怕，佩特罗内勒。不过他们或许会盘问你。」

「盘问？」

===Original===
"Yes; about me."

"My treasure, my jewel," exclaimed Pétronelle in alarm, "have those devils—?"

"No, no; nothing has happened as yet, but, you know, in these times there is always danger."

===Chinese===
「是的；问我的事。」

「我的宝贝，我的心肝，」佩特罗内勒惊叫起来，「难道那些恶鬼——？」

「不，不；眼下还没出什么事，不过你知道，这样的年月，危险总是有的。」

===Original===
"Good God! Holy Mary! Mother of God!"

"Nothing'll happen if you try to keep quite calm and do exactly as I tell you. Go to the kitchen, and wait there until I call you. If the soldiers come in and question you, if they try to frighten you, remember that we have nothing to fear from men, and that our lives are in God's keeping."

All the while that Juliette spoke, she was watching the heap of paper being gradually reduced to ashes. She tried to fan the flames as best she could, but some of the correspondence was on tough paper, and was slow in being consumed. Pétronelle, tearful but obedient, prepared to leave the room. She was overawed by her mistress' air of aloofness, the pale face rendered ethereally beautiful by the sufferings she had gone through. The eyes glowed large and magnetic, as if in presence of spiritual visions beyond mortal ken; the golden hair looked like a saintly halo above the white, immaculate young brow.

===Chinese===
「老天爷！圣母马利亚！天主之母啊！」

「只要你沉住气，完全照我说的做，就不会出事。到厨房去，在那儿等我叫你。要是士兵进来盘问你，要是他们想吓唬你，记住：我们无须惧怕任何人，我们的性命都握在上帝手里。」

朱丽叶特说话的当儿，眼睛一直盯着那堆纸渐渐烧成灰烬。她竭力扇风助燃，可有些信是用韧性厚纸写的，烧得很慢。佩特罗内勒含着泪，却依旧听话，准备退出房间。小姐那一身遗世独立的神情气度令她心生敬畏——那张苍白的脸，因经历的苦难而透出一种超凡脱俗的美。一双大眼睛灼灼放光，摄人心魄，仿佛正望着凡人肉眼所不能见的灵界幻象；金色的头发罩在洁白无瑕的年轻额头上，宛如一圈圣徒的光环。

===Original===
Pétronelle made the sign of the cross, as if she were in the presence of a saint.

As she opened the door there was a sudden draught, and the last flickering flame died out in the ashpan. Juliette, seeing that Pétronelle had gone, hastily turned over the few half burnt fragments of paper that were left. In none of them had the writing remained legible. All that was compromising to Déroulède was effectually reduced to dust. The small wick in the lamp at the foot of the Virgin and Child had burned itself out for want of oil; there was no means for Juliette to strike another light and to destroy what remained. The leather case was, of course, still there, with its sides ripped open, an indestructible thing.

There was nothing to be done about that. Juliette after a second's hesitation threw it among her dresses in the valise.

Then she too went out of the room.

===Chinese===
佩特罗内勒画了个十字，仿佛眼前站着一位圣徒。

她开门的当儿，猛地灌进来一股风，灰盘里最后一点摇曳的火苗熄灭了。朱丽叶特见佩特罗内勒已经走了，急忙翻检剩下的几张烧了一半的纸片。没有一张上面还留着可以辨认的字迹。凡能连累德鲁莱德的东西，都已彻底化为灰土。圣母圣婴像脚下那盏小灯的灯芯，油尽自灭；朱丽叶特再没有法子点出第二处火，来销毁剩余的东西。那只皮匣自然还在，侧面已被割开——真是个毁不掉的物件。

这事再也无计可施。朱丽叶特略一迟疑，便把它扔进行李箱，塞进自己的衣裳中间。

随后，她也走出了房间。

## XIV A Happy Moment / XIV 幸福的时刻

===Original===
The search in the Citizen-Deputy’s bedroom had proved as fruitless as that in his study. Merlin was beginning to have vague doubts as to whether he had been effectively fooled.

His manner towards Déroulède had undergone a change. He had become suave and unctuous, a kind of elephantine irony pervading his laborious attempts at conciliation. He and the Public Prosecutor would be severely blamed for this day’s work, if the popular Deputy, relying upon the support of the people of Paris, chose to take his revenge.

In France, in this glorious year of the Revolution, there was but one step between censure and indictment. And Merlin knew it. Therefore, although he had not given up all hope of finding proofs of Déroulède’s treason, although by the latter’s attitude he remained quite convinced that such proof did exist, he was already reckoning upon the cat’s paw, the sop he would offer to that Cerberus, the Committee of Public Safety, in exchange for his own exculpation in the matter.

This sop would be Juliette, the denunciator instead of Déroulède the denounced.

===Chinese===
对公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）卧室的搜查，同对他书房的搜查一样，落得个一无所获。梅尔兰（Merlin）开始隐隐生疑：自己是不是结结实实地让人给愚弄了。

他对德鲁莱德（Déroulède）的态度已然变了样。他变得和婉殷勤、甜腻油滑，在他那费尽气力的修好姿态里，透出一种笨重如象的讥诮。

倘若这位深得民心的议员倚仗巴黎民众的支持，决意报复，那么他和公诉人（Public Prosecutor）都得为这一天干下的事，受到严厉的责难。

在法兰西，在这光荣的革命之年，从谴责到控诉，只隔一步之遥。这层道理梅尔兰深知。因此，尽管他还没有放弃寻获德鲁莱德叛国罪证的全部希望，尽管从后者的态度看，他仍深信这样的罪证确然存在，他却已经在盘算那只替罪的爪子，那块预备抛给刻耳柏洛斯（Cerberus）——救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）——的糕点，好换取自己在这一桩事情上的开脱。

===Original===
But he was still seeking for the proofs.

Somewhat changing his tactics, he had allowed Déroulède to join his mother in the living-room, and had betaken himself to the kitchen in search of Anne Mie, whom he had previously caught sight of in the hall. There he also found old Pétronelle, whom he could scare out of her wits to his heart’s content, but from whom he was quite unable to extract any useful information. Pétronelle was too stupid to be dangerous, and Anne Mie was too much on the alert.

But, with a vague idea that a cunning man might choose the most unlikely places for the concealment of compromising property, he was ransacking the kitchen from floor to ceiling.

===Chinese===
这块糕点便是朱丽叶特（Juliette）：拿告发的人，去顶替被告发的德鲁莱德。

不过，他还在搜寻罪证。

他稍稍换了战术，容许德鲁莱德到起居室去陪母亲，自己则跑进厨房去找安娜·米（Anne Mie）——方才他在大厅里曾瞥见她的身影。在那里，他还遇见了老佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）：要把她吓得魂飞魄散，尽可以随心所欲；可要从她嘴里榨出半点有用的消息，却万万不能。佩特罗内勒蠢得构不成危害，安娜·米却警觉得厉害。

===Original===
In the living-room Déroulède was doing his best to reassure his mother, who, in her turn, was forcing herself to be brave, and not to show by her tears how deeply she feared for the safety of her son. As soon as Déroulède had been freed from the presence of the soldiers, he had hastened back to his study, only to find that Juliette had gone, and that the letter-case had also disappeared. Not knowing what to think, trembling for the safety of the woman he adored, he was just debating whether he would seek for her in her own room, when she came towards him across the landing.

There seemed a halo around her now. Déroulède felt that she had never been so beautiful and to him so unattainable. Something told him then, that at this moment she was as far away from him, as if she were an inhabitant of another, more ethereal planet.

===Chinese===
不过，他隐隐想着，一个狡诈的人兴许会挑最出人意料的地方，来藏匿那些足以定罪的物件，于是便把厨房从地板到天花板翻了个底朝天。

起居室里，德鲁莱德正竭力宽慰他的母亲；而母亲也在强自撑持，装出勇敢的模样，不让眼泪泄露她为儿子的安危怀着多深的忧惧。德鲁莱德一脱开士兵的看押，便急急奔回书房，却发现朱丽叶特已经不在，那只信匣也不见了踪影。他不知该作何想，为心中爱慕的女人的安危惴惴颤抖，正踌躇着要不要去她自己的房间寻她，忽见她已经穿过楼梯平台，朝他走来。

===Original===
When she saw him coming towards her, she put a finger to her lips, and whispered:

“Sh! sh! the papers are destroyed, burned.”

“And I owe my safety to you!”

He had said it with his whole soul, an infinity of gratitude filled his heart, a joy and pride in that she had cared for his safety.

But at his words she had grown paler than she was before. Her eyes, large, dilated, and dark, were fixed upon him with an intensity of gaze which almost startled him. He thought that she was about to faint, that the emotions of the past half hour had been too much for her overstrung nerves. He took her hand, and gently dragged her into the living-room.

===Chinese===
此刻，她的周身仿佛罩着一轮光晕。德鲁莱德觉得，她从未这样美过，也从未于他这样可望而不可即。有什么在他心底低语：就在这一刻，她离他那样遥远，仿佛是另一个星球上的居民——一个更加空灵缥缈的星球。

她见他自己迎面走来，便把一根手指按在唇上，悄声说道：

「嘘！嘘！文件都毁掉了，烧掉了。」

「而我的性命，是您救下来的！」

这句话，他说得全心全意；无限的感激涨满了他的心——她把他的安危放在了心上，这使他欣喜，也使他骄傲。

===Original===
She sank into a chair, as if utterly weary and exhausted, and he, forgetting his danger, forgetting the world and all else besides, knelt at her feet, and held her hands in his.

She sat bolt upright, her great eyes still fixed upon him. At first it seemed as if he could not be satiated with looking at her; he felt as if he had never, never really seen her. She had been a dream of beauty to him ever since that awful afternoon when he had held her, half fainting, in his arms, and had dragged her under the shelter of his roof.

From that hour he had worshipped her: she had cast over him the magic spell of her refinement, her beauty, that aroma of youth and innocence which makes such a strong appeal to the man of sentiment.

===Chinese===
可听了这话，她的脸色变得比先前更苍白。她那双大眼睛，瞳仁散大，颜色幽深，一瞬不瞬地凝望着他，那份专注到极处的凝视几乎令他心惊。他以为她就要晕厥，以为过去半个钟头里的种种惊悸，已超出了她绷得过紧的神经所能承受的限度。他握住她的手，轻轻把她拽进起居室。

她瘫倒进一张椅子里，仿佛疲惫衰竭到了极点；而他，忘了自身的危险，忘了世界，忘了一切身外的一切，跪在她的脚边，把她的双手握在自己手中。

她端端正正地坐着，一双大眼睛依旧凝望着他。起初，他看她仿佛永远看不够；他觉得自己仿佛从来不曾、从来不曾真正看见过她。从那个可怕的午后起，她之于他，便是一场美的幻梦——那个午后，他把她半昏不醒地抱在怀里，把她拽进了自己屋檐的庇护之下。

===Original===
He had worshipped her and not tried to understand. He would have deemed it almost sacrilege to pry into the mysteries of her inner self, of that second nature in her which at times made her silent, and almost morose, and cast a lurid gloom over her young beauty.

And though his love for her had grown in intensity, it had remained as heaven born as he deemed her to be—the love of a mortal for a saint, the ecstatic adoration of a St. Francis for his Madonna.

Sir Percy Blakeney had called Déroulède an idealist. He was that, in the strictest sense, and Juliette had embodied all that was best in his idealism.

===Chinese===
从那一刻起，他便崇拜上了她：她的温文、她的美貌，还有那青春与纯真的芬芳气息——最能打动多情男子心肠的，正是这种气息——一齐对他施下了魔咒。

他只管崇拜她，却不去试图理解。去窥探她内心深处的奥秘，去窥探她那重天性的幽暗底面——它有时使她沉默，近乎阴郁，并给她青春的容貌罩上一层狰狞的阴翳——在他眼里几乎近于亵渎。

他对她的爱虽然愈燃愈烈，却始终同他所认定的她本人一样，是出自天国的——那是一个凡人对圣者的爱，是一位圣方济各（St. Francis）对他的圣母那出神入迷的崇仰。

===Original===
It was for the first time today, that he had held her hand just for a moment longer than mere conventionality allowed. The first kiss on her fingertips had sent the blood rushing wildly to his heart; but he still worshipped her, and gazed upon her as upon a divinity.

She sat bolt upright in the chair, abandoning her small, cold hands to his burning grasp.

His very senses ached with the longing to clasp her in his arms, to draw her to him, and to feel her pulses beat closer against his. It was almost torture now to gaze upon her beauty—that small, oval face, almost like a child’s, the large eyes which at times had seemed to be blue but which now appeared to be a deep, unfathomable colour, like the tempestuous sea.

===Chinese===
珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）曾说德鲁莱德是个理想主义者。从最严格的意义上说，他确然当得起这个称呼；而朱丽叶特，正是他理想主义之中一切最美好之处的化身。

直到今天，他才头一回把她的手握得比寻常礼数所容许的长了那么一瞬。第一次吻她的指尖，血便狂野地直冲心口；可他依旧在崇拜她，凝望她如凝望一尊神明。

她端端正正地坐在椅子里，把她那双小小的、冰凉的手，任凭他灼热的掌握攥着。

===Original===
“Juliette!” he murmured at last, as his soul went out to her in a passionate appeal for the first kiss.

A shudder seemed to go through her entire frame, her very lips turned white and cold, and he, not understanding, timorous, chivalrous and humble, thought that she was repelled by his ardour and frightened by a passion to which she was too pure to respond.

Nothing but that one word had been spoken—just her name, an appeal from a strong man, overmastered at last by his boundless love—and she, poor, stricken soul, who had so much loved, so deeply wronged him, shuddered at the thought of what she might have done, had Fate not helped her to save him.

Half ashamed of his passion, he bowed his dark head over her hands, and, once more forcing himself to be calm now, he kissed her fingertips reverently.

===Chinese===
他满心渴望把她搂进臂弯，把她拉向自己，感觉她的脉搏贴着自己的脉搏跳动，这渴望竟使他百骸俱痛。此刻凝望她的美，几乎成了一种酷刑——那张小小的鹅蛋脸，几乎像个孩子的脸；那双大眼睛，有时看来似是蓝色，此刻却显出一种深沉莫测的颜色，像风暴喧嚣的大海。

「朱丽叶特！」他终于喃喃唤道，灵魂向她奔涌而去，热切地恳求着那初吻。

一阵战栗仿佛掠过她全身，连她的双唇都变得苍白冰冷；而他，不明就里，既怯懦，又骑士气十足，又满怀谦卑，只道她是被自己的热忱吓退了，是被那份激情惊骇了——她那样纯洁，本无从回应。

说出口的，只有那一个词——不过是她的名字，一条刚强的汉子终于被自己无边的爱制伏时发出的呼唤——而她，那可怜的、备受创伤的灵魂，曾经那样深爱，又那样深重地辜负了他，此刻一想到，若非命运出手救下了他，她本会干出怎样的事来，便浑身战栗。

===Original===
When he looked up again the hard lines in her face had softened, and two tears were slowly trickling down her pale cheeks.

“Will you forgive me, madonna?” he said gently. “I am only a man and you are very beautiful. No—don’t take your little hands away. I am quite calm now, and know how one should speak to angels.”

Reason, justice, rectitude—everything was urging Juliette to close her ears to the words of love, spoken by the man whom she had betrayed. But who shall blame her for listening to the sweetest sound the ears of a woman can ever hear—the sound of the voice of the loved one in his first declaration of love?

===Chinese===
他为自己的热情感到几分羞赧，把一头黑发低垂在她的手上，此刻重新强自镇定下来，虔敬地吻着她的指尖。

「您肯原谅我吗，我的圣母？」他温柔地说。「我终究只是个凡人，而您又这样美。不——别把您的小手抽回去。我现在完全平静了，懂得该怎样同天使说话。」

理智、公义、正直——一切都催逼着朱丽叶特掩上耳朵，不去听这个被她出卖的人口中吐出的爱语。可是，一个女人的耳朵所能听到的最甜美的声音——心爱之人初次表白爱情时的嗓音——听听这个，又有谁忍心责怪她呢？

===Original===
She sat and listened, whilst he whispered to her those soft, endearing words, of which a strong man alone possesses the enchanting secret.

She sat and listened, whilst all around her was still. Madame Déroulède, at the farther end of the room, was softly muttering a few prayers.

They were all alone these two in the mad and beautiful world, which man has created for himself—the world of romance—that world more wonderful than any heaven, where only those may enter who have learned the sweet lesson of love. Déroulède roamed in it at will. He had created his own romance, wherein he was as a humble worshipper, spending his life in the service of his madonna.

===Chinese===
她坐着，听着，听他向她低语那些温柔的、令人心醉的字眼——那种迷人的秘法，唯有刚强的男子才懂得。

她坐着，听着，四周一片静寂。德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）在屋子另一头，轻声念着几句祷词。

这两个人，孤零零地留在一个癫狂而美丽的世界里——那个世人替自己营造的世界，浪漫的世界——那世界比任何天堂都更奇妙，唯有修过爱情这门甜美功课的人才进得去。德鲁莱德在其中任意遨游。他早已营造了自己的罗曼史，在这段罗曼史里，他是一个谦卑的崇拜者，一生一世服侍着他的圣母。

===Original===
And she too forgot the earth, forgot the reality, her oath, her crime and its punishment, and began to think that it was good to live, good to love, and good to have at her feet the one man in all the world whom she could fondly worship.

Who shall tell what he whispered? Enough that she listened and that she smiled; and he, seeing her smile, felt happy.

===Chinese===
而她也忘了尘世，忘了现实，忘了誓言、她的罪行和它的惩罚，开始觉得活着是好的，爱是好的，让普天之下唯一一个她能痴心崇拜的男人拜倒在自己脚边，也是好的。

他低声诉说的究竟是什么，谁能说得清？只要她听着，她笑了，也就够了；而他，见她微笑，便觉得幸福。

## XV Detected / XV 败露

===Original===
The opening and shutting of the door roused them both from their dreams.

Anne Mie, pale, trembling, with eyes looking wild and terrified, had glided into the room.

Déroulède had sprung to his feet. In a moment he had thrust his own happiness into the background at sight of the poor child’s obvious suffering. He went quickly towards her, and would have spoken to her, but she ran past him up to Madame Déroulède, as if she were beside herself with some unexplainable terror.

===Chinese===
开门又关门的声音，把他们两人都从各自的梦想中唤醒。

安娜·米溜进了屋子，脸色苍白，浑身发颤，眼中透着狂乱与惊怖。

德鲁莱德一跃而起。一看见这可怜的孩子分明在受苦，他霎时便把自己的幸福抛到了脑后。他快步向她走去，正想开口说话，她却从他身边跑了过去，直扑到德鲁莱德夫人跟前，像是被一种无从解释的恐怖逼得失了魂。

===Original===
“Anne Mie,” he said firmly, “what is it? Have those devils dared—”

In a moment reality had come rushing back upon him with full force, and bitter reproaches surged up in his heart against himself, for having in this moment of selfish joy forgotten those who looked up to him for help and protection.

He knew the temper of the brutes who had been set upon his track, knew that low-minded Merlin and his noisome ways, and blamed himself severely for having left Anne Mie and Pétronelle alone with him even for a few moments.

===Chinese===
「安娜·米，」他斩钉截铁地说，「怎么回事？那些恶鬼莫非胆敢——」

刹那之间，现实以全部的重量涌回他心头；他心里对自己的痛切责备翻涌而起：竟在这自私的欢愉时刻，把那班仰望他救助与庇护的人忘在了脑后。

他深知那班被派来盯他梢的畜生是什么脾性，深知那个下作的梅尔兰和他那些腌臜的行事作风，于是狠狠自责：竟让安娜·米和佩特罗内勒跟他单独相处，哪怕只是那么片刻。

===Original===
But Anne Mie quickly reassured him.

“They have not molested us much,” she said, speaking with a visible effort and enforced calmness. “Pétronelle and I were together, and they made us open all the cupboards and uncover all the dishes. They then asked us many questions.”

“Questions? Of what kind?” asked Déroulède.

===Chinese===
可是安娜·米很快便叫他放了心。

「他们没有怎么为难我们，」她说，看得出费了好大一番力气，强作镇定。「佩特罗内勒同我在一起，他们逼我们打开所有的柜橱，揭开所有盖着的盘盅。随后又盘问了好多话。」

「盘问？都问些什么？」德鲁莱德问。

===Original===
“About you, Paul,” replied Anne Mie, “and about maman, and also about—about the citizeness, your guest.”

Déroulède looked at her closely, vaguely wondering at the strange attitude of the child. She was evidently labouring under some strong excitement, and in her thin, brown little hand she was clutching a piece of paper.

“Anne Mie! Child,” he said very gently, “you seem quite upset—as if something terrible had happened. What is that paper you are holding, my dear?”

===Chinese===
「问的是你，保罗，」安娜·米答道，「还问起妈妈，也问起——问起那位女公民，您的客人。」

德鲁莱德定睛望着她，隐隐纳闷这孩子的神情怎么这样古怪。她分明被一种强烈的激动煎熬着；她那只瘦瘦的褐色小手里，紧紧攥着一张纸。

「安娜·米！我的孩子，」他十分温存地说，「你像是心神大乱——仿佛出了什么可怕的事。你手里拿着的那张纸是什么，亲爱的？」

===Original===
Anne Mie gazed down upon it. She was obviously making frantic efforts to maintain her self-possession.

Juliette at first sight of Anne Mie seemed literally to have been turned to stone. She sat upright, rigid as a statue, her eyes fixed upon the poor, crippled girl as if upon an inexorable judge, about to pronounce sentence upon her of life or death.

Instinct, that keen sense of coming danger which Nature sometimes gives to her elect, had told her that, within the next few seconds, her doom would be sealed; that Fate would descend upon her, holding the sword of Nemesis; and it was Anne Mie’s tiny, half-shrivelled hand which had placed that sword into the grasp of Fate.

===Chinese===
安娜·米低头凝视着那张纸。她分明在拼死撑住自己，不让自己失态。

朱丽叶特一望见安娜·米，简直就像当真化成了石头。她端坐着，僵直如一尊雕像，两眼死死盯住这可怜的跛足姑娘，仿佛盯住一位铁面无私的法官——那法官正要宣判她的生死。

本能——大自然有时赐给她少数宠儿的那种预感大祸将临的敏锐知觉——早已告诉她：不出几秒，她的劫数便要注定；命运将手执复仇女神涅墨西斯（Nemesis）之剑向她降临；而把那柄剑放进命运手中的，正是安娜·米那只细小的、半已枯缩的手。

===Original===
“What is that paper? Will you let me see it, Anne Mie?” repeated Déroulède.

“Citizen Merlin gave it to me just now,” began Anne Mie more quietly; “he seems very wroth at finding nothing compromising against you, Paul. They were a long time in the kitchen, and now they have gone to search my room and Pétronelle’s; but Merlin—oh! that awful man!—he seemed like a beast infuriated with his disappointment.”

“Yes, yes.”

===Chinese===
「那张纸上是什么？肯给我瞧瞧吗，安娜·米？」德鲁莱德又问了一遍。

「这张纸是梅尔兰公民方才交给我的，」安娜·米较为平静地开了口；「他没搜出什么能连累你的东西，保罗，似乎大为震怒。他们在厨房里待了好半天，如今又去搜我的房间和佩特罗内勒的房间了；可是梅尔兰——哦！那个可怕的人！——他像一头因失望而狂性大发的野兽。」

「是，是。」

===Original===
“I don’t know what he hoped to get out of me, for I told him that you never spoke to your mother or to me about your political business, and that I was not in the habit of listening at the keyholes.”

“Yes. And—”

“Then he began to speak of—of our guest—but, of course, there again I could tell him nothing. He seemed to be puzzled as to who had denounced you. He spoke about an anonymous denunciation, which reached the Public Prosecutor early this morning. It was written on a scrap of paper, and thrown into the public box, it seems, and—”

===Chinese===
「我不知道他想从我嘴里掏出什么，因为我告诉他，你从来不曾对你母亲、也不曾对我说过你的政治上的事，还说我不是惯于趴在钥匙孔上偷听的人。」

「是。后来呢——」

「于是他开始说起——说起我们的客人——可这个，我自然也是无可奉告。他似乎弄不清究竟是谁告发了你。他提起一桩匿名告发，说是今天一早就送到了公诉人手里。据说是写在一张小纸片上，扔进了公众告发信箱的，还有——」

===Original===
“It is indeed very strange,” said Déroulède, musing over this extraordinary occurrence, and still more over Anne Mie’s strange excitement in the telling of it. “I never knew I had a hidden enemy. I wonder if I shall ever find out—”

“That is just what I said to Citizen Merlin,” rejoined Anne Mie.

“What?”

===Chinese===
「这可真是桩怪事，」德鲁莱德说，一面思忖着这桩出奇的变故，而更使他纳罕的，是安娜·米讲述此事时那异样的激动。「我从不知道自己有个隐身的仇敌。不知道能不能有查明的一天——」

「我对梅尔兰公民说的正是这句话，」安娜·米应声道。

「什么？」

===Original===
“That I wondered if you, or—or any of us who love you, will ever find out who your hidden enemy might be.”

“It was a mistake to talk so fully with such a brute, little one.”

“I didn’t say much, and I thought it wisest to humour him, as he seemed to wish to talk on that subject.”

===Chinese===
「我说，不知道你，或者——或者我们这班爱你的人，能不能有查明的一天，查出你这个隐身的仇敌究竟是谁。」

「同这样一头畜生说这么多话，本就是个错，小家伙。」

「我没有多说什么；我看他像是想谈那个题目，便觉得顺着他周旋是最明智的。」

===Original===
“Well? And what did he say?”

“He laughed, and asked me if I would very much like to know.”

“I hope you said No, Anne Mie?”

===Chinese===
「后来呢？他说了什么？」

「他笑了笑，问我是不是非常想知道。」

「我想你是回答了『不』的吧，安娜·米？」

===Original===
“Indeed, indeed, I said Yes,” she retorted with sudden energy, her eyes fixed now upon Juliette, who still sat rigid and silent, watching every movement of Anne Mie from the moment in which she began to tell her story.

“Would I not wish to know who is your enemy, Paul—the creature who was base and treacherous enough to attempt to deliver you into the hands of those merciless villains? What wrong had you done to anyone?”

“Sh! Hush, Anne Mie! you are too excited,” he said, smiling now, in spite of himself, at the young girl’s vehemence over what he thought was but a trifle—the discovery of his own enemy.

===Chinese===
「实实在在，我说的正是『是』，」她突然激动地驳道，两眼此刻直盯住朱丽叶特；而朱丽叶特从她开口讲述的那一刻起，便一直僵坐着，一言不发，注视着安娜·米的一举一动。

「你的仇敌是谁，我难道会不想知道吗，保罗——那个家伙何等卑鄙奸诈，竟企图把你送进那班狠毒恶棍的手里？你又何曾亏待过什么人？」

「嘘！别作声，安娜·米！你太激动了，」他说。查出他自己的仇敌，在他看来不过是桩小事；见这姑娘为此如此激愤，他不由自主地微笑了。

===Original===
“I am sorry, Paul. How can I help being excited,” rejoined Anne Mie with quaint, pathetic gentleness, “when I speak of such base treachery, as that which Merlin has suggested?”

“Well? And what did he suggest?”

“He did more than suggest,” whispered Anne Mie almost inaudibly; “he gave me this paper—the anonymous denunciation which reached the Public Prosecutor this morning—he thought one of us might recognise the handwriting.”

===Chinese===
「对不起，保罗。说起这样的卑鄙背叛——梅尔兰影射出的那桩背叛——教我怎么激动得起来呢，」安娜·米应道，语调出奇地温存，带着几分惹人怜悯。

「后来呢？他究竟影射了什么？」

「他岂止是影射，」安娜·米低语道，声音低得几乎听不见；「他把这张纸给了我——就是今天一早送到公诉人手里的那份匿名告发——他想着，我们中间或许有人认得出那笔迹。」

===Original===
Then she paused, some five steps away from Déroulède, holding out towards him the crumpled paper, which up to now she had clutched determinedly in her hand. Déroulède was about to take it from her, and just before he had turned to do so, his eyes lighted on Juliette.

She said nothing, she had merely risen instinctively, and had reached Anne Mie’s side in less than the fraction of a second.

It was all a flash, and there was dead silence in the room, but in that one-hundredth part of a second, Déroulède had read guilt in the face of Juliette.

===Chinese===
说罢她便停住了，离德鲁莱德约莫五步远，把那张皱巴巴的纸朝他递过去；直到此刻，她一直把它牢牢攥在手里。德鲁莱德正要伸手去接，而就在他转身去接的一刹那，他的目光落到了朱丽叶特身上。

她一言不发，只是出于本能霍然起身，一瞬未及，人已到了安娜·米身旁。

一切恍如电光石火，屋里死一般寂静；然而就在那百分之一秒里，德鲁莱德已从朱丽叶特的脸上读出了负罪之色。

===Original===
It was nothing but instinct, a sudden, awful, unexplainable revelation. Her soul seemed suddenly to stand before him in all its misery and in all its sin.

It was as if the fire from heaven had descended in one terrific crash, burying beneath its devastating flames his ideals, his happiness, and his divinity. She was no longer there. His madonna had ceased to be.

There stood before him a beautiful woman, on whom he had lavished all the pent-up treasures of his love, whom he had succoured, sheltered, and protected, and who had repaid him thus.

===Chinese===
这无非是本能，一种突如其来的、可怕的、无从解释的启示。她的灵魂仿佛蓦地立到了他的面前，带着它全部的悲惨，全部的罪孽。

仿佛天火轰然一声降下，用它那毁灭一切的烈焰，把他的理想、他的幸福、他奉为神圣的那个偶像，统统埋葬在底下了。她已不在那里。他的圣母不复存在了。

站在他面前的，是一个美丽的女人：他曾把郁积心底的爱的珍藏，尽数倾注在她身上；他救助过她，庇护过她，保全过她；而她对他的报答，竟是这个。

===Original===
She had forced an entry into his house; she had spied upon him, dogged him, lied to him. The moment was too sudden, too awful for him to make even a wild guess at her motives. His entire life, his whole past, the present, and the future, were all blotted out in this awful dispersal of his most cherished dream. He had forgotten everything else save her appalling treachery; how could he even remember that once, long ago, in fair fight, he had killed her brother?

She did not even try now to hide her guilt.

A look of appeal, touching in its trustfulness, went out to him, begging him to spare her further shame. Perhaps she felt that love, such as his, could not be killed in a flash.

===Chinese===
她硬是闯进了他的家门；她窥伺过他，盯过他的梢，对他撒过谎。这时刻来得太突然、太可怕，他连她的动机都来不及胡乱猜上一猜。他的一生，他的全部过去、现在与将来，都在这场他最珍爱的幻梦的可怕破灭中一并被抹去了。除了她那可怖的背叛，他把一切都忘了；哪里还记得，很久以前，他曾在一场光明正大的决斗里杀死了她的哥哥？

此刻她甚至不曾试图掩饰自己的罪责。

一道哀恳的目光朝他投来，那份信赖动人得叫人心疼，求他免她再受进一步的羞辱。或许她觉得，像他那样的爱，总不至于在一瞬之间就被杀死。

===Original===
His entire nature was full of pity, and to that pity she made a final appeal, lest she should be humiliated before Madame Déroulède and Anne Mie.

And he, still under the spell of those magic moments when he had knelt at her feet, understood her prayer, and closing his eyes just for one brief moment in order to shut out forever that radiant vision of a pure angel whom he had worshipped, turned quietly to Anne Mie.

“Give me that paper, Anne Mie,” he said coldly. “I may perhaps recognise the handwriting of my most bitter enemy.”

===Chinese===
他整个的天性里充满了悲悯；她便向这悲悯作最后的吁求，只求不在德鲁莱德夫人和安娜·米面前蒙受羞辱。

而他，仍未脱出方才跪在她脚边那些魔幻时刻的咒力，听懂了她的默祷，便把眼睛闭上短短一瞬，好把那位他曾经膜拜的纯洁天使的光辉形象永远遮断，随即静静地转向安娜·米。

「把那张纸给我，安娜·米，」他冷冷地说，「我最凶恶的仇敌的笔迹，我说不定还认得出来。」

===Original===
“ ’Tis unnecessary now,” replied Anne Mie slowly, still gazing at the face of Juliette, in which she too had read what she wished to read.

The paper dropped out of her hand.

Déroulède stooped to pick it up. He unfolded it, smoothed it out, and then saw that it was blank.

===Chinese===
「如今不必了，」安娜·米慢慢答道，眼睛依旧望着朱丽叶特的脸；她也从那张脸上，读出了自己想要读出的东西。

纸从她手里落了下来。

德鲁莱德俯身把它拾起。他把它展开，抚平，随即看出那是一张白纸。

===Original===
“There is nothing written on this paper,” he said mechanically.

“No,” rejoined Anne Mie; “no other words save the story of her treachery.”

“What you have done is evil and wicked, Anne Mie.”

===Chinese===
「这纸上什么字也没有，」他木然地说。

「不错，」安娜·米应道，「纸上再没有别的字，只写着她那背叛的故事。」

「你干的事又恶又毒，安娜·米。」

===Original===
“Perhaps so; but I had guessed the truth, and I wished to know. God showed me this way, how to do it, and how to let you know as well.”

“The less you speak of God just now, Anne Mie, the better, I think. Will you attend to maman? she seems faint and ill.”

Madame Déroulède, silent and placid in her armchair, had watched the tragic scene before her, almost like a disinterested spectator. All her ideas and all her thoughts had been paralysed, since the moment when the first summons at the front door had warned her of the imminence of the peril to her son.

===Chinese===
「也许吧；可我早已猜出真相，我要弄个明白。是上帝把这条路指给我，教我怎样去做，也教我怎样让你知道。」

「我想，安娜·米，此刻你少提上帝为好。你去照看一下妈妈好不好？她像是发晕，身子难受。」

德鲁莱德夫人默然安坐在扶手椅里，看着眼前这场惨剧，几乎像个漠不相关的旁观者。自从前门第一声传唤叩响、警告她儿子危难迫在眉睫的那一刻起，她的一切观念、一切思绪便都僵住了。

===Original===
The final discovery of Juliette’s treachery had left her impassive. Since her son was in danger, she cared little as to whence that danger had come.

Obedient to Déroulède’s wish, Anne Mie was attending to the old lady’s comforts. The poor, crippled girl was already feeling the terrible reaction of her deed.

In her childish mind she had planned this way, in which to bring the traitor to shame. Anne Mie knew nothing, cared nothing, about the motives which had actuated Juliette; all she knew was that a terrible Judas-like deed had been perpetrated against the man, on whom she herself had lavished her pathetic, hopeless love.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特背叛的最终败露，并未使她动容。儿子既已身临险境，这险从何而来，她便顾不上计较了。

安娜·米依着德鲁莱德的心意，去照料老太太了。这可怜的跛足姑娘，已经开始尝到她那桩行径可怕的报应。

在她孩子气的心思里，她早就筹划好了这条叫叛徒当众蒙羞的路子。朱丽叶特受着什么动机驱使，安娜·米一无所知，也一概不问；她只知道，一桩可怕的、犹大式的勾当，已经加在了那个男人身上——而她曾把自己可怜而无望的爱，尽数倾注在那个人身上。

===Original===
All the pent-up jealousy which had tortured her for the past three weeks rose up, and goaded her into unmasking her rival.

Never for a moment did she doubt Juliette’s guilt. The god of love may be blind, tradition has so decreed it, but the demon of jealousy has a hundred eyes, more keen than those of the lynx.

Anne Mie, pushed aside by Merlin’s men when they forced their way into Déroulède’s study, had, nevertheless, followed them to the door. When the curtains were drawn aside and the room filled with light, she had seen Juliette enthroned, apparently calm and placid, upon the sofa.

===Chinese===
三个星期来折磨着她的那股郁积的妒火，此刻全部涌了起来，驱策着她去揭掉她情敌的面具。

朱丽叶特的罪责，她从没有一刻怀疑。爱神或许是盲目的，传统向来如此裁定；可嫉妒的魔鬼却生着一百只眼睛，比猞猁的眼还要尖利。

梅尔兰的手下强闯德鲁莱德的书房时，把她推到了一旁；她却仍旧跟到了门口。帷幔拉开、满屋通明之时，她望见朱丽叶特端坐在沙发上，显得那样安宁静穆。

===Original===
It was instinct, the instinct born of her own rejected passion, which caused her to read in the beautiful girl’s face all that lay hidden behind the pale, impassive mask. That same second sight made her understand Merlin’s hints and allusions. She caught every inflection of his voice, heard everything, saw everything.

And in the midst of her anxiety and her terrors for the man she loved, there was the wild, primitive, intensely human joy at the thought of bringing that enthroned idol, who had stolen his love, down to earth at last.

Anne Mie was not clever; she was simple and childish, with no complexity of passions or devious ways of intellect. It was her elemental jealousy which suggested the cunning plan for the unmasking of Juliette. She would make the girl cringe and fear, threaten her with discovery, and through her very terror shame her before Paul Déroulède.

===Chinese===
正是本能——由她自己被拒的痴情所生的本能——使她从这个美丽姑娘的脸上，读出了那苍白无波的面具背后隐藏的一切。也是同一种洞彻一切的直觉，使她听懂了梅尔兰的暗示与影射。他声调里的每一丝抑扬她都捉住了，什么都听见了，什么都看见了。

而在她为心上人悬心吊胆、惊惶恐惧的当儿，另有一种狂野的、原始的、十足人性的欢喜：一想到终于能把那尊窃据了他的爱的宝座上的偶像拖下尘寰，她就感到欢欣。

安娜·米并不聪明；她单纯，孩子气，没有繁复纠缠的激情，也没有迂曲的心计。替她出下这个揭穿朱丽叶特的乖巧主意的，正是她那原始本能的嫉妒。她要叫那姑娘畏缩、害怕，拿败露来恐吓她，再借她自己的恐惧，叫她在保罗·德鲁莱德面前出丑。

===Original===
And now it was all done; it had all occurred as she had planned it. Paul knew that his love had been wasted upon a liar and a traitor, and Juliette stood pale, humiliated, a veritable wreck of shamed humanity.

Anne Mie had triumphed, and was profoundly, abjectly wretched in her triumph. Great sobs seemed to tear at her very heartstrings. She had pulled down Paul’s idol from her pedestal, but the one look she had cast at his face had shown her that she had also wrecked his life.

He seemed almost old now. The earnest, restless gaze had gone from his eyes; he was staring mutely before him, twisting between nerveless fingers that blank scrap of paper, which had been the means of annihilating his dream.

===Chinese===
如今一切都已办成；一切都如她所筹划的那样发生了。保罗已知道他的爱错付给了一个撒谎的叛徒；而朱丽叶特立在那里，面色惨白，受尽屈辱，活脱脱一副蒙羞人性的残骸。

安娜·米胜利了，而在胜利之中，她深深地、卑屈地凄苦着。剧烈的呜咽仿佛在撕扯她的心弦。她把保罗的偶像从神座上拉了下来，可她朝他脸上投去的那一眼向她表明：她同时也毁掉了他的一生。

他此刻像是骤然见老了。他眼中那种恳切而不宁的凝望没有了；他默然直勾勾地望着前方，全无气力的手指拧弄着那张空白的小纸片——正是这张纸片，充当了毁灭他幻梦的工具。

===Original===
All energy of attitude, all strength of bearing, which were his chief characteristics, seemed to have gone. There was a look of complete blankness, of hopelessness in his listless gesture.

“How he loved her!” sighed Anne Mie, as she tenderly wrapped the shawl round Madame Déroulède’s shoulders.

Juliette had said nothing; it seemed as if her very life had gone out of her. She was a mere statue now, her mind numb, her heart dead, her very existence a fragile piece of mechanism. But she was looking at Déroulède. That one sense in her had remained alive: her sight.

===Chinese===
他姿态中所有的精力，举止中所有的力量——那原是他最显著的特质——似乎都消失了。他那没精打采的姿态里，带着一副全然空茫、全然绝望的神色。

「他是多么爱她啊！」安娜·米叹息道，一边温柔地把披巾拢上德鲁莱德夫人的肩头。

朱丽叶特一言未发；她的生命仿佛已从她的躯壳里流尽了。她此刻只是一尊石像：神志麻木，心如死灰，连她的存在也不过是件脆弱的机件罢了。可她还在望着德鲁莱德。她身上只有这一种官能还活着：视觉。

===Original===
She looked and looked: and saw every passing sign of mental agony on his face: the look of recognition of her guilt, the bewilderment at the appalling crash, and now that hideous deathlike emptiness of his soul and mind.

Never once did she detect horror or loathing. He had tried to save her from being further humiliated before his mother, but there was no hatred or contempt in his eyes, when he realised that she had been unmasked by a trick.

She looked and looked, for there was no hope in her, not even despair. There was nothing in her mind, nothing in her soul, but a great pall-like blank.

===Chinese===
她望着，望着，把他脸上每一掠过的精神剧痛都看在眼里：认出她罪孽的那一瞥，面对可怕崩塌的惶惑，以及此刻他灵魂与神智间那可怖的、死一般的空虚。

她自始至终没有在他身上看出恐怖或憎恶。他曾竭力使她免于在母亲面前再受羞辱；而当他明白她是被一条诡计揭穿时，他眼里既没有仇恨，也没有鄙夷。

她望着，望着，因为她心里已没有希望，连绝望也没有。她的心神里、灵魂里，什么都没有了，只剩下一大片柩衣般死寂的空白。

===Original===
Then gradually, as the minutes sped on, she saw the strong soul within him make a sudden fight against the darkness of his despair: the movement of the fingers became less listless; the powerful, energetic figure straightened itself out; remembrance of other matters, other interests than his own began to lift the overwhelming burden of his grief.

He remembered the letter-case containing the compromising papers. A vague wonder arose in him as to Juliette’s motives in warding off, through her concealment of it, the inevitable moment of its discovery by Merlin.

The thought that her entire being had undergone a change, and that she now wished to save him, never once entered his mind; if it had, he would have dismissed it as the outcome of maudlin sentimentality, the conceit of the fop, who believes his personality to be irresistible.

===Chinese===
而后，一分钟一分钟过去，她渐渐看见他体内那坚强的灵魂骤然奋起，与绝望的黑暗相搏：手指的动作不那么疲沓了；那矫健有力的身躯重新挺直了；想起别的事情、想起一些非关自身的关切，压在他心头那沉重的悲哀重负便开始松动。

他记起了那只装着罪证文件的信匣。朱丽叶特把信匣藏起来，借以推迟梅尔兰搜出它的那个必然时刻——她这样做居心何在？他心里隐隐浮起一丝疑讶。

说她整个人已起了变化、说她如今一心要救他——这样的念头，从未在他心中闪现过；纵然闪现过，他也会把它当作伤感的滥情而一笔挥去，当作纨绔子弟的自负——那种自以为个人魅力无可抗拒的自负。

===Original===
His own self-torturing humility pointed but to the one conclusion: that she had fooled him all along; fooled him when she sought his protection; fooled him when she taught him to love her; fooled him, above all, at the moment when, subjugated by the intensity of his passion, he had for one brief second ceased to worship in order to love.

When the bitter remembrance of that moment of sweetest folly rushed back to his aching brain, then at last did he look up at her with one final, agonised look of reproach, so great, so tender, and yet so final, that Anne Mie, who saw it, felt as if her own heart would break with the pity of it all.

But Juliette had caught the look too. The tension of her nerves seemed suddenly to relax. Memory rushed back upon her with tumultuous intensity. Very gradually her knees gave beneath her, and at last she knelt down on the floor before him, her golden head bent under the burden of her guilt and her shame.

===Chinese===
他那自我折磨的谦卑，只指向一个结论：她自始至终在愚弄他；她来求他庇护时，愚弄他；她教他爱上她时，愚弄他；而尤其在那样的时刻愚弄他——那时，他被自己炽烈激情的强烈震慑，曾有短短一瞬，停止了崇拜，转而去爱。

当那阵最甜蜜的愚行的辛酸回忆猛然涌回他作痛的头脑时，他终于抬起头来，向她投去最后的一瞥——那是痛楚的、责备的一瞥，那样深沉，那样温柔，却又那样决绝；安娜·米看见了这一眼，只觉得自己的心都要为这一切的怜悯而碎了。

可朱丽叶特也接住了这一眼。她紧绷的神经仿佛骤然松弛下来。回忆以汹涌之势猛然涌回她的心头。她的双膝渐渐软了下去，终于在他面前的地板上跪了下去，金色的头颅低垂着，负着她罪孽与羞耻的重担。

## XVI Under Arrest / XVI 被捕

===Original===
Déroulède did not attempt to go to her.

Only presently, when the heavy footsteps of Merlin and his men were once more heard upon the landing, she quietly rose to her feet.

She had accomplished her act of humiliation and repentance, there before them all. She looked for the last time upon those whom she had so deeply wronged, and in her heart spoke an eternal farewell to that great, and mighty, and holy love which she had called forth and then had so hopelessly crushed.

Now she was ready for the atonement.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）没有试图走到她跟前去。

直到片刻之后，梅尔兰（Merlin）和他手下人沉重的脚步声再度在楼梯平台上响起，她才悄悄地站起身来。

她那谦卑悔罪的举动，已经当着众人的面完成了。她最后一次望了望那些被她深深冤屈的人，在心中向那伟大、雄浑而神圣的爱诀别——那爱是她唤起的，却又被她绝望地碾碎了。

如今，她已准备好去赎罪了。

===Original===
Merlin had already swaggered into the room. The long and arduous search throughout the house had not improved either his temper or his personal appearance. He was more covered with grime than he had been before, and his narrow forehead had almost disappeared beneath the tangled mass of his ill-kempt hair, which he had perpetually tugged forward and roughed up in his angry impatience.

One look at his face had already told Juliette what she wished to know. He had searched her room, and found the fragments of burnt paper, which she had purposely left in the ashpan.

How he would act now was the one thing of importance left for Juliette to ponder over. That she would not escape arrest and condemnation was at once made clear to her. Merlin’s look of sneering contempt, when he glanced towards her, had told her that.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰早已大摇大摆地走进屋来。这趟搜遍全宅的漫长苦差，既没有改善他的脾气，也没有改善他的仪表。他比先前更是满身污垢，窄窄的额头几乎全埋没在那蓬乱不洁的乱发底下——那头发，正是他在恼怒焦躁中不住往前拽、胡乱抓挠的结果。

只消看他一眼，朱丽叶特（Juliette）想知道的事便有了答案。他搜过她的房间，找到了那几片烧剩的纸屑——是她故意留在灰斗里的。

他如今将如何行事，是留给朱丽叶特思量的唯一要紧之事。她逃不过被捕与定罪——这一点立刻就向她挑明了。梅尔兰朝她掠来一眼，那讥诮轻蔑的神情，已经告诉了她。

===Original===
Déroulède himself had been conscious of a feeling of intense relief when the men reentered the room. The tension had become unendurable. When he saw his dethroned madonna kneel in humiliation at his feet, an overwhelming pain had wrenched his very heartstrings.

And yet he could not go to her. The passionate, human nature within him felt a certain proud exultation at seeing her there.

She was not above him now, she was no longer akin to the angels.

===Chinese===
而德鲁莱德自己，在那些人重新走进屋里时，心头涌起的却是一阵强烈的如释重负。先前那份紧张，已变得叫人无法忍受。眼见他那位被拉下神坛的圣母屈辱地跪在自己脚下，一阵压倒一切的剧痛绞紧了他全部的心弦。

可他终究不能走到她跟前去。他周身那炽热的人之天性，见她跪在那里，竟隐隐感到一种骄傲的快意。

她不再高踞于他之上了，她不再是天使的同类了。

===Original===
He had given no further thought to his own immediate danger. Vaguely he guessed that Merlin would find the leather case. Where it was he could not tell; perhaps Juliette herself had handed it to the soldiers. She had only hidden it for a few moments, out of impulse perhaps, fearing lest, at the first instant of its discovery, Merlin might betray her.

He remembered now those hints and insinuations which had gone out from the Terrorist to Juliette whilst the search was being conducted in the study. At the time he had merely looked upon these as a base attempt at insult, and had tortured himself almost beyond bearing, in the endeavour to refrain from punishing that evil-mouthed creature, who dared to bandy words with his madonna.

But now he understood, and felt his very soul writhing with shame at the remembrance of it all.

===Chinese===
至于自己眼前的危险，他已不再去想。他隐约猜到，梅尔兰会找到那只皮面信匣。它如今在何处，他说不上来；也许是朱丽叶特亲手把它交给了士兵。她不过是把匣子藏了短短片刻，也许是出于一时冲动，唯恐它一被搜见，梅尔兰头一个瞬间就会把她出卖。

此刻他记起来了：搜查在书房里进行时，那恐怖分子向朱丽叶特抛去的那些暗示与影射。当时他只把它们看作一桩卑鄙的侮辱企图；为了克制住不去惩罚那个满嘴恶毒的东西——那东西竟敢同他的圣母斗嘴——他几乎把自己折磨得痛不可当。

可如今他明白了；一想起这一切，他只觉得整个灵魂都羞愧得扭作一团。

===Original===
Oh yes; the return of Merlin and his men, the presence of these grimy, degraded brutes, was welcome now. He would have wished to crowd in the entire world, the universe and its population, between him and his fallen idol.

Merlin’s manner towards him had lost nothing of its ironical benevolence. There was even a touch of obsequiousness apparent in the ugly face, as the representative of the people approached the popular Citizen-Deputy.

“Citizen-Deputy,” began Merlin, “I have to bring you the welcome news, that we have found nothing in your house that in any way can cast suspicion upon your loyalty to the Republic. My orders, however, were to bring you before the Committee of Public Safety, whether I had found proofs of your guilt or not. I have found none.”

===Chinese===
哦，是的；梅尔兰一伙的归来，这些满身污垢、堕落不堪的粗坯的在场，如今反倒是大受欢迎的了。他恨不得把整个世界、整个宇宙连同它的芸芸众生，统统塞进他和他那倒下的偶像之间。

梅尔兰对他的态度，丝毫没有丢掉那副讥讽的和善。当这位人民代表走近这位深得民心的公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）时，那张丑脸上甚至还显出几分谄媚。

「公会议员，」梅尔兰开口说道，「我得向您报个好消息：我们在您宅子里什么也没搜到，没有任何东西能对您效忠共和国一事投下丝毫疑影。不过，我的命令是：不论搜没搜到您有罪的证据，都得把您带到救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）去。我没有搜到。」

===Original===
He was watching Déroulède keenly, hoping even at this eleventh hour to detect a look or a sign, which would furnish him with the proofs for which he was seeking. The slightest suggestion of relief on Déroulède’s part, a sigh of satisfaction, would have been sufficient at this moment, to convince him and the Committee of Public Safety that the Citizen-Deputy was guilty after all.

But Déroulède never moved. He was sufficiently master of himself not to express either surprise or satisfaction. Yet he felt both—satisfaction not for his own safety, but because of his mother and Anne Mie, whom he would immediately send out of the country, out of all danger; and also because of her, of Juliette Marny, his guest, who, whatever she may have done against him, had still a claim on his protection. His feeling of surprise was less keen, and quite transient. Merlin had not found the letter-case. Juliette, stricken with tardy remorse perhaps, had succeeded in concealing it. The matter had practically ceased to interest him. It was equally galling to owe his betrayal or his ultimate safety to her.

He kissed his mother tenderly, bidding her goodbye, and pressed Anne Mie’s timid little hand warmly between his own. He did what he could to reassure them, but, for their own sakes, he dared say nothing before Merlin, as to his plans for their safety.

After that he was ready to follow the soldiers.

===Chinese===
他紧盯着德鲁莱德，巴望即便在这最后关头，也能捕捉到一道眼色、一个迹象，好向他提供他正苦求不得的证据。此刻，只消德鲁莱德流露出些许如释重负的影子，发出一声快意的叹息，就足以让他和救国委员会确信：这位公会议员终究是有罪的。

可德鲁莱德纹丝不动。他把自己克制得极好，既不露惊讶，也不露欣慰。然而两者他都感受到了——欣慰，不是为了自己安然无恙，而是为了他的母亲和安娜·米（Anne Mie），他会立即送她们出境，脱离一切险境；也是为了她，为了朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）——他的客人，无论她对他做过什么，依然有权得到他的保护。至于惊讶，则淡漠得多，而且转瞬即逝。梅尔兰没有找到那只信匣。朱丽叶特——也许是迟来的悔恨刺中了她——终归把它藏住了。这件事实际上已不再让他挂怀。自己的败露也好，最终的生还也好，都要拜她所赐，这滋味同样苦涩难咽。

他温柔地吻别母亲，又把安娜·米那只怯生生的小手热热地握在自己两手中间。他尽其所能宽慰她们；可为了她们自身的缘故，他在梅尔兰面前，对自己为她们安全所作的安排，半个字也不敢露。

这之后，他便准备跟士兵们走了。

===Original===
As he passed close to Juliette he bowed, and almost inaudibly whispered:

“Adieu!”

She heard the whisper, but did not respond. Her look alone gave him the reply to his eternal farewell.

His footsteps and those of his escort were heard echoing down the staircase, then the hall door to open and shut. Through the open window came the sound of hoarse cheering as the popular Citizen-Deputy appeared in the street.

===Chinese===
打朱丽叶特身边经过时，他微微一躬身，声音低得几乎听不见，耳语道：

「Adieu!（别了！）」

她听见了这声耳语，却没有作答。唯有一瞥目光，给了他那声永诀道别以回答。

楼梯间里，回荡着他与押解者远去的脚步声，随后是门厅大门开而复关的声响。这位深得民心的公会议员一出现在街上，沙哑的欢呼声便透过敞开的窗户传了进来。

===Original===
Merlin, with two men beside him, remained under the portico; he told off the other two to escort Déroulède as far as the Hall of Justice, where sat the members of the Committee of Public Safety. The Terrorist had a vague fear that the Citizen-Deputy would speak to the mob.

An unruly crowd of women had evidently been awaiting his appearance. The news had quickly spread along the streets that Merlin, Merlin himself, the ardent, bloodthirsty Jacobin, had made a descent upon Paul Déroulède’s house, escorted by four soldiers. Such an indignity, put upon the man they most trusted in the entire assembly of the Convention, had greatly incensed the crowd. The women jeered at the soldiers as soon as they appeared, and Merlin dared not actually forbid Déroulède to speak.

“*À la lanterne, vieux crétin!*” shouted one of the women, thrusting her fist under Merlin’s nose.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰带着两个人留在门廊底下；他点派另外两人，把德鲁莱德一路押送到司法宫（Hall of Justice）去——救国委员会的成员们就在那里坐镇。这恐怖分子隐隐担心，公会议员会对暴民开口。

显然，一群桀骜难驯的妇女早已在等候他露面。消息沿着街巷不胫而走：梅尔兰——正是梅尔兰本人，那个狂热嗜血的雅各宾党人（Jacobin）——带着四名大兵，突袭了保罗·德鲁莱德的宅邸。国民公会满堂议员中他们最信赖的人，竟受此奇耻大辱，人群被激怒到了极点。士兵们一露面，妇女们便嘘声奚落；而梅尔兰当真不敢禁止德鲁莱德开口。

「*À la lanterne, vieux crétin!*（吊上路灯去，老蠢货！）」一名妇女抡起拳头，直杵到梅尔兰的鼻子底下，高声喊道。

===Original===
“Give the word, Citizen-Deputy,” rejoined another, “and we’ll break his ugly face. *Nous lui casserons la gueule!*”

“*À la lanterne! À la lanterne!*”

One word from Déroulède now would have caused an open riot, and in those days self-defence against the mob was construed into enmity against the people.

===Chinese===
「您只管下个令，公会议员，」另一个应声道，「我们就砸烂他那副丑脸。*Nous lui casserons la gueule!*（我们砸烂他的嘴脸！）」

「*À la lanterne! À la lanterne!*（吊上路灯去！吊上路灯去！）」

这时候，德鲁莱德只需吐出一个字，便会激起一场明火执仗的暴乱；而在那个年月，自卫以抗暴民，就会被曲解为与人民为敌。

===Original===
Merlin’s work, too, was not yet accomplished. He had had no intention of escorting Déroulède himself; he had still important business to transact inside the house which he had just quitted, and had merely wished to get the Citizen-Deputy well out of the way, before he went upstairs again.

Moreover, he had expected something of a riot in the streets. The temper of the people of Paris was at fever heat just now. The hatred of the populace against a certain class, and against certain individuals, was only equalled by their enthusiasm in favour of others.

They had worshipped Marat for his squalor and his vices; they worshipped Danton for his energy and Robespierre for his calm; they worshipped Déroulède for his voice, his gentleness and his pity, for his care of their children and the eloquence of his speech.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰的差事，也还没有办完。他原本就无意亲自押送德鲁莱德；他还有要紧的事，要在这座刚刚离开的宅子里办理，只是想把公会议员先打发得远远的，然后自己再上楼去。

再者，他原就料到街上会有几分暴乱。巴黎民众的脾气，此刻正烧得像发热病一般。民众对某一阶级、某几个人的仇恨，同他们对另一些人的热情，恰恰一样炽烈，难分高下。

他们崇拜马拉（Marat），是为他的邋遢与恶德；崇拜丹东（Danton），是为他的精力；崇拜罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre），是为他的沉静；而崇拜德鲁莱德，是为他的声音、他的温厚、他的悲悯，为他对他们孩子的照拂，和他演说的雄辩。

===Original===
It was that eloquence which Merlin feared now; but he little knew the type of man he had to deal with.

Déroulède’s influence over the most unruly, the most vicious populace the history of the world has ever known, was not obtained through fanning its passions. That popularity, though brilliant, is always ephemeral. The passions of a mob will invariably turn against those who have helped to rouse them. Marat did not live to see the waning of his star; Danton was dragged to the guillotine by those whom he had taught to look upon that instrument of death as the only possible and unanswerable political argument; Robespierre succumbed to the orgies of bloodshed he himself had brought about. But Déroulède remained master of the people of Paris for as long as he chose to exert that mastery. When they listened to him they felt better, nobler, less hopelessly degraded.

He kept up in their poor, misguided hearts that last flickering sense of manhood which their bloodthirsty tyrants, under the guise of Fraternity and Equality, were doing their best to smother.

Even now, when he might have turned the temper of the small crowd outside his door to his own advantage, he preferred to say nothing; he even pacified them with a gesture.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰此刻害怕的，正是这份雄辩；可他要对付的是哪一类人，他心里全然无数。

德鲁莱德对这世上自有历史以来最桀骜不驯、最凶顽败坏的民众所拥有的影响力，并不是靠煽动他们的激情得来的。那种威望，纵然辉煌，总归昙花一现；暴民的激情，迟早要反噬那些帮他们点燃激情的人。马拉没来得及亲眼看见自己星光的黯淡；丹东，是被他亲手教会把那件死亡的器械奉为唯一可能、无可答辩的政治论据的那伙人，拖上断头台的；罗伯斯庇尔，则在他自己掀起的血腥狂宴中覆灭。而德鲁莱德，只要他愿意行使那份驾驭之力，便始终是巴黎民众的主人。听他讲话的时候，他们觉得自己好了一些，高贵了一些，不那么无可救药地堕落了。

他把那一缕最后的、摇曳欲熄的为人尊严，保存在他们可怜而被引入歧途的心里——而他们那些嗜血的暴君，正打着博爱与平等的旗号，竭力要把这尊严扼杀。

便在此时，他本可以把门外这一小群人的激愤扭转为于己有利的声势，却宁可一言不发；他甚至只做了一个手势，便把他们安抚下去。

===Original===
He well knew that those whom he incited against Merlin now would, once their blood was up, probably turn against him in less than half-an-hour.

Merlin, who all along had meant to return to the house, took his opportunity now. He allowed Déroulède and the two men to go on ahead, and beat a hasty retreat back into the house, followed by the jeers of the women.

“*À la lanterne, vieux crétin!*” they shouted as soon as the hall door was once more closed in their faces. A few of them began hammering against the door with their fists; then they realised that their special favourite, Citizen-Deputy Déroulède, was marching along between two soldiers, as if he were a prisoner. The word went round that he was under arrest, and was being taken to the Hall of Justice—a prisoner.

This was not to be. The mob of Paris had been taught that it was the master in the city, and it had learned its lesson well. For the moment it had chosen to take Paul Déroulède under its special protection, and as a guard of honour to him—the women in ragged kirtles, the men with bare legs and stripped to the waist, the children all yelling, hooting, and shrieking—followed him, to see that none dared harm him.

===Chinese===
他心里很清楚：此刻被他煽起来反对梅尔兰的这些人，一旦热血上头，不出半小时，多半就会掉转头来对付他自己。

梅尔兰原就打算回到宅子里去，此刻便抓住了时机。他让德鲁莱德和那两个人先行，自己则在妇女们的嘲骂声中，慌忙退回宅内。

门厅大门再度当着他们的面关上，他们便立刻喊了起来：「*À la lanterne, vieux crétin!*（吊上路灯去，老蠢货！）」有几个人开始抡起拳头砸门；随后他们才醒悟过来：他们最宠爱的人物——公会议员德鲁莱德——正走在两个士兵中间，活像个囚犯。消息传开了：他被捕了，正作为囚犯被押往司法宫。

但这样的事，是断断容不得的。巴黎的暴民素来受的教导是：他们才是这座城市的主人；这一课，他们学得极好。这一刻，他们决意把保罗·德鲁莱德置于自己的特别保护之下，并充作他的仪仗卫队——身穿破烂衬裙的妇女，光着两腿、赤着上身的汉子，喊的喊、嘘的嘘、尖叫的尖叫的孩子们——一路跟随他，看谁敢伤他一根毫毛。

## XVII Atonement / XVII 赎罪

===Original===
Merlin waited a while in the hall, until he heard the noise of the shrieking crowd gradually die away in the distance, then with a grunt of satisfaction he one more mounted the stairs.

All these events outside had occurred during a very few minutes, and Madame Déroulède and Anne Mie had been too anxious as to what was happening in the streets, to take any notice of Juliette.

They had not dared to step out on to the balcony to see what was going on, and, therefore, did not understand what the reopening and shutting of the front door had meant.

The next instant, however, Merlin’s heavy, slouching footsteps on the stairs had caused Anne Mie to look round in alarm.

===Chinese===
梅尔兰（Merlin）在大厅里等了片刻，直到听见那群尖叫的人喧闹的声浪渐渐消散在远处，才满意地哼了一声，重又登上楼梯。

外面这一连串变故，前后不过短短几分钟；德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）和安娜·米（Anne Mie）一心悬念着街上的动静，全然没有留意到朱丽叶特（Juliette）。

她们不敢走到阳台上去看看外面究竟出了什么事，因此也就不明白，前门重新开了又关上，到底意味着什么。

然而紧接着，楼梯上传来梅尔兰那沉重拖沓的脚步声，吓得安娜·米惶然回过头来。

===Original===
“It is only the soldiers come back for me,” said Juliette quietly.

“For you?”

“Yes; they are coming to take me away. I suppose they did not wish to do it in the presence of Mr. Déroulède, for fear—”

She had no time to say more. Anne Mie was still looking at her in awed and mute surprise, when Merlin entered the room.

===Chinese===
「不过是士兵回来提我来了。」朱丽叶特平静地说。

「提您？」

「是的；他们是来带我走的。我想，他们是不愿意当着德鲁莱德先生的面下手，恐怕——」

她来不及再往下说了。梅尔兰走进房来时，安娜·米还怔怔地望着她，又是惊惧，又是哑然的错愕。

===Original===
In his hand he held a leather case, all torn, and split at one end, and a few tiny scraps of half-charred paper. He walked straight up to Juliette, and roughly thrust the case and papers into her face.

“These are yours?” he said roughly.

“Yes.”

“I suppose you know where they were found?”

She nodded quietly in reply.

===Chinese===
他手里拿着一只皮匣子，通体撕裂，一端绽开，另外还有几片烧焦了一半的碎纸屑。他径直走到朱丽叶特面前，把匣子和纸片粗暴地直戳到她脸前。

「这些是你的东西？」他粗声粗气地说。

「是的。」

「想必你知道它们是在哪儿搜出来的？」

她平静地点一点头，算是回答。

===Original===
“What were these papers which you burnt?”

“Love letters.”

“You lie!”

She shrugged her shoulders.

“As you please,” she said curtly.

===Chinese===
「你烧掉的是些什么文件？」

「情书。」

「你撒谎！」

她耸了耸肩。

「随你的便。」她淡淡地答道。

===Original===
“What were these papers?” he repeated, with a loud obscene oath which, however, had not the power to disturb the young girl’s serenity.

“I have told you,” she said: “love letters, which I wished to burn.”

“Who was your lover?” he asked.

Then as she did not reply he indicated the street, where cries of “Déroulède! Vive Déroulède!” still echoed from afar.

===Chinese===
「这些到底是什么文件？」他重复道，中间夹着一句响亮而污秽的咒骂；可这句咒骂却全然搅扰不了这年轻姑娘的宁静。

「我已经告诉你了，」她说：「是情书，我想把它们烧掉。」

「你的情夫是谁？」他问。

见她不答，他便朝街上指了一指——那边，「Déroulède! Vive Déroulède!」（德鲁莱德！德鲁莱德万岁！）的呼喊，还在远处回荡。

===Original===
“Were the letters from him?”

“No.”

“You had more than one lover, then?”

He laughed, and a hideous leer seemed further to distort his ugly countenance.

===Chinese===
「那些信是他写的？」

「不是。」

「那么，你的情夫不止一个？」

他放声大笑，一脸狰狞的淫邪，仿佛把他那张丑陋的面孔扭得更其丑陋了。

===Original===
He thrust his face quite close to hers, and she closed her eyes, sick with the horror of this contact with the degraded wretch. Even Anne Mie had uttered a cry of sympathy at sight of this evil-smelling, squalid creature torturing, with his close proximity, the beautiful, refined girl before him.

With a rough gesture he put his clawlike hand under her delicate chin, forcing her to turn round and to look at him. She shuddered at the loathsome touch, but her quietude never forsook her for a moment.

It was into the power of wretches such as this man, that she had wilfully delivered the man she loved. This brutish creature’s familiarity put the finishing touch to her own degradation, but it gave her the courage to carry through her purpose to the end.

“You had more than one lover, then?” said Merlin, with a laugh which would have pleased the devil himself. “And you wished to send one of them to the guillotine in order to make way for the other? Was that it?”

===Chinese===
他把脸凑到几乎贴着她的脸，她闭上了眼睛——同这个堕落下贱的恶棍如此贴近，叫她恶心欲呕。眼见这个恶臭熏人、邋遢不堪的家伙，这样一步步逼近着，折磨面前这位美丽高雅的姑娘，连安娜·米也发出了一声不忍的惊呼。

他粗暴地一抬手，把那只鹰爪般的手伸到她秀气的下颏底下，逼她转过脸来看他。那令人作呕的触碰使她战栗，可她的沉静自若却一刻也不曾离开她。

她所爱的男人，正是被她存心亲手送进了这等恶棍的掌心。这畜生般的家伙的狎近，给她自身的堕落添上了最后一笔，却也给了她把主意贯彻到底的勇气。

「这么说，你的情夫不止一个？」梅尔兰说道，那笑声连魔鬼本人听了也要称心。「你想把其中一个送上断头台，好给另一个腾出位子？是不是这样？」

===Original===
“Was that it?” he repeated, suddenly seizing one of her wrists, and giving it a savage twist, so that she almost screamed with the pain.

“Yes,” she replied firmly.

“Do you know that you brought me here on a fool’s errand?” he asked viciously; “that the Citizen-Deputy Déroulède cannot be sent to the guillotine on mere suspicion, eh? Did you know that, when you wrote out that denunciation?”

“No; I did not know.”

===Chinese===
「是不是这样？」他又说了一遍，猛地攥住她的一只手腕，狠狠地一拧，拧得她几乎痛得叫出声来。

「是的。」她坚定地答道。

「你可知道，你害得我到这儿来空跑了一趟？」他恶狠狠地问，「公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）德鲁莱德，是光凭猜疑就能送上断头台去的吗，呃？你写下那份告发信的时候，可知道这一层？」

「不；我不知道。」

===Original===
“You thought we could arrest him on mere suspicion?”

“Yes.”

“You knew he was innocent?”

“I knew it.”

===Chinese===
「你以为光凭猜疑就能逮捕他？」

「是的。」

「你知道他是清白的？」

「我知道。」

===Original===
“Why did you burn your love letters?”

“I was afraid that they would be found, and would be brought under the notice of the Citizen-Deputy.”

“A splendid combination, ma foi!” said Merlin, with an oath, as he turned to the two other women, who sat pale and shrinking in a corner of the room, not understanding what was going on, not knowing what to think or what to believe. They had known nothing of Déroulède’s plans for the escape of Marie Antoinette, they didn’t know what the letter-case had contained, and yet they both vaguely felt that the beautiful girl, who stood up so calmly before the loathsome Terrorist, was not a wanton, as she tried to make out, but only misguided, mad perhaps—perhaps a martyr.

“Did you know anything of this?” queried Merlin roughly from trembling Anne Mie.

===Chinese===
「你为什么要烧掉你的情书？」

「我怕它们会被人搜出来，送到公会议员的眼皮底下。」

「真是绝妙的连环计，ma foi（说真的）！」梅尔兰咒骂一声，转身朝着屋里的另外两个女人；她们脸色苍白，瑟缩地坐在屋角，不明白出了什么事，不知该作何想，也不知该信什么。德鲁莱德营救玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）脱逃的计划，她们一无所知；那只信匣里装过什么，她们也不知情；可她们俩都隐隐感觉到，这个在面目可憎的恐怖分子面前站得如此安详的美丽姑娘，并不像她竭力装出的那样，是个放荡的女人，她只是误入歧途，也许是疯了——也许，是一位殉道者。

「这件事你可知道一点底细？」梅尔兰粗声粗气地问浑身发抖的安娜·米。

===Original===
“Nothing,” she replied.

“No one knew anything of my private affairs or of my private correspondence,” said Juliette coldly; “as you say, it was a splendid combination. I had hoped that it would succeed. But I understand now that Citizen-Deputy Déroulède is a personage of too much importance to be brought to trial on mere suspicion, and my denunciation of him was not based on facts.”

“And do you know, my fine aristocrat,” sneered Merlin viciously, “that it is not wise either to fool the Committee of Public Safety, or to denounce without cause one of the representatives of the people?”

“I know,” she rejoined quietly, “that you, Citizen Merlin, are determined that someone shall pay for this day’s blunder. You dare not now attack the Citizen-Deputy, and so you must be content with me.”

===Chinese===
「什么都不知道。」她答道。

「谁也不知道我的私事，或是我的私人信函，」朱丽叶特冷冷地说，「正如您所说，这是一出绝妙的连环计。我本指望它会成功。可我现在明白了，公会议员德鲁莱德是这样一位显要人物，光凭猜疑是不能送交审判的，而我对他的告发，并无事实根据。」

「那么你可知道，我标致的贵族小姐，」梅尔兰恶毒地讥笑道，「愚弄救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety），或是无缘无故告发人民代表中的一员，都算不得明智？」

「我知道，」她平静地应道，「您，梅尔兰公民，已经拿定了主意，非要一个人来为今天这场失算付出代价不可。如今您不敢再去碰那位公会议员，便只好拿我来将就了。」

===Original===
“Enough of this talk now; I have no time to bandy words with aristos,” he said roughly. “Come now, follow the men quietly. Resistance would only aggravate your case.”

“I am quite prepared to follow you. May I speak two words to my friends before I go?”

“No.”

“I may never be able to speak to them again.”

===Chinese===
「废话到此为止；我没有工夫同贵族老爷们斗嘴。」他粗声粗气地说。「来吧，安安静静地跟他们走。抵抗只会加重你的罪名。」

「我完全愿意跟您走。临行之前，容我跟我的朋友说两句话，行吗？」

「不行。」

「我也许再也没有机会同他们说话了。」

===Original===
“I have said No, and I mean No. Now then, forward. March! I have wasted too much time already.”

Juliette was too proud to insist any further. She had hoped, by one word, to soften Madame Déroulède’s and Anne Mie’s heart towards her. She did not know whether they believed that miserable lie which she had been telling to Merlin; she only guessed that for the moment they still thought her the betrayer of Paul Déroulède.

But that one word was not to be spoken. She would have to go forth to her certain trial, to her probable death, under the awful cloud, which she herself had brought over her own life.

She turned quietly, and walked towards the door, where the two men already stood at attention.

===Chinese===
「我说了不行，就是不行。好了，往前走。开步走！我已经耽搁了太多时间。」

朱丽叶特的自尊心太强，不肯再往下恳求。她原指望凭一句话，就能叫德鲁莱德夫人和安娜·米的心向她软下来。她不知道她们信不信她对梅尔兰编造的那段可怜的谎话；她只是猜到，此刻她们多半还把她当作出卖保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）的人。

可那句话终究没能说出口。她只能顶着那团由她亲手罩上自己生命的可怕阴云，走向那场注定要来的审判，走向那多半是死亡的结局。

她静静地转过身，朝门口走去；那两条汉子已在门边立正等候。

===Original===
Then it was that some heaven-born instinct seemed suddenly to guide Anne Mie. The crippled girl was face to face with a psychological problem, which in itself was far beyond her comprehension, but vaguely she felt that it was a problem. Something in Juliette’s face had already caused her to bitterly repent her action towards her, and now, as this beautiful, refined woman was about to pass from under the shelter of this roof, to the cruel publicity and terrible torture of that awful revolutionary tribunal, Anne Mie’s whole heart went out to her in boundless sympathy.

Before Merlin or the men could prevent her, she had run up to Juliette, taken her hand, which hung listless and cold, and kissed it tenderly.

Juliette seemed to wake as if from a dream. She looked down at Anne Mie with a glance of hope, almost of joy, and whispered:

“It was an oath—I swore it to my father and my dead brother. Tell him.”

===Chinese===
恰在此时，一种天赐的直觉仿佛蓦地引导着安娜·米。这个跛足的姑娘正面对着一个心理上的难题，它本身远远超出了她的理解力，可她却隐隐觉得，那是一个难题。朱丽叶特脸上的某种神色，早已使她为自己待她的行径悔恨万分；而此刻，眼看这位美丽高雅的女人就要离开这屋檐的庇护，被抛到那座骇人的革命法庭上，去承受残忍的当众曝露与可怕的酷刑折磨，安娜·米的整颗心都化作无边的同情，向她涌去。

不等梅尔兰或那两条汉子拦阻，她已经奔到朱丽叶特跟前，握住她那只垂着、冰冷无力的手，温柔地吻了一下。

朱丽叶特仿佛从一场梦中醒来。她垂眼望着安娜·米，目光里透出希望，几乎是喜悦，随即低声说道：

「那是一个誓言——我对我的父亲和我死去的哥哥发过誓。告诉他。」

===Original===
Anne Mie could only nod; she could not speak, for her tears were choking her.

“But I’ll atone—with my life. Tell him,” whispered Juliette.

“Now then,” shouted Merlin, “out of the way, hunchback, unless you want to come along too.”

“Forgive me,” said Anne Mie through her tears.

===Chinese===
安娜·米只能点头；她说不出话来，泪水哽住了她的喉咙。

「但我会赎罪的——用我的性命。告诉他。」朱丽叶特低声说。

「好了没有，」梅尔兰喝道，「滚开，驼背，除非你也想跟着一块儿走。」

「原谅我。」安娜·米含泪说道。

===Original===
Then the men pushed her roughly aside. But at the door Juliette turned to her once more, and said:

“Pétronelle—take care of her—”

And with a firm step she followed the soldiers out of the room.

Presently the front door was heard to open, then to shut with a loud bang, and the house in the Rue Ecole de Médecine was left in silence.

===Chinese===
随后，那两条汉子把她粗暴地推向一旁。可走到门口，朱丽叶特又一次回头对她说道：

「佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）——照顾好她——」

说罢，她迈着坚定的步伐，跟着士兵们走出了房间。

片刻之后，便听得前门开了，跟着砰的一声重重关上，医学院街（Rue Ecole de Médecine）上的这所宅子，便沉入了一片寂静。

## XVIII In the Luxembourg Prison / XVIII 卢森堡狱中

===Original===
Juliette was alone at last—that is to say, comparatively alone, for there were too many aristocrats, too many criminals and traitors, in the prisons of Paris now, to allow of any seclusion of those who were about to be tried, condemned, and guillotined.

The young girl had been marched through the crowded streets of Paris, followed by a jeering mob, who readily recognised in the gentle, high-bred girl the obvious prey, which the Committee of Public Safety was wont, from time to time to throw to the hungry hydra-headed dog of the Revolution.

Lately the squalid spectators of the noisome spectacle on the Place de la Guillotine had had few of these very welcome sights: an aristocrat—a real, elegant, refined woman, with white hands and proud, pale face—mounting the steps of the same scaffold on which perished the vilest criminals and most degraded brutes.
===Chinese===
朱丽叶特终于独自一人了——也就是说，只是相对而言的独自一人；因为如今巴黎各座监狱里的贵族、罪犯和叛徒实在太多，那些即将受审、定罪、送上断头台的人，根本谈不上有什么清静的独处。

这位年轻姑娘被押着穿过巴黎拥挤的街道，身后跟着一群嘲骂起哄的暴民；他们一眼就认出，这个温柔而教养高贵的姑娘，正是救国委员会惯于时不时抛出去的那种现成的猎物——抛给革命那头饥饿的九头怪犬。

近来，断头台广场（Place de la Guillotine）上那出龌龊刑戏的脏看客们，已经难得见到这类极合他们胃口的景象了：一位贵族——一位真正的、优雅而有教养的女人，一双手白皙，一张脸高傲而苍白——正登上那同一座刑台的台阶，最卑劣的罪犯、最下贱的畜生，从前也正是在那上面毙命的。

===Original===
Madame Guillotine was, above all, catholic in her tastes, her gaunt arms, painted blood red, were open alike to the murderer and the thief, the aristocrats of ancient lineage, and the proletariat from the gutter.

But lately the executions had been almost exclusively of a political character. The Girondins were fighting their last upon the bloody arena of the Revolution. One by one they fell still fighting, still preaching moderation, still foretelling disaster and appealing to that people, whom they had roused from one slavery, in order to throw it headlong under a tyrannical yoke more brutish, more absolute than before.

There were twelve prisons in Paris then, and forty thousand in France, and they were all full. An entire army went round the country recruiting prisoners. There was no room for separate cells, no room for privacy, no cause or desire for the most elementary sense of delicacy.
===Chinese===
断头台夫人（Madame Guillotine）的口味最是来者不拒：她那两条漆成血色的枯瘦臂膀，对凶手和小偷，对古老门第的贵族和对阴沟里爬出来的无产者，一律敞开着。

然而近来，处决几乎清一色地带上了政治色彩。吉伦特派正在革命那血腥的角斗场上作最后的苦战。他们一个接一个倒下去，倒下去时仍在抗争，仍在鼓吹温和，仍在预言大祸将临，仍向那班民众苦苦呼吁——而当初正是他们把那班民众从一种奴役中唤醒，为的却是把他们一头栽进一副比先前更野蛮、更专横的暴政枷锁之下。

那时巴黎有十二座监狱，全法国有四万座，全都塞得满满当当。整整一支军队在全国各地巡行，招募囚犯。没有地方安置单人囚室，没有地方容人保有私密，最起码的廉耻之心，既无用武之地，也无人在意。

===Original===
Women, men, children—all were herded together, for one day, perhaps two, and a night or so, and then death would obliterate the petty annoyances, the womanly blushes caused by this sordid propinquity.

Death levelled all, erased everything.

When Marie Antoinette mounted the guillotine she had forgotten that for six weeks she practically lived day and night in the immediate companionship of a set of degraded soldiery.
===Chinese===
女人、男人、孩子——全都驱作一堆关在一起，关上一天，或许两天，再搭上个把夜晚；随后死亡便会来勾销那些细碎的烦扰，勾销这腌臜的共居在女人脸上惹起的红晕。

死亡抹平一切，勾销一切。

玛丽·安托瓦内特登上断头台的时候，早已忘却：整整六个星期，她实际上日夜同一伙堕落下流的兵厮守在一处。

===Original===
Juliette, as she marched through the streets between two men of the National Guard, and followed by Merlin, was hooted and jeered at, insulted, pelted with mud. One woman tried to push past the soldiers, and to strike her in the face—a woman! not thirty!—and who was dragging a pale, squalid little boy by the hand.

“*Crache donc sur l’aristo, voyons!*” the woman said to this poor, miserable little scrap of humanity as the soldiers pushed her roughly aside. “Spit on the aristocrat!” And the child tortured its own small, parched mouth so that, in obedience to its mother, it might defile and bespatter a beautiful, innocent girl.

The soldiers laughed, and improved the occasion with another insulting jest. Even Merlin forgot his vexation, delighted at the incident.
===Chinese===
朱丽叶特由两名国民自卫军士兵一左一右押着走过街道，身后跟着梅尔兰；人们向她发出嘘声、恣意嘲骂，侮辱她，朝她掷烂泥。一个女人试图从士兵身边挤过去打她的脸——一个女人！还不到三十岁！——手里还拖着一个面色灰败、衣衫龌龊的小男孩。

「*Crache donc sur l’aristo, voyons!*（来，朝这贵族吐口唾沫呀！）」士兵们把她粗鲁地推到一旁时，这女人对自己那一小团可怜巴巴的血肉这样说道。于是那孩子便折磨起自己干渴的小嘴，好遵从母命，去玷污、去唾污一个美丽无辜的姑娘。

士兵们放声大笑，乘兴又编了一句侮辱的俏皮话。连梅尔兰也忘了满腹的懊恼，为这场热闹乐不可支。

===Original===
But Juliette had seen nothing of it all.

She was walking as in a dream. The mob did not exist for her; she heard neither insult nor vituperation. She did not see the evil, dirty faces pushed now and then quite close to her; she did not feel the rough hands of the soldiers jostling her through the crowd: she had gone back to her own world of romance, where she dwelt alone now with the man she loved. Instead of the squalid houses of Paris, with their eternal device of Fraternity and Equality, there were beautiful trees and shrubs of laurel and of roses around her, making the air fragrant with their soft, intoxicating perfumes; sweet voices from the land of dreams filled the atmosphere with their tender murmur, whilst overhead a cloudless sky illumined this earthly paradise.

She was happy—supremely, completely happy. She had saved him from the consequences of her own iniquitous crime, and she was about to give her life for him, so that his safety might be more completely assured.
===Chinese===
可这一切，朱丽叶特什么也没有看见。

她走着，恍如梦中。那群暴民于她并不存在；辱骂与诟詈她一概听不见。她看不见那些时不时几乎贴到她脸上的邪恶污秽的面孔，也感觉不到士兵们推搡着她挤过人群的粗手：她已经回到了自己那个浪漫故事的世界，如今在那世界里，只有她同她心爱的男人独处。眼前不再是巴黎那些龌龊的房屋，不再有屋墙上那永恒的「博爱」与「平等」的标语；她四周是美丽的树木，是月桂与蔷薇的丛丛灌木，柔和醉人的芳香熏得空气甜丝丝的；梦乡传来的甜美嗓音，以温柔的絮语充盈着四下的空气，而头顶上万里无云的晴空，正朗朗照着这人间的乐园。

她是幸福的——至高无上地、完完全全地幸福。她已经把他从她自己那桩罪行的后果里救了出来，而她即将为他献出生命，好叫他的安全得到更加周全的保障。

===Original===
Her love for him he would never know; now he knew only her crime, but presently, when she would be convicted and condemned, confronted with a few scraps of burned paper and a torn letter-case, then he would know that she had stood her trial, self-accused, and meant to die for him.

Therefore the past few moments were now wholly hers. She had the rights to dwell on those few happy seconds when she listened to the avowal of his love. It was ethereal, and perhaps not altogether human, but it was hers. She had been his divinity, his madonna; he had loved in her that, which was her truer, her better self.

What was base in her was not truly her. That awful oath, sworn so solemnly, had been her relentless tyrant; and her religion—a religion of superstition and of false ideals—had blinded her, and dragged her into crime.
===Chinese===
她对他的爱，他永远不会知道；如今他知道的，只是她的罪行。但不久之后，待她被定罪、被判死刑，同那几片烧焦的纸屑、那只撕破的信匣当面对质之时，他就会明白：她是自首自诉、挺身受审，决意替他去死的。

因此，刚过去的这段时光，如今整个儿属于她了。她有权细细回味那幸福得屈指可数的几秒钟——那时，她正聆听着他吐露爱慕。那爱慕缥缈如天际的气息，或许并不全然属于人间，可它是她的。她曾是他奉为神圣的偶像，是他的圣母；他在她身上所爱的，是她那个更真实、更美好的自我。

她身上卑污的那一部分，其实并非真正的她。那个宣誓得如此庄重森严的可怖誓言，才是驱策她的冷酷暴君；而她的信仰——一种由迷信与虚妄理想凑成的信仰——蒙住了她的眼睛，把她拖进了罪恶。

===Original===
She had arrogated to herself that which was God’s alone—“Vengeance!” which is not for man.

That through it all she should have known love, and learned its tender secrets, was more than she deserved. That she should have felt his burning kisses on her hand was heavenly compensation for all she would have to suffer.

And so she allowed them to drag her through the sansculotte mob of Paris, who would have torn her to pieces then and there, so as not to delay the pleasure of seeing her die.
===Chinese===
她把唯独属于上帝的东西僭据到了自己身上——「复仇！」——而那，原不是留给凡人的。

而在这一切的罪孽之中，她竟还能识得爱情，还能懂得它温柔的奥秘，这已远非她所应得。她竟还能感到他炽热的吻落在自己的手上——这便是上天的补偿，补偿她此后必得承受的一切苦难。

于是她听凭他们拖着自己，穿过巴黎的无套裤汉群；那群人恨不得当场把她撕成碎片，好不耽误亲眼看她死去的那份乐子。

===Original===
They took her to the Luxembourg, once the palace of the Medici, the home of proud “Monsieur” in the days of the Great Monarch, now a loathsome, overfilled prison.

It was then six o’clock in the afternoon, drawing towards the close of this memorable day. She was handed over to the governor of the prison, a short, thickset man in black trousers and black-shag woollen shirt, and wearing a dirty red cap, with tricolour rosette on the side of his unkempt head.

He eyed her up and down as she passed under the narrow doorway, then murmured one swift query to Merlin:
===Chinese===
他们把她押到了卢森堡监狱——那里曾是美第奇家族的宫殿，大君主时代是骄横的王弟「Monsieur」殿下的府邸，如今成了一座可憎的、人满为患的监狱。

其时正是下午六点钟，这个不寻常的日子已近尾声。她被移交给监狱的典狱长——一个又矮又粗壮的汉子，穿着黑裤子、黑色粗绒毛衬衫，头上歪戴一顶肮脏的红便帽，蓬乱的脑袋一侧别着一枚三色帽花。

她从那道狭窄的门洞底下走过时，他把她从头到脚打量了一遍，随即飞快地向梅尔兰低声问了一句：

===Original===
“Dangerous?”

“Yes,” replied Merlin laconically.

“You understand,” added the governor; “we are so crowded. We ought to know if individual attention is required.”

“Certainly,” said Merlin, “you will be personally responsible for this prisoner to the Committee of Public Safety.”

“Any visitors allowed?”

“Certainly not, without the special permission of the Public Prosecutor.”
===Chinese===
「危险么？」

「危险，」梅尔兰言简意赅地答道。

「您明白，」典狱长补充道，「我们这儿挤得要命。总得弄清楚，是不是需要对这个犯人单独照看。」

「那是自然，」梅尔兰说，「这个犯人，得由你本人向救国委员会负责。」

「准不准探监？」

「断乎不准——除非有公诉人的特别许可。」

===Original===
Juliette heard this brief exchange of words over her future fate.

No visitor would be allowed to see her. Well, perhaps that would be best. She would have been afraid to meet Déroulède again, afraid to read in his eyes that story of his dead love, which alone might have destroyed her present happiness.

And she wished to see no one. She had a memory to dwell on—a short, heavenly memory. It consisted of a few words, a kiss—the last one—on her hand, and that passionate murmur which had escaped from his lips when he knelt at her feet:

“Juliette!”
===Chinese===
朱丽叶特听着这几句短短的对答，字字都关乎她日后的命运。

不会有人获准来看她了。也罢，这样或许倒好。她本会害怕再见到德鲁莱德，害怕从他眼中读出他那段已然死去的爱情的故事——唯独那个故事，才可能毁掉她此刻的幸福。

而她也不愿见任何人。她有一段回忆可供盘桓——短短的、天堂一般的回忆。那回忆只有几句话，一个吻——落在她手上的最后一个吻——还有他跪在她脚边时唇间逸出的那声热切的低唤：

「朱丽叶特！」

## XIX Complexities / XIX 千头万绪

===Original===
Citizen-Deputy Déroulède had been privately interviewed by the Committee of Public Safety, and temporarily allowed to go free.

The brief proceedings had been quite private, the people of Paris were not to know as yet that their favourite was under a cloud. When he had answered all the questions put to him, and Merlin—just returned from his errand at the Luxembourg Prison—had given his version of the domiciliary visitation in the Citizen-Deputy’s house, the latter was briefly told that for the moment the Republic had no grievance against him.

But he knew quite well what that meant. He would be henceforth under suspicion, watched incessantly, as a mouse is by the cat, and pounced upon, the moment time would be considered propitious for his final downfall.

The inevitable waning of his popularity would be noted by keen, jealous eyes; and Déroulède, with his sure knowledge of mankind and of character, knew well enough that his popularity was bound to wane sooner or later, as all such ephemeral things do.

===Chinese===
公会议员德鲁莱德（Citizen-Deputy Déroulède）受到救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）的私下传讯，暂时获准自由离去。

这番简短的审讯办得极其隐秘，巴黎的人民暂且还不得知晓，他们爱戴的宠儿已经蒙上了阴影。待他一一回答了提出的种种问题，刚从卢森堡监狱（Luxembourg Prison）办完差事回来的梅尔兰（Merlin），也把入户搜查（domiciliary visitation）公会议员宅邸的经过讲出了他的一套说法，这才简短地告知后者：眼下，共和国（the Republic）对他并无不满。

可他十分清楚这话意味着什么。从此他就要处在猜疑之下，受到无休无止的监视，像老鼠之于猫一般，一待时机被视为成熟，便会猛扑上来，叫他最终覆灭。

他声望的必然衰退，逃不过那些敏锐而嫉妒的眼睛；而德鲁莱德凭着他对世人、对品性的透彻认识，心里也明白得很：他的声望迟早必要消退，一切过眼云烟的东西都是如此。

===Original===
In the meanwhile, during the short respite which his enemies would leave him, his one thought and duty would be to get his mother and Anne Mie safely out of the country.

And also—

He thought of *her*, and wondered what had happened. As he walked swiftly across the narrow footbridge, and reached the other side of the river, the events of the past few hours rushed upon his memory with terrible, overwhelming force.

A bitter ache filled his heart at the remembrance of her treachery. The baseness of it all was so appalling. He tried to think if he had ever wronged her; wondered if perhaps she loved someone else, and wished *him* out of her way.

===Chinese===
眼下，趁着敌人留给他的短暂喘息，他唯一的心思和职责，便是把母亲和安娜·米（Anne Mie）平安地送出国境。

此外还有——

他想到了她，心中揣想着不知出了什么事。他快步走过窄窄的步行桥，到了河对岸，过去这几个钟头里的种种变故，便以可怕而不可抗拒的力量，一齐涌进他的记忆。

一想到她的背叛，一阵苦痛便堵满了他的心。这一切是何等卑鄙，令人胆寒。他竭力回想自己可曾委屈过她；又猜想，莫非她爱上了别人，巴不得*他*走开，别挡着她的路。

===Original===
But, then, he had been so humble, so unassuming in his love. He had arrogated nothing unto himself, asked for nothing, demanded nothing in virtue of his protecting powers over her.

He was torturing himself with this awful wonderment of why she had treated him thus.

Out of revenge for her brother’s death—that was the only explanation he could find, the only palliation for her crime.

He knew nothing of her oath to her father, and, of course, had never heard of the sad history of this young, sensitive girl placed in one terrible moment between her dead brother and her demented father. He only thought of common, sordid revenge for a sin he had been practically forced to commit.

===Chinese===
可话说回来，他爱得那样谦卑，那样不事声张。他不曾把什么据为己有，不曾求过什么，也不曾凭仗护佑她的权力索要过什么。

他就这样拿这个可怕的疑团折磨自己：她为什么这样待他。

为替她哥哥的死复仇——这是他唯一能找到的解释，也是她的罪愆唯一可宽宥之处。

他对她立给父亲的誓言一无所知，自然也从未听说过这个年轻敏感的姑娘那伤心的身世——在可怕的一瞬之间，她被夹在了死去的哥哥和神志失常的父亲之间。他只当这是一桩寻常而卑污的复仇，为着他实则是被逼着犯下的一桩罪孽。

===Original===
And how he had loved her! Yes, *loved*—for that was in the past now.
