She had ceased to be a saint or a madonna; she had fallen from her pedestal so low that he could not find the way to descend and grope after the fragments of his ideal.

At his own door he was met by Anne Mie in tears.

===Chinese===
而他曾经多么爱她啊！是的，曾经*爱*过——因为如今都已成过去。

她不再是圣女，不再是圣母了；她从她的基座上跌落得那样低，以致他找不到路径走下去，去摸索他理想摔碎的残片。

在自家门口，安娜·米含着眼泪迎住了他。

===Original===
“She has gone,” murmured the young girl. “I feel as if I had murdered her.”

“Gone? Who? Where?” queried Déroulède rapidly, an icy feeling of terror gripping him by the heartstrings.

“Juliette has gone,” replied Anne Mie; “those awful brutes took her away.”

“When?”

===Chinese===
「她走了，」年轻姑娘喃喃道，「我觉得，就像是我亲手谋害了她。」

「走了？谁？去哪儿了？」德鲁莱德急促地追问，一股冰冷的恐怖之感，紧紧攫住了他的心弦。

「朱丽叶特（Juliette）走了，」安娜·米答道，「那帮可怕的畜生把她带走了。」

「什么时候？」

===Original===
“Directly after you left. That man Merlin found some ashes and scraps of paper in her room—”

“Ashes?”

“Yes; and a torn letter-case.”

“Great God!”

“She said that they were love letters, which she had been burning for fear you should see them.”

“She said so? Anne Mie, Anne Mie, are you quite sure?”

===Chinese===
「就在你走后不久。那个梅尔兰在她房间里搜出一些灰烬和碎纸屑——」

「灰烬？」

「是的；还有一只撕裂的信匣。」

「伟大的上帝啊！」

「她说是些情书，她一直拿去烧了，怕让你看见。」

「她这么说的？安娜·米，安娜·米，你十分确定吗？」

===Original===
It was all so horrible, and he did not quite understand it all; his brain, which was usually so keen and so active, refused him service at this terrible juncture.

“Yes; I am quite sure,” continued Anne Mie, in the midst of her tears. “And oh! that awful Merlin said some dastardly things. But she persisted in her story, that she had—another lover. Oh, Paul, I am sure it is not true. I hated her because—because—you loved her so, and I mistrusted her, but I cannot believe that she was quite as base as that.”

“No, no, child,” he said in a toneless, miserable voice; “she was not so base as that. Tell me more of what she said.”

===Chinese===
这一切太可怕了，他不能全然明白；他那颗平日敏锐活跃的头脑，在这个可怕的关头，竟不肯再为他效劳。

「是的；我十分确定，」安娜·米泣不成声，接着说道。「哦！那个可恶的梅尔兰说了些卑鄙无耻的话。可她一口咬定她的说法，说她还有——另一个情夫。哦，保罗（Paul），我敢肯定这不是真的。我恨她，因为——因为——你那么爱她，我信不过她；可我不相信，她竟会下贱到那般地步。」

「不，不，孩子，」他用一种平板而凄苦的声音说道；「她没有那么下贱。再跟我说说她还说些什么。」

===Original===
“She said very little else. But Merlin asked her whether she had denounced you so as to get you out of the way. He hinted that—that—”

“That I was her lover too?”

“Yes,” murmured Anne Mie.

She hardly liked to look at him; the strong face had become hard and set in its misery.

===Chinese===
「别的她几乎没说什么。可梅尔兰问她，是不是为了把你除掉才告发你。他还暗示说——暗示说——」

「说我也曾是她的情夫？」

「是的，」安娜·米喃喃道。

她几乎不忍看他；那张坚毅的脸，已因痛苦而变得僵硬、凝定。

===Original===
“And she allowed them to say all this?” he asked at last.

“Yes. And she followed them without a murmur, as Merlin said she would have to answer before the Committee of Public Safety, for having fooled the representatives of the people.”

“She’ll answer for it with her life,” murmured Déroulède. “And with mine!” he added half audibly.

Anne Mie did not hear him; her pathetic little soul was filled with a great, an overwhelming pity of Juliette and for Paul.

===Chinese===
「而这一切，她竟由着他们说出口？」他终于问道。

「是的。她一声不吭地就跟着他们走了；照梅尔兰的说法，她愚弄了人民代表，得到救国委员会去交代。」

「她要拿性命来交代这笔账的，」德鲁莱德喃喃道。「还有我的！」他半出声地补了一句。

安娜·米没有听见；她那可怜的小小灵魂里，满溢着对朱丽叶特、对保罗的一片深沉的、压倒一切的怜悯。

===Original===
“Before they took her away,” she said, placing her thin, delicate-looking hands on his arm. “I ran to her, and bade her farewell. The soldiers pushed me roughly aside; but I contrived to kiss her—and then she whispered a few words to me.”

“Yes? What were they?”

“ ‘It was an oath,’ she said. ‘I swore it to my father and to my dead brother. Tell him,’ ” repeated Anne Mie slowly.

===Chinese===
「在他们带她走之前，」她说道，把她那双看上去纤弱秀气的手搭在他的臂上。「我跑过去同她道别。士兵们把我粗暴地推向一旁；可我还是想法子吻了她——随后她跟我低声说了几句话。」

「哦？说了什么？」

「『那是一个誓言，』她说，『我对我的父亲和我死去的哥哥发过誓。告诉他。』」安娜·米缓缓地复述道。

===Original===
An oath!

Now he understood, and oh! how he pitied her. How terribly she must have suffered in her poor, harassed soul when her noble, upright nature fought against this hideous treachery.

That she was true and brave in herself, of that Déroulède had no doubt. And now this awful sin upon her conscience, which must be causing her endless misery.

===Chinese===
一个誓言！

如今他明白了，哦！他多么怜惜她。她那高贵正直的天性起而抗拒这桩丑恶的背叛时，她那可怜的、备受煎熬的灵魂，该受了多少可怕的苦楚。

至于她本人真诚而勇敢，这一点德鲁莱德毫不怀疑。而如今这桩可怕的罪压在她的良心上，必然正叫她受着无尽的苦。

===Original===
And, alas! the atonement would never free her from the load of self-condemnation. She had elected to pay with her life for her treason against him and his family. She would be arraigned before a tribunal which would inevitably condemn her. Oh! the pity of it all!

One moment’s passionate emotion, a lifelong superstition and mistaken sense of duty, and now this endless misery, this terrible atonement of a wrong that could never be undone.

And she had never loved him!

That was the true, the only sting which he knew now; it rankled more than her sin, more than her falsehood, more than the shattering of his ideal.

===Chinese===
可叹啊！赎罪永远也卸不下那自我谴责的重负。她已选定用性命，来偿付她对他、对他一家犯下的背叛。她将被提审到一座注定要给她定罪的法庭上。哦！这一切多么可痛！

一时间的激情冲动，一生对誓言的迷信与错认的职责，换来的是如今这无尽的悲苦，是这场为一件永难挽回的错事而作的可怕赎罪。

而她从来没有爱过他！

这才是他此刻识得的真正而唯一的刺痛；它比她的罪孽、比她的谎言、比他理想的破灭，更在心头隐隐灼烧。

===Original===
With a passionate desire for his safety, she had sacrificed herself in order to atone for the material evil which she had done.

But there was the wreck of his hopes and of his dreams!

Never until now, when he had irretrievably lost her, did Déroulède realise how great had been his hopes; how he had watched day after day for a look in her eyes, a word from her lips, to show him that she too—his unattainable saint—would one day come to earth, and respond to his love.

And now and then, when her beautiful face lighted up at sight of him, when she smiled a greeting to him on his return from his work, when she looked with pride and admiration on him from the public bench in the assemblies of the Convention—then he had begun to hope, to think, to dream.

===Chinese===
她一心切望他平安，便牺牲了自己，来赎偿她所造成的实实在在的祸害。

可他的希望、他的梦想，已然成了一片废墟！

直到此刻，直到他已无可挽回地失去了她，德鲁莱德才明白自己当年的希望何其宏大；他曾日复一日地盼着，盼她眼中的一瞥、她唇边的一句话，向他表明她——他可望而不可即的圣女——有朝一日也会降临尘世，回应他的爱。

而时不时地，当她美丽的容颜因见到他而焕发光彩，当他从公务中归来时她微笑相迎，当她在国民公会（the Convention）大会的公共旁听席上带着自豪与钦佩望着他——那时他便开始希望，开始思量，开始做梦了。

===Original===
And it was all a sham! A mask to hide the terrible conflict that was raging within her soul, nothing more.

She did not love him, of that he felt convinced. Man like he did not understand to the full that great and wonderful enigma, which has puzzled the world since primeval times: a woman’s heart.

The eternal contradictions which go to make up the complex nature of an emotional woman were quite incomprehensible to him. Juliette had betrayed him to serve her own sense of what was just and right, her revenge and her oath. Therefore she did not love him.

It was logic, sound commonsense, and, aided by his own diffidence where women were concerned, it seemed to him irrefutable.

===Chinese===
而这一切全是假象！不过是一副面具，遮掩着她灵魂深处正翻腾的可怕挣扎，仅此而已。

她不爱他，这一点他深信不疑。像他这样的男人，还不能全然参透那个自太古以来便困惑着世人的伟大而奇妙的谜：女人的心。

那些构成一个多情女子复杂天性的永恒矛盾，他全然无法理解。朱丽叶特背叛了他，是为了顺从她自己认定的公正与道义，为了她的复仇，为了她的誓言。因此她不爱他。

这是逻辑，是健全的常识；再加上他对女人的事素来缺乏自信，这道理在他看来便无可辩驳了。

===Original===
To a man like Paul Déroulède, a man of thought, of purpose, and of action, the idea of being false to the thing loved, of hate and love being interchangeable, was absolutely foreign and unbelievable. He had never hated the thing he loved or loved the thing he hated. A man’s feelings in these respects are so much less complex, so much less contradictory.

Would a man betray his friend? No—never. He might betray his enemy, the creature he abhorred, whose downfall would cause him joy. But his friend? The very idea was repugnant, impossible to an upright nature.

Juliette’s ultimate access of generosity in trying to save him, when she was at last brought face to face with the terrible wrong she had committed, *that* he put down to one of those noble impulses of which he knew her soul to be fully capable, and even then his own diffidence suggested that she did it more for the sake of his mother or for Anne Mie rather than for him.

Therefore what mattered life to him now? She was lost to him forever, whether he succeeded in snatching her from the guillotine or not. He had but little hope to save her, but he would not owe his life to her.

===Chinese===
对于保罗·德鲁莱德这样一个有思想、有意志、有行动的人来说，背叛所爱的东西、恨与爱可以互换这类念头，是完全陌生的，难以置信的。他从未恨过自己所爱的东西，也从未爱过自己所恨的东西。男人在这方面的情感要简单得多，矛盾也要少得多。

一个男人会背叛他的朋友吗？不会——永远不会。他或许会背叛他的敌人，背叛他憎恶的那种人——那人的倒台会使他欢喜。可他的朋友？这念头本身就叫正直的天性深恶痛绝，断然不可能。

朱丽叶特最后那阵挺身相救的慷慨冲动——其时她终于直面了自己所犯的可怕罪愆——*那*，他归结为她灵魂完全可能生出的那种高贵冲动之一；即便如此，他那份缺乏自信仍在悄悄提示他：她这样做，多半是为了他的母亲，为了安娜·米，而不是为了他。

那么，如今这条命于他又有何要紧？她已经永远不再属于他，无论他能否把她从断头台上抢救下来。救她的希望本就微茫，但他决不愿把自己这条命欠在她手里。

===Original===
Anne Mie, seeing him wrapped in his own thoughts, had quietly withdrawn. Her own good sense told her already that Paul Déroulède’s first step would be to try and get his mother out of danger, and out of the country, while there was yet time.

So, without waiting for instructions, she began that same evening to pack up her belongings and those of Madame Déroulède.

There was no longer any hatred in her heart against Juliette. Where Paul Déroulède had failed to understand, there Anne Mie had already made a guess. She firmly believed that nothing now could save Juliette from death, and a great feeling of tenderness had crept into her heart, for the woman whom she had looked upon as an enemy and a rival.

She too had learnt in those brief days the great lesson that revenge belongs to God alone.

===Chinese===
安娜·米见他这般沉浸在自己的思绪里，便悄悄退了出去。她凭自己的见识已经料到，保罗·德鲁莱德的第一步，必是趁着还来得及，设法把母亲送出险境、送出国去。

于是，她不等吩咐，当天晚上便开始收拾自己的东西和德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）的行李。

她心里对朱丽叶特，已不再有半点怨恨。保罗·德鲁莱德没能明白的地方，安娜·米却早已猜到了几分。她坚信如今无论什么都救不了朱丽叶特的命了，一股深沉的柔情悄然潜入她的心头——为着那个她曾视作仇敌、视作情敌的女人。

在那短短的几天里，她也学到了那伟大的一课：复仇，唯独属于上帝。

## XX The Cheval Borgne / XX 独眼马客栈

===Original===
It was close upon midnight.

The place had become suffocatingly hot; the fumes of rank tobacco, of rancid butter, and of raw spirits hung like a vapour in midair.

The principal room in the Auberge du Cheval Borgne had been used for the past five years now as the chief meeting-place of the ultra-sansculotte party of the Republic.

===Chinese===
时已近午夜。

屋子里热得叫人透不过气来；呛人的劣质烟草、馊败黄油和生烧酒的气味，像一团雾气悬在半空。

独眼马客栈（Auberge du Cheval Borgne）的这间正厅，近五年来一直被当作共和国极端无套裤汉派（ultra-sansculotte）的主要聚会场所。

===Original===
The house itself was squalid and dirty, up one of those mean streets which, by their narrow way and shelving buildings, shut out sun, air, and light from their miserable inhabitants.

The Cheval Borgne was one of the most wretched-looking dwellings in this street of evil repute. The plaster was cracked, the walls themselves seemed bulging outward, preparatory to a final collapse. The ceilings were low, and supported by beams black with age and dirt.

At one time it had been celebrated for its vast cellarage, which had contained some rare old wines. And in the days of the Grand Monarch young bucks were wont to quit the gay salons of the ladies, in order to repair to the Cheval Borgne for a night’s carouse.

===Chinese===
房子本身又龌龊又肮脏，坐落在某条寒碜的街上——这种街巷狭窄逼仄，两旁的楼房倾斜欲坠，把阳光、空气和亮光统统挡在外面，照拂不到那些可怜的住户。

独眼马是这条声名狼藉的街上看上去最破败的住所之一。灰泥龟裂，墙壁本身似乎正向外鼓凸，为最终的坍塌做着准备。天花板低矮，靠年深日久、积满污垢而发黑的横梁支撑着。

想当年，它曾以宽大的酒窖而闻名，窖里藏过一些稀有的陈年佳酿。还在「大君主」（Grand Monarque）路易十四的年代，翩翩贵公子们惯于撇下贵妇们寻欢作乐的客厅，跑到独眼马来通宵纵酒狂欢。

===Original===
In those days the vast cellarage was witness of many a dark encounter, of many a mysterious death; could the slimy walls have told their own tale, it would have been one which would have put to shame the wildest chronicles of M. Vidoq.

Now it was no longer so.

Things were done in broad daylight on the Place de la Révolution: there was no need for dark, mysterious cellars, in which to accomplish deeds of murder and of revenge.

===Chinese===
在那个年代，那座宽大的酒窖见证过多少桩阴险的勾结、多少桩神秘的死亡；那黏滑的四壁若能开口讲出自己的故事，讲出来必定叫维多克（Vidoq）先生最荒诞不经的纪事也相形见绌。

如今情形已不再如此。

事情都光天化日地在革命广场（Place de la Révolution）上办：再也不需要阴暗诡秘的地窖，去成就那些谋杀与复仇的勾当了。

===Original===
Rats and vermin of all sorts worked their way now in the underground portion of the building. They ate up each other, and held their orgies in the cellars, whilst men did the same sort of thing in the rooms above.

It was a club of Equality and Fraternity. Any passerby was at liberty to enter and take part in the debates, his only qualification for this temporary membership being an inordinate love for Madame la Guillotine.

It was from the sordid rooms of the Cheval Borgne that most of the denunciations had gone forth which led but to the one inevitable ending﻿—death.

===Chinese===
如今，老鼠和形形色色的害虫在这座房子的地下部分钻来钻去。它们互相吞吃，在酒窖里恣意狂欢；而楼上房间里的人，干的也是同一类勾当。

这是一个平等博爱俱乐部。任何过路人都可以随意进门，参加辩论；取得这份临时会员资格的唯一条件，便是对断头台夫人（Madame la Guillotine）爱得如痴如狂。

那些通向唯一不可避免结局——死亡——的告发，大多数就是从独眼马这几间肮脏屋子发出去的。

===Original===
They sat in conclave here, some twoscore or so at first, the rabid patriots of this poor, downtrodden France. They talked of Liberty mostly, with many oaths and curses against the tyrants, and then started a tyranny, an autocracy, ten thousand times more awful than any wielded by the dissolute Bourbons.

And this was the temple of Liberty, this dark, damp, evil-smelling brothel, with is narrow, cracked windowpanes, which let in but an infinitesimal fraction of air, and that of the foulest, most unwholesome kind.

The floor was of planks roughly put together; now they were worm-eaten, bare, save for a thick carpet of greasy dust, which deadened the sound of booted feet. The place only boasted of a couple of chairs, both of which had to be propped against the wall lest they should break, and bring the sitter down upon the floor; otherwise a number of empty wine barrels did duty for seats, and rough deal boards on broken trestles for tables.

===Chinese===
他们在这里聚会密商，起初约莫四十来人，全是这个受苦受难、惨遭践踏的法兰西的狂热爱国者。他们连咒带骂地痛斥暴君，大谈特谈自由，随后却建立起一种暴政、一种独裁，比荒淫无度的波旁王室所施行的一切还要可怕一万倍。

这便是自由的神殿——这所阴暗、潮湿、恶臭熏天的妓院，它那窄小开裂的窗玻璃，只放进一丝微乎其微的空气，而且还偏偏是最污浊、最不卫生的那一种。

地板是粗粗拼拢的木板；如今虫蛀斑驳，光裸着，只厚厚铺着一层油腻的积尘，把穿靴子的脚步声都闷哑了。这地方引以为豪的只有两把椅子，两把都得斜靠在墙上撑住，免得散了架，把坐着的人摔到地板上去；此外便是一排空酒桶权充座位，几块粗糙的松木板架在断裂的支架上权充桌子。

===Original===
There had once been a paper on the walls, now it hung down in strips, showing the cracked plaster beneath. The whole place had a tone of yellowish-grey grime all over it, save where, in the centre of the room, on a rough double post, shaped like the guillotine, a scarlet cap of Liberty gave a note of lurid colour to the dismal surroundings.

On the walls here and there the eternal device, so sublime in conception, so sordid in execution, recalled the aims of the so-called club: “*Liberté, Fraternité, Egalité, sinon la Mort.*”

Below the device, in one or two corners of the room, the wall was further adorned with rough charcoal sketches, mostly of an obscene character, the work of one of the members of the club, who had chosen this means of degrading his art.

===Chinese===
墙上原本糊过花纸，如今一条条耷拉下来，露出底下龟裂的灰泥。整个地方一片灰黄色的污浊调子；唯有屋子正中，在一根形似断头台的粗糙双柱上，一顶猩红的自由帽，给这阴惨的四壁点上一笔刺目的颜色。

墙上东一处西一处，写着那句永恒的格言——立意何其崇高，践行何其卑污——昭示着这个所谓的俱乐部的宗旨：「*Liberté, Fraternité, Egalité, sinon la Mort.*」（自由、博爱、平等，否则便是死亡。）

格言底下，屋子的一两个角落里，墙上还添了几笔粗糙的炭笔涂鸦，多半属于淫秽一路，出自俱乐部某位成员的手笔——他就是挑了这个法子来糟践自己的艺术。

===Original===
Tonight the assembly had been reduced to less than a score.

Even according to the dictates of these apostles of Fraternity: “*la guillotine va toujours*”﻿—the guillotine goes on always. She had become the most potent factor in the machinery of government, of this great Revolution, and she had been daily, almost hourly fed through the activity of this nameless club, which held its weird and awesome sittings in the dank coffee-room of the Cheval Borgne.

The number of the active members had been reduced. Like the rats in the cellars below, they had done away with one another, swallowed one another up, torn each other to pieces in this wild rage for a Utopian fraternity.

Marat, founder of the organisation, had been murdered by a girl’s hand; but Charon, Manuel, Osselin had gone the usual way, denounced by their colleagues, Rabaut, Custine, Bison, who in their turn were sent to the guillotine by those more powerful, perhaps more eloquent, than themselves.

===Chinese===
今晚到会的人已减少到不足二十。

甚至照着这帮博爱使徒的信条：*la guillotine va toujours*（断头台永远运转不停）。断头台夫人已成了这个伟大革命的政府机器里最强劲的部件；靠这家无名俱乐部的积极活动，她天天、几乎每个钟头都得到喂养——这家俱乐部就在独眼马那间阴湿的咖啡室里，举行着它那些诡异骇人的集会。

积极会员的数目已见缩减。正像楼下地窖里的老鼠一样，他们彼此除掉对方，互相吞噬，互相撕成碎片，全为了那股追求乌托邦式博爱的狂热劲头。

这个组织的缔造者马拉（Marat），已经死在一个姑娘的手里；而夏龙（Charon）、曼努埃尔（Manuel）、奥斯兰（Osselin）也都走了那条老路，被同僚拉博（Rabaut）、屈斯蒂纳（Custine）、比松（Bison）告发；这几位转过来又被比他们更有势力、也许更能言善辩的人送上了断头台。

===Original===
It was merely a case of who could shout the loudest at an assembly of the National Convention.

“*La guillotine va toujours!*”

After the death of Marat, Merlin became the most prominent member of the club﻿—he and Foucquier-Tinville, his bosom friend, Public Prosecutor, and the most bloodthirsty homicide of this homicidal age.

Bosom friend both, yet they worked against one another, undermining each other’s popularity, whispering persistently, one against the other: “He is a traitor!” It had become just a neck-to-neck race between them towards the inevitable goal﻿—the guillotine.

===Chinese===
这不过是看谁在国民公会（National Convention）的会场上嗓门喊得最响罢了。

*La guillotine va toujours!*（断头台永远运转不停！）

马拉死后，梅尔兰（Merlin）成了俱乐部里最显赫的人物——还有他的莫逆之交富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville），公诉人（Public Prosecutor），这个杀人如麻的年代里最嗜血成性的凶手。

两人虽为莫逆之交，却暗地里互相对着干，各自拆毁对方的声望，一个冲着一个喋喋不休地咬耳朵：「他是个叛徒！」在他们之间，这已成了并驾齐驱的一场赛跑，朝着那个在劫难逃的目标奔去——断头台。

===Original===
Foucquier-Tinville is in the ascendant for the moment. Merlin had been given a task which he had failed to accomplish. For days now, weeks even, the debates of this noble assembly had been chiefly concerned with the downfall of Citizen-Deputy Déroulède. His popularity, his calm security in the midst of this reign of terror and anarchy, had been a terrible thorn in the flesh of these rabid Jacobins.

And now the climax had been reached. An anonymous denunciation had roused the hopes of these sanguinary patriots. It all sounded perfectly plausible. To try and save that traitor, Marie Antoinette, the widow of Louis Capet, was just the sort of scheme that would originate in the brain of Paul Déroulède.

He had always been at heart an aristocrat, and the feeling of chivalry for a persecuted woman was only the outward signs of his secret adherence to the hated class.

Merlin had been sent to search the Deputy’s house for proofs of the latter’s guilt.

===Chinese===
眼下是富基耶-坦维尔占了上风。交给梅尔兰的一项任务，他没有办成。这些天来，甚至一连几个星期，这个高贵议会的辩论，主要都是围着德鲁莱德（Déroulède）公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）的垮台打转。他的声望，他在这恐怖统治与无政府状态之中那份从容的安然，早已是这群狂热雅各宾派（Jacobin）心头一根可怕的肉中刺。

而如今，好戏到了高潮。一封匿名告发信，重新燃起了这帮嗜血爱国者的希望。这一切听上去入情入理，天衣无缝。想要搭救那个女叛徒——玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette），路易·卡佩（Louis Capet）的遗孀——这种勾当，恰恰正是只会从保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）的脑袋里生出来的主意。

他骨子里从来就是个贵族；对受迫害的女人怀有侠义之心，不过是他暗中效忠那个遭人切齿痛恨的阶级而露在外面的征象。

于是梅尔兰奉派去搜查这位议员的家，搜查罪证。

===Original===
And Merlin had come back empty-handed.

The arrest of a female aristo﻿—the probable mistress of Déroulède, who obviously had denounced him﻿—was but small compensation for the failure of the more important capture.

As soon as Merlin joined his friends in the low, ill-lit, evil-smelling room he realised at once that there was a feeling of hostility against him.

===Chinese===
而梅尔兰却是两手空空地回来了。

抓到一个女贵族——多半是德鲁莱德的情妇，明摆着告发他的就是她——这点收获，远抵偿不了那个更要紧的猎物的失手。

梅尔兰一走进那间低矮、昏暗、恶臭熏人的屋子，来到朋友们中间，立刻就觉出人们对他满怀敌意。

===Original===
Tinville, enthroned on one of the few chairs of which the Cheval Borgne could boast, was surrounded by a group of surly adherents.

On the rough trestles a number of glasses, half filled with raw potato-spirit, gave the keynote to the temper of the assembly.

All those present were dressed in the black-shag spencer, the seedy black breeches, and down-at-heel boots, which had become recognised as the distinctive uniform of the sansculotte party. The inevitable Phrygian cap, with its tricolour cockade, appeared on the heads of all those present, in various stages of dirt and decay.

===Chinese===
坦维尔高踞在独眼马拿得出手的仅有的几把椅子当中的一把上，四周围着一群面色阴沉的党羽。

粗糙的支架上摆着几只玻璃杯，半盛着劣质马铃薯烧酒，给满座会众的情绪定下了基调。

在场的人一色穿着黑粗绒斯宾塞短外套、寒酸的黑马裤和后跟磨塌的靴子——这一身装束如今已被公认为无套裤汉（sansculotte）一派的独特制服。人人头上都少不了那顶缀着三色帽徽的弗里吉亚帽，只是脏污朽坏的程度各各不同。

===Original===
Tinville had chosen to assume a sarcastic tone with regard to his whilom bosom friend, Merlin. Leaning both elbows on the table, he was picking his teeth with a steel fork, and in the intervals of his interesting operation, gave forth his views on the broad principles of patriotism.

Those who sat round him felt that his star was in the ascendant and assumed the position of satellites. Merlin as he entered had grunted a sullen “Good-eve,” and sat himself down in a remote corner of the room.

His greeting had been responded to with a few jeers and a good many dark, threatening looks. Tinville himself had bowed to him with mock sarcasm and an unpleasant leer.

===Chinese===
坦维尔拿定了主意，对昔日好友梅尔兰要摆出一副尖酸讥诮的腔调。他两肘支在桌上，拿一把钢叉剔着牙，在这桩有趣营生的间隙里，就爱国的大道理发表高见。

围着他坐的人觉得他吉星高照，便纷纷自居为他的卫星。梅尔兰进屋时闷闷地哼了一声「晚上好」，独自在屋子角落里坐下了。

人们回报他问候的，是几声奚落，外加许多阴沉的、充满威胁意味的目光。坦维尔本人也朝他躬了躬身，一脸假惺惺的讥诮，眼神叫人极不痛快。

===Original===
One of the patriots, a huge fellow, almost a giant, with heavy, coarse fists and broad shoulders that obviously suggested coal-heaving, had, after a few satirical observations, dragged one of the empty wine barrels to Merlin’s table, and sat down opposite him.

“Take care, Citizen Lenoir,” said Tinville, with an evil laugh, “Citizen-Deputy Merlin will arrest you instead of Deputy Déroulède, whom he has allowed to slip through his fingers.”

“Nay; I’ve no fear,” replied Lenoir, with an oath. “Citizen Merlin is too much of an aristo to hurt anyone; his hands are too clean; he does not care to do the dirty work of the Republic. Isn’t that so, Monsieur Merlin?” added the giant, with a mock bow, and emphasising the appellation which had fallen into complete disuse in these days of equality.

“My patriotism is too well known,” said Merlin roughly, “to fear any attacks from jealous enemies; and as for my search in the Citizen-Deputy’s house this afternoon, I was told to find proofs against him, and I found none.”

===Chinese===
有个爱国者是个大块头，几乎称得上巨人，拳头又沉又粗，肩膀宽阔，明摆着是运煤工出身；他冷嘲热讽了几句之后，把一只空酒桶拖到梅尔兰的桌前，在他的对面坐下。

「小心点，勒努瓦（Lenoir）公民，」坦维尔恶狠狠地笑道，「梅尔兰公会议员要来抓你，顶替那个从指缝里溜掉的德鲁莱德议员。」

「不；我什么都不怕，」勒努瓦骂了一声，答道。「梅尔兰公民贵族老爷气太重，伤不了人；他的手太干净，不屑于替共和国干脏活。是不是这样，梅尔兰先生？」巨汉补了一句，讥诮地躬了躬身，特意加重了这个称呼——在这人人讲平等的年月里，它早已彻底废而不用了。

「我的爱国名声尽人皆知，」梅尔兰粗声粗气地说，「不怕任何心怀妒忌的仇敌攻讦；至于今天下午在公会议员家里的搜查，上头叫我去找对他不利的罪证，我什么也没找到。」

===Original===
Lenoir expectorated on the floor, crossed his dark hairy arms over the table, and said quietly:

“Real patriotism, as the true Jacobin understands it, makes the proofs it wants and leaves nothing to chance.”

A chorus of hoarse murmurs of “*Vive la Liberté!*” greeted this harangue of the burly coal-heaver.

Feeling that he had gained the ear and approval of the gallery, Lenoir seemed, as it were, to spread himself out, to arrogate to himself the leadership of this band of malcontents, who, disappointed in their lust of Déroulède’s downfall, were ready to exult over that of Merlin.

===Chinese===
勒努瓦往地板上啐了一口，把两条黑黢黢、毛茸茸的胳膊在桌上一交叠，平平静静地说：

「真正的爱国主义，照真正的雅各宾派的理解，就是需要什么样的罪证，便自己炮制什么样的罪证，绝不听凭运气。」

一片嘶哑的「*Vive la Liberté!*」（自由万岁！）附和声，向这位粗壮运煤工的高谈阔论喝起彩来。

勒努瓦觉得看客们已经在侧耳倾听、交口称许，于是似乎愈发得意忘形，俨然以这伙心怀不满的人的首领自居——这伙人眼看德鲁莱德垮台的欲念落了空，正巴不得拿梅尔兰的垮台来痛快一番。

===Original===
“You were a fool, Citizen Merlin,” said Lenoir with slow significance, “not to see that the woman was playing her own game.”

Merlin had become livid under the grime on his face. With this ill-kempt sansculotte giant in front of him, he almost felt as if he were already arraigned before that awful, merciless tribunal, to which he had dragged so many innocent victims.

Already he felt, as he sat ensconced behind a table in the far corner of the room, that he was a prisoner at the bar, answering for his failure with his life.

His own laws, his own theories now stood in bloody array against him. Was it not he who had framed the indictments against General Custine for having failed to subdue the cities of the south? against General Westerman and Brunet and Beauharnais for having failed and failed and failed?

===Chinese===
「你真是个蠢货，梅尔兰公民，」勒努瓦一字一顿、意味深长地说，「竟没看出那个女人在玩她自己的把戏。」

梅尔兰满脸的污垢底下泛起了青紫色。面对这个邋里邋遢的无套裤汉巨人，他几乎觉得自己已经站到了那座可怕而毫无怜悯的法庭上受审——他曾亲手把多少无辜的牺牲品拖去过那里。

他蜷在屋子最远那个角落的桌子后面，已经真真切切地感到自己是被告席上的囚徒，正要拿性命为自己的失败作交代。

如今，他自己定下的法律、他自己鼓吹的理论，正血淋淋地摆开阵仗反噬他本人。起草控告屈斯蒂纳将军的罪状的，难道不是他吗？——只因将军未能征服南方诸城。控告韦斯特曼（Westerman）将军、布吕内（Brunet）将军和博阿尔内（Beauharnais）将军的，难道不也是他吗？——只因他们失败、失败、一败再败？

===Original===
And now it was his turn.

These bloodthirsty jackals had been cheated of their prey; they would tear him to pieces in compensation of their loss.

“How could I tell?” he murmured roughly, “the woman had denounced him.”

A chorus of angry derision greeted this feeble attempt at defence.

===Chinese===
如今，轮到他自己了。

这帮嗜血成性的豺狼眼看嘴边的猎物落了空，便要把他撕成碎片，权当补偿。

「我怎么会知道？」他粗声粗气地咕哝道，「那女人告发了他。」

这番软弱无力的辩白，迎来的是一片怒气冲冲的讥笑。

===Original===
“By your own law, Citizen-Deputy Merlin,” commented Tinville sarcastically, “it is a crime against the Republic to be suspected of treason. It is evident, however, that it is quite one thing to frame a law and quite another to obey it.”

“What could I have done?”

“Hark at the innocent!” rejoined Lenoir, with a sneer. “What could he have done? Patriots, friends, brothers, I ask you, what could he have done?”

The giant had pushed the wine cask aside, it rolled away from under him, and in the fullness of his contempt for Merlin and his impotence, he stood up before them all, strong in his indictment against treasonable incapacity.

===Chinese===
「照你自己定的法律，梅尔兰公会议员，」坦维尔讥诮地说，「涉嫌叛国，本身就是一种反对共和国的罪行。不过显而易见，制定法律是一回事，服从法律又是另一回事。」

「我又能怎么办？」

「听听这位清白人吧！」勒努瓦冷笑着接过话头。「他能怎么办？爱国者们，朋友们，兄弟们，我倒要问问你们，他能怎么办？」

巨人把酒桶往旁边一搡，酒桶从他身下滚了开去；怀着对梅尔兰及其无能的满腹轻蔑，他当众站起身来，正气凛然地控诉那叛国式的无能。

===Original===
“I ask you,” he repeated, with a loud oath, “what any patriot would do, what you or I would have done, in the house of a man whom we all *know* is a traitor to the Republic? Brothers, friends, Citizen-Deputy Merlin found a heap of burnt paper in a grate, he found a letter-case which had obviously contained important documents, and he asks us what he could do!”

“Déroulède is too important a man to be tried without proofs. The whole mob of Paris would have turned on us for having arraigned him, for having dared lay hands upon his sacred person.”

“Without proofs? Who said there were no proofs?” queried Lenoir.

===Chinese===
「我问你们，」他重复道，夹着一声响亮的咒骂，「随便哪个爱国者——你，或者我——走进一个我们全都*知道*背叛了共和国的人的家里，会怎么做？兄弟们，朋友们，梅尔兰公会议员在一个炉膛里发现了一堆烧剩的纸，发现了一只显然装过重要文件的信匣，他倒来问我们，他能怎么办！」

「德鲁莱德这样的人物，没有罪证是不能审判的。全巴黎的暴民会立刻调转头来对付我们，只因我们传讯了他，只因我们竟敢碰他神圣不可侵犯的人身。」

「没有罪证？谁说没有罪证？」勒努瓦反问道。

===Original===
“I found the burnt papers and torn letter-case in the woman’s room. She owned that they were love letters, and that she had denounced Déroulède in order to be rid of him.”

“Then let me tell you, Citizen-Deputy Merlin, that a true patriot would have found those papers in Déroulède’s, and not the woman’s room; that in the hands of a faithful servant of the Republic those documents would not all have been destroyed, for he would have ‘found’ one letter addressed to the Widow Capet, which would have proved conclusively that Citizen-Deputy Déroulède was a traitor. That is what a true patriot would have done﻿—what I would have done. *Pardi!* since Déroulède is so important a personage, since we must all put on kid gloves when we lay hands upon him, then let us fight him with other weapons. Are we aristocrats that we should hesitate to play the part of jackal to this cunning fox? Citizen-Deputy Merlin, are you the son of some ci-devant duke or prince that you dared not *forge* a document which would bring a traitor to his doom? Nay; let me tell you, friends, that the Republic has no use for curs, and calls him a traitor who allows one of her enemies to remain inviolate through his cowardice, his terror of that intangible and fleeting shadow﻿—the wrath of a Paris mob.”

Thunderous applause greeted this peroration, which had been delivered with an accompaniment of violent gestures and a wealth of obscene epithets, quite beyond the power of the mere chronicler to render. Lenoir had a harsh, strident voice, very high pitched, and he spoke with a broad, provincial accent, somewhat difficult to locate, but quite unlike the hoarse, guttural tones of the low-class Parisian. His enthusiasm made him seem impressive. He looked, in his ragged, dust-stained clothes, the very personification of the squalid herd which had driven culture, art, refinement to the scaffold in order to make way for sordid vice, and satisfied lusts of hate.

===Chinese===
「烧剩的纸和撕破的信匣，是在那个女人的房间里搜出来的。她承认那是些情书，还承认她告发德鲁莱德，就是为了甩掉他。」

「那么让我告诉你，梅尔兰公会议员：一个真正的爱国者，那些纸就该是从德鲁莱德的房间里搜出来的，而不是那个女人的房间；这些东西到了共和国一个忠心仆人的手里，绝不会全都销毁，因为他会替自己『找』出一封写给卡佩寡妇（the Widow Capet）的信，铁证如山，证明德鲁莱德公会议员是个叛徒。这才是真正的爱国者干的事——也是我干的事。*Pardi!*（哼！）既然德鲁莱德是这么一位大人物，既然我们个个都得戴上小山羊皮手套才敢碰他，那咱们就换别的家伙跟他斗。难道我们是贵族老爷不成，竟至于不敢给这只狡诈的狐狸当走狗？梅尔兰公会议员，你是不是哪位前贵族（ci-devant）公爵或者亲王的儿子，竟不敢*伪造*一纸文书，把一个叛徒送上绝路？不；让我告诉你们，朋友们：共和国用不着孬种；谁出于怯懦——出于对那捉摸不住、转瞬即逝的影子——巴黎暴民怒火的恐惧——听任她的一个敌人安然无恙，共和国就说他是叛徒。」

雷鸣般的掌声为这篇结尾宏论喝彩；这篇宏论是伴着猛烈的比划和满口污言秽语发表出来的，那副猥亵粗鄙之状，绝非区区一个记述者所能摹绘。勒努瓦的嗓音沙哑刺耳，调门极高，说话带着浓重的外省口音，一时难以断定出自哪一府县，却全然不同于巴黎下等人那种嘶哑的喉音。他那股热忱使他显得颇有声势。他一身褴褛、尘垢满面的衣衫，活脱脱就是那群龌龊乌合之众的化身——正是这帮人把文化、艺术、优雅统统驱赶上了断头台，好给卑污的恶癖腾出地盘，好餍足他们仇恨的兽欲。

## XXI A Jacobin Orator / XXI 雅各宾演说家

===Original===
Tinville alone had remained silent during Lenoir's impassioned speech. It seemed to be his turn now to become surly. He sat picking his teeth, and staring moodily at the enthusiastic orator, who had so obviously diverted popular feeling in his own direction. And Tinville brooked popularity only for himself.

"It is easy to talk now, Citizen—er—Lenoir. Is that your name? Well, you are a comparative stranger here, Citizen Lenoir, and have not yet proved to the Republic that you can do ought else but talk."

"If somebody did not talk, Citizen Tinville—is that your name?" rejoined Lenoir, with a sneer—"if somebody didn't talk, nothing would get done. You all sit here, and condemn the Citizen-Deputy Merlin for being a fool, and I must say I am with you there, but—"

"*Pardi!* tell us your 'but' citizen," said Tinville, for the coal-heaver had paused, as if trying to collect his thoughts. He had dragged a wine barrel to collect his thoughts. He had dragged a wine barrel close to the trestle table, and now sat astride upon it, facing Tinville and the group of Jacobins. The flickering tallow candle behind him threw into bold silhouette his square, massive head, crowned with its Phrygian cap, and the great breadth of his shoulders, with the shabby knitted spencer and low, turned-down collar.

===Chinese===
勒努瓦（Lenoir）那番慷慨激昂的演说进行之际，唯独坦维尔（Tinville）一言不发。看来如今该轮到他来沉着脸了。他坐在那里剔着牙齿，闷闷不乐地盯着那位热情洋溢的演说家——此人显然已把众人的情绪引向了自己一边。而坦维尔能容得的声望，唯有他自己的一份。

「如今要说是容易的，公民——呃——勒努瓦。你是叫这个名字吧？唔，你在这儿还算个生面孔，勒努瓦公民，你还没向共和国证明，除了空谈你还能干出别的什么名堂来。」

「可要是没人开口说话呢，坦维尔公民——你是叫这个名字吧？」勒努瓦冷笑着反驳道——「要是没人说话，那就什么也办不成。你们一个个坐在这儿，骂梅尔兰（Merlin）公会议员是个蠢货；这一点我倒要承认，跟你们的看法一致，可是——」

「*Pardi!*（哼！）那就把你那个『可是』说出来吧，公民，」坦维尔说道，因为那运煤工顿住了话头，仿佛在凝神思索。他拖来一只酒桶，好凝神思索。他把酒桶拖到支架长桌旁边，如今正跨坐在桶上，面对着坦维尔和那一群雅各宾派。他身后那支闪烁的牛脂蜡烛，把他那方正厚实的头颅——头顶上还戴着弗里吉亚帽（Phrygian cap）——连同那宽阔的双肩，以及破旧的针织短上衣和低低的翻领，清晰地勾成一幅黑色的剪影。

===Original===
He had long, thin hands, which were covered with successive coats of coal dust, and with these he constantly made weird gestures, as if in the act of gripping some live thing by the throat.

"We all know that the Deputy Déroulède is a traitor, eh?" he said, addressing the company in general.

"We do," came with uniform assent from all those present.

"Then let us put it to the vote. The Ayes mean death, the Noes freedom."

"Ay, ay!" came from every hoarse, parched throat; and twelve gaunt hands were lifted up demanding death for Citizen-Deputy Déroulède.

===Chinese===
他那双手细长，一层层裹着煤灰；这双手不停地做着些怪异的比划，仿佛正一把掐住什么活物的咽喉。

「我们大家都知道，德鲁莱德（Déroulède）公会议员是个卖国贼，对吧？」他冲着在场众人说道。

「知道，」在场的人异口同声地应道。

「那咱们就来表决吧。赞成的，意味着死亡；反对的，意味着自由。」

「赞成！赞成！」每一副嘶哑干渴的喉咙里都吼了出来；十二只枯瘦的手举了起来，要求处死德鲁莱德公会议员。

===Original===
"The Ayes have it," said Lenoir quietly, "Now all we need do is to decide how best to carry out our purpose."

Merlin, very agreeably surprised to see public attention thus diverted from his own misdeeds, had gradually lost his surly attitude. He too dragged one of the wine barrels, which did duty for chairs, close to the trestle table, and thus the members of the nameless Jacobin club made a compact group, picturesque in its weird horror, its uncompromising, flaunting ugliness.

"I suppose," said Tinville, who was loth to give up his position as leader of these extremists—"I suppose, Citizen Lenoir, that you are in position to furnish me with proofs of the Citizen-Deputy's guilt?"

"If I furnish you with such proofs, Citizen Tinville," retorted the other, "will you, as Public Prosecutor, carry the indictment through?"

===Chinese===
「赞成通过，」勒努瓦平静地说道，「眼下我们要办的，只是决定用什么法子最好地达到我们的目的。」

梅尔兰见众人的注意这样从他的过失上岔了开去，又惊又喜，阴沉的态度便渐渐消了。他也拖了一只充当椅子用的酒桶，凑到支架长桌旁边；于是，这个无名雅各宾俱乐部的成员们便挤作紧密的一团，那模样诡异而可怖，丑陋得不加遮掩、张扬刺目，倒也别有一幅图画感。

「我想，」坦维尔说道，他不甘心放弃这群极端分子的领袖地位——「我想，勒努瓦公民，你有办法向我提供这位公会议员罪行的证据吧？」

「如果我给你这样的证据，坦维尔公民，」对方反唇相讥，「你，身为公诉人（Public Prosecutor），肯把起诉贯彻到底吗？」

===Original===
"It is my duty to publicly accuse those who are traitors to the Republic."

"And you, Citizen Merlin," queried Lenoir, "will you help the Republic to the best of your ability to be rid of a traitor?"

"My services to the cause of our great Revolution are too well known—" began Merlin.

But Lenoir interrupted him with impatience.

"*Pardi!* but we'll have no rhetoric now, Citizen Merlin. We all know that you have blundered, and that the Republic cares little for those of her sons who have failed, but whilst you are still Minister of Justice the people of France have need of you—for bringing *other* traitors to the guillotine."

===Chinese===
「公开指控那些背叛共和国的人，是我的职责。」

「那么你呢，梅尔兰公民，」勒努瓦问道，「你可愿意竭尽所能，帮共和国除掉一个卖国贼？」

「我对我们伟大革命事业的贡献，是尽人皆知的——」梅尔兰开口道。

可勒努瓦不耐烦地打断了他。

「*Pardi!*（哼！）眼下可不要什么漂亮辞令，梅尔兰公民。我们都知道你栽了跟头，共和国也不怎么把她那些失了手的儿子放在心上；可只要你还是司法部长，法兰西人民就还需要你——去把*别的*卖国贼送上断头台。」

===Original===
He spoke this last phrase slowly and significantly, lingering on the word "other," as if he wished its whole awesome meaning to penetrate well into Merlin's brain.

"What is your advice then, Citizen Lenoir?"

Apparently, by unanimous consent, the coal-heaver, from some obscure province of France, had been tacitly acknowledged the leader of the band. Merlin, still in terror for himself, looked to him for advice; even Tinville was ready to be guided by him. All were at one in their desire to rid themselves of Déroulède, who by his clean living, his aloofness from their own hideous orgies and deadly hates, seemed a living reproach to them all; and they all felt that in Lenoir there must exist some secret dislike of the popular Citizen-Deputy, which would give him a clear insight of how best to bring about his downfall.

"What is your advice?" had been Merlin's query, and everyone there listened eagerly for what was to come.

"We are all agreed," commenced Lenoir quietly, "that just at this moment it would be unwise to arraign the Citizen-Deputy without material proof. The mob of Paris worship him, and would turn against those who had tried to dethrone their idol. Now, Citizen Merlin failed to furnish us with proofs of Déroulède's guilt. For the moment he is a free man, and I imagine a wise one; within two days he will have quitted this country, well knowing that, if he stayed long enough to see his popularity wane, he would also outstay his welcome on earth altogether."

===Chinese===
他说最后这半句时缓慢而有深意，在那个「别的」上逗留不去，仿佛一心要让它全部可怖的含义，深深钻进梅尔兰的脑子里。

「那么依你之见呢，勒努瓦公民？」

显然，出于一致的心意，这个来自法兰西某个无名省份的运煤工，已被默认为这一伙人的头目。梅尔兰自己还在惶惶自危，便指望他出主意；连坦维尔也准备听他调遣。所有人都同心一意，想把德鲁莱德除掉——此人生活清白，同他们那些狰狞的狂欢与不共戴天的仇恨保持距离，在他们眼中无异于活生生的一句谴责；而他们都感到，勒努瓦心里必定藏着对这位得人心的公会议员的某种隐秘的厌恶，这会使他洞悉如何最妥善地促成其垮台。

「依你之见？」——这是梅尔兰的问话；在场人人都急切地等着下文。

「我们大家都同意，」勒努瓦平静地开口道，「就在此刻，没有实在的证据就提审这位公会议员，是不明智的。巴黎的民众崇拜他，会转而攻击那些企图推倒他们偶像的人。如今，梅尔兰公民没能给我们拿出德鲁莱德罪行的证据。眼下他是个自由人，想必还是个有几分头脑的自由人；不出两天，他就会离开这个国家了，因为他心里明白，倘若他滞留到亲眼看见自己的声望消退，那他在这个世上受人欢迎的日子也就彻底过完了头。」

===Original===
"Ay! Ay!" said some of the men approvingly, whilst others laughed hoarsely at the weird jest.

"I propose, therefore," continued Lenoir after a slight pause, "that it shall be Citizen-Deputy Déroulède himself who shall furnish to the people of France proofs of his own treason against the Republic."

"But how? But how?" rapid, loud and excited queries greeted this extraordinary suggestion from the provincial giant.

"By the simplest means imaginable," retorted Lenoir with imperturbable calm. "Isn't there a good proverb which our grandmothers used to quote, that if you only give a man a sufficient length of rope, he is sure to hang himself? We'll give our aristocratic Citizen-Deputy plenty of rope, I'll warrant, if only our present Minister of Justice," he added, indicating Merlin, "will help us in the little comedy which I propose that we should play."

===Chinese===
「是啊！是啊！」几个人称许地应道，另一些人则为这阴森的打趣哑声大笑起来。

「因此我提议，」勒努瓦稍稍顿了顿，接着说道，「就让德鲁莱德公会议员本人，向法兰西的人民提供他背叛共和国的罪证。」

「可怎么弄？怎么弄？」急促、响亮而亢奋的追问，四下里迎向这个外省巨人的奇想。

「用想得出来的最简单的法子，」勒努瓦泰然自若地答道。「咱们祖母们不是常爱引一句好谚语吗——只要给一个人足够长的绳子，他准会拿去吊死自己。只要我们现任的司法部长，」他加了一句，朝梅尔兰指了指，「肯在我们提议要上演的这出小喜剧里助我们一臂之力，我敢担保，咱们就会给这位贵族气派的公会议员送上足够长的绳子。」

===Original===
"Yes! Yes! Go on!" said Merlin excitedly.

"The woman who denounced Déroulède—that is our trump card," continued Lenoir, now waxing enthusiastic with his own scheme and his own eloquence. "She denounced him. Ergo, he had been her lover, whom she wished to be rid of—why? Not, as Citizen Merlin supposed, because he had discarded her. No, no; she had another lover—she has admitted that. She wished to be rid of Déroulède to make way for the other, because he was too persistent—ergo, because he loved her."

"Well, and what does that prove?" queried Tinville with dry sarcasm.

"It proves that Déroulède, being in love with the woman, would do much to save her from the guillotine."

"Of course."

===Chinese===
「对！对！说下去！」梅尔兰兴奋地说。

「那个告发德鲁莱德的女人——那是我们的一张王牌，」勒努瓦接着说道，此刻他为自己的计谋、为自己的口才而愈发热血沸腾。「她告发了他。由此可知，他曾做过她的情夫，她想摆脱他——为什么？并非如梅尔兰公民所设想的那样，因为他抛弃了她。不，不；她另有一个情夫——这她已经招认了。她想摆脱德鲁莱德，好给另一个人腾出位子，只因为德鲁莱德纠缠得太紧——由此可知，因为他爱她。」

「哼，那又证明了什么呢？」坦维尔冷冰冰地讥诮道。

「它证明，德鲁莱德既已爱上了这个女人，就会不惜代价，把她从断头台上救下来。」

「那还用说。」

===Original===
"*Pardi!* let him try, say I," rejoined Lenoir placidly. "Give him the rope with which to hang himself."

"What does he mean?" asked one or two of the men, whose dull brains had not quite as yet grasped the full meaning of this monstrous scheme.

"You don't understand what I mean, citizens; you think I am mad, or drunk, or a traitor like Déroulède? *Eh bien!* give me your attention five minutes longer, and you shall see. Let me suppose that we have reached the moment when the woman—what is her name? Oh! ah! yes! Juliette Marny—stands in the Hall of Justice on her trial before the Committee of Public Safety. Citizen Foucquier-Tinville, one of our greatest patriots, reads the indictment against her: the papers surreptitiously burnt, the torn, mysterious letter-case found in her room. If these are presumed, in the indictment, to be treasonable correspondence with the enemies of the Republic, condemnation follows at once, then the guillotine. There is no defence, no respite. The Minister of Justice, according to Article IX of the Law framed by himself, allows no advocate to those directly accused of treason. But," continued the giant, with slow and calm impressiveness, "in the case of ordinary, civil indictments, offences against public morality or matters pertaining to the penal code, the Minister of Justice allows the accused to be publicly defended. Place Juliette Marny in the dock on a treasonable charge, she will be hustled out of the court in a few minutes, amongst a batch of other traitors, dragged back to her own prison, and executed in the early dawn, before Déroulède has had time to frame a plan for her safety or defence. If, then, he tries to move heaven and earth to rescue the woman he loves, the mob of Paris may—who knows?—take his part warmly. They are mad where Déroulède is concerned; and we all know that two devoted lovers have ere now found favour with the people of France—a curious remnant of sentimentalism, I suppose—and the popular Citizen-Deputy knows better than anyone else on earth, how to play upon the sentimental feelings of the populace. Now, in the case of a penal offence, mark where the difference would be! The woman Juliette Marny, arraigned for wantonness, for an offence against public morals; the burnt correspondence, admitted to be the letters of a lover—her hatred for Déroulède suggesting the false denunciation. Then the Minister of Justice allows an advocate to defend her. She has none in court; but think you Déroulède would not step forward, and bring all the fervour of his eloquence to bear in favour of his mistress? Can you hear his impassioned speech on her behalf?—I can—the rope, I tell you, citizens, with which he'll hang himself. Will he admit in open court that the burnt correspondence was another lover's letters? No!—a thousand times no!—and, in the face of his emphatic denial of the existence of another lover for Juliette, it will be for our clever Public Prosecutor to bring him down to an admission that the correspondence was his, that it was treasonable, that she burnt them to save him."

He paused, exhausted at last, mopping his forehead, then drinking large gulps of brandy to ease his parched throat.

===Chinese===
「*Pardi!*（哼！）要我说，就让他去试好啦，」勒努瓦心平气和地应道。「把那根吊死他自己的绳子递到他手里。」

「他这话是什么意思？」一两个人问道，他们迟钝的脑子还没全然领悟这桩骇人计谋的完整含义。

「你们没明白我的意思，公民们；你们当我是疯了，还是喝醉了，还是跟德鲁莱德一样是个卖国贼？*Eh bien!*（那好！）再给我五分钟的注意，你们就会明白。且容我设想，我们已到了这样的时刻：那个女人——她叫什么来着？哦！啊！对了！朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）——正站在司法大厅（Hall of Justice）里，在救国委员会面前受审。富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville）公民——我们最伟大的爱国者之一——宣读对她的起诉书：被人暗中焚毁的信件，以及从她房中搜出的那只撕裂的、可疑的信匣。倘若起诉书里推定这些是她同共和国的敌人通敌叛国的往来书信，那便当场定罪，继而上断头台。没有辩护，没有缓刑。依照他自己亲手炮制的法令第九条，司法部长是不许给直接被控叛国罪的人指派辩护人的。可是，」巨人接着说道，语调缓慢而沉静，威严逼人，「倘若是一般的民事诉讼、有伤风化的罪行或属于刑律范围的案件，司法部长便准许被告当众辩护。若把朱丽叶特·马尔尼以叛国罪名放上被告席，不出几分钟，她就会同一批别的卖国贼一道被轰出法庭，拖回她自己所在的监狱，在破晓时分处决——德鲁莱德还来不及拟出任何保全她或为她辩护的计划。到那时，他若想竭尽天上人间的一切手段，去营救他所爱的女人，巴黎的民众或许——谁知道呢？——会热烈地站到他一边。凡牵涉德鲁莱德的事，他们都是狂热得失去理智的；而且我们都知道，一对忠贞相爱的恋人，往日也曾博得法兰西人民的欢心——我猜想，这是感伤情调的一种古怪的残余——而这位得人心的公会议员，比世上任何人都更懂得，怎样去拨动民众感伤的心弦。如今，若换成刑律上的罪名，且看清其中的差别！朱丽叶特·马尔尼这个女人，被控淫佚放荡、有伤风化；焚毁的通信，被承认是一个情夫的情书——她之告发德鲁莱德，则是出于对他的仇恨，乃是一场诬告。这时，司法部长便准许指派辩护人为她辩护。法庭上她一个辩护人也没有；可你们想想，德鲁莱德岂会不挺身向前，以他全部雄辩的热忱，为他的情妇力争？你们可听得见他为她发表的慷慨激昂的辩词？——我听得见——那便是绳子，我对你们说，公民们，他就要拿这根绳子吊死自己。他会在公开的法庭上承认，那焚毁的通信是另一个情夫写的情书吗？不会！——一万个不会！——而当着他斩钉截铁地否认朱丽叶特另有一个情夫，我们那位精明的公诉人，就有本事步步进逼，逼得他招认：那些书信是他写的，是叛国的通信，她烧掉它们，是为了救他。」

他停下来，终于力竭了，一面抹着额头，随即又大口大口地灌下白兰地，好润一润冒烟的喉咙。

===Original===
A veritable chorus of enthusiasm greeted the end of his long peroration. The Machiavelian scheme, almost devilish in its cunning, in its subtle knowledge of human nature and of the heartstrings of a noble organisation like Déroulède's, commended itself to these patriots, who were thirsting for the downfall of a superior enemy.

Even Tinville lost his attitude of dry sarcasm; his thin cheeks were glowing with the lust of the fight.

Already for the past few months, the trials before the Committee of Public Safety had been dull, monotonous, uninteresting. Charlotte Corday had been a happy diversion, but otherwise it had been the case of various deputies, who had held views that had become too moderate, or of the generals who had failed to subdue the towns or provinces of the south.

===Chinese===
他这番长长的结语收束之时，迎来了一片名副其实的热烈喝彩。这马基雅维利式的计谋，狡诈得几乎近于魔鬼，又如此精微地洞悉人性，洞悉德鲁莱德那样高贵天性的心弦，正投合这些爱国者的心意——他们正渴盼着一个高出一筹的敌人覆灭。

连坦维尔也收起了他那副冷冰冰的讥诮腔调；他瘦削的面颊上，燃烧着搏杀的贪欲。

这些月来，救国委员会面前的审讯本已索然、单调、乏味。夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）还算是一桩可喜的插曲，此外无非是些议员，因为持了变得过于温和的政见；再不然就是些将军，因为没能平定南方的城镇和省份。

===Original===
But now this trial on the morrow—the excitement of it all, the trap laid for Déroulède, the pleasure of seeing him take the first step towards his own downfall. Everyone there was eager and enthusiastic for the fray. Lenoir, having spoken at such length, had now become silent, but everyone else talked, and drank brandy, and hugged his own hate and likely triumph.

For several hours, far into the night, the sitting was continued. Each one of the score of members had some comment to make on Lenoir's speech, some suggestion to offer.

Lenoir himself was the first to break up this weird gathering of human jackals, already exulting over their prey. He bad his companions a quiet good night, then passed out into the dark street.

After he had gone there were a few seconds of complete silence in the dark and sordid room, where men's ugliest passions were holding absolute sway. The giant's heavy footsteps echoed along the ill-paved street, and gradually died away in the distance.

===Chinese===
而如今，明天就要开庭——这一切多么令人亢奋：为德鲁莱德设下的圈套，眼看他自己迈出走向覆灭的第一步，是何等快意。在场人人都跃跃欲试，热血沸腾地等着这场厮杀。勒努瓦说了这许久的话，如今已归于沉默；旁的人却你一言我一语，喝着白兰地，各自拥着自己的仇恨，拥着那可望到手的胜利。

会接连开了好几个钟头，直到深夜。二十来个成员，人人都要对勒努瓦的演说评点几句，提出点建议。

勒努瓦自己头一个散了这群人豺般的怪异聚会——他们已在为到手的猎物欢欣鼓舞。他向同伴们平静地道了晚安，便走出门去，融进黑暗的街道。

他走后，那间黑暗而污浊的房间里静了片刻，全然无声——这里正由人类最丑恶的激情主宰着一切。巨人沉重的脚步声，在铺石残破的街上回响，渐渐消失在远处。

===Original===
Then at last Foucquier-Tinville, the Public Prosecutor, spoke:

"And who is that man?" he asked, addressing the assembly of patriots.

Most of them did not know.

"A provincial from the north," said one of the men at last; "he has been here several times before now, and last year he was a fairly constant attendant. I believe he is a butcher by trade, and I fancy he comes from Calais. He was originally brought here by Citizen Brogard, who is good patriot enough."

One by one the members of this bond of Fraternity began to file out of the Cheval Borgne. They nodded curt good nights to each other, and then went to their respective abodes, which surely could not be dignified with the name of home.

===Chinese===
这时，公诉人富基耶-坦维尔终于开口说道：

「那人到底是谁？」他问在座的一众爱国者。

他们大都不晓得。

「北边来的外省人，」一个人终于说道；「他以前来过好几回，去年还相当常来。我记得他做的是屠夫行当，我琢磨他是加来（Calais）人。当初是布罗加（Brogard）公民把他带到这儿来的，那人是够格的爱国者。」

这个博爱结社的成员们，开始一个接一个从独眼马客栈里鱼贯而出。他们彼此生硬地点头道了晚安，便各自回到住处去——那些住处，实在当不起「家」这样的名号。

===Original===
Tinville remained one of the last; he and Merlin seemed suddenly to have buried the hatchet, which a few hours ago had threatened to destroy one or the other of these whilom bosom friends.

Two or three of the most ardent of these ardent extremists had gathered round the Public Prosecutor, and Merlin, the framer of the Law of the Suspect.

"What say you, citizens?" said Tinville at last quietly. "That man Lenoir, meseems, is too eloquent—eh?"

"Dangerous," pronounced Merlin, whilst the others nodded approval.

"But his scheme is good," suggested one of the men.

===Chinese===
坦维尔留到了最后；他同梅尔兰仿佛骤然间已把那柄战斧埋了下去——就在几个钟头以前，那柄战斧还眼看要在这对昔日挚友之间，置一人为死地。

这些狂热极端分子中最狂热的两三个人，围拢到公诉人和《嫌疑犯法令》的炮制者梅尔兰身边。

「你们看呢，公民们？」坦维尔终于平静地说道。「那个勒努瓦，依我看，口才太好了——嗯？」

「危险，」梅尔兰断言道，旁的人都点头称是。

「可他的计策是好计策，」其中一个人说。

===Original===
"And we'll avail ourselves of it," assented Tinville, "but afterwards—"

He paused, and once more everyone nodded approval.

"Yes; he is dangerous. We'll leave him in peace tomorrow, but afterwards—"

With a gentle hand Tinville caressed the tall double post, which stood in the centre of the room, and which was shaped like the guillotine. An evil look was on his face: the grin of a death-dealing monster, savage and envious. The others laughed in grim content. Merlin grunted a surly approval. He had no cause to love the provincial coal-heaver who had raised a raucous voice to threaten him.

Then, nodding to one another, the last of the patriots, satisfied with this night's work, passed out into the night.

===Chinese===
「我们会用他的计策，」坦维尔应道，「但事成之后——」

他顿住了，众人又一次点头称是。

「不错；他是危险的。明天我们就让他安生一天，可事成之后——」

坦维尔伸出一只手，温柔地爱抚着竖立在房间中央的那根高高的双柱——那形状恰似一座断头台。他脸上带着一种邪恶的神情：那是索命的怪物才有的狞笑，既凶残，又嫉恨。旁人阴森森地笑着，心满意足。梅尔兰闷哼一声，表示赞同。他对那个外省运煤工毫无爱惜之理——那家伙曾扯起嘶哑的嗓门，扬言要威胁他来着。

随后，最后几个爱国者互相对点着头，对这一夜的勾当心满意足，走进夜色里去了。

===Original===
The watchman was making his rounds, carrying his lantern, and shouting his customary cry:

"Inhabitants of Paris, sleep quietly. Everything is in order, everything is at peace."

===Chinese===
巡夜人正打着他的更，提着灯笼，吆喝着他照例的喊声：

「巴黎的居民们，安心睡觉吧。一切秩序井然，一切安宁无事。」

## XXII The Close of Day / XXII 一日将尽

===Original===
Déroulède had spent the whole of this same night in a wild, impassioned search for Juliette.

Earlier in the day, soon after Anne Mie's revelations, he had sought out his English friend, Sir Percy Blakeney, and talked over with him the final arrangements for the removal of Madame Déroulède and Anne Mie from Paris.

Though he was a born idealist and a Utopian, Paul Déroulède had never for a moment had any illusions with regard to his own popularity. He knew that at any time, and for any trivial cause, the love which the mob bore him would readily turn to hate. He had seen Mirabeau's popularity wane, La Fayette's, Desmoulin's—was it likely that *he* alone would survive the inevitable death of so ephemeral a thing?

Therefore, whilst he was in power, whilst he was loved and trusted, he had, figuratively and actually, put his house in order. He had made full preparations for his own inevitable downfall, for that probable flight from Paris of those who were dependent upon him.

===Chinese===
就在同一个夜晚，德鲁莱德（Déroulède）整夜都在疯狂而炽热地搜寻朱丽叶特（Juliette）。

这天早些时候，就在安娜·米（Anne Mie）吐露实情之后不久，他便找到了他的英国朋友珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney），同他商定了把德鲁莱德夫人（Madame Déroulède）和安娜·米送出巴黎的最后安排。

保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）虽是天生的理想主义者、空想家，对自己的声望却从未有过片刻的幻想。他心里明白，无论何时，也无论为着怎样一桩微不足道的缘由，民众对他的爱戴都可能说变就变，转成仇恨。他亲眼见过米拉波（Mirabeau）的声望如何消歇，见过拉法耶特（La Fayette）的、德穆兰（Desmoulin）的——如此短命之物，注定要死；难道单单*他*一人，就能从这必然的覆亡中幸免吗？

因此，趁着还在当权，趁着还受人爱戴、被人信任，他无论在譬喻上还是在实际上，都已把家务整顿妥帖。他早已为自己的必然垮台做好了万全准备，也为那些依附于他的人可能逃离巴黎一事，做下了周密安排。

===Original===
He had, as far back as a year ago, provided himself with the necessary passports, and bespoken with his English friend certain measures for the safety of his mother and his crippled little relative. Now it was merely a question of putting these measures into execution.

Within two hours of Juliette Marny's arrest, Madame Déroulède and Anne Mie had quitted the house in the Rue Ecole de Médecine. They had but little luggage with them, and were ostensibly going into the country to visit a sick cousin.

The mother of the popular Citizen-Deputy was free to travel unmolested. The necessary passports which the safety of the Republic demanded were all in perfect order, and Madame Déroulède and Anne Mie passed through the north gate of Paris an hour before sunset, on that 24th day of Fructidor.

Their large travelling chaise took them some distance on the North Road, where they were to meet Lord Hastings and Lord Anthony Dewhurst, two of The Scarlet Pimpernel's most trusted lieutenants, who were to escort them as far as the coast, and thence see them safely aboard the English yacht.

On that score, therefore, Déroulède had no anxiety. His chief duty was to his mother and to Anne Mie, and that was now fully discharged.

===Chinese===
早在一年之前，他就为自己备下了必需的通行证，并同他的英国朋友商定了某些措施，以保他的母亲和他那位身有残疾的小表妹的安全。如今，剩下的只是把这些措施付诸实行了。

朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）被捕之后不出两个钟头，德鲁莱德夫人和安娜·米便离开了医学院街（Rue Ecole de Médecine）上的那所宅子。她们随身行李极少，名义上是下乡去探望一位患病的表亲。

这位深得人心的公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）的母亲，尽可以一路无阻地出行。共和国（the Republic）的安危所要求的一切必需证件，无不齐备完好；果月（Fructidor）二十四日那天，日落前一个钟头，德鲁莱德夫人和安娜·米便走出了巴黎的北城门。

一辆宽大的旅行马车载着她们沿北方大道驶出了一段路；她们要在那里同黑斯廷斯勋爵（Lord Hastings）和安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）会合——这二位是红花侠（The Scarlet Pimpernel）最受信任的副手，要一路护送她们直到海边，再从那里送她们平安登上英国游艇。

因此，在这件事上，德鲁莱德全然放心。他最主要的责任是对他的母亲和安娜·米的，如今这项责任已经完全尽到了。

===Original===
Then there was old Pétronelle.

Ever since the arrest of her young mistress the poor old soul had been in a state of mind bordering on frenzy, and no amount of eloquence on Déroulède's part would persuade her to quit Paris without Juliette.

"If my pet lamb is to die," she said amidst heartbroken sobs, "then I have no cause to live. Let those devils take me along too, if they want a useless, old woman like me. But if my darling is allowed to go free, then what would become of her in this awful city without me? She and I have never been separated; she wouldn't know where to turn for a home. And who would cook for her and iron out her kerchiefs, I'd like to know?"

===Chinese===
此外还有老佩特罗内勒（Pétronelle）。

自从她的年轻女主人被捕以来，这个可怜的老好人就处在一种近乎癫狂的精神状态里；任凭德鲁莱德费尽唇舌，也劝不动她肯在没有朱丽叶特的情况下离开巴黎。

「要是我的宝贝羔羊要死了，」她在泣不成声中说，「那我也没有活着的道理了。那些恶鬼要是肯要我这没用的老太婆，就让他们把我也一道带走吧。可要是放我的宝贝儿走，那没有了我，在这座可怕的城市里，她会成什么样呢？她跟我从来没有分开过；她连去哪儿找个安身的地方都不知道。还有谁给她做饭，谁给她熨头巾？我倒想问问。」

===Original===
Reason and common sense were, of course, powerless in face of this sublime and heroic childishness. No one had the heart to tell the old woman that the murderous dog of the Revolution seldom loosened its fangs, once they had closed upon a victim.

All Déroulède could do was to convey Pétronelle to the old abode, which Juliette had quitted in order to come to him, and which had never been formally given up. The worthy soul, calmed and refreshed, deluded herself into the idea that she was waiting for the return of her young mistress, and became quite cheerful at sight of the familiar room.

Déroulède had provided her with money and necessaries. He had but few remaining hopes in his heart, but among them was the firmly implanted one that Pétronelle was too insignificant to draw upon herself the terrible attention of the Committee of Public Safety.

By the nightfall he had seen the good woman safely installed. Then only did he feel free.

===Chinese===
面对这种崇高而英勇的稚气，讲道理、说常识，自然是毫无用处的。谁也没有这份狠心去告诉那老妇人：革命这条杀人的恶犬，一旦獠牙咬住了猎物，是很少松口的。

德鲁莱德所能做的，只有把佩特罗内勒送回那处旧居——朱丽叶特当初为了搬去与他同住而离开的老宅，而且从未正式退掉。这位善良的老妇人，心神既安，精神一爽，便自我催眠，只当自己是在等年轻女主人回来；一见到那间熟悉的屋子，就变得相当快活起来。

德鲁莱德给她留下了钱和种种必需品。他心中残存的希望已经寥寥无几，但其中一个却扎得极深：佩特罗内勒太微不足道，决引不来救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）那可怕的注意。

到入夜时分，他才把这位好妇人安顿得妥妥帖帖。直到那时，他才觉得自己一身自由了。

===Original===
At last he could devote himself to what seemed to him the one, the only, aim of his life—to find Juliette.

A dozen prisons in this vast Paris!

Over five thousand prisoners on that night, awaiting trial, condemnation and death.

Déroulède at first, strong in his own power, his personality, had thought that the task would be comparatively easy.

===Chinese===
终于，他可以把整个身心交给那个在他看来是他一生中唯一的、独一无二的目标了——去寻找朱丽叶特。

浩浩巴黎，竟有十二座监狱！

就在那一夜，五千多名囚犯，正等着受审、等着定罪、等着死亡。

德鲁莱德起初自恃权势在手、人格有力，还以为这桩差事会相对容易。

===Original===
At the Palais de Justice they would tell him nothing: the list of new arrests had not yet been handled in by the commandant of Paris, Citizen Santerre, who classified and docketed the miserable herd of aspirants for the next day's guillotine.

The lists, moreover, would not be completed until the next day, when the trials of the new prisoners would already be imminent.

The work of the Committee of Public Safety was done without much delay.

===Chinese===
在司法宫（Palais de Justice），他们什么都不肯告诉他：新捕人员的名单还没有由巴黎司令官桑特尔公民（Citizen Santerre）呈送上来；那班次日断头台的可悲候刑者，全要经他分类登记、编列造册。

况且，这些名单要到第二天方能造齐，而到那时，新囚犯的审判已是迫在眉睫了。

救国委员会的公务，向来办得雷厉风行，绝少耽搁。

===Original===
Then began Déroulède's weary quest through those twelve prisons of Paris. From the Temple to the Conciergerie, from Palais Condé to the Luxembourg, he spent hours in the fruitless search.

Everywhere the same shrug of the shoulders, the same indifferent reply to his eager query:

"Juliette Marny? *Inconnue.*"

===Chinese===
于是，德鲁莱德开始了在巴黎那十二座监狱之间疲惫不堪的寻访。从坦普尔监狱（the Temple）到巴黎古监狱（the Conciergerie），从孔代宫（Palais Condé）到卢森堡监狱（the Luxembourg），他一连耗费了几个钟头，搜寻却毫无结果。

到处都是同样的一耸肩，对他急切的探问，是同样一句漠然的回答：

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼？*Inconnue*（查无此人）。」

===Original===
Unknown! She had not yet been docketed, not yet classified; she was still one of that immense flock of cattle, sent in ever-increasing numbers to the slaughterhouse.

Presently, tomorrow, after a trial which might last ten minutes, after a hasty condemnation and quick return to prison, she would be listed as one of the traitors, whom this great and beneficent Republic sent daily to the guillotine.

Vainly did Déroulède try to persuade, to entreat, to bribe. The sullen guardians of these twelve charnel-houses knew nothing of individual prisoners.

===Chinese===
查无此人！她还没有登记造册，还没有归类编档；她还是那浩浩荡荡的牛群中的一头，正以日益增多的数目，一批批赶往屠宰场。

过不了多久——就在明天——一场也许持续不过十分钟的审讯，一阵仓促的定罪，随即将她押回狱中，她就会列进叛国者的名册——那个伟大而仁慈的共和国，天天都把这般人送上断头台。

德鲁莱德又劝说、又恳求、又想行贿，全属徒劳。这十二座藏骸所的那些阴沉看守，对个别的囚犯一概一无所知。

===Original===
But the Citizen-Deputy was allowed to look for himself. He was conducted to the great vaulted rooms of the Temple, to the vast ballrooms of the Palais Condé, where herded the condemned and those still awaiting trial; he was allowed to witness there the grim farcical tragedies, with which the captives beguiled the few hours which separated them from death.

Mock trials were acted there; Tinville was mimicked; then the Place de la Révolution; Samson the headsman, with a couple of inverted chairs to represent the guillotine.

Daughters of dukes and princes, descendants of ancient lineage, acted in these weird and ghastly comedies. The ladies, with hair bound high over their heads, would kneel before the inverted chairs, and place the snow-white necks beneath this imaginary guillotine. Speeches were delivered to a mock populace, whilst a mock Santerre ordered a mock roll of drums to drown the last flow of eloquence of the supposed victim.

===Chinese===
不过，这位公会议员倒是获准亲自查看。人们把他领到坦普尔监狱那穹顶高阔的大厅，领到孔代宫那宽敞的舞厅——已被定罪的和仍在候审的人，成群圈禁在那里；还容许他在那里目睹那些阴惨惨的闹剧式悲剧——被囚的人们，就靠着这些来消磨把他们与死亡隔开的那短短几个钟头。

他们在那里排演假审：有人模仿坦维尔（Tinville）；接着是革命广场（Place de la Révolution）的一场；还有刽子手桑松（Samson），用两把翻倒的椅子权充断头台。

公爵和王侯的女儿们，古老世家的后裔，在这些离奇而阴惨的喜剧里串演角色。那些贵妇把头发高高盘在头顶，跪在翻倒的椅子跟前，把雪白的脖颈探到那想象中的断头台底下。她们向假想的民众发表演说，而假扮的桑特尔便喝令假作的鼓声齐鸣，好淹没那假想中的受难者最后倾吐的滔滔陈词。

===Original===
Oh! the horror of it all—the pity, pathos, and misery of this ghastly parody, in the very face of the sublimity of death!

Déroulède shuddered when first he beheld the scene, shuddered at the very thought of finding Juliette amongst these careless, laughing, thoughtless mimes.

His own, his beautiful Juliette, with her proud face and majestic, queenlike gestures; it was a relief not to see her there.

"Juliette Marny? *Inconnue*," was the final word he heard about her.

===Chinese===
哦！这一切是何等恐怖——这场阴森滑稽戏里的怜悯、哀婉与悲苦，恰恰是在死亡的庄严崇高面前演出来的！

德鲁莱德初见这幅景象时不寒而栗；一想到说不定会在这些无忧无虑、嬉笑打闹、全无心机的即兴演员中间遇见朱丽叶特，他也不寒而栗。

那是他的朱丽叶特，他美丽的朱丽叶特，有她那骄傲的面容、那雍容华贵、女王一般的仪态；没有在那里见到她，倒是一桩宽慰。

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼？*Inconnue*（查无此人），」这是他听到的关于她的最后一句话。

===Original===
No one told him that by Deputy Merlin's strictest orders she had been labelled "dangerous," and placed in a remote wing of the Luxembourg Palace, together with a few, who, like herself, were allowed to see no one, communicate with no one.

Then when the *couvre-feu* had sounded, when all public places were closed, when the night watchman had begun his rounds, Déroulède knew that his quest for that night must remain fruitless.

But he could not rest. In and out the tortuous streets of Paris he roamed during the better part of that night. He was now only awaiting the dawn to publicly demand the right to stand beside Juliette.

A hopeless misery was in his heart, a longing for a cessation of life; only one thing kept his brain active, his mind clear: the hope of saving Juliette.

===Chinese===
谁也没有告诉他：凭着梅尔兰（Merlin）议员最严厉的命令，她已被贴上「危险分子」的标签，安置到卢森堡宫一处偏僻的侧翼，同寥寥几个人关在一起——那些人像她一样，不许见任何人，不许同任何人通消息。

随后，*couvre-feu*（宵禁）号响过了，各处公共场所都上了门，巡夜人开始了一处处巡查，德鲁莱德知道，这一夜的寻找注定要一无所获了。

可他没法安歇。那一夜的大半时光，他都在巴黎曲折回环的街巷里游走，穿进穿出。此刻他只是在等天亮——等到天明，他就要公开要求站到朱丽叶特身旁的权利。

他心里怀着一种无望的悲苦，一种盼着生命就此止息的渴念；只有一件事还在叫他的头脑保持活跃、他的心智保持清明：救出朱丽叶特的那点希望。

===Original===
The dawn was breaking in the far east when, wandering along the banks of the river, he suddenly felt a touch on his arm.

"Come to my hovel," said a pleasant, lazy voice close to his ear, whilst a kindly hand seemed to drag him away from the contemplation of the dark, silent river. "And a demmed, beastly place it is too, but at least we can talk quietly there."

Déroulède, roused from his meditation, looked up, to see his friend, Sir Percy Blakeney, standing close beside him. Tall, débonnair, well-dressed, he seemed by his very presence to dissipate the morbid atmosphere which was beginning to weigh upon Déroulède's active mind.

===Chinese===
遥远的东方天色破晓，他正沿着河岸游荡，忽然觉得手臂上被人轻轻一碰。

「到我那破窝里去吧，」一个悦耳而慵懒的声音在他耳边说道；与此同时，一只好意的手，似乎要把他从对那黑暗无声的河水的出神凝望中拽开。「那是个该死的、糟透了的地方，可至少我们能安安静静地谈谈。」

德鲁莱德从沉思中惊醒，抬起头来，看见他的朋友珀西·布莱克尼爵士就站在他身旁。高高的个子，倜傥的风度，讲究的衣着，仿佛单凭他本人在场，就把那开始压上德鲁莱德活跃心灵的病态阴郁，驱散了几分。

===Original===
Déroulède followed him readily enough through the intricate mazes of old Paris, and down the Rue des Arts, until Sir Percy stopped outside a small hostelry, the door of which stood wide open.

"Mine host has nothing to lose from footpads and thieves," explained the Englishman as he guided his friend through the narrow doorway, then up a flight of rickety stairs, to a small room on the floor above. "He leaves all doors open for anyone to walk in, but, la! the interior of the house looks so uninviting that no one is tempted to enter."

"I wonder you care to stay here," remarked Déroulède, with a momentary smile, as he contrasted in his mind the fastidious appearance of his friend with the dinginess and dirt of these surroundings.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德爽爽快快地跟着他，穿过老巴黎迷宫般盘错的街巷，又沿艺术街（Rue des Arts）走下去，直到珀西爵士在一座小客栈外停住脚——那客栈的门正大敞着。

「店主人压根儿没有什么怕拦路强盗和小偷来图谋的东西，」这位英国人一面解释，一面引着朋友穿过窄窄的门道，又登上一段吱呀欲坠的楼梯，来到楼上一间小屋。「他把门户全敞着，随什么人走进来；可是，哎呀！这宅子里面看着那么不讨人喜欢，谁也没有心思进去。」

「我倒奇怪你怎么肯待在这里，」德鲁莱德说道，浮起一瞬即逝的微笑；他心里正把朋友那副挑剔讲究的模样，同四周这晦暗龌龊的环境两相对照。

===Original===
Sir Percy deposited his large person in the capacious depths of a creaky chair, stretched his long limbs out before him, and said quietly:

"I am only staying in this demmed hole until the moment when I can drag you out of this murderous city."

Déroulède shook his head.

"You'd best go back to England, then," he said, "for I'll never leave Paris now."

===Chinese===
珀西爵士把他那魁伟的身躯安放进一把吱呀作响的宽大扶手椅的深处，把他的长腿伸到身前，平静地说：

「我待在这个该死的鬼窝里，只是为了等到那个时刻——等我能够把你拖出这座杀人城市的时刻。」

德鲁莱德摇了摇头。

「那你最好回英格兰去，」他说，「因为我如今决不会离开巴黎了。」

===Original===
"Not without Juliette Marny, shall we say?" rejoined Sir Percy placidly.

"And I fear me that she has placed herself beyond our reach," said Déroulède sombrely.

"You know that she is in the Luxembourg Prison?" queried the Englishman suddenly.

"I guessed it, but could find no proof."

"And that she will be tried tomorrow?"

"They never keep a prisoner pining too long," replied Déroulède bitterly. "I guessed that too."

===Chinese===
「不带朱丽叶特·马尔尼，是不肯走的，可以这么说吧？」珀西爵士安然自若地应道。

「可我怕她已经把自己放到了我们够不着的地方，」德鲁莱德阴沉地说。

「你可知道，她是在卢森堡监狱？」英国人突然问道。

「我猜是这样，可我找不出凭据。」

「也知道她明天就要受审？」

「他们从不让囚犯在牢里憔悴得太久，」德鲁莱德辛酸地答道。「这个我也猜到了。」

===Original===
"What do you mean to do?"

"Defend her with the last breath in my body."

"You love her still, then?" asked Blakeney, with a smile.

"Still?" The look, the accent, the agony of a hopeless passion conveyed in that one word, told Sir Percy Blakeney all that he wished to know.

"Yet she betrayed you," he said tentatively.

"And to atone for that sin—an oath, mind you, friend, sworn to her father—she is already to give her life for me."

===Chinese===
「那你打算怎么办？」

「用我最后一口气为她辩护。」

「这么说，你到现在还爱着她？」布莱克尼带着微笑问道。

「还？」那目光，那声调，那一个字里所含的绝望痴情的剧痛，把布莱克尼爵士想知道的一切，都告诉了他。

「可她背叛了你，」他试探着说。

「而为了赎偿那桩罪——记住，朋友，那是对她父亲立下的誓言——她如今就要拿性命来抵付我了。」

===Original===
"And you are prepared to forgive?"

"To understand *is* to forgive," rejoined Déroulède simply, "and I love her."

"Your madonna!" said Blakeney, with a gently ironical smile.

"No; the woman I love, with all her weaknesses, all her sins; the woman to gain whom I would give my soul, to save whom I will give my life."

"And she?"

"She does not love me—would she have betrayed me else?"

===Chinese===
「而你准备宽恕了？」

「理解，*便是*宽恕，」德鲁莱德朴实地答道，「我爱她。」

「你的圣母！」布莱克尼说，带着一丝温和的讥诮的笑意。

「不；是我所爱的那个女人，带着她一切的弱点、一切的罪；那个女人，为要赢得她，我甘愿交出灵魂，为要救她，我甘愿献出生命。」

「那她呢？」

「她不爱我——不然她岂会背叛我？」

===Original===
He sat beside the table, and buried his head in his hands. Not even his dearest friend should see how much he had suffered, how deeply his love had been wounded.

Sir Percy said nothing, a curious, pleasant smile lurked round the corners of his mobile mouth. Through his mind there flitted the vision of beautiful Marguerite, who had so much loved yet so deeply wronged him, and, looking at his friend, he thought that Déroulède too would soon learn all the contradictions, which wage a constant war in the innermost recesses of a feminine heart.

He made a movement as if he would say something more, something of grave import, then seemed to think better of it, and shrugged his broad shoulders, as if to say:

"Let time and chance take their course now."

===Chinese===
他在桌旁坐下，把头埋进双手之中。就连他最亲的朋友，也不该看见他经受过多少苦，看见他的爱被伤得多么深。

珀西爵士什么也没说，一丝奇特的、悦人的笑意，潜伏在他那张灵活多变的嘴角。美丽的玛格丽特（Marguerite）的身影掠过他的心头——她曾那样深爱着他，却又那样深深地亏负了他；他望着自己的朋友，心想德鲁莱德不久也会明白那一切的矛盾——在一个女人内心的最幽深处，它们进行着永不停歇的交战。

他动了一下，仿佛还想再说什么，说一句分量极重的话；随即又像改了主意，耸了耸那宽阔的肩膀，仿佛在说：

「如今，且让时间与机缘各自行事吧。」

===Original===
When Déroulède looked up again Sir Percy was sitting placidly in the armchair, with an absolutely blank expression on his face.

"Now that you know how much I love her, my friend," said Déroulède as soon as he had mastered his emotions, "will you look after her when they have condemned me, and save her for my sake?"

A curious, enigmatic smile suddenly illumined Sir Percy's earnest countenance.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德再抬起头来时，珀西爵士已经安安稳稳地坐在扶手椅里，脸上的表情是一片全然的空白。

「如今你知道我多么爱她了，我的朋友，」德鲁莱德一俟镇定住自己的心绪，便说道，「等他们给我定了罪，你可肯照看她，看在我的分上救下她？」

一丝奇特的、莫测高深的微笑，突然照亮了珀西爵士那张诚挚的脸。

===Original===
"Save her? Do you attribute supernatural powers to me, then, or to The League of The Scarlet Pimpernel?"

"To you, I think," rejoined Déroulède seriously.

Once more it seemed as if Sir Percy were about to reveal something of great importance to his friend, then once more he checked himself. The Scarlet Pimpernel was, above all, farseeing and practical, a man of action and not of impulse. The glowing eyes of his friend, his nervous, febrile movements, did not suggest that he was in a fit state to be entrusted with plans, the success of which hung on a mere thread.

Therefore Sir Percy only smiled, and said quietly:

"Well, I'll do my best."

===Chinese===
「救下她？那么，你是把超自然的神通派给了我呢，还是派给了红花侠同盟（The League of The Scarlet Pimpernel）？」

「我想，是派给了你，」德鲁莱德郑重地应道。

珀西爵士又一次仿佛就要向朋友披露一件极关紧要的事，又一次克制住了自己。红花侠首先是个深谋远虑、脚踏实地的人，是行动的人，不是凭一时冲动行事的人。他那朋友灼灼发亮的眼睛，他神经质的、发热般躁动的举止，都不像是一个可以把机密计划托付给他的人——那计划的成败，只悬在一根细丝上。

于是珀西爵士只是笑了笑，平静地说：

「好吧，我尽力而为。」

## XXIII Justice / XXIII 正义

===Original===
The day had been an unusually busy one.

Five and thirty prisoners, arraigned before the bar of the Committee of Public Safety, had been tried in the last eight hours—an average of rather more than four to the hour; twelve minutes and a half in which to send a human creature, full of life and health, to solve the great enigma which lies hidden beyond the waters of the Styx.

And Citizen-Deputy Foucquier-Tinville, the Public Prosecutor, had surpassed himself. He seemed indefatigable.

===Chinese===
这一天，忙得异乎寻常。

三十五名囚犯被押到救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）的审判栏前，在刚刚过去的八个钟头里审讯完毕——平均每点钟要审四个有余；也就是说，用十二分半钟，便把一个满蓄着生命与健康的人打发走，去解开那个隐藏在斯提克斯（Styx）冥河水波之外的大谜。

而公会议员（Citizen-Deputy）富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville）——共和国的公诉人（Public Prosecutor）——更是打破了他自己的纪录。他似乎永不知疲倦。

===Original===
Each of these five and thirty prisoners had been arraigned for treason against the Republic, for conspiracy with her enemies, and all had to have irrefutable proofs of their guilt brought before the Committee of Public Safety. Sometimes a few letters, written to friends abroad, and seized at the frontier; a word of condemnation of the measures of the extremists; and expression of horror at the massacres on the Place de la Révolution, where the guillotine creaked incessantly—these were irrefutable proofs; or else perhaps a couple of pistols, or an old family sword seized in the house of a peaceful citizen, would be brought against a prisoner, as an irrefutable proof of his warlike dispositions against the Republic.

Oh! it was not difficult!

Out of five and thirty indictments, Foucquier-Tinville had obtained thirty convictions.

No wonder his friends declared that he had surpassed himself. It had indeed been a glorious day, and the glow of satisfaction as much as the heat, caused the Public Prosecutor to mop his high, bony cranium before he had adjourned for the much-needed respite for refreshment.

===Chinese===
这三十五名囚犯，个个都以背叛共和国、阴谋勾结她的敌人之罪受到提审，而人人非得有不容辩驳的罪证呈到救国委员会面前不可。有时是几封写给国外朋友的信，在边境上被截了下来的；一句谴责极端派措施的话；一声对革命广场（Place de la Révolution）上大屠杀的惊呼——断头台在那广场上吱嘎作响，从无停歇——这些便是不容辩驳的罪证；再不然，或许是一对手枪，或是从一户安分守己的公民家里搜出的一柄祖传旧剑，被拿来指证囚犯，作为他心怀武力反抗共和国之图谋的不容辩驳的罪证。

哦！这有什么难的！

三十五份起诉状里，富基耶-坦维尔拿到了三十份定罪。

难怪他的朋友们宣称，他打破了自己的纪录。这实在是光辉的一天；那心满意足的灼热，同暑气一道，逼得这位公诉人在退庭去享受那急需的小憩与茶点之前，先揩了揩他那高耸的、瘦骨嶙峋的脑壳。

===Original===
The day's work was not yet done.

The "politicals" had been disposed of, and there had been such an accumulation of them recently that it was difficult to keep pace with the arrests.

And in the meanwhile the criminal record of the great city had not diminished. Because men butchered one another in the name of Equality, there were none the fewer among the Fraternity of thieves and petty pilferers, of ordinary cutthroats and public wantons.

And these too had to be dealt with by law. The guillotine was impartial, and fell with equal velocity on the neck of the proud duke and the gutter-born fille de joie, on a descendant of the Bourbons and the wastrel born in a brothel.

===Chinese===
这一天的公务，还没有办完。

那批「政治犯」已经处置完毕；近来这类人积压得如此之多，审讯的脚步简直追不上逮捕的速度。

与此同时，这座大城的罪案簿也并未变薄。人们纵然以平等之名自相屠戮，窃贼与鼠窃狗偷之辈的行帮，寻常的凶手与公然卖笑的娼妇，人数却并不因此减少分毫。

而这些人，也同样得由法律来处置。断头台是不偏不倚的：它以同样的速度落下，砍在骄傲的公爵的颈上，也砍在阴沟里出身的*fille de joie*（烟花女子）的颈上；砍在波旁王室后裔的颈上，也砍在妓院里生下的浪荡儿的颈上。

===Original===
The ministerial decrees favoured the proletariat. A crime against the Republic was indefensible, but one against the individual was dealt with, with all the paraphernalia of an elaborate administration of justice. There were citizen judges and citizen advocates, and the rabble, who crowded in to listen to the trials, acted as honorary jury.

It was all thoroughly well done. The citizen criminals were given every chance.

The afternoon of this hot August day, one of the last of glorious Fructidor, had begun to wane, and the shades of evening to slowly creep into the long, bare room where this travesty of justice was being administered.

===Chinese===
各部的法令偏袒无产者。反对共和国的罪行，无可辩护；而反对个人的罪行，却要摆出精细司法的全部排场来审理。有公民法官，有公民辩护人，还有那蜂拥而来旁听审讯的暴民，权充名誉陪审团。

一切都办得周详到家。公民罪犯们得到了种种机会。

这炎热的八月天——光辉的果月（Fructidor）行将告尽的几日之一——午后已渐渐消残，暮色开始缓缓爬进那间狭长而空荡的屋子；就在那里，这场正义的滑稽摹演，正由法律一板一眼地主持着。

===Original===
The Citizen-President sat at the extreme end of the room, on a rough wooden bench, with a desk in front of him littered with papers.

Just above him, on the bare, whitewashed wall, the words: "*La République: une et indivisible*," and below them the device: "*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité!*"

To the right and left of the Citizen-President, four clerks were busy making entries in that ponderous ledger, that amazing record of the foulest crimes the world has ever known, the *Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire*.

At present no one is speaking, and the grating of the clerks' quill pens against the paper is the only sound which disturbs the silence of the hall.

===Chinese===
公民主席（Citizen-President）坐在屋子最里端的一条粗木长凳上，面前一张桌子，摊满乱堆着的文件。

正在他头顶上方，光秃秃的白灰墙上写着一行字：「*La République: une et indivisible*（共和国：统一而不可分割）」；其下是那条信条：「*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité!*（自由、平等、博爱！）」

在公民主席的左右两侧，四名书记官正忙着往那部沉甸甸的册簿里做记录——那是一部惊人的档案，记着世间所知最污浊的罪行：《革命法庭公报》（*Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire*）。

此刻没有人说话；书记官们的鹅毛笔刮擦纸面的沙沙声，是搅扰大厅岑寂的唯一声响。

===Original===
In front of the President, on a bench lower than his, sits Citizen Foucquier-Tinville, rested and refreshed, ready to take up his occupation, for as many hours as his country demands it of him.

On every desk a tallow candle, smoking and spluttering, throws a weird light, and more weird shadows, on the faces of clerks and President, on blank walls and ominous devices.

In the centre of the room a platform surrounded by an iron railing is ready for the accused. Just in front of it, from the tall, raftered ceiling above, there hangs a small brass lamp, with a green abat-jour.

===Chinese===
主席前下方，一条比他那条更低些的凳子上，坐着富基耶-坦维尔公民。他歇过了，养足了神，随时准备重操旧业——只要国家向他要，多少个钟头他都奉陪。

每张桌上点着一支牛油烛，冒着烟，毕毕剥剥地爆着灯花，把一层怪诞的光，和更加怪诞的影子，投在书记官们和主席的脸上，投在空白的墙上和那些不祥的信条上。

屋子中央，一座四周围着铁栏杆的高台，已经备好，专候被告。高台正前方，从头顶上那高高的、椽子裸露的天花板下，悬着一盏小小的黄铜灯，配着绿色的*abat-jour*（灯罩）。

===Original===
Each side of the long, whitewashed walls there are three rows of benches, beautiful old carved oak pews, snatched from Notre Dame and from the Churches of St. Eustache and St. Germain l'Auxerrois. Instead of the pious worshippers of medieval times, they now accommodate the lookers-on of the grim spectacle of unfortunates, in their brief halt before the scaffold.

The front row of these benches is reserved for those citizen-deputies who desire to be present at the debates of the Tribunal Révolutionnaire. It is their privilege, almost their duty, as representatives of the people, to see that the sittings are properly conducted.

These benches are already well filled. At one end, on the left, Citizen Merlin, Minister of Justice, sits; next to him Citizen-Minister Lebrun; also Citizen Robespierre, still in the height of his ascendancy, and watching the proceedings with those pale, watery eyes of his and that curious, disdainful smile, which have earned for him the nickname of "the sea-green incorruptible."

===Chinese===
白灰长墙的每一侧，都排着三行长凳，那是一件件精美的雕花老橡木座椅，是从巴黎圣母院（Notre Dame）、圣厄斯塔什教堂（St. Eustache）和圣日耳曼奥塞尔教堂（St. Germain l'Auxerrois）里强行搬来的。中世纪那些虔诚的礼拜者已经不在了；如今坐在上面的，是一出阴惨戏码的看客——戏里的不幸者们，正在奔赴断头台之前，作最后一段短暂的停留。

这些长凳的前排，专为愿意列席革命法庭（Tribunal Révolutionnaire）辩论的公会议员们保留。作为人民的代表，出席旁听是他们的特权，也几乎是他们的职责——好监督开庭是否进行得规规矩矩。

这些长凳已经坐得满满当当。左端坐着司法部长梅尔兰（Merlin）公民；他旁边是部长勒布伦（Lebrun）公民；再是罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）公民，此刻正权势鼎盛，用他那一双苍白而水汪汪的眼睛，和那一丝古怪的、倨傲的微笑，注视着庭审的进行——正是这副神情，为他赢得了「海绿色的不可腐蚀者」的绰号。

===Original===
Other well-known faces are there also, dimly outlined in the fast-gathering gloom. But everyone notes Citizen-Deputy Déroulède, the idol of the people, as he sits on the extreme end of a bench on the right, with arms tightly folded across his chest, the light from the hanging lamp falling straight on his dark head and proud, straight brows, with the large, restless, eager eyes.

Anon the Citizen-President rings a hand-bell, and there is a discordant noise of hoarse laughter and loud curses, some pushing, jolting, and swearing, as the general public is admitted into the hall.

Heaven save us! What a rabble! Has humanity really such a scum?

===Chinese===
还有几张知名的面孔，也在迅速聚拢的昏暗里影影绰绰地显出来。但人人都注意到了公会议员德鲁莱德（Déroulède），人民的偶像：他坐在右边一条长凳的最末端，双臂紧紧交叠在胸前；吊灯的光直直照在他那深色的头发、他那两道骄傲而挺直的浓眉上，照着那双不安分的、灼灼渴望的大眼睛。

不多时，公民主席摇响了一只手铃；普通公众被放进大厅，顿时响起一片刺耳的喧哗——嘶哑的哄笑，响亮的咒骂，推推搡搡、跌跌撞撞，还夹着满口的亵渎。

上天救救我们！好一群乌合之众！人类当真出产这样一摊浮渣么？

===Original===
Women with a single ragged kirtle and shift, through the interstices of which the naked, grime-covered flesh shows shamelessly: with bare legs, and feet thrust into heavy sabots, hair dishevelled, and evil, spirit-sodden faces: women without a semblance of womanhood, with shrivelled, barren breasts, and dry, parched lips, that have never known how to kiss. Women without emotion save that of hate, without desire, save for the satisfaction of hunger and thirst, and lust for revenge against their sisters less wretched, less unsexed than themselves. They crowd in, jostling one another, swarming into the front rows of the benches, where they can get a better view of the miserable victims about to be pilloried before them.

And the men without a semblance of manhood. Bent under the heavy care of their own degradation, dead to pity, to love, to chivalry; dead to all save an inordinate longing for the sight of blood.

And God help them all! for there were the children too. Children—save the mark!—with pallid, precocious little faces, pinched with the ravages of starvation, gazing with dim, filmy eyes on this world of rapacity and hideousness. Children who have seen death!

===Chinese===
女人们，身上只挂着一件破烂的外裙和贴身衬衣，布片的缝隙间，污垢蒙身的赤裸皮肉不知羞耻地袒露着：光着两条腿，脚塞在笨重的木鞋里，头发蓬乱，一张张凶恶的、被烧酒泡烂了的脸：这些女人没有一点女人的样子，皱缩的、不生育的乳房，又干又焦的嘴唇，从来不懂得亲吻为何物。她们没有感情，除了恨；没有欲望，除了果腹解渴，和对复仇的渴望——要向那些姐妹们复仇，那些比自己少一分悲惨、还不曾像自己这样被夺尽女性天性的姐妹。她们蜂拥而入，互相推搡着，涌上长凳的前排，好把那班即将被拉到他们面前示众的可怜牺牲者，看得更加真切。

而那些男人，也没有一丝男人的模样。他们被自身堕落这副沉重的心事压弯了腰，对怜悯、对爱、对侠义之道，一概死了心；对一切都死了心，只剩下对亲眼目睹鲜血的一种贪得无厌的渴求。

上帝救救他们所有的人吧！因为还有那些孩子。孩子——恕我出此一言！——一张张苍白早熟的小脸，被饥饿的蹂躏碾得干瘪凹陷，用昏暗浑浊的眼睛，凝望着这个贪暴而狰狞的世界。这些孩子，是见过死亡的！

===Original===
Oh, the horror of it! Not beautiful, peaceful death, a slumber or a dream, a loved parent or fond sister or brother lying all in white amidst a wealth of flowers, but death in its most awesome aspect, violent, lurid, horrible.

And now they stare around them with eager, greedy eyes, awaiting the amusement of the spectacle; gazing at the President, with his tall Phrygian cap; at the clerks wielding their indefatigable quill pens, writing, writing, writing; at the flickering lights, throwing clouds of sooty smoke, up to the dark ceiling above.

Then suddenly the eyes of one little mite—a poor, tiny midget not yet in her teens—alight on Paul Déroulède's face, on the opposite side of the rooms.

===Chinese===
哦，这有多骇人！不是那种美丽的、安详的死亡——一场酣睡，或一个梦，亲爱的父母、慈爱的姐妹或兄弟，一身皆白，躺在繁花锦簇之中——而是死亡最狰狞的面目：暴烈的、血腥的、恐怖的。

而此刻，他们用急切而贪婪的眼睛四下打量，等着这出戏文给他们解闷；呆望着那位头戴高高弗里吉亚帽的主席；呆望着那班挥舞着不知疲倦的鹅毛笔的书记官，写呀，写呀，写个不停；呆望着那摇曳的烛光，把一团一团乌黑的油烟，送向上方昏暗的天花板。

猛可里，一个小不点儿的目光——一个可怜巴巴的、瘦小得还不到十三岁的小人儿——落到了屋子另一头保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）的脸上。

===Original===
"*Tiens!* Papa Déroulède!" she says, pointing an attenuated little finger across at him, and turning eagerly to those around her, her eyes dilating in wishful recollection of a happy afternoon spent in Papa Déroulède's house, with fine white bread to eat in plenty, and great jars of foaming milk.

He rouses himself from his apathy, and his great earnest eyes lose their look of agonised misery, as he responds to the greeting of the little one.

For one moment—oh! a mere fraction of a second—the squalid faces, the miserable, starved expressions of the crowd, soften at sight of him. There is a faint murmur among the women, which perhaps God's recording angel registered as a blessing. Who knows?

===Chinese===
「*Tiens*（瞧）！德鲁莱德爸爸！」她伸出一根瘦棱棱的小指头，朝他那边指着，又急切地转向身边的人；她的眼睛睁得老大，一心神往地回想起在德鲁莱德爸爸家里度过的那个幸福的下午——有上好的白面包，管够地吃，还有一大罐一大罐冒着泡沫的牛奶。

他从麻木中惊醒过来，回应那小东西的招呼；他那一双恳切的大眼睛，一时褪尽了痛楚悲苦的神色。

有一瞬间——哦！不过一秒的零头——人群里那些邋遢污秽的脸，那些悲惨的、饿瘪了的神情，一见着他便柔和下来。女人们中间起了一阵低低的絮语，上帝那司掌记录的天使，或许会把它记作一声祝福。谁知道呢？

===Original===
Foucquier-Tinville suppresses a sneer, and the Citizen-President impatiently rings his hand-bell again.

"Bring forth the accused!" he commands in stentorian tones.

There is a movement of satisfaction among the crowd, and the angel of God is forced to hide his face again.

===Chinese===
富基耶-坦维尔压下一声冷笑，公民主席不耐烦地又把他的手铃摇响。

「把被告带上来！」他用洪钟般的嗓音喝令。

人群里起了一阵快意的骚动，上帝的天使，只得再一次掩起他的脸。

## XXIV The Trial of Juliette / XXIV 朱丽叶特受审

===Original===

It is all indelibly placed on record in the *Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire*, under date 25th Fructidor, year I of the Revolution.

Anyone who cares may read, for the Bulletin is in the Archives of the Bibliothèque Nationale of Paris.

One by one the accused had been brought forth, escorted by two men of the National Guard in ragged, stained uniforms of red, white, and blue; they were then conducted to the small raised platform in the centre of the hall, and made to listen to the charge brought against them by Citizen Foucquier-Tinville, the Public Presecutor.

===Chinese===

这一切都凿凿有据，永志于《革命法庭公报》（*Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire*）之中，日期为革命元年果月二十五日。

凡有兴致者皆可一读，因为《公报》就存放在巴黎的法国国家图书馆（Bibliothèque Nationale）的档案里。

被告们被一个个提上堂来，由两名国民自卫军士兵押解着，他们身上那红、白、蓝三色的军服破烂不堪、污渍斑斑；随后被告们被引到大厅中央那座小小的平台上，听候公诉人（Public Prosecutor）富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville）公民向他们宣读的指控。

===Original===

They were petty charges mostly: pilfering, fraud, theft, occasionally arson or manslaughter. One man, however, was arraigned for murder with highway robbery, and a woman for the most ignoble traffic, which evil feminine ingenuity could invent.

These two were condemned to the guillotine, the others sent to the galleys at Brest or Toulon﻿—the forger along with the petty thief, the housebreaker with the absconding clerk.
