There was no room in the prison for ordinary offences against the criminal code; they were overfilled already with so-called traitors against the Republic.

Three women were sent to the penitentiary at the Salpêtriere, and were dragged out of the court shrilly protesting their innocence, and followed by obscene jeers from the spectators on the benches.

===Chinese===

大多是些小罪名：小偷小摸、欺诈、盗窃，偶尔也有纵火或过失杀人。然而有一个男人被控犯了杀人兼拦路抢劫之罪，还有一个女人被控操持邪恶的女性机巧所能想出的最卑贱的营生。

这两人被判上断头台，其余的发往布雷斯特或土伦去服划桨苦役——伪造犯与顺手牵羊的小偷同列，破门入户者与卷款潜逃的伙计同行。

监狱里已经腾不出地方来关押违反刑律的普通罪犯了；那里早已被所谓的背叛共和国的卖国贼塞得满满当当。

三名妇人被发往萨尔佩特里埃（Salpêtriere）监狱收押，被拖出法庭时还尖声申辩着自己的清白，旁听席上的观众则报以一片下流的哄笑。

===Original===

Then there was a momentary hush.

Juliette Marny had been brought in.

She was quite calm, and exquisitely beautiful, dressed in a plain grey bodice and kirtle, with a black band round her slim waist and a soft white kerchief folded across her bosom. Beneath the tiny, white cap her golden hair appeared in dainty, curly profusion; her childlike, oval face was very white, but otherwise quite serene.

She seemed absolutely unconscious of her surroundings, and walked with a firm step up to the platform, looking neither to the right nor to the left of her.

===Chinese===

随后是一阵短暂的屏息静默。

朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）被带进来了。

她十分平静，美得清雅绝伦，穿一件素灰色的紧身衣和衬裙，纤细的腰间束着一道黑带，胸前叠着一方柔软的白色头巾。小小的白色便帽底下，露出一头蓬松卷曲、精巧动人的金发；她那孩子气的鹅蛋脸十分苍白，除此之外却是一片安详。

她似乎全然没有觉察到自己身处的环境，迈着坚定的步子走上平台，目光既不朝右也不朝左。

===Original===

Therefore she did not see Déroulède. A great, a wonderful radiance seemed to shine in her large eyes﻿—the radiance of self-sacrifice.

She was offering not only her life, but everything a woman of refinement holds most dear, for the safety of the man she loved.

A feeling that was almost physical pain, so intense was it, overcame Déroulède, when at last he heard her name loudly called by the Public Prosecutor.

All day he had waited for this awful moment, forgetting his own misery, his own agonised feeling of an irretrievable loss, in the horrible thought of what *she* would endure, what *she* would think, when first she realised the terrible indignity, which was to be put upon her.

===Chinese===

因此她没有看见德鲁莱德（Déroulède）。一双大眼睛里仿佛闪耀着一片宏大而奇妙的光辉——那是自我牺牲的光辉。

她所奉献的不仅是自己的生命，还有教养有素的女人视若至宝的一切，为的是她所爱的那个男人的安全。

当德鲁莱德终于听到公诉人高声唤出她的名字时，一种几乎等同于肉体疼痛的感觉把他淹没了——那感觉强烈至此。

他等这可怕的时刻已经等了一整天，在一念可怕的思量中忘却了自身的苦楚、自己那无可挽回的失落带来的锥心之痛——那思量便是：*她*将承受什么，*她*会怎么想，当*她*头一回意识到那即将加在她身上的可怖屈辱的时候。

===Original===

Yet for the sake of her, of her chances of safety and of ultimate freedom, it was undoubtedly best that it should be so.

Arraigned for conspiracy against the Republic, she was liable to secret trial, to be brought up, condemned, and executed before he could even hear of her whereabouts, before he could throw himself before her judges and take all guilt upon himself.

Those suspected of treason against the Republic forfeited, according to Merlin’s most iniquitous Law, their rights of citizenship, in publicity of trial and in defence.

It all might have been finished before Déroulède knew anything of it.

The other way was, of course, more terrible. Brought forth amongst the scum of criminal Paris, on a charge, the horror of which, he could but dimly hope that she was too innocent to fully understand, he dared not even think of what she would suffer.

===Chinese===

然而为了她，为了她保全性命、终获自由的机会，事情无疑最好还是如此。

若以阴谋反对共和国的罪名受审，她便可能被秘密审判——还不及他打听到她的下落，还不及他扑到她的法官面前把一切罪名揽到自己身上，她就会被提堂、定罪、处决。

依照梅尔兰（Merlin）那部最不公不义的法令，凡被怀疑背叛共和国者，即丧失其公民权利——既丧失公开受审之权，亦丧失自行辩护之权。

这一切，可能早在德鲁莱德略有所闻之前，便已完结了。

另一条路当然更为可怕。被拖到罪恶巴黎的人渣中间，站到一桩罪名之下——他只能隐隐指望，她那样天真无邪，未必全然懂得这罪名的可怖——至于她将承受什么，他连想都不敢想。

===Original===

But undoubtedly it was better so.

The mud thrown at her robes of purity could never cling to her, and at least her trial would be public; he would be there to take all infamy, all disgrace, all opprobrium on himself.

The strength of his appeal would turn her judges’ wrath from her to him; and after these few moments of misery, she would be free to leave Paris, France, to be happy, and to forget him and the memory of him.

An overwhelming, all-compelling love filled his entire soul for the beautiful girl, who had so wronged, yet so nobly tried to save him. A longing for her made his very sinews ache; she was no longer madonna, and her beauty thrilled him, with the passionate, almost sensuous desire to give his life for her.

The indictment against Juliette Marny has become history now.

===Chinese===

但无疑，这样要好些。

掷向她那纯洁衣袍的污泥，永远沾不上她分毫；而至少她的审判将是公开的：他会在场，把一切恶名、一切耻辱、一切唾骂都揽到自己身上。

他申诉的力量会把她的法官们的怒火从她引向他自己；熬过这几刻的苦难之后，她便可自由地离开巴黎、离开法兰西，去享受幸福，去忘掉他，忘掉与他有关的一切记忆。

一种无可抗拒、令人不能自己的爱，充满了他的整个灵魂，为了这个美丽的姑娘——她曾那样深地冤屈了他，却又那样高尚地舍身救他。对她的思慕令他筋骨作痛；她不再是圣母了，她的美令他心旌摇荡，激起一种炽热的、近乎感官的渴望，要为她献出自己的生命。

对朱丽叶特·马尔尼的起诉，如今已成历史。

===Original===

On that day, the 25th Fructidor, at seven o’clock in the evening, it was read out by the Public Prosecutor, and listened to by the accused﻿—so the Bulletin tells us﻿—with complete calm and apparent indifference. She stood up in that same pillory where once stood poor, guilty Charlotte Corday, where presently would stand proud, guiltless Marie Antoinette.

And Déroulède listened to the scurrilous document, with all the outward calm his strength of will could command. He would have liked to rise from his seat then and there, at once, and in mad, purely animal fury have, with a blow of his fist, quashed the words in Foucquier-Tinville’s lying throat.

But for her sake he was bound to listen, and, above all, to act quietly, deliberately, according to form and procedure, so as in no way to imperil her cause.

===Chinese===

那一天，果月二十五日，晚上七时，公诉人宣读了起诉书，而被告——据《公报》所载——以全然的平静与表面的漠然听受了它。她站上那同一座示众的耻辱台：可怜而有罪的夏洛特·科黛（Charlotte Corday）曾在那里站过；而不久之后，高傲而无罪的玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）也将站上去。

德鲁莱德听人宣读这份恶毒下流的文书，竭尽其意志之力，维持着外表的平静。他真想当即、立刻从座位上站起身来，以疯狂而纯乎兽性的暴怒，一拳把那些谎话砸灭在富基耶-坦维尔那撒谎的喉咙里。

可是为了她，他必须听下去，而且尤其必须行事平静、从容，循规蹈矩、照章办理，好丝毫不危及她的案子。

===Original===

Therefore he listened whilst the Public Prosecutor spoke.

“Juliette Marny, you are hereby accused of having, by a false and malicious denunciation, slandered the person of a representative of the people; you caused the Revolutionary Tribunal, through this same mischievous act, to bring a charge against this representative of the people, to institute a domiciliary search in his house, and to waste valuable time, which otherwise belonged to the service of the Republic. And this you did, not from a misguided sense of duty towards your country, but in wanton and impure spirit, to be rid of the surveillance of one who had your welfare at heart, and who tried to prevent your leading the immoral life which had become a public scandal, and which has now brought you before this court of justice, to answer to a charge of wantonness, impurity, defamation of character, and corruption of public morals. In proof of which I now place before the court your own admission, that more than one citizen of the Republic has been led by you into immoral relationship with yourself; and further, your own admission, that your accusation against Citizen-Deputy Déroulède was false and mischievous; and further, and finally, your immoral and obscene correspondence with some persons unknown, which you vainly tried to destroy. In consideration of which, and in the name of the people of France, whose spokesman I am, I demand that you be taken hence from this Hall of Justice to the Place de la Révolution, in full view of the citizens of Paris and its environs, and clad in a soiled white garment, emblem of the smirch upon your soul, that there you be publicly whipped by the hands of Citizen Samson, the public executioner; after which, that you be taken to the prison of the Salpêtriere, there to be further detained at the discretion of the Committee of Public Safety. And now, Juliette Marny, you have heard the indictment preferred against you, have you anything to say, why the sentence which I have demanded shall not be passed upon you?”

===Chinese===

于是，公诉人说话时，他听着。

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼，你现被指控：以虚假而恶毒的告发，诽谤了一位人民代表的人格；且正是经由这同一恶毒行径，你致使革命法庭对这位人民代表提起控告，对其宅邸施行入户搜查，并虚掷了本应归于共和国公务的宝贵光阴。你行此之事，并非出于对祖国那种被引入歧途的责任感，而是出于淫佚不洁的心性，为的是摆脱一个把你的福祉挂在心上、竭力阻止你过那种已成公共丑闻的不道德生活之人的监视；正是那种生活，如今把你带到这座法庭之前，来听候淫佚、污秽、诽谤人格与败坏公共道德等项罪名的追究。为证明上述各节，我今将你的自供呈于法庭：其一，你自认曾诱使共和国一个以上的公民与你本人发生不道德的关系；其二，你自认对德鲁莱德公会议员所作的告发，纯属虚假、纯属恶意；其三，亦即最后，你与若干身份不明之人往来的不道德而淫秽的书信——你曾妄图将其销毁，而未得逞。有鉴于此，并以法兰西人民——我即其代言人——之名，我要求将你从此地、从这座司法大厅（Hall of Justice）解往革命广场，置于巴黎及其近郊公民们的众目睽睽之下，令你身穿一件污秽的白衣——那是你灵魂污点的表征——由公共刽子手桑松（Samson）公民之手，将你当众鞭笞；鞭笞之后，将你解往萨尔佩特里埃监狱，由救国委员会（Committee of Public Safety）酌情决定继续羁押。现在，朱丽叶特·马尔尼，你已听取了对你的起诉，你可有什么话要说，说明为何不应将我所要求的判决施加于你？」

===Original===

Jeers, shouts, laughter, and curses greeted this speech of the Public Prosecutor.

===Chinese===

公诉人这篇演说，迎来的是奚落、叫喊、哄笑与咒骂。

===Original===

All that was most vile and most bestial in this miserable, misguided people struggling for Utopia and Liberty, seemed to come to the surface, whilst listening to the reading of this most infamous document.

The delight of seeing this beautiful, ethereal woman, almost unearthly in her proud aloofness, smirched with the vilest mud to which the vituperation of man can contrive to sink, was a veritable treat to the degraded wretches.

The women yelled hoarse approval; the children, not understanding, laughed in mirthless glee; the men, with loud curses, showed their appreciation of Foucquier-Tinville’s speech.

As for Déroulède, the mental agony he endured surpassed any torture which the devils, they say, reserve for the damned. His sinews cracked in his frantic efforts to control himself; he dug his fingernails into his flesh, trying by physical pain to drown the sufferings of his mind.

He thought that his reason was tottering, that he would go mad if he heard another word of this infamy. The hooting and yelling of that filthy mob sounded like the cries of lost souls, shrieking from hell. All his pity for them was gone, his love for humanity, his devotion to the suffering poor.

===Chinese===

这个为乌托邦与自由而挣扎的可悲而误入歧途的民族，其中最卑污、最兽性的东西，仿佛都在倾听这份最声名狼藉的文书被宣读之际，一齐泛上了表面。

眼看这位美丽的、超凡脱俗的女人——她那高傲的孤迥几乎不似人间之物——被泼上人类辱骂之辞所能沉到的最污秽的污泥，这种快意，对那班堕落的可怜虫来说，是一场名副其实的盛馔。

女人们哑着嗓子喊好；孩子们懵然不解，却兀自笑得没有一点欢意；男人们大声咒骂着，表示他们对富基耶-坦维尔这篇演说的赏识。

至于德鲁莱德，他所承受的精神酷刑，超出了人们传说中留给被打入地狱者的任何苦刑。他在疯狂克己的努力中，筋腱格格作响；他把指甲掐进肉里，想以肉体的疼痛来淹没心灵的苦楚。

他只觉自己的神智摇摇欲坠，只觉再听一个字这种丑恶，自己就要发疯。那群污糟暴民起哄的嘘声与号叫，听上去就像迷途的亡魂自地狱深处发出的尖啸。他对他们的一切怜悯都消失了，他对人类之爱、他对受苦穷人的赤忱，俱已烟消。

===Original===

A great, an immense hatred for this ghastly Revolution and the people it professed to free filled his whole being, together with a mad, hideous desire to see them suffer, starve, die a miserable, loathsome death. The passion of hate, that now overwhelmed his soul, was at least as ugly as theirs. He was, for one brief moment, now at one with them in their inordinate lust for revenge.

Only Juliette throughout all this remained calm, silent, impassive.

She had heard the indictment, heard the loathsome sentence, for her white cheeks had gradually become ashy pale, but never for a moment did she depart from her attitude of proud aloofness.

She never once turned her head towards the mob who insulted her. She waited in complete passiveness until the yelling and shouting had subsided, motionless save for her fingertips, which beat an impatient tattoo upon the railing in front of her.

The Bulletin says that she took out her handkerchief and wiped her face with it. *Elle s’essuya le front qui fut perlé de sueur.* The heat had become oppressive.

===Chinese===

对这场阴森可怖的革命、对它口口声声要解放的人民，一种巨大的、无边的恨意充满了他的全身，还夹杂着一种疯狂的、狰狞的欲望，要眼看他们受苦、挨饿、死得凄惨而可憎。此刻席卷他灵魂的这股恨的激情，至少同他们的恨一样丑恶。有短短的一瞬，他同他们化为一体，同样怀着那无节制的复仇贪欲。

自始至终，唯独朱丽叶特平静、沉默、不动声色。

起诉她听了，那可憎的判决她也听了——她那白皙的面颊已渐渐灰白如烬——但她那高傲孤迥的姿态，一刻也不曾改易。

她从不曾朝侮辱她的暴民回过一次头。她全然漠然地等着，直到那嚎叫与喧嚷平息下去；她纹丝不动，唯有指尖例外——那些指尖正不耐烦地在她面前的栏杆上敲出细碎的鼓点。

《公报》记道，她掏出手帕，拭了拭脸。*Elle s’essuya le front qui fut perlé de sueur.*（她拭去了额上沁出的汗珠。）暑热已变得逼人。

===Original===

The atmosphere was overcharged with the dank, penetrating odour of steaming, dirty clothes. The room, though vast, was close and suffocating, the tallow candles flickering in the humid, hot air threw the faces of the President and clerks into bold relief, with curious caricature effects of light and shade.

The petrol lamp above the head of the accused had flared up, and begun to smoke, causing the chimney to crack with a sharp report. This diversion effected a momentary silence among the crowd, and the Public Prosecutor was able to repeat his query:

“Juliette Marny, have you anything to say in reply to the charge brought against you, and why the sentence which I have demanded should not be passed against you?”

The sooty smoke from the lamp came down in small, black, greasy particles; Juliette with her slender fingertips flicked one of these quietly off her sleeve, then she replied:

===Chinese===

空气里塞满了蒸腾的脏衣服发散出的那股潮湿而钻骨刺鼻的气味。这座大厅虽然宏敞，却窒闷得令人窒息；牛脂蜡烛在湿热的空气里闪烁摇曳，把庭长和书记员们的面孔衬得格外突出，明暗之间显出古怪的漫画式效果。

被告头顶上方的煤油灯轰然窜起火苗，继而冒起烟来，灯罩啪地一声脆响，裂了开来。这场小插曲令人群静了片刻，公诉人于是得以重申他的问话：

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼，对于所提的指控，你有什么可答辩的？为什么不应把我所要求的判决施加于你？」

灯里的油烟化作油腻腻的黑色微粒簌簌落下；朱丽叶特用纤细的指尖，静静地把一粒从袖口上弹去，然后答道：

===Original===

“No; I have nothing to say.”

“Have you instructed an advocate to defend you, according to your rights of citizenship, which the Law allows?” added the Public Prosecutor solemnly.

Juliette would have replied at once; her mouth had already framed the No with which she meant to answer.

===Chinese===

「不；我没有什么要说的。」

「依照法律所准许的你的公民权利，你可曾延请辩护人来为你辩护？」公诉人庄严地补问道。

朱丽叶特本要立刻作答；她口中已凝成那个她准备作答的「不」字。

===Original===

But now at last had come Déroulède’s hour. For this he had been silent, had suffered and had held his peace, whilst twice twenty-four hours had dragged their weary lengths along, since the arrest of the woman he loved.

In a moment he was on his feet before them all, accustomed to speak, to dominate, to command.

“Citizeness Juliette Marny has entrusted me with her defence,” he said, even before the No had escaped Juliette’s white lips, “and I am here to refute the charges brought against her, and to demand in the name of the people of France full acquittal and justice for her.”

===Chinese===

然而此刻，德鲁莱德的时辰终于到了。为了这一刻，自他所爱的女人被捕以来，在两个昼夜疲惫而漫长地拖行的时光里，他一直沉默着、忍受着、缄口不言。

转眼之间，他已站起身来，面对着满堂众人——那个素来惯于发言、惯于统御、惯于号令的人。

「朱丽叶特·马尔尼女公民已把她的辩护托付于我，」不等那个「不」字从朱丽叶特苍白的唇间逸出，他便说道，「我此刻在此，就是要驳斥强加于她的罪名，并以法兰西人民之名，为她要求完全开释与公正。」

## XXV The Defence / XXV 辩护

===Original===
Intense excitement, which found vent in loud applause, greeted Déroulède's statement.

"*Ça ira! ça ira! vas-y Déroulède!*" came from the crowded benches round; and men, women, and children, wearied with the monotony of the past proceedings, settled themselves down for a quarter of an hour's keen enjoyment.

If Déroulède had anything to do with it, the trial was sure to end in excitement. And the people were always ready to listen to their special favourite.

The citizen-deputies, drowsy after the long, oppressive day, seemed to rouse themselves to renewed interest. Lebrun, like a big, shaggy dog, shook himself free from creeping somnolence. Robespierre smiled between his thin lips, and looked across at Merlin to see how the situation affected him. The enmity between the Minister of Justice and Citizen Déroulède was well known, and everyone noted, with added zest, that the former wore a keen look of anticipated triumph.

High up, on one of the topmost benches, sat Citizen Lenoir, the stage-manager of this palpitating drama. He looked down, with obvious satisfaction, at the scene which he himself had suggested last night to the members of the Jacobin Club. Merlin's sharp eyes had tried to pierce the gloom, which wrapped the crowd of spectators, searching vainly to distinguish the broad figure and massive head of the provincial giant.

===Chinese===
一阵强烈的激动，化作雷鸣般的掌声迸发出来，迎接着德鲁莱德的陈词。

拥挤的四旁旁听席上响起一片「*Ça ira! ça ira! vas-y Déroulède!*（会好的！会好的！上啊，德鲁莱德！）」；男男女女、老老少少，被前面那些单调的程序磨得疲惫不堪，此时都安顿好身子，预备痛痛快快地乐上一刻钟。

只要有德鲁莱德插手其事，这场审讯就保准会在一片激动中收场。而人民也一向乐于倾听他们这位格外受宠的人讲话。

公会议员们被这一整天冗长而沉闷的折磨弄得昏昏欲睡，此时似乎重新振作起精神，兴致又生。勒布伦（Lebrun）像一条毛蓬蓬的大狗，抖擞了一下，从悄悄袭来的困倦中挣脱出来。罗伯斯庇尔薄薄的嘴唇间浮起一丝笑意，隔着场子朝梅尔兰望过去，看这局面对他有何影响。司法部长与德鲁莱德公民之间的仇怨是尽人皆知的，于是人人格外津津有味地注意到：前者脸上带着一副急切盼望胜利的灼灼神色。

高踞在最高一层旁听席上的，是勒努瓦公民——这出扣人心弦的大戏的幕后导演。他俯瞰着场中的情景，分明心满意足：这正是他昨夜亲自向雅各宾俱乐部的成员们出的主意。梅尔兰那双锐利的眼睛曾竭力想穿透笼罩着旁听人群的昏暗，枉然地想从里面辨认出那外省巨汉宽宽的身形和硕大的头颅。

===Original===
The light from the petrol lamp shone full on Déroulède's earnest, dark countenance as he looked Juliette's infamous accuser full in the face, but the tallow candles, flickering weirdly on the President's desk, threw Tinville's short, spare figure and large, unkempt head into curious grotesque silhouette.

Juliette apparently had lost none of her calm, and there was no one there sufficiently interested in her personality to note the tinge of delicate colour which, at the first word of Déroulède, had slowly mounted to her pale cheeks.

Tinville waited until the wave of excitement had broken upon the shoals of expectancy.

Then he resumed:

"Then, Citizen Déroulède, what have *you* to say, why sentence should not be passed upon the accused?"

===Chinese===
汽油灯的光亮，满照着德鲁莱德那张恳切而黝黑的面孔——他正直直地盯着朱丽叶特那个恶名昭著的告发者；而庭长桌上几支怪异地闪烁摇曳的牛脂蜡烛，却把坦维尔那矮小干瘦的身形和蓬乱的大脑袋，映成一幅古怪狰狞的剪影。

朱丽叶特表面上丝毫不失她的镇定；满场之中，没有一个人对她的为人怀有足够的兴趣，去留意那一抹淡淡的红色——德鲁莱德话音方起，它便已缓缓地涌上她那苍白的双颊。

坦维尔一直等待着，直到那阵激动的浪潮，在期待的浅滩上撞碎平息。

随后他接着说：

「那么，德鲁莱德公民，*你*可有什么要说，为何不应当对被告宣判？」

===Original===
"I have to say that the accused is innocent of every charge brought against her in your indictment," replied Déroulède firmly.

"And how do you substantiate this statement, Citizen-Deputy?" queried Tinville, speaking with mock unctuousness.

"Very simply, Citizen Tinville. The correspondence to which you refer did not belong to the accused, but to me. It consisted of certain communications, which I desired to hold with Marie Antoinette, now a prisoner in the Conciergerie, during my state there as lieutenant-governor. The Citizeness Juliette Marny, by denouncing me, was serving the Republic, for my communications with Marie Antoinette had reference to my own hopes of seeing her quit this country and take refuge in her own native land."

Gradually, as Déroulède spoke, a murmur, like the distant roar of a monstrous breaker, rose among the crowd on the upper benches. As he continued quietly and firmly, so it grew in volume and in intensity, until his last words were drowned in one mighty, thunderous shout of horror and execration.

===Chinese===
「我要说的是：你公诉状里控告被告的每一条罪名，被告都一概无辜，」德鲁莱德斩钉截铁地答道。

「那公会议员又拿什么来证实这句话呢？」坦维尔问道，说话间带着一副假惺惺的甜腻腔调。

「再简单不过，坦维尔公民。你方才所指的那批信件并不属于被告，而属于我。那是我有意同玛丽·安托瓦内特——如今正被囚于巴黎古监狱——进行的若干通信；我在那里驻任副总督（lieutenant-governor）期间，便存着这个心思。朱丽叶特·马尔尼女公民告发了我，正是在为共和国效力，因为我同玛丽·安托瓦内特的通信，所关涉的无非是我自己的指望——盼着见她离开这个国家，遁回她自己的故土去。」

德鲁莱德往下说的时候，上层旁听席的人群里渐渐升起一阵嗡嗡的喧声，像远处一头巨浪的咆哮。他不动声色、字字坚定地继续说下去，那喧声便随之愈涨愈高、愈来愈烈，直到他最后几句话，被淹没在一片惊天动地的怒吼里——那是惊骇与咒骂一齐迸发出的雷鸣。

===Original===
Déroulède, the friend and idol of the people, the privileged darling of this unruly population, the father of the children, the friend of the women, the sympathiser in all troubles, Papa Déroulède as the little ones called him—he a traitor, self-accused, plotting and planning for an ex-tyrant, a harlot who had called herself a queen, for Marie Antoinette the Austrian, who had desired and worked for the overthrow of France! He, Déroulède, a traitor!

In one moment, as he spoke, the love which in their crude hearts they bore him, that animal primitive love, was turned to sudden, equally irresponsible hate. He had deceived them, laughed at them, tried to bribe them by feeding their little ones!

Bah! the bread of the traitor! It might have choked the children.

Surprise at first had taken their breath away. Already they had marvelled why he should stand up to defend a wanton. And now, probably feeling that he was on the point of being found out, he thought it better to make a clean breast of his own treason, trusting in his popularity, in his power over the people.

Bah!!!

Not one extenuating circumstance did they find in their hardened hearts for him.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德——人民的朋友和偶像，这群无法无天的暴民独得恩宠的心肝宝贝，孩子们的爹，妇道们的友人，一切苦难中的同情者，小家伙们唤作「德鲁莱德老爹」的那一位——他竟是个叛徒！自己招认的叛徒！替一个旧日的暴君——那个僭称王后的娼妇——暗中运筹策划，替奥地利女人玛丽·安托瓦内特——那个一心指望、又一直卖力图谋倾覆法兰西的女人——出谋定计！他，德鲁莱德，是个叛徒！

就在他说话的当儿，他们那粗粝的心里对他怀有的那份爱——那种兽性的、原初的爱——霎时转变成了突然的、同样不经思索的恨。他欺骗了他们，嘲弄了他们，还想用喂饱他们小家伙的法子来收买他们！

呸！叛徒的面包！不定早就把孩子们噎死了。

起初，那份惊愕叫他们连气都透不过来。他们本来已在暗暗纳罕：他怎么竟站出来替一个荡妇辩护。而如今，多半是眼看自己就要败露，他索性把自己的叛国行为和盘托出，还指望凭他的声望、凭他对民众的魔力来过关。

呸！！！

在他们那颗冷酷的铁石心肠里，找不出一桩可以替他开脱的情节。

===Original===
He had been their idol, enshrined in their squalid, degraded minds, and now he had fallen, shattered beyond recall, and they hated and loathed him as much as they had loved him before.

And this his enemies noted, and smiled with complete satisfaction.

Merlin heaved a sigh of relief. Tinville nodded his shaggy head, in token of intense delight.

What that provincial coal-heaver had foretold had indeed come to pass.

The populace, that most fickle of all fickle things in this world, had turned all at once against its favourite. This Lenoir had predicted, and the transition had been even more rapid than he had anticipated.

Déroulède had been given a length of rope, and, figuratively speaking, had already hanged himself.

===Chinese===
他曾是他们的偶像，供奉在他们那肮脏而堕落的魂灵里；如今他倒下了，摔得粉碎，再也扶不起来，于是他们便恨他、厌他，恰如先前爱他爱得那般深。

敌人们把这一切看在眼里，笑得心满意足。

梅尔兰如释重负地舒了一口气。坦维尔把毛发蓬乱的大脑袋点了点，表示说不出的痛快。

那个外省运煤工预言过的事，果真应验了。

民众——这世上一切反复无常之物中最反复无常的东西——霎时间便调转头来，对付起自己的宠儿来。这一点勒努瓦早有预言，而这一转变来得甚至比他预料的还要迅疾。

德鲁莱德被人递过去一截绳子；打个比方说，他已经把自己吊上了。

===Original===
The reality was a mere matter of a few hours now. At dawn tomorrow the guillotine; and the mob of Paris, who yesterday would have torn his detractors limb from limb, would on the morrow be dragging him, with hoots and yells and howls of execration, to the scaffold.

The most shadowy of all footholds, that of the whim of a populace, had already given way under him. His enemies knew it, and were exulting in their triumph. He knew it himself, and stood up, calmly defiant, ready for any event, if only he succeeded in snatching her beautiful head from the ready embrace of the guillotine.

Juliette herself had remained as if entranced. The colour had again fled from her cheeks, leaving them paler, more ashen than before. It seemed as if in this moment she suffered more than human creature could bear, more than any torture she had undergone hitherto.

He would not owe his life to her.

That was the one overwhelming thought in her, which annihilated all others. His love for her was dead, and he would not accept the great sacrifice at her hands.

Thus these two in the supreme moment of their life saw each other, yet did not understand. A word, a touch would have given them both the key to one another's heart, and it now seemed as if death would part them forever, whilst that great enigma remained unsolved.

===Chinese===
而实际执行，如今只剩下几个钟头的事了。明天拂晓便是断头台；昨天，凡是诋毁他的人，巴黎暴民恨不得将其撕成碎块；明天，他们就要拽着他——伴着呵斥、尖叫和咒骂的嗥声——把他拖上刑台。

一切立足之地中最虚幻无凭的一种——民众心血来潮的宠信——已在他脚下塌陷。敌人心里明白，正陶醉在胜利里。他自己也明白，却平静而桀骜地站着，作好了应付一切事变的准备，只要能把她的美人头，从断头台那随时张开的怀抱里夺回来。

朱丽叶特自己则一直怔怔地立着，仿佛魂魄出窍。血色又一次从她的双颊上逃走，留下比先前更灰败的苍白。仿佛在这一刻，她受的煎熬已超出凡人所能忍受的限度，比她迄今经受过的任何酷刑都更厉害。

他不肯把性命欠在她手里。

这一个念头在她心中压倒一切，碾灭了其余种种。他对她的爱已经死了，他不肯从她手里接受这场重大的牺牲。

于是这两人处在一生最紧要的关头，彼此望得见，却谁也不懂谁。一个字、一次触碰，本可以把开启两颗心的钥匙一并交到他们手里；而如今看来，死亡就要将他们永远分开，那个巨大的谜却依然无人解开。

===Original===
The Public Prosecutor had been waiting until the noise had somewhat subsided, and his voice could be heard above the din, then he said, with a smile of ill-concealed satisfaction:

"And is the court, then, to understand, Citizen-Deputy Déroulède, that it was you who tried to burn the treasonable correspondence and to destroy the case which contained it?"

"The treasonable correspondence was mine, and it was I who destroyed it."

"But the accused admitted before Citizen Merlin that she herself was trying to burn certain love letters, that would have brought to light her illicit relationship with another man than yourself," argued Tinville suavely. The rope was perhaps not quite long enough; Déroulède must have all that could be given him, ere this memorable sitting was adjourned.

===Chinese===
公诉人一直在等喧声稍稍平息，等自己的声音能盖过嘈杂被听清，然后他带着一副掩饰不住的得意笑容说：

「那么，本法庭是否可以这样理解，德鲁莱德公会议员：试图烧毁那批叛国信件、销毁装信信匣的，正是你自己？」

「叛国信件是我的，销毁它们的也是我。」

「可是被告在梅尔兰公民面前亲口招认，说她自己正要烧的是几封情书——那会揭出她同别人、而不是同你的私情，」坦维尔圆滑温和地争辩道。绳子或许还不够长；趁着这值得纪念的休会之前，凡能递给德鲁莱德的，都得让他拿足。

===Original===
Déroulède, however, instead of directing his reply straight to his enemy, now turned towards the dense crowd of spectators, on the benches opposite to him.

"Citizens, friends, brothers," he said warmly, "the accused is only a girl, young, innocent, knowing nothing of peril or of sin. You all have mothers, sisters, daughters—have you not watched those dear to you in the many moods of which a feminine heart is capable; have you not seen them affectionate, tender, and impulsive? Would you love them so dearly but for the fickleness of their moods? Have you not worshipped them in your hearts, for those sublime impulses which put all man's plans and calculations to shame? Look on the accused, citizens. She loves the Republic, the people of France, and feared that I, an unworthy representative of her sons, was hatching treason against our great mother. That was her first wayward impulse—to stop me before I committed the awful crime, to punish me, or perhaps only to warn me. Does a young girl calculate, citizens? She acts as her heart dictates; her reason but awakes from slumber later on, when the act is done. Then comes repentance sometimes: another impulse of tenderness which we all revere. Would you extract vinegar from rose leaves? Just as readily could you find reason in a young girl's head. Is that a crime? She wished to thwart me in my treason; then, seeing me in peril, the sincere friendship she had for me gained the upper hand once more. She loved my mother, who might be losing a son; she loved my crippled foster-sister; for *their* sakes, not for mine—a traitor's—did she yield to another, a heavenly impulse, that of saving me from the consequences of my own folly. Was *that* a crime, citizens? When you are ailing, do not your mothers, sisters, wives tend you? when you are seriously ill, would they not give their heart's blood to save you? and when, in the dark hours of your lives, some deed which you would not openly avow before the world overweights your soul with its burden of remorse, is it not again your womenkind who come to you, with tender words and soothing voices, trying to ease your aching conscience, bringing solace, comfort, and peace? And so it was with the accused, citizens. She had seen my crime, and longed to punish it; she saw those who had befriended her in sorrow, and she tried to ease their pain by taking *my* guilt upon her shoulders. She has suffered for the noble lie, which she had told on my behalf, as no woman has ever been made to suffer before. She has stood, white and innocent as your newborn children, in the pillory of infamy. She was ready to endure death, and what was ten thousand times worse than death, because of her own warmhearted affection. But you, citizens of France, who, above all, are noble, true, and chivalrous, you will not allow the sweet impulses of young and tender womanhood to be punished with the ban of felony. To you, women of France, I appeal in the name of your childhood, your girlhood, your motherhood; take her to your hearts, she is worthy of it, worthier now for having blushed before you, worthier than any heroine in the great roll of honour of France."

His magnetic voice went echoing along the rafters of the great, sordid Hall of Justice, filling it with a glory it had never known before. His enthusiasm thrilled his hearers, his appeal to their honour and chivalry roused all the finer feelings within them. Still hating him for his treason, his magical appeal had turned their hearts towards her.

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德却不把回答直冲着他的死对头，此刻他转过身来，面向对面旁听席上密密层层的人群。

「公民们，朋友们，兄弟们，」他满腔热忱地说，「被告不过是个姑娘，年纪轻轻，天真无邪，不识凶险，也不识罪孽。你们人人都有母亲、姐妹、女儿——你们难道不曾看着你们心爱的人，在一颗女性的心灵所能怀有的种种心绪中起伏多变；难道不曾见过她们一往情深、温存体贴、易于冲动？若非她们心绪这般善变无常，你们还会爱她们爱得这般深吗？你们心中难道不曾把她们崇拜如神——为了那些叫男子汉的种种盘算计谋全都相形见绌的崇高天性冲动？公民们，看看被告吧。她热爱共和国，热爱法兰西人民，唯恐我——法兰西儿郎中一个不肖的代表——正在暗中酝酿叛国，出卖我们伟大的母亲。这便是她头一个任性冲动的由来——要在我犯下可怕的罪行之前拦住我，惩罚我，或者也许只是警告我。公民们，一个年轻姑娘会盘算吗？她只照着心之所使去行事；她的理智要到日后、待事情做完了，才从酣睡中醒来。于是有时便来了悔悟：那是又一阵温柔的天性冲动，我们人人对它都怀着敬意。你能从玫瑰花瓣里榨出醋来吗？同样地，你也休想在一个年轻姑娘的脑袋里找到盘算。这算得上罪吗？她原想拦阻我的叛国；随后见我身陷险境，她对我的一片真挚友情便重新占了上风。她爱我的母亲——母亲或将失去儿子；她爱我那跛足的义妹；是为了*她们*，而不是为了我——一个叛徒——她才听凭另一种冲动，一种天国的冲动，把我从我自己愚蠢行径的后果里拯救出来。公民们，*这*难道是罪吗？你们小病缠身的时候，服侍你们的难道不是你们的母亲、姐妹、妻子？你们沉疴在身的时候，她们难道不肯掏出心头之血来救你们？而当你们一生中晦暗的时辰，某件不肯在大庭广众之前公开自认的行径，以悔恨的重负压得你们的灵魂透不过气，再度来到你们身边的，难道不又是你们的女性眷属？——她们说着温存的话、带着宽慰的声气，设法抚慰你们作痛的良知，带来慰藉、安稳与宁帖？被告的情形，恰恰如此，公民们。她看出了我的罪行，一心要惩罚它；她又看见了那些在她患难之中待她亲厚的人，便想将*我的*罪责揽上自己的肩头，好减轻他们的痛苦。为了那番替我而说的崇高的谎言，她所受的苦，是从前任何一个女人都不曾被迫受过的。她像你们新生的婴孩一般洁白无瑕，却站在耻辱的示众台上。出于她自己那副火热的心肠，她本已准备领受死亡——以及比死亡恶劣一万倍的东西。可是你们，法兰西的公民们，你们首先是高贵、真诚、有骑士风度的，你们决不会容许，年轻娇嫩的女性那甜蜜的天性冲动，落得个以重罪论处的下场。法兰西的女人们啊，我以你们的童年、你们的少女时代、你们的为母之职的名义，向你们呼吁：把她收进你们的心里吧，她配得上；如今她在你们面前红过脸，她便更配得上了；比之法兰西伟大光荣名册上的任何一位女英雄，她都更配得上。」

他那富有魔力的声音，沿着司法宫大殿龌龊的椽木回荡不绝，让这座大殿生出了它前所未有的光辉。他的热忱叫听众热血沸腾，他向他们的荣誉感与骑士精神发出的呼吁，把他们心底一切较高尚的情操全都唤醒了。他们仍旧恨他的叛国之罪；然而他那神奇的呼吁，已把他们的心转向了她。

===Original===
They had listened to him without interruption, and now at last, when he paused, it was very evident, by muttered exclamations and glances cast at Juliette, that popular feeling, which up to the present had practically ignored her, now went out towards her personality with overwhelming sympathy.

Obviously at the present moment, if Juliette's fate had been put to the plebiscite, she would have been unanimously acquitted.

Merlin, as Déroulède spoke, had once or twice tried to read his friend Foucquier-Tinville's enigmatical expression, but the Public Prosecutor, with his face in deep shadow, had not moved a muscle during the Citizen-Deputy's noble peroration. He sat at his desk, chin resting on hand, staring before him with an expression of indifference, almost of boredom.

Now, when Déroulède finished speaking, and the outburst of human enthusiasm had somewhat subsided, he rose slowly to his feet, and said quietly:

===Chinese===
他们自始至终听他讲完，没有一声打断；此刻他停了下来，从一片低低的惊叹和投向朱丽叶特的眼光里，可以清清楚楚地看出：大众的情感——此前几乎把她当作无物——如今正带着排山倒海一般的同情，涌向她这个人。

显然，此刻若把朱丽叶特的命运交由大众表决，她会获判一致无罪。

德鲁莱德讲话的时候，梅尔兰有一两次想揣摩他的朋友富基耶-坦维尔那副谜一般的神情；可是公诉人半张脸深深藏在暗影里，这位公会议员那篇崇高宏论的整段时间里，他脸上的肌肉纹丝未动。他坐在桌前，手托着下巴，直勾勾地望着前面，一脸漠然，几乎是不耐烦的样子。

此刻德鲁莱德言毕，那阵人性热忱的迸发已稍稍平息，他便缓缓站起身来，平平静静地说：

===Original===
"So you maintain, Citizen-Deputy, that the accused is a chaste and innocent girl, unjustly charged with immorality?"

"I do," protested Déroulède loudly.

"And will you tell the court why you are so ready to publicly accuse yourself of treason against the Republic, knowing full well all the consequences of your action?"

"Would any Frenchman care to save his own life at the expense of a woman's honour?" retorted Déroulède proudly.

A murmur of approval greeted these words, and Tinville remarked unctuously:

"Quite so, quite so. We esteem your chivalry, Citizen-Deputy. The same spirit, no doubt, actuates you to maintain that the accused knew nothing of the papers which you say you destroyed?"

===Chinese===
「那么你一口咬定，公会议员，被告是个贞洁无辜的姑娘，那桩伤风败俗的罪名加在她身上是冤枉的？」

「我一口咬定，」德鲁莱德高声抗辩。

「那你能不能告诉本法庭，你明知自己这一举动的全部后果，却这般情愿地当众自认叛国、与共和国为敌，是何道理？」

「可有一个法国人肯拿一个女人的名誉作代价，来保住自己的性命？」德鲁莱德昂然反诘。

这句话引得一阵低低的赞许，坦维尔甜腻腻地接道：

「说得对，说得对。我们敬重你的骑士风度，公会议员。想必还是这同一股精神，支撑着你一口咬定：你说你销毁的那些文件，被告毫不知情？」

===Original===
"She knew nothing of them. I destroyed them; I did not know that they had been found; on my return to my house I discovered that the Citizeness Juliette Marny had falsely accused herself of having destroyed some papers surreptitiously."

"She said they were love letters."

"It is false."

"You declare her to be pure and chaste?"

"Before the whole world."

"Yet you were in the habit of frequenting the bedroom of this pure and chaste girl, who dwelt under your roof," said Tinville with slow and deliberate sarcasm.

===Chinese===
「她毫不知情。信是我销毁的；我不知道它们已被搜出。我回到家里，才发觉朱丽叶特·马尔尼女公民已假意自认，说她偷偷销毁了几份文件。」

「她说那是情书。」

「此言不实。」

「你声称她纯洁贞静？」

「当着全世界，我都这么说。」

「可是你却惯常出入这个住在你屋檐底下的、纯洁贞静的姑娘的卧室，」坦维尔慢条斯理、字字带刺地说。

===Original===
"It is false."

"If it be false, Citizen Déroulède," continued the other with the same unctuous suavity, "then how comes it that the correspondence which you admit was treasonable, and therefore presumably secret—how comes it that it was found, still smouldering, in the chaste young woman's bedroom, and the torn letter-case concealed among her dresses in a valise?"

"It is false."

"The Minister of Justice, Citizen-Deputy Merlin, will answer for the truth of that."

"It is the truth," said Juliette quietly.

Her voice rang out clear, almost triumphant, in the midst of the breathless pause, caused by the previous swift questions and loud answers.

===Chinese===
「此言不实。」

「既然不实，德鲁莱德公民，」对方接着说，还是那副甜腻的圆滑，「那么你承认是叛国、因而想必是极端机密的那批信件——怎么会在那位贞静的年轻女人的卧室里被搜出，兀自阴燃未熄；那撕破的信匣，又怎么会藏在她一只旅行皮箱的衣裳底下？」

「此言不实。」

「这件事是真是假，司法部长梅尔兰公会议员可以作证。」

「这是真的，」朱丽叶特平平静静地说。

先前那一串疾如骤雨的问话和高声的回答，逼得全场屏住了气息；就在这片屏息的静默之中，她的声音清朗朗地响起来，几乎带着凯旋的意味。

===Original===
Déroulède now was silent.

This one simple fact he did not know. Anne Mie, in telling him the events in connection with the arrest of Juliette, had omitted to give him the one little detail, that the burnt letters were found in the young girl's bedroom.

Up to the moment when the Public Prosecutor confronted him with it, he had been under the impression that she had destroyed the papers and the letter-case in the study, where she had remained alone after Merlin and his men had left the room. She could easily have burnt them there, as a tiny spirit lamp was always kept alight on a side table for the use of smokers.

This little fact now altered the entire course of events. Tinville had but to frame an indignant ejaculation:

===Chinese===
德鲁莱德此刻哑口无言。

这么一个简单的实情，他并不知道。安娜·米向他讲述朱丽叶特被捕的经过时，漏掉了那个小小的细节：烧剩的信，是在那姑娘的卧室里搜出来的。

直到公诉人把这一点当面摆到他眼前之前，他一直以为她是在书房里销毁了那些文件和信匣的——梅尔兰一行人离开屋子之后，她独自留在那里。在那儿她尽可以轻而易举地把它们烧掉，因为靠边的小桌上总点着一盏小小的酒精灯，是供吸烟的人取火用的。

这个小小的事实，就此扭转了整个事态的进程。坦维尔只消发出一声义愤填膺的感叹：

===Original===
"Citizens of France, see how you are being befooled and hoodwinked!"

Then he turned once more to Déroulède.

"Citizen Déroulède—" he began.

But in the tumult that ensued he could no longer hear his own voice. The pent-up rage of the entire mob of Paris seemed to find vent for itself in the howls with which the crowd now tried to drown the rest of the proceedings.

As their brutish hearts had been suddenly melted on behalf of Juliette, in response to Déroulède's passionate appeal, so now they swiftly changed their sympathetic attitude to one of horror and execration.

===Chinese===
「法兰西的公民们，看看你们是怎样被愚弄、被蒙在鼓里的吧！」

随后他再一次转向德鲁莱德。

「德鲁莱德公民——」他刚开了腔。

然而接踵而来的鼎沸人声，已盖过了他自己的嗓音。全巴黎暴民郁积的怒火，仿佛全找到了宣泄的出口——此刻人们用一片狂嚎，力图把余下的审讯一概淹没。

他们的兽心方才还曾因德鲁莱德一番热切的呼吁，向着朱丽叶特忽然软化；此刻却以同样快的速度，把那副同情的面孔换成了惊骇与诅咒。

===Original===
Two people had fooled and deceived them. One of these they had reverenced and trusted, as much as their degraded minds were capable of reverencing anything, therefore *his* sin seemed doubly damnable.

He and that paleface aristocrat had for weeks now, months, or years perhaps, conspired against the Republic, against the Revolution, which had been made by a people thirsting for liberty. During these months and years *he* had talked to them, and they had listened; he had poured forth treasures of eloquence, cajoled them, as he had done just now.

The noise and hubbub were growing apace. If Tinville and Merlin had desired to infuriate the mob, they had more than succeeded. All that was most bestial, most savage in this awful Parisian populace rose to the surface now in one wild, mad desire for revenge.

The crowd rushed down from the benches, over one another's heads, over children's fallen bodies; they rushed down because they wanted to get at him, their whilom favourite, and at his palefaced mistress, and tear them to pieces, hit them, scratch out their eyes. They snarled like so many wild beasts, the women shrieked, the children cried, and the men of the National Guard, hurrying forward, had much ado to keep back this flood-tide of hate.

Had any of them broken loose, from behind the barrier of bayonets hastily raised against them, it would have fared ill with Déroulède and Juliette.

===Chinese===
有两个人愚弄了他们，欺骗了他们。其中一个，他们原本敬若神明、深信不疑——以他们那堕落的头脑所能敬奉的程度而言——因此*他*的罪孽便显得加倍不可饶恕。

他和那个白脸皮的女贵族，这几个星期、这几个月、或许这几年来，一直串通一起，密谋反对共和国，反对革命——那可是渴望自由的民众亲手造就的革命。这几个月、这几年间，*他*一直在向他们游说，他们也一直侧耳听着；他滔滔不绝，倾倒出雄辩的宝藏，花言巧语哄得他们团团转，就像方才那样。

喧声与哄闹一刻紧似一刻地高涨。倘若坦维尔和梅尔兰存心要把暴民激怒，那他们不仅如愿，还远远超出了所愿。这可怕的巴黎民众身上最兽性、最野蛮的一切，此刻都翻涌到面上来，汇成一股狂野疯狂的复仇欲。

人群从旁听席上蜂拥而下，从别人的头顶上爬过去，从跌倒的孩子们身上踩过去；他们冲下来，为的是扑向他——他们昔日宠爱的人——扑向他的白脸情妇，把他们撕成碎片，拳打脚踢，抠出眼珠。他们像一窝野兽似的龇牙狂嗥，女人们尖声怪叫，孩子们放声大哭；国民自卫军的士兵们赶忙上前，拼尽九牛二虎之力，才堵住这股仇恨的怒潮。

仓促间架起、用来阻挡他们的刺刀屏障后面，但凡有一人挣脱冲出，德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特便要凶多吉少了。

===Original===
The President wildly rang his bell, and his voice, quivering with excitement, was heard once or twice above the din.

"Clear the court! Clear the court!"

But the people refused to be cleared out of court.

"*À la lanterne les traîtres! Mort à Déroulède. À la lanterne! l'aristo!*"

And in the thickest of the crowd, the broad shoulders and massive head of Citizen Lenoir towered above the others.

===Chinese===
庭长疯了似的摇铃；他那因激动而发颤的嗓音，从鼎沸的人声里透出来一两次。

「清场！清场！」

可是民众拒不离开法庭。

「*À la lanterne les traîtres! Mort à Déroulède. À la lanterne! l'aristo!*（把叛徒吊上路灯去！德鲁莱德去死。吊路灯去！那贵族！）」

而在人群最稠密的地方，勒努瓦公民那宽阔的肩膀和硕大的脑袋，高高地凸出在众人之上。

===Original===
At first it seemed as if he had been urging on the mob in its fury. His strident voice, with its broad provincial accent, was heard distinctly shouting loud vituperations against the accused.

Then at a given moment, when the tumult was at its height, when the National Guard felt their bayonets giving way before this onrushing tide of human jackals, Lenoir changed his tactics.

"*Tiens! c'est bête!*" he shouted loudly, "we shall do far better with the traitors when we get them outside. What say you, citizens? Shall we leave the judges here to conclude the farce, and arrange for its sequel ourselves outside the 'Tigre Jaune'?"

At first but little heed was paid to his suggestion, and he repeated it once or twice, adding some interesting details:

"One is freer in the streets, where these apes of the National Guard can't get between the people of France and their just revenge. Ma foi!" he added, squaring his broad shoulders, and pushing his way through the crowd towards the door, "I for one am going to see where hangs the most suitable *lanterne*."

===Chinese===
起初看那样子，他倒像是在给暴民的狂怒煽风点火。他沙哑刺耳的嗓音带着浓重的外省口音，分明听得见，正在高声咒骂被告。

随后在约定的时刻——闹到最凶的当口，国民自卫军眼看自己的刺刀快顶不住这群豺狼人潮的冲涌了——勒努瓦忽然换了一副手法。

「*Tiens! c'est bête!*（瞧！真蠢！）」他扯开嗓门高喊，「等把这帮叛徒弄到外头去，咱们跟他们算账要痛快得多。公民们，你们说呢？咱们把审判官们留在这儿去收这出闹剧的场，散场后的续集，咱们自个儿到『黄虎』（Tigre Jaune）外头去安排，如何？」

起初，他的提议没怎么被人理会；他便重说了一两遍，还添上几桩有意思的细节：

「在街上自在得多——国民自卫军那班猴崽子挤不进中间去，拦不住法兰西人民施行他们正当的复仇。*Ma foi!*（说实在的！）」他挺起宽阔的肩膀，从人堆里朝门口挤过去，又补了一句，「我头一个就去看哪儿吊着一盏最合适的 *lanterne*（路灯）。」

===Original===
Like a flock of sheep the crowd now followed him.

"The nearest *lanterne*!" they shouted. "In the streets—in the streets! *À la lanterne!* The traitors!"

And with many a jeer, many a loathsome curse, and still more loathsome jests, some of the crowd began to file out. A few only remained to see the conclusion of the farce.

===Chinese===
这一回，人群像一窝绵羊似的，跟着他走了。

「最近的那盏 *lanterne*（路灯）！」他们高喊。「上街去——上街去！*À la lanterne!*（吊路灯去！）那些叛徒！」

于是一路带着嘲骂、带着污秽的诅咒、还带着更加污秽的笑谑，人群里的一部分开始鱼贯而出。留下来的不过寥寥数人，要看这出闹剧的收场。

## XXVI Sentence of Death / XXVI 死刑判决

===Original===

The *Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire* tells us that both the accused had remained perfectly calm during the turmoil which raged within the bare walls of the Hall of Justice.
Citizen-Deputy Déroulède, however, so the chroniclers aver, though outwardly impassive, was evidently deeply moved. He had very expressive eyes, clear mirrors of the fine, upright soul within, and in them there was a look of intense emotion as he watched the crowd, which he had so often dominated and controlled, now turning in hatred against him.
He seemed actually to be seeing with a spiritual vision, his own popularity wane and die.
But when the thick of the crowd had pushed and jostled itself out of the hall, that transient emotion seemed to disappear, and he allowed himself quietly to be led from the front bench, where he had sat as a privileged member of the National Convention, to a place immediately behind the dock, and between two men of the National Guard.
From that moment he was a prisoner, accused of treason against the Republic, and obviously his mock trial would be hurried through by his triumphant enemies, whilst the temper of the people was at boiling point against him.

===Chinese===

《革命法庭公报》（*Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire*）告诉我们说：在司法大厅（Hall of Justice）那空荡荡的四壁之内横行肆虐的一片骚乱中，两名被告都始终保持着十足的平静。
然而，据编年史家们断言，德鲁莱德（Déroulède）公会议员虽然外表纹丝不动，内心显然深受触动。他生就一双极富表现力的眼睛，是他胸中那高尚正直灵魂的澄澈明镜；此刻，眼看着他曾屡屡驾驭、操控的那群人转而满怀憎恨地掉头与他为敌，那双眼睛里便盛满强烈的激动。
他仿佛真的凭着一副心灵的视象，眼睁睁看着自己的声望衰微、消殒。
但等到人群的大部推推搡搡挤出大厅之后，那阵转瞬即逝的激动似乎便消散了；他听任人们安安静静地把自己从前排席位——他原是以国民公会（National Convention）特权议员之身坐在那里的——引到紧贴被告席后方的位置上，夹在两名国民自卫军士兵中间。
从那一刻起，他便是一名囚徒了，罪名是背叛共和国；显而易见，趁民众对他的愤懑正沸滚到极点，他那班志得意满的敌人会把这场做样子的审判匆匆草草办完。

===Original===

Complete silence had succeeded to the raging tumult of the past few moments. Nothing now could be heard in the vast room, save Foucquier-Tinville’s hastily whispered instructions to the clerk nearest to him, and the scratch of the latter’s quill pen against the paper.
The President was, with equal rapidity, affixing his signature to various papers handed up to him by the other clerks. The few remaining spectators, the deputies, and those among the crowd who had elected to see the close of the debate, were silent and expectant.
Merlin was mopping his forehead as if in intense fatigue after a hard struggle; Robespierre was coolly taking snuff.

===Chinese===

方才那阵狂暴的喧腾平息之后，继之而来的是全然的静默。偌大的厅堂里，此刻唯一可闻的，只有富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville）匆匆低语、向离他最近的那名书记员交代指示之声，以及后者羽毛笔在纸上刮擦的沙沙声响。
庭长也在以同样迅疾的速度，在其他书记员呈递上来的各色文书上逐一签名。留下未走的寥寥几名观众、众位议员，以及人群当中那些自愿要看辩论收场的看客，都默不作声，翘首以待。
梅尔兰（Merlin）不住地抹拭着额头，仿佛苦斗过后疲乏已极；罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）则若无其事地嗅着鼻烟。

===Original===

From where Déroulède stood, he could see Juliette’s graceful figure silhouetted against the light of the petrol lamp. His heart was torn between intense misery at having failed to save her and a curious, exultant joy at thought of dying beside her.
He knew the procedure of this revolutionary tribunal well﻿—knew that within the next few moments he too would be condemned, that they would both be hustled out of the crowd and dragged through the streets of Paris, and finally thrown into the same prison, to herd with those who, like themselves, had but a few hours to live.
And then tomorrow at dawn, death for them both under the guillotine. Death in public, with all its attendant horrors: the packed tumbril; the priest, in civil clothes, appointed by this godless government, muttering conventional prayers and valueless exhortations.
And in his heart there was nothing but love for her﻿—love and an intense pity﻿—for the punishment she was suffering was far greater than her crime. He hoped that in her heart remorse would not be too bitter; and he looked forward with joy to the next few hours, which he would pass near her, during which he could perhaps still console and soothe her.
She was but the victim of an ideal, of Fate stronger than her own will. She stood, an innocent martyr to the great mistake of her life.

===Chinese===

从德鲁莱德站立之处，他望得见朱丽叶特那婀娜的身姿映衬在煤油灯的灯光前。他的心被撕作两半：一半是未能救她脱险的深重悲苦，一半是想到即将死在她身旁时涌起的一种奇异的、狂喜般的欢愉。
他对这座革命法庭的程序了如指掌——他知道，不出几刻，他自己也定遭判罪；知道他们俩都会被人从人丛中推搡出去，拖过巴黎的街衢，最后被投进同一座监狱，与那些同他们一样只剩几个小时好活的人杂处一群。
然后便是明日拂晓，断头台下，两人同赴一死。当众受死，连同随之而来的一切恐怖：塞得满满当当的死囚车；由这个不信神的政府指派、身穿便服的教士，口中喃喃，诵着刻板的祷文与一文不值的训诫。
而他心中对她唯有爱——爱与深切的怜惜——因为她所受的惩罚，远比她的罪行为重。他但愿她心中的悔恨不至于太过苦涩；他怀着欣悦盼望着接下来的几个钟头——他将要在她身旁度过的那几个钟头，其间他或许还能安慰她、宽抚她。
她不过是一个理想的牺牲品，是比她自身意志更为强大的命运的牺牲品。她立在那里，是她一生那个大错的无辜殉难者。

===Original===

But the minutes sped on. Foucquier-Tinville had evidently completed his new indictments.
The one against Juliette Marny was read out first. She was now accused of conspiring with Paul Déroulède against the safety of the Republic, by having cognisance of a treasonable correspondence carried on with the prisoner, Marie Antoinette; by virtue of which accusation the Public Prosecutor asked her if she had anything to say.
“No,” she replied loudly and firmly. “I pray to God for the safety and deliverance of our Queen, Marie Antoinette, and for the overthrow of this Reign of Terror and Anarchy.”
These words, registered in the *Bulletin du Tribunal Révolutionnaire* were taken as final and irrefutable proofs of her guilt, and she was then summarily condemned to death.

===Chinese===

然而光阴分分秒秒飞驰而去。富基耶-坦维尔显然已把他的几份新起诉书拟就了。
对朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）的那一份先被宣读出来。她如今被控与保罗·德鲁莱德（Paul Déroulède）合谋危害共和国之安全，所据者，乃是她知悉那桩与在押囚犯玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）往来的叛国通信；凭此指控，公诉人（Public Prosecutor）问她可有什么话要说。
「不，」她高声而坚定地答道。「我向上帝祈祷，愿我们的王后玛丽·安托瓦内特平安得救，愿这恐怖统治与无政府状态终遭倾覆。」
这几句话一经载入《公报》，便被认作她罪责成立的最终的、无可辩驳的铁证；于是她当即便被草草判处了死刑。

===Original===

She was then made to step down from the dock and Déroulède to stand in her place.
He listened quietly to the long indictment which Foucquier-Tinville had already framed against him the evening before, in readiness for this contingency. The words “treason against the Republic” occurred conspicuously and repeatedly. The document itself is at one with the thousands of written charges, framed by that odious Foucquier-Tinville during these periods of bloodshed, and which in themselves are the most scathing indictments against the odious travesty of Justice, perpetrated with his help.
Self-accused, and avowedly a traitor, Déroulède was not even asked if he had anything to say; sentence of death was passed on him, with the rapidity and callousness peculiar to these proceedings.
After which Paul Déroulède and Juliette Marny were led forth, under strong escort, into the street.

===Chinese===

随后，她被叫下被告席，德鲁莱德则站到了她的位置上。
他静听罢那份长篇起诉书——那是富基耶-坦维尔前一夜便已针对他拟就、以备此番变局的。「背叛共和国」的字样赫然在纸，一再出现。这份文书本身，与那个可憎的富基耶-坦维尔在这许多流血时期里草拟的成千上万份书面控状别无二致；而那些控状本身，便是对那幕凭他襄助而犯下的可憎司法丑剧，最入骨的控诉。
自行控告自己、并公然自承为叛徒的德鲁莱德，甚至没有被问过一句可有话要说；死刑便宣判在他身上，带着这类程序所特有的迅疾与冷酷。
这之后，保罗·德鲁莱德与朱丽叶特·马尔尼便在一支重兵押解之下，被带出厅外，解上了街头。

## XXVII The Fructidor Riots / XXVII 果月骚乱

===Original===
Many accounts, more or less authentic, have been published of the events known to history as the “Fructidor Riots.”

But this is how it all happened: at any rate it is the version related some few days later in England to the Prince of Wales by no less a personage than Sir Percy Blakeney; and who indeed should know better than The Scarlet Pimpernel himself?

Déroulède and Juliette Marny were the last of the batch of prisoners who were tried on that memorable day of Fructidor.

There had been such a number of these, that all the covered carts in use for the conveyance of prisoners to and from the Hall of Justice had already been despatched with their weighty human load; thus it was that only a rough wooden cart, hoodless and rickety, was available, and into this Déroulède and Juliette were ordered to mount.

===Chinese===
关于史册上称作「果月骚乱」的那些事件，世人已刊行过许多记述，或真或假，不一而足。

但事情的原委本是如此：至少，下面这个说法，是几天之后在英格兰讲给威尔士亲王（the Prince of Wales）听的——讲述者不是别人，正是珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）；而除了红花侠（The Scarlet Pimpernel）本人，还有谁会知道得更清楚呢？

在那个值得纪念的果月日子里受审的一批囚犯当中，德鲁莱德（Déroulède）和朱丽叶特·马尔尼（Juliette Marny）是最后两个。

这批囚犯人数实在太多，一向用来押解囚犯往返司法宫大殿的那些带篷马车，都已满载着沉重的人口打发走了；于是只剩下一辆粗陋的木车——没有车篷，吱嘎欲散——可供使用，便命德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特上了这辆车。

===Original===
It was now close on nine o’clock in the evening. The streets of Paris, sparsely illuminated here and there with solitary oil lamps swung across from house to house on wires, presented a miserable and squalid appearance. A thin, misty rain had begun to fall, transforming the ill-paved roads into morasses of sticky mud.

The Hall of Justice was surrounded by a howling and shrieking mob, who, having imbibed all the stores of brandy in the neighbouring drinking bars, was now waiting outside in the dripping rain for the express purpose of venting its pent-up, spirit-sodden lust of rage against the man whom it had once worshipped, but whom now it hated. Men, women, and even children swarmed round the principal entrances of the Palais de Justice, along the bank of the river as far as the Pont au Change, and up towards the Luxembourg Palace, now transformed into the prison, to which the condemned would no doubt be conveyed.

Along the riverbank, and immediately facing the Palais de Justice, a row of gallows-shaped posts, at intervals of a hundred yards or more, held each a smoky petrol lamp, at a height of some eight feet from the ground.

===Chinese===
这时已将近晚上九点钟。巴黎的街道，只有稀稀落落的几处被孤零零的油灯照亮——那些灯用铁丝从这家房屋拉到那家房屋，悬空晃荡——一派凄惨污浊的景象。一阵薄薄的、雾蒙蒙的细雨已经开始落下来，把铺得坑洼不平的马路泡成了黏糊糊的泥沼。

司法宫大殿被一片狂嗥尖叫的暴民团团围住；这些人把邻近酒馆里的白兰地存货灌了个底朝天，此刻就在滴水的雨里守在外面，专为的要发泄那郁积于胸、被烈酒泡透的暴怒——冲着他们从前奉若神明、如今恨之入骨的那个人。男人、女人、甚至孩子，密密层层围住了司法宫的几处主要入口，沿河岸一直蔓延到兑换桥（Pont au Change），又向上朝卢森堡宫（Luxembourg Palace）涌去——那里如今已改作监狱，被判了刑的人想必就要解送到那儿去。

沿着河岸，正对着司法宫，一排绞架模样的木柱，每隔一百来码便是一根，每根柱上挂着一盏冒烟的汽油灯，离地约莫八英尺。

===Original===
One of these lamps had been knocked down, and from the post itself there now hung ominously a length of rope, with a noose at the end.

Around this improvised gallows a group of women sat, or rather squatted, in the mud; their ragged shifts and kirtles, soaked through with the drizzling rain, hung dankly on their emaciated forms; their hair, in some cases grey, and in others dark or straw-coloured, clung matted round their wet faces, on which the dirt and the damp had drawn weird and grotesque lines.

The men were restless and noisy, rushing aimlessly hither and thither, from the corner of the bridge, up the Rue du Palais, fearful lest their prey be conjured away ere their vengeance was satisfied.

Oh, how they hated their former idol now! Citizen Lenoir, with his broad shoulders and powerful, grime-covered head, towered above the throng; his strident voice, with its raucous, provincial accent, could be distinctly heard above the din, egging on the men, shouting to the women, stirring up hatred against the prisoners, wherever it showed signs of abating in intensity.

===Chinese===
其中一盏灯已被打落；柱身上此刻不祥地垂着一段绳子，绳梢上打好了绞索。

在这座临时凑成的绞架四周，一群女人坐在泥里——不如说是蹲在泥里；她们褴褛的衬衫和裙子被毛毛雨浸得透湿，湿漉漉地贴在枯瘦的身子上；她们的头发，有的花白，有的乌黑，也有的稻草般枯黄，一绺绺粘结成毡，缠在湿淋淋的脸周；那一张张脸上，污垢和潮气画出了一道道古怪狰狞的纹路。

男人们则躁动喧哗，没头没脑地东奔西窜，从桥角一路奔上宫殿街（Rue du Palais），生怕不等他们把仇报够，猎物就被人用法术弄走了。

啊，他们如今多么痛恨昔日那位偶像！勒努瓦（Lenoir）公民那宽阔的肩膀、那沾满污垢的硕大头脑，高高地耸出人群之上；他沙哑刺耳、带着浓重外省口音的嗓音，在鼎沸喧声中分外听得真切，催着男人们，冲女人们喊叫，哪里人们对囚犯的仇恨显出松动的苗头，他就在哪里把它重新煽起。

===Original===
The coal-heaver, hailing from some distant province, seemed to have set himself the grim task of provoking the infuriated populace to some terrible deed of revenge against Déroulède and Juliette.

The darkness of the street, the fast-falling mist which obscured the light from the meagre oil lamps, seemed to add a certain weirdness to this moving, seething multitude. No one could see his neighbour. In the blackness of the night the muttering or yelling figures moved about like some spectral creatures from hellish regions—the Akous of Brittany who call to those about to die; whilst the women squatting in the oozing mud, beneath that swinging piece of rope, looked like a group of ghostly witches, waiting for the hour of their Sabbath.

As Déroulède emerged into the open, the light from a swinging lantern in the doorway fell upon his face. The foremost of the crowd recognised him; a howl of execration went up to the cloud-covered sky, and a hundred hands were thrust out in deadly menace against him.

It seemed as if they wished to tear him to pieces.

===Chinese===
这个来自远方外省的运煤工，仿佛给自己派下了一桩冷酷的差事：去撩拨这被激怒的人群，逼他们干出一桩可怕的复仇勾当，来对付德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特。

街上的黑暗，加上那急速下坠的雨雾遮蔽了微弱油灯的光，似乎给这群涌动沸腾的人又添了几分鬼气。谁也看不见身旁的人。在漆黑的夜里，那些喃喃低语或放声嘶嚎的人影来回游动，活像从地狱里出来的幽灵——布列塔尼传说中专向将死之人呼唤的阿库（Akous）；而蹲在渗水的泥浆里、悬在那段晃荡绳索底下的女人们，瞧上去就像一群鬼气森森的女巫，正等着她们安息日的时辰。

德鲁莱德一露面，门口那盏摇晃的灯笼的光正照上他的脸。最前头的人群认出了他；一声咒骂的嗥叫冲向密云遮蔽的天空，上百只手朝他直伸出去，含着致命的威胁。

看那样子，他们恨不得把他撕成碎片。

===Original===
“*À la lanterne! À la lanterne! le traître!*”

He shivered slightly, as if with the sudden blast of cold, humid air, but he stepped quietly into the cart, closely followed by Juliette.

The strong escort of the National Guard, with Commandant Santerre and his two drummers, had much ado to keep back the mob. It was not the policy of the revolutionary government to allow excesses of summary justice in the streets: the public execution of traitors on the Place de la Révolution, the processions in the tumbrils, were thought to be wholesome examples for other would-be traitors to mark and digest.

Citizen Santerre, military commandant of Paris, had ordered his men to use their bayonets ruthlessly, and, to further overawe the populace, he ordered a prolonged roll of drums, lest Déroulède took it into his head to speak to the crowd.

===Chinese===
「*À la lanterne! À la lanterne! le traître!*（吊路灯去！吊路灯去！那个叛徒！）」

他微微一颤，像是被骤然袭来的又冷又湿的风吹的，随即安安静静地登上木车，朱丽叶特紧跟着他。

国民自卫军那支强有力的卫队——由桑特尔（Santerre）司令官带着他的两名鼓手随行——费了好大的力气才把暴民拦住。听任草草了事的私刑在街头逞凶，并非革命政府的政策：叛徒在革命广场（Place de la Révolution）上被当众处决、死囚车上的游行示众，这些在当局看来，正是有益的样板，好让其他心怀叵测的叛徒看在眼里、记在心里。

巴黎的军事司令官桑特尔公民已下令手下毫不留情地动用刺刀；为了进一步震慑民众，他又命人擂起一阵绵延不断的鼓声，只防德鲁莱德一时兴起，开口向人群讲话。

===Original===
But Déroulède had no such intention: he seemed chiefly concerned in shielding Juliette from the cold; she had been made to sit in the cart beside him, and he had taken off his coat, and was wrapping it round her against the penetrating rain.

The eyewitnesses of these memorable events have declared that, at a given moment, he looked up suddenly with a curious, eager expression in his eyes, and then raised himself in the cart and seemed to be trying to penetrate the gloom round him, as if in search of a face, or perhaps a voice.

“*À la lanterne! À la lanterne!*” was the continual hoarse cry of the mob.

Up to now, flanked in their rear by the outer walls of the Palais de Justice, the soldiers had found it a fairly easy task to keep the crowd at bay. But there came a time when the cart was bound to move out into the open, in order to convey the prisoners along, by the Rue du Palais, up to the Luxembourg Prison.

===Chinese===
可德鲁莱德根本没有这个念头：他一心顾着的，似乎只是替朱丽叶特挡寒；人们让她在车里挨着他坐下，他便脱下自己的外衣，裹在她身上，抵御那无孔不入的雨。

这段值得纪念的事变的目击者们后来说起，在某一时刻，他忽然抬起头来，眼里带着一种古怪而急切的神情，随后在车里直起身子，似乎竭力想穿透四周的昏暗，仿佛在寻找一张面孔，或者也许是一个声音。

「*À la lanterne! À la lanterne!*（吊路灯去！吊路灯去！）」——暴民嘶哑的喊声不绝于耳。

直到此刻，士兵们的背后有司法宫的外墙护着，拦住人群倒还算得上轻松。可是总有一刻，木车不得不驶入开阔地带，押着囚犯沿宫殿街一路往卢森堡监狱去。

===Original===
This task, however, had become more and more difficult every moment. The people of Paris, who for two years had been told by its tyrants that it was supreme lord of the universe, was mad with rage at seeing its desires frustrated by a few soldiers.

The drums had been greeted by terrific yells, which effectually drowned their roll; the first movement of the cart was hailed by a veritable tumult.

Only the women who squatted round the gallows had not moved from their position of vantage; one of these Maegaeras was quietly readjusting the rope, which had got out of place.

But all the men and some of the women were literally besieging the cart, and threatening the soldiers, who stood between them and the object of their fury.

===Chinese===
然而，这项差事每过一刻便添一分艰难。两年来，他们的暴君们一直对巴黎民众说，他们自己就是全宇宙至高无上的主宰；如今眼看自己的心愿竟被区区几个士兵挫败，他们气得发了狂。

鼓声换来的是震天的狂叫，把鼓点的滚动彻头彻尾地淹没了；木车头一挪动，便招来一场名副其实的骚乱。

唯有蹲在绞架四周的女人们没有离开她们那有利的位置；这班梅盖拉（Maegaeras，希腊神话中的复仇女妖）中的一个，正不声不响地重新调整那根挪了位的绳子。

可男人们和一部分女人，实实在在把木车围困得水泄不通，威吓着那些士兵——正是这些士兵，挡在他们泄愤的目标跟前。

===Original===
It seemed as if nothing now could save Déroulède and Juliette from an immediate and horrible death.

“*A mort! A mort! À la lanterne les traîtres!*”

Santerne himself, who had shouted himself hoarse, was at a loss what to do. He had sent one man to the nearest cavalry barracks, but reinforcements would still be some little time coming; whilst in the meanwhile his men were getting exhausted, and the mob, more and more excited, threatened to break through their line at every moment.

There was not another second to be lost.

===Chinese===
看来眼下已没有任何力量，能把德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特从迫在眉睫的可怕死亡里救出来了。

「*A mort! A mort! À la lanterne les traîtres!*（处死他！处死他！把叛徒吊上路灯去！）」

桑特尔本人嗓子都喊哑了，一时也拿不出主意。他已派了一个人去最近的骑兵营求援，可援兵还得过一会儿才能到；而这当口，他手下的人已渐渐筋疲力尽，那愈来愈亢奋的暴民，随时都可能冲破他们的防线。

一秒钟也不能再耽搁了。

===Original===
Santerre was for letting the mob have its way, and he would willingly have thrown it the prey for which it clamoured; but orders were orders, and in the year I of the Revolution it was not good to disobey.

At this supreme moment of perplexity he suddenly felt a respectful touch on his arm.

Close behind him a soldier of the National Guard—not one of his own men—was standing at attention, and holding a small, folded paper in his hand.

“Sent to you by the Minister of Justice,” whispered the soldier hurriedly. “The citizen-deputies have watched the tumult from the Hall; they say, you must not lose an instant.”

===Chinese===
桑特尔本想任由暴民称心如意，他们吵嚷着索要的那份猎物，他满可以痛快地抛给他们；可是命令就是命令，在革命元年，违抗命令可不是闹着玩的事。

正在这踌躇莫决的紧要关头，他忽然觉得有人恭恭敬敬地碰了碰他的胳膊。

紧挨在他背后，站着一个国民自卫军的士兵——不是他手底下的人——正立正站着，手里捏着一张折起的小纸条。

「这是司法部长派人送来的，」那士兵急促地低声说，「公会议员们一直在这大殿里看着这场骚乱；他们说，您一刻也不能再耽搁。」

===Original===
Santerre withdrew from the front rank, up against the side of the cart, where a rough stable lantern had been fixed. He took the paper from the soldier’s hand, and, hastily tearing it open, he read it by the dim light of the lantern.

As he read, his thick, coarse features expressed the keenest satisfaction.

“You have two more men with you?” he asked quickly.

“Yes, citizen,” replied the man, pointing towards his right; “and the Citizen-Minister said you would give me two more.”

===Chinese===
桑特尔从前排退了出来，紧靠到木车一侧——那儿挂着一盏粗陋的马厩灯笼。他从士兵手里接过纸条，匆匆撕开，就着灯笼昏暗的光看起来。

他一面读，那副粗厚糙陋的面相便显出说不出的快意。

「你身边还有两个人？」他急促地问。

「是的，公民，」那人答道，朝他右手边一指，「部长公民说，您还会再给我两个人。」

===Original===
“You’ll take the prisoners quietly across to the Prison of the Temple—you understand that?”

“Yes, citizen; Citizen Merlin has given me full instructions. You can have the cart drawn back a little more under the shadow of the portico, where the prisoners can be made to alight; they can then be given into my charge. You in the meantime are to stay here with your men, round the empty cart, as long as you can. Reinforcements have been sent for, and must soon be here. When they arrive you are to move along with the cart, as if you were making for the Luxembourg Prison. This manoeuvre will give us time to deliver the prisoners safely at the Temple.”

The man spoke hurriedly and peremptorily, and Santerne was only too ready to obey. He felt relieved at thought of reinforcements, and glad to be rid of the responsibility of conducting such troublesome prisoners.

The thick mist, which grew more and more dense, favoured the new manoeuvre, and the constant roll of drums drowned the hastily given orders.

===Chinese===
「您要悄悄地把囚犯送到坦普尔监狱（the Temple）那边去——您明白这个吗？」

「是的，公民；梅尔兰（Merlin）公民已给了我全套的指示。您可以叫人把车再往柱廊的阴影底下退一退，让囚犯在那儿下车，然后交到我手里。您呢，趁这工夫带着您的人守在这儿，围着空车，能守多久便守多久。援兵已经派人去调，不久就该到了。他们一到，您就随着车往前走，只装作是往卢森堡监狱去。这一着，便给我们腾出工夫，把囚犯平平安安送到坦普尔。」

那人急促而专断地说完，桑特尔巴不得立刻照办。一想到有援兵，他心里便宽了；能卸下押送这般棘手囚犯的重担，他也乐得轻松。

愈来愈浓的厚雾正有利于这一番新的调度，而不停歇的鼓声又盖过了那些匆匆下达的命令。

===Original===
The cart was drawn back into the deepest shadow of the great portico, and whilst the mob were howling their loudest, and yelling out frantic demands for the traitors, Déroulède and Juliette were summarily ordered to step out of the cart. No one saw them, for the darkness here was intense.

“Follow quietly!” whispered a raucous voice in their ears as they did so, “or my orders are to shoot you where you stand.”

But neither of them had any wish for resistance. Juliette, cold and numb, was clinging to Déroulède, who had placed a protecting arm round her.

Santerne had told off two of his men to join the new escort of the prisoners, and presently the small party, skirting the walls of the Palais de Justice, began to walk rapidly away from the scene of the riot.

Déroulède noted that some half-dozen men seemed to be surrounding him and Juliette, but the drizzling rain blurred every outline. The blackness of the night too had become absolutely dense, and in the distance the cries of the populace grew more and more faint.

===Chinese===
木车被拉回到大柱廊最深的阴影里；正当暴民嗥得最凶、发疯似地叫嚷着要交出叛徒的时候，德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特被断然喝令下车。没有人看见他们，因为这里的黑暗浓得化不开。

「安安静静地跟着走！」他们照办时，一个沙哑的嗓音在耳边低声说，「不然我奉到的命令，就是把你们就地枪毙。」

可他们两人谁也没有反抗的意思。朱丽叶特又冷又木，紧紧偎着德鲁莱德；他伸出一条胳膊护着她。

桑特尔已拨出两个部下，加入囚犯的新护卫队；不多时，这一小队人贴着司法宫的墙根，开始快步离开骚乱的场地。

德鲁莱德注意到，似乎有六七个人围拢在他和朱丽叶特四周，可毛毛的细雨把一切轮廓都洇得模糊了；夜的黑也已浓得十足，远处民众的喊声愈来愈细，渐渐远了。

## XXVIII The Unexpected / XXVIII 意外之变

===Original===

The small party walked on in silence. It seemed to consist of a very few men of the National Guard, whom Santerne had placed under the command of the soldier who had transmitted to him the orders of the Citizen-Deputies.

Juliette and Déroulède both vaguely wondered whither they were being led; to some other prison mayhap, away from the fury of the populace. They were conscious of a sense of satisfaction at thought of being freed from that pack of raging wild beasts.

Beyond that they cared nothing. Both felt already the shadow of death hovering over them. The supreme moment of their lives had come, and had found them side by side.

What neither fear nor remorse, sorrow nor joy, could do, that the great and mighty Shadow accomplished in a trice.

===Chinese===

这一小队人马默然前行。看来，队伍不过由寥寥几名国民自卫军（National Guard）士兵组成；桑特尔（Santerre）已把他们交给一名士兵指挥——方才正是此人把公会议员（Citizen-Deputies）们的命令传给他本人的。

朱丽叶特（Juliette）与德鲁莱德（Déroulède）两人都隐隐纳闷，不知要把他们押往何处；兴许是另一座监狱吧，好躲开民众的狂怒。一想到将摆脱那一群暴怒的野兽，他们心底便浮起一丝快意。

除此之外，他们已无所萦怀。两人都已然觉出死亡的阴影在自己头顶盘旋。生命中最崇高的时刻已经来临，而它降临之时，他们正肩并着肩。

无论恐惧还是悔恨，无论悲愁还是欢愉都无能为力的事，那伟大而威严的暗影却转瞬便告完成。

===Original===

Juliette, looking death bravely in the face, held out her hand, and sought that of the man she loved.

There was not one word spoken between them, not even a murmur.

Déroulède, with the unerring instinct of his own unselfish passion, understood all that the tiny hand wished to convey to him.

In a moment everything was forgotten save the joy of this touch. Death, or the fear of death, had ceased to exist. Life was beautiful, and in the soul of these two human creatures there was perfect peace, almost perfect happiness.

With one grasp of the hand they had sought and found one another’s soul. What mattered the yelling crowd, the noise and tumult of this sordid world? They had found one another, and, hand-in-hand, shoulder-to-shoulder, they had gone off wandering into the land of dreams, where dwelt neither doubt nor treachery, where there was nothing to forgive.

===Chinese===

朱丽叶特昂然直面死亡，伸出手去，探寻她所爱之人的手。

两人之间没有说出一句话，连一声低语也没有。

德鲁莱德凭着自己那无私深情所独具的精准直觉，领会了那只纤手欲向他传递的一切。

顷刻之间，万般俱忘，唯余这一触带来的欢愉。死亡，或是对死亡的惧怕，已不复存在。生命是美好的；而这两个人儿的灵魂之中，盛着全然的安宁，近乎全然的幸福。

就在这一握之中，他们彼此寻觅，并寻见了对方的灵魂。那嘶声叫嚷的人群、这个龌龊世界的喧嚣与纷扰，又算得了什么？他们已经寻到了彼此；手挽着手，肩并着肩，他们双双飘游到梦土之上——那里不栖疑忌，不栖背叛，那里没有需要宽恕的事。

===Original===

He no longer said: “She does not love me—would she have betrayed me else?” He felt the clinging, trustful touch of her hand, and knew that, with all her faults, her great sin and her lasting sorrow, her woman’s heart, Heaven’s most priceless treasure, was indeed truly his.

And she knew that he had forgiven—nay, that he had naught to forgive—for Love is sweet and tender, and judges not. Love is Love—whole, trustful, passionate. Love is perfect understanding and perfect peace.

And so they followed their escort whithersoever it chose to lead them.

Their eyes wandered aimlessly over the mist-laden landscape of this portion of deserted Paris. They had turned away from the river now, and were following the Rue des Arts. Close by on the right was the dismal little hostelry, La Cruche Cassée, where Sir Percy Blakeney lived. Déroulède, as they neared the place, caught himself vaguely wondering what had become of his English friend.

But it would take more than the ingenuity of the Scarlet Pimpernel to get two noted prisoners out of Paris today. Even if—

===Chinese===

他不会再说了：「她并不爱我——否则她又怎会出卖我？」他感受着她手心依恋而信赖的紧贴，于是明白：纵有她一切的过错、她滔天的罪愆与绵长的哀伤，她那颗女人的心——上天最珍贵的无价之宝——确乎真真切切是属于他的。

而她也知道，他已经宽恕了——不，他根本无可宽恕——因为爱是甜美而温柔的，爱从不裁断。爱就是爱——完整、信赖、炽烈。爱是全然的体谅，全然的安宁。

于是，押解的人要把他们带往哪里，他们便跟随到哪里。

他们的目光漫无目标，游移在这一片荒废的巴黎一隅雾气弥漫的景色之上。此刻他们已离开了河边，正循着艺术街（Rue des Arts）前行。右手近旁，便是那家凄凉的小客栈——「破壶」客栈（La Cruche Cassée），珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）就住在那里。临近那地方时，德鲁莱德发觉自己正隐隐琢磨，不知他那英国朋友如今下落如何。

然而，要想在今天把两名鼎鼎大名的囚犯弄出巴黎去，纵有红花侠（Scarlet Pimpernel）的巧智天工，只怕也还不够。即便——

===Original===

“Halt!”

The word of command rang out clearly and distinctly through the rain-soaked atmosphere.

Déroulède threw up his head and listened. Something strange and unaccountable in that same word of command had struck his sensitive ear.

Yet the party had halted, and there was a click as of bayonets or muskets levelled ready to fire.

===Chinese===

「站住！」

那声口令响彻雨意浸透的空气，清晰而分明。

德鲁莱德昂起头来，凝神细听。那声口令里有一点古怪而说不出的东西，撞在了他灵敏的耳朵上。

然而队伍已然停住，跟着响起一阵咔哒之声，仿佛是刺刀或火枪端平待放的动静。

===Original===

All had happened in less than a few seconds. The next moment there was a loud cry:

“*À moi*, Déroulède! ’tis the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

A vigorous blow from an unseen hand had knocked down and extinguished the nearest street lantern.

Déroulède felt that he and Juliette were being hastily dragged under an adjoining doorway even as the cheery voice echoed along the narrow street.

===Chinese===

这一切发生在短短几秒钟之内。紧接着便是一声高喊：

「*À moi*（到我这儿来），德鲁莱德！是红花侠！」

一只看不见的手猛力一击，把最近的那盏街灯击倒打灭。

就在那欢快的嗓音顺着窄街回荡之际，德鲁莱德觉出自己和朱丽叶特正被匆匆拽进邻近的一个门洞底下。

===Original===

Half-a-dozen men were struggling below in the mud, and there was a plentiful supply of honest English oaths. It looked as if the men of the National Guard had fallen upon one another, and had it not been for those same English oaths perhaps Déroulède and Juliette would have been slower to understand.

“Well done, Tony! Gadzooks, Ffoulkes, that was a smart bit of work!”

The lazy, pleasant voice was unmistakable, but, God in heaven! where did it come from?

Of one thing there could be no doubt. The two men despatched by Santerne were lying disabled on the ground, whilst three other soldiers were busy pinioning them with ropes.

===Chinese===

六七条汉子正在下面泥地里扭打成一团，嘴里夹着满口原汁原味的英国骂语。看上去，倒像是国民自卫军的士兵们自己人打了起来；若不是那些英国骂语，只怕德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特还要更慢一些才醒悟过来。

「干得漂亮，托尼（Tony）！老天，福尔克斯（Ffoulkes），这一手干得真叫利落！」

那懒洋洋、悦耳的嗓音是绝不会认错的；可是，天上的上帝啊！它究竟是从哪儿传来的？

有一点毋庸置疑：桑特尔派来的那两名士兵已经躺倒在地、动弹不得，另有三名士兵正忙着用绳子把他们捆缚结实。

===Original===

What did it all mean?

“La, friend Déroulède! you had not thought, I trust, that I would leave Mademoiselle Juliette in such a demmed, uncomfortable hole?”

And there, close beside Déroulède and Juliette, stood the tall figure of the Jacobin orator, the bloodthirsty Citizen Lenoir. The two young people gazed and gazed, then looked again, dumbfounded, hardly daring to trust their vision, for through the grime-covered mask of the gigantic coal-heaver a pair of merry blue eyes was regarding them with lazy amusement.

“La! I do look a miserable object, I know,” said the pseudo coal-heaver at last, “but ’twas the only way to get those murderous devils to do what I wanted. A thousand pardons, mademoiselle; ’twas I brought you to such a terrible pass, but la! you are amongst friends now. Will you deign to forgive me?”

===Chinese===

这一切究竟是什么意思？

「哎呀，德鲁莱德朋友！我相信您总不至于以为，我会把朱丽叶特小姐撇在那么个该死的、憋闷难受的破洞里吧？」

而就在德鲁莱德与朱丽叶特近旁，立着那个雅各宾演说家、嗜血成性的勒努瓦（Lenoir）公民的高大身影。两个年轻人怔怔地望了又望，再望一遍，惊得目瞪口呆，几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛：透过那巨型送煤工污垢满面的假面，一双快活的蓝眼睛正带着悠然的兴味打量着他们。

「哎呀！我知道，我这副模样瞧上去实在够惨的，」那个假送煤工终于开了口，「可要叫那帮杀人不眨眼的恶魔乖乖照我的意思办，这是唯一的法子。万分抱歉，小姐；是我把您害到这般可怕的田地的。可是哎呀！您如今是在朋友中间了。肯屈尊饶恕我么？」

===Original===

Juliette looked up. Her great, earnest eyes, now swimming in tears, sought those of the brave man who had so nobly stood by her and the man she loved.

“Blakeney—” began Déroulède.

But Sir Percy quickly interrupted him:

“Hush, man! we have but a few moments. Remember you are in Paris still, and the Lord only knows how we shall all get out of this murderous city tonight. I have said that you and mademoiselle are among friends. That is all for the moment. I had to get you together, or I should have failed. I could only succeed by subjecting you and mademoiselle to terrible indignities. Our League could plan but one rescue, and I had to adopt the best means at my command to have you condemned and led away together. Faith!” he added, with a pleasant laugh, “my friend Tinville will not be pleased when he realises that Citizen Lenoir has dragged the Citizen-Deputies by the nose.”

===Chinese===

朱丽叶特抬起头来。她那双恳切的大眼睛此刻盈满泪水，寻向那个勇敢的人——他曾那样高洁地守在她与她所爱之人的身旁。

「布莱克尼——」德鲁莱德刚一开口。

可珀西爵士急忙打断了他：

「嘘，伙计！我们只有寥寥片刻。记着你们还在巴黎，今晚咱们一伙人如何逃出这座杀人魔城，只有上天知道。我说过，您和小姐如今是在朋友中间。此刻，话只说到这里。我必须设法把你们俩弄到一处，否则我便会功亏一篑。而要成功，我就只能让阁下和小姐去受那等可怕的屈辱。我们的同盟（League）所能筹划的只有一次营救；我不得不使出我手中最好的手段，让你们二人同被判罪、又一道被押解出去。说真的！」他带着一声轻快的笑又补了一句，「等我那位坦维尔（Tinville）朋友回过神来，发觉勒努瓦公民把公会议员们牵着鼻子耍了一道，他心里准不会痛快。」

===Original===

Whilst he spoke he had led Déroulède and Juliette into a dark and narrow room on the ground floor of the hostelry, and presently he called loudly for Brogard, the host of this uninviting abode.

“Brogard!” shouted Sir Percy. “Where is that ass Brogard? La! man,” he added as Citizen Brogard, obsequious and fussy, and with pockets stuffed with English gold, came shuffling along, “where do you hide your engaging countenance? Here! another length of rope for the gallant soldiers. Bring them in here, then give them that potion down their throats, as I have prescribed. Demm it! I wish we need not have brought them along, but that devil Santerre might have been suspicious else. They’ll come to no harm, though, and can do us no mischief.”

He prattled along merrily. Innately kind and chivalrous, he wished to give Déroulède and Juliette time to recover from their dazed surprise.

===Chinese===

他说话的当儿，已把德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特领进客栈底层一间又暗又窄的屋子里，随即高声呼唤起这处乏人问津的住处的店主布罗加（Brogard）来。

「布罗加！」珀西爵士吼道，「那个蠢驴布罗加躲到哪儿去了？哎呀！老兄，」他见布罗加公民满脸谄笑、手脚忙乱，口袋里塞满英国金币，趿趿拉拉地蹭将过来，便又添上一句，「你把你那张讨人喜欢的脸盘藏到哪儿去了？来！再拿一截绳子，给那些英勇的士兵们。把他们带进这里，然后照我开好的方子，把那剂药水灌进他们的喉咙。该死！但愿咱们本不必把他们捎带而来，可不然的话，桑特尔那个魔头兴许要起疑心。不过他们吃不了亏，也坏不了咱们的事。」

他兴头十足、絮絮不休。他天性仁厚侠义，有意让德鲁莱德和朱丽叶特有工夫从那阵昏眩的惊愕中缓过神来。

===Original===

The transition from dull despair to buoyant hope had been so sudden: it had all happened in less than three minutes.

The scuffle had been short and sudden outside. The two soldiers of Santerne had been taken completely unawares, and the three young lieutenants of the Scarlet Pimpernel had fallen on them with such vigour that they had hardly had time to utter a cry of “Help!”

Moreover, that cry would have been useless. The night was dark and wet, and those citizens who felt ready for excitement were busy mobbing the Hall of Justice, a mile and a half away. One or two heads had appeared at the small windows of the squalid houses opposite, but it was too dark to see anything, and the scuffle had very quickly subsided.

===Chinese===

从沉沉的绝望到振翼的希望，这一转变来得何其突然：这一切前后不到三分钟。

外面那场扭打短促而突然。桑特尔的两名士兵全然猝不及防；红花侠的三位年轻副手扑向他们时来势那般凶猛，他们竟连喊一声「救命」的工夫都没有捞着。

何况，喊了也是徒劳。夜色又黑又湿，那些正摩拳擦掌、渴望刺激的公民们，都忙着围攻一英里半之外的司法大厅（Hall of Justice）去了。对面那些邋遢小屋的小窗里探出过一两个脑袋，然而天黑得什么也看不见，那场扭打也就很快平息下去。

===Original===

All was silent now in the Rue des Arts, and in the grimy coffee-room of the Cruche Cassée two soldiers of the National Guard were lying bound and gagged, whilst three others were gaily laughing, and wiping their rain-soaked hands and faces.

In the midst of them all stood the tall, athletic figure of the bold adventurer who had planned this impudent coup.

“La! we’ve got so far, friends, haven’t we?” he said cheerily, “and now for the immediate future. We must all be out of Paris tonight, or the guillotine for the lot of us tomorrow.”

===Chinese===

此刻艺术街上一片寂静；在「破壶」那间污黑的咖啡室里，两名国民自卫军士兵手脚被捆、口中塞物，直挺挺躺着，另外三个人却欢欢喜喜地大笑着，擦净他们被雨水浸透的手脸。

站在这一群人正中的，是那个大胆的冒险家颀长健硕的身影——这场放肆无礼的奇袭，正是他一手擘画的。

「哎呀！咱们已经走到了这一步，朋友们，可不是么？」他兴冲冲地说，「如今就看眼下的下一步了。今晚咱们必须全都逃出巴黎，不然明天咱们这伙人就得统统上断头台。」

===Original===

He spoke gaily, and with that pleasant drawl of his which was so well known in the fashionable assemblies of London; but there was a ring of earnestness in his voice, and his lieutenants looked up at him, ready to obey him in all things, but aware that danger was looming threateningly ahead.

Lord Anthony Dewhurst, Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, and Lord Hastings, dressed as soldiers of the National Guard, had played their part to perfection. Lord Hastings had presented the order to Santerre, and the three young bucks, at the word of command from their chief, had fallen upon and overpowered the two men whom the commandant of Paris had despatched to look after the prisoners.

So far all was well. But how to get out of Paris? Everyone looked to the Scarlet Pimpernel for guidance.

===Chinese===

他说得轻快，带着他那副在伦敦时髦集会上声名远播的悦耳拖腔；但他的话音里自有一股郑重的声气，他的副手们仰头望着他，万事俱愿听命于他，却也明白危险正在前方阴沉沉地逼来。

安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）、安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士与黑斯廷斯勋爵（Lord Hastings）一身国民自卫军士兵的装束，把各自的戏份演得滴水不漏。黑斯廷斯勋爵把命令呈给了桑特尔；那三位风度翩翩的少年郎，一闻他们首领的口令，便一齐扑上，制服了巴黎城防司令派来看管囚犯的那两个人。

到此为止，一切顺遂。可是，怎么逃出巴黎去呢？人人都在望着红花侠，等待他的指引。

===Original===

Sir Percy now turned to Juliette, and with the consummate grace which the elaborate etiquette of the times demanded, he made her a courtly bow.

“Mademoiselle de Marny,” he said, “allow me to conduct you to a room, which though unworthy of your presence will, nevertheless, enable you to rest quietly for a few minutes, whilst I give my friend Déroulède further advice and instructions. In the room you will find a disguise, which I pray you to don with all haste. La! they are filthy rags, I own, but your life and—and ours depend upon your help.”

Gallantly he kissed the tips of her fingers, and opened the door of an adjoining room to enable her to pass through; then he stood aside, so that her final look, as she went, might be for Déroulède.

===Chinese===

珀西爵士这时转向朱丽叶特，带着那个时代繁文缛节所要求的一派圆熟的优雅，向她深深行了一个宫廷式的鞠躬礼。

「德·马尔尼小姐，」他说道，「请容我引您到一间屋子去。那屋子固然配不上您的驾临，却好歹能让您安安静静地歇上几分钟，让我向我的朋友德鲁莱德再作些叮嘱与交代。屋里备有一套化装的衣裳，求您火速换上。哎呀！我不否认，那都是些腌臜的破布，可您的性命——还有我们的性命——全都指望着您帮忙了。」

他殷勤有礼地吻了吻她的指尖，随即推开隔壁那间屋的门，请她进去；然后自己退立一旁，好让她临去之际，那最后一眼可以留给德鲁莱德。

===Original===

As soon as the door had closed upon her he once more turned to the men.

“Those uniforms will not do now,” he said peremptorily; “there are bundles of abominable clothes here, Tony. Will you all don them as quickly as you can? We must all look as filthy a band of sansculottes tonight as ever walked the streets of Paris.”

His lazy drawl had deserted him now. He was the man of action and of thought, the bold adventurer who held the lives of his friends in the hollow of his hand.

===Chinese===

门在她身后一关上，他便重新转向那几个人。

「那些军服如今使不得了，」他以不容置辩的口气说道，「托尼，这儿有几捆腌臜衣裳。请你们大家以最快的速度换上吧。今晚，咱们这一伙看上去，得像巴黎街头上走动的任何一伙无套裤汉（sansculottes）一样污糟才成。」

这会儿，他那懒洋洋的拖腔收敛无踪了。他成了那个能思能行的人，那个把朋友们性命攥在自己掌心的大胆冒险家。

===Original===

The four men hastily obeyed. Lord Anthony Dewhurst—one of the most elegant dandies of London society—had brought forth from a dank cupboard a bundle of clothes, mere rags, filthy but useful.

Within ten minutes the change was accomplished, and four dirty, slouchy figures stood confronting their chief.

“That’s capital!” said Sir Percy merrily.

===Chinese===

四个人急忙遵办。安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵——伦敦社交界最风雅的纨绔子弟之一——从一只阴湿的碗柜里捧出一捆衣裳，净是些破布烂片，污秽不堪，却大有用场。

不到十分钟，改装事毕，四副邋里邋遢、没精打采的身影立在他们的首领面前。

「妙极了！」珀西爵士欢然说道。

===Original===

“Now for Mademoiselle de Marny.”

Hardly had he spoken when the door of the adjoining room was pushed open, and a horrible apparition stood before the men. A woman in filthy bodice and skirt, with face covered in grime, her yellow hair, matted and greasy, thrust under a dirty and crumpled cap.

A shout of rapturous delight greeted this uncanny apparition.

Juliette, like the true woman she was, had found all her energy and spirits now that she felt that she had an important part to play. She woke from her dream to realise that noble friends had risked their lives for the man she loved and for her.

===Chinese===

「如今该轮到德·马尔尼小姐了。」

他话音未落，隔壁那间屋的门就被推开，一个骇人的形影立在了众人面前。一个妇人，穿着污秽的紧身胸衣和裙子，满脸烟垢，黄发粘结油腻，硬塞进一顶脏皱的帽子底下。

迎着这副诡异的形影，爆出一阵狂喜的欢呼。

朱丽叶特到底是真正的女性，此刻既觉出自己身负重任，便把浑身的气力与精神都唤了回来。她从迷梦中醒来，方才懂得：那些高贵的友人已经为了她所爱的男人、也为了她，把性命都豁出去了。

===Original===

Of herself she did not think; she only remembered that her presence of mind, her physical and mental strength, would be needed to carry the rescue to a successful end.

Therefore with the rags of a Paris *tricotteuse* she had also donned her personality. She played her part valiantly, and one look at the perfection of her disguise was sufficient to assure the leader of this band of heroes that his instructions would be carried through to the letter.

Déroulède too now looked the ragged sansculotte to the life, with bare and muddy feet, frayed breeches, and shabby, black-shag spencer. The four men stood waiting together with Juliette, whilst Sir Percy gave them his final instructions.

===Chinese===

她没有为自己着想分毫；她只记着，要把这场营救进行到功成圆满，用得上她的临机镇定，用得上她的体力与心力。

于是，她换上一身巴黎女织工（*tricotteuse*，断头台旁织线的贫妇）的褴褛衣衫时，也把那类人物的魂魄一并穿戴在了身上。她奋勇扮演着自己的角色；这位群英之首只消看一眼她那无懈可击的化装，便足可确信：他的指示必将一字不差地贯彻到底。

德鲁莱德如今看上去也活脱脱是个褴褛的无套裤汉了：光脚沾满泥浆，短裤磨得绽边，还有一件寒碜的黑粗呢斯宾塞短外套。四个人同朱丽叶特一起立定等候，珀西爵士则向他们颁布最后的指令。

===Original===

“We’ll mix with the crowd,” he said, “and do all that the crowd does. It is for us to see that that unruly crowd does what we want. Mademoiselle de Marny, a thousand congratulations. I entreat you to take hold of my friend Déroulède’s hand, and not to let go of it, on any pretext whatever. La! not a difficult task, I ween,” he added, with his genial smile; “and yours, Déroulède, is equally easy. I enjoin you to take charge of Mademoiselle Juliette, and on no account to leave her side until we are out of Paris.”

“Out of Paris!” echoed Déroulède, with a troubled sigh.

“Aye!” rejoined Sir Percy boldly; “out of Paris! with a howling mob at our heels causing the authorities to take double precautions. And above all remember, friends, that our rallying cry is the shrill call of the sea-mew thrice repeated. Follow it until you are outside the gates of Paris. Once there, listen for it again; it will lead you to freedom and safety at last. Aye! Outside Paris, by the grace of God.”

===Chinese===

「咱们要混进人群，」他说，「人群做什么，咱们便做什么；而叫那群无法无天的家伙做咱们要他们做的事，正是咱们的本分。德·马尔尼小姐，恭喜之至。恳请您挽住我朋友德鲁莱德的手，无论何种借口，都不要松开。哎呀！我料想这差事不难，」他带着那副和蔼的笑容又道，「德鲁莱德，您的差事一样容易。我嘱托您照应朱丽叶特小姐，在我们逃出巴黎之前，千万不可离开她的身旁。」

「逃出巴黎！」德鲁莱德应声喃道，喟然发出一声忧愁的叹息。

「正是！」珀西爵士慨然接道，「逃出巴黎！身后还坠着一群嚎叫的暴民，逼得当局加倍戒备。朋友们，顶要紧的是记牢：咱们的集合暗号，是海鸥的锐鸣，连啼三遍。循着它走，一直走到巴黎城门之外。到了那里，再留意听它再度响起；它终会把你们引向自由与安全。正是！托上帝的恩典，逃出巴黎。」

===Original===

The hearts of his hearers thrilled as they heard him. Who could help but follow this brave and gallant adventurer, with the magic voice and the noble bearing?

“And now en route!” said Blakeney finally, “that ass Santerre will have dispersed the pack of yelling hyenas with his cavalry by now. They’ll to the Temple prison to find their prey; we’ll in their wake. *A moi*, friends! and remember the seagull’s cry.”

Déroulède drew Juliette’s hand in his.

“We are ready,” he said; “and God bless the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

Then the five men, with Juliette in their midst, went out into the street once more.

===Chinese===

听他说这番话时，闻者的心弦无不震颤。这位嗓音如具魔力、风仪高贵堂堂的英勇侠客，谁人能不追随？

「现在，en route（上路）！」布莱克尼最后说道，「桑特尔那头蠢驴，这会儿该已用他的骑兵把那群嚎叫的鬣狗驱散了。他们要到坦普尔监狱去找他们的猎物；咱们就跟在他们后头。*A moi*（到我这儿来），朋友们！记住海鸥的啼声。」

德鲁莱德把朱丽叶特的手拢进自己的掌中。

「我们准备好了，」他说，「愿上帝保佑红花侠。」

于是，五条汉子簇拥着朱丽叶特走在中间，再一次走出屋门，踏上了街头。

## XXIX Père Lachaise / XXIX 拉雪兹神父公墓

===Original===
It was not difficult to guess which way the crowd had gone; yells, hoots, and hoarse cries could be heard from the farther side of the river.

Citizen Santerne had been unable to keep the mob back until the arrival of the cavalry reinforcements. Within five minutes of the abduction of Déroulède and Juliette the crowd had broken through the line of soldiers, and had stormed the cart, only to find it empty, and the prey disappeared.

“They are safe in the Temple by now!” shouted Santerne hoarsely, in savage triumph at seeing them all baffled.

At first it seemed as if the wrath of the infuriated populace, fooled in its lust for vengeance, would vent itself against the commandant of Paris and his soldiers; for a moment even Santerre’s ruddy cheeks had paled at the sudden vision of this unlooked for danger.

===Chinese===
人群朝哪个方向去了，并不难猜；河对岸传来一片尖叫、嘘声和嘶哑的呐喊。
